# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2005

## Introduction by Dravida dāsa

Gratitude. And not just run-of-the-mill gratitude, but a deep, abiding thankfulness that grows with time. That is the primary sentiment devotees express toward Śrīla Prabhupāda in this year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offerings, and indeed every year. So I’d like to take this opportunity to thank Śrīla Prabhupāda myself: Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for allowing me to help produce your Vyāsa-pūjā book since 1987. Every year when the time rolls around to solicit the homages, edit them, choose and process the pictures, design the cover, lay out the text and pictures, and finally send off the book to the printer, I and the other devotees involved feel supremely blessed for this small service. It’s a great meditation on you — reading the varied voices of praise from your many followers in far-flung corners of the world, and seeing once again the images of you in a wide variety of situations during your great twelve-year-long worldwide preaching adventure starting in 1965. Thank you, thank you, for every year giving us this month of powerful and purifying absorption in your glories. We all feel spiritually refreshed, renewed, and enlivened by it.

This year we had several reports on special celebrations and increased preaching efforts inspired by your 108thappearance anniversary in 2004. From Mumbai’s reprise of your 1971 Cross Maidan pandal program to the North American Padayātrā, devotees are feeling your presence and empowerment to preach in a big way. Here is a moving excerpt from Avadhūta Śiromaṇi Prabhu, the leader of the North American Padayātrā:

To live on Padayātrā is to live by your mercy, in your association. To be on the road means to have a festival everywhere we go. We don’t need to do anything except keep moving, and just as ants come to sugar, *jīvas* come to Padayātrā to receive mercy and hear the holy name.

Nothing is lacking, and your presence is the most beautiful form of reciprocation with our endeavors.

All this is to your credit. You have faith in the program and let us participate in this eternal pastime, and even if the facilities are minimal — for example, no trucks, no advance party, only the wagon, the animals, and, most important, Their Lordships and your presence — still the success is predominant. All glories to your vision! And all glories to your mercy upon us and those living entities receiving the seeds of love along the way!

It’s easy to be thankful when things are good, but even better is to thank you for being with us through all the difficulties we have passed — those heavy moments like in North Carolina when a car hit us, or the four months we spent in a semi-arid region of Texas, or the danger of being exposed to people who want to exploit us, or more recently the passing away of Kāna (one of our four-legged Padayātrīs) in front of the Deities, leaving us by the side of the road on the coast of Mexico with no idea how to proceed.

If the glorious moments when all goes well are sweet, these difficulties are even sweeter, because in them you have showed your support and confirmed our faith that if we preach, everything will be there, even the solutions to problems that seem impossibly difficult. The mystical arrangements are always there.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being with us. It is our desire to take you not only to Vṛndāvana but around the five continents in this simple Padayātrā, walking, chanting, and depending on the mercy of the Lord.

Another heartfelt account came from Candraśekhara Prabhu, head of the ISKCON Prison Ministry. He explains how thousands of inmates are feeling the soothing waves of your mercy through your books and tapes, and expressing their gratitude in letters. He quotes in full a letter from a Bhakta Tim Shipley, incarcerated in Texas. An excerpt:

Though I have only heard Prabhupāda’s voice on tapes, he chants with me in the morning. I am delighted to have his voice resound so clear in my heart and mind. The bhajans I have heard him sing and the lessons I have heard him teach linger in my memory like the most beautiful moments I have experienced. A tear flows down my cheeks as I realize I am removed from serving Srila Prabhupada because of my sin and can only pray to someday serve his devotees. O Chandra, let us glorify our acarya together! Let’s serve Srila Prabhupada joyfully. I am his servant. Please accept my association and devotion to Srila Prabhupada. He has brought the light of the Bhagavata to me.

But the brightest highlight of this year’s book is certainly His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami’s final offering. Last year in his Vyāsa-pūjā homage he wrote:

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, many of your disciples and grand-disciples, as well as *gurukulīs,* are stuck with so many *anarthas*. As bad, or worse, some are experiencing great suffering — even more than before they joined Kṛṣṇa consciousness. For now they have so much knowledge, but too many bad habits, attachments, fears, and lack of faith.

So now I am 55 years old. I never expected to live this long. You have given me a wonderful Kṛṣṇa conscious life. I have experienced practically everything I wanted to experience in material as well as spiritual arenas. I have even just finished another 35-country world tour in your service.

So in the mood of Vāsudeva Datta, I would like to ask you, Can you arrange that their sufferings come to me so that many can be freed from their anguish and thus joyfully serve you and return to Kṛṣṇa with fewer encumbrances?

A month before Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, and several months after writing this offering, he learned he had terminal cancer. In this year’s offering he writes:

By the time this Vyāsa-pūja book comes out, unless you have changed your plans for me, I will already have been called out of this present body to assist you in the higher realms. I am trying to help as many devotees as I can before going, but how can I do this if you do not make me more of a puppet? So in this offering I say good bye to Johnny Boy, John Favors, Ghanaśyāma dāsa, Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, and Swami Kṛṣṇapāda, and I am fully ready to embrace the new (or old) identity you have provided for me.

This last phase was very difficult. I am that so-called spiritual warrior who is really just a beggar, full of gratitude, for I have been blessed by the best and have been too greedy to settle for anything less, but who is passing or have passed the final test only due to your unlimited mercy.

The often amazing pastimes we read in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* are no more astounding than some of the ways Śrīla Prabhupāda chooses to exalt his dearest devotees. And his Vyāsa-pūjā book has a small but significant role in chronicling these events in the sublime progression of Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Draviḍa dāsa  
Editor

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Mukunda Datta Dasa

Vyāsadeva is the literary incarnation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa. After the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Vyāsadeva’s vision into the future anomalies of this age prompted him to compile numerous Vedic literatures, out of the profound compassion he felt for the living beings burning in the conflagration that is material existence. Particularly in *Śrīmad-bhāgavatam*, Vyāsa showed how the material sufferings of the living entities, in fact superfluous to them, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of pure devotional service. Those who repeat and exemplify Vyāsadeva’s teachings without any adulterating changes are just as authoritative as Vyāsa himself, even thousands of years later. As Śrīla Prabhupāda states in his purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 18.75, “Actually there is no difference between hearing directly from Kṛṣṇa and hearing directly from Kṛṣṇa via a bona fide spiritual master like Vyāsa.” For ordinary persons, the purport of the *Vedas* can be revealed only by hearing submissively from such a bona fide guru in disciplic succession. This is the process of *guru-paramparā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda also explains Vyāsa-pūjā as follows in his *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya-līlā* 20.82, purport):

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura says that all writers after Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura who are pure devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and who have tried to describe the Lord’s activities are to be considered like Vyāsa. Śrīla Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura is the original Vyāsadeva in describing *caitanya-līlā*, and all others who follow in his footsteps by describing Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s pastimes are also to be called Vyāsadeva. The bona fide spiritual master is called Vyāsa because he is a representative of Vyāsa. Worshiping the birthday of such a spiritual master is called Vyāsa-pūjā.

Since no bona fide guru is functionally different from Vyāsadeva, the occasion of formal worship held annually on his appearance anniversary is rightly called Vyāsa-pūjā, the worship of Vyāsa. Thus one who worships the bona fide guru — especially Śrīla Prabhupāda — also worships all the previous *ācārya*s appearing within his disciplic succession, including Vyāsadeva himself. But why stop at Vyāsa? One worships Kṛṣṇa by worshiping those most dear to Him. Actually, no one but Kṛṣṇa can act as the *jagad-guru;* others do so as much as they represent Him in both practice and precept. On the occasion of his own guru’s disappearance in 1936, Śrīla Prabhupāda shared his profound insights into this matter.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura describes the bona fide guru as follows:

The spiritual master is receiving benediction from the ocean of mercy. Just as a cloud pours water on a forest fire to extinguish it, so the spiritual master delivers the materially afflicted world by extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence. I offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of such a spiritual master, who is an ocean of auspicious qualities. \[*Śrī Gurv-aṣṭaka* 1\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda has compared this ocean of auspiciousness to Lord Kṛṣṇa, but as Kṛṣṇa’s representative, he himself is also identified with that ocean — just as the sun and the sunshine are nondifferent. Thus Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy “droppeth as the gentle rain from heaven.” Rain clouds are formed through evaporation of water from the ocean. Every sentient being eternally belongs in the nectar ocean of *bhakti —* swimming, drinking deep, and fully relishing his or her own unique taste of service to the Lord within that ocean. Similarly, all ISKCON devotees swim in the ocean of nectarean service to Śrīla Prabhupāda and his great mission. The rain of mercy drawn from the ocean of the nectar of devotion falls upon us by the divine grace of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of Śrīla Prabhupāda, and soothes all the burning miseries in this material world.

His Divine grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is uniquely worthy of Vyāsa-pūjā worship. The reasons for this are as numerous as the offerings found within this book, and even many such books remain insufficient to fully glorify His Divine Grace. But Śrīla Prabhupāda represents both Lord Kṛṣṇa and Śrīla Vyāsadeva in two salient ways: his extraordinary compassion clearly resembles that of Lord Nityānanda, and his prolific dissemination of such works of Vedic literature as *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* make him easily comparable to Śrīla Vyāsadeva.

Generally, devotees pray for but a drop of the spiritual master’s mercy (*kṛpā-bindu*), for that alone is sufficient. Yet the world has now been given far more than a drop. Lord Nityānanda, the original guru, opened all floodgates to the ocean of love of Godhead, and largely through his representative Śrīla Prabhupāda the entire ocean has surged upon this world. The devotees are altogether submerged in this ocean of love, and even the nondevotees cannot avoid it — they too bob and float within its floodwaters. Śrīla Prabhupāda has inundated almost every place with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and it is still spreading. Like Lord Nityānanda, Śrīla Prabhupāda didn’t discriminate between who was fit or unfit, and in this mood he preached far more widely than any previous *ācārya.* Who can fathom such divine grace? It is a miraculous wonder.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books are the chief vehicle through which he extended such great mercy to the whole world, the basis of such essential preaching. Vyāsadeva kindly made the Lord’s instructions accessible to all mankind in the form of the Vedic literature, and similarly, Śrīla Prabhupāda did so for the modern age, composing his clear and impeccably authoritative Bhaktivedānta purports, which devotees study, relish, and distribute worldwide. Especially in the form of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-bhāgavatam, Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, and *The Nectar of Devotion*, Śrīla Prabhupāda culled the *creme de la creme* of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava canon and presented it in an easily understandable format that remains unparalleled both in style and content.

As if each and every such volume were another drop in his gentle rain of mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books purify all conditioned souls, pouring upon them transcendental songs about Govinda, thus increasing the limits of the ocean of transcendental bliss.

Therefore, each year all those who have been touched by such rare mercy joyfully celebrate the day of his appearance in this world as the most blessed event, Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā.

Mukunda Datta dāsa

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Anuttama dāsa

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

It is customary and appropriate to offer glorification to you, our founder-*ācārya* and spiritual master, on this your holy appearance day. It is also proper to offer appreciation for the many spiritual gifts you have given to world, and to me.

While pondering my personal transcendental debt to you, I was struck by the thought that I not only owe you for the spiritual reality you have shown me, but in fact, for all meaningful aspects of my life, for the very journey I am living. This journey, Śrīla Prabhupāda, has been enlightened, energized, and defined by you — in ways that I often overlook. This year, I wish to acknowledge my debt for these gifts. The gifts that I often forget I owe to you, the perhaps temporal yet essential gifts that make for my very life on this earth.

We aren’t supposed to wish for one, but, as I was given one by Your Divine Grace, I must thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for my “beautiful and excellent wife.” Without your blessings that transformed me, she would not have crossed my path (nor cared a fig about a materialist like me). I am grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for my wife and partner.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the wonderful son that you have given me. He will do great things for you and the world. Without your influence, he could not be part of me. Only by your kindness is he in my life. I am grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — for my son.

I have so many friends all over the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, by your grace: in Spain, Washington, India, London, Los Angeles, Australia, Hungary, Toronto, Denver, and Russia — everywhere. And I love them all dearly. I would not have such friends but for your tying the knot of devotion that binds us. Without these friends I have no life. I am so grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — for my friends.

I have a lovely house, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Without you and your service, I don’t need, and would not have accepted, such a house. It was a gift from you. It protects me, it welcomes those dear to me, and it nurtures my strength. I am grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — for my house, for your house, for Gaura-Nitāi’s house.

I have a community, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I have many communities — all over the world! Places where I am welcomed in love, fed like family, and enriched. Without you, I would never have met — or cared about — these people that now define me and uplift me. I am grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — for these sacred places, my homes away from home.

I have a business for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You opened the doors to develop it. You close the doors that threaten it. Only you know how to utilize the profits. Without you, it entangles. With you, it serves. I am grateful to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda — for showing me how to work in devotion, free from drudgery.

These are just a few of many things, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The list of your gifts forms my day. I travel. I speak. I entertain. I worship. I eat. I read. I walk. I wonder. I listen. I pray. I cry. I teach. I fear. I hope. All of it — all of it, has been inspired and shaped by you.

Before meeting you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I had no purpose. No vision, no mission, and no (real) devotion. I had no life. Without you, I have no life, nor wife, nor son, nor home, nor friends, nor community, nor business, nor mission. I would not have a reason to feel happy when I wake in the morning. I would have no reason to love and to keep faith through the darkness.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the great and transcendental things you have given me, and — for my life.

Your humble servant,  
Anuttama dāsa

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja writes in *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* “Essential truth spoken concisely is true eloquence.”

Your ability to distill sublime philosophy and down-to-earth practicality into powerful *sūtras* never ceases to amaze me.

A Sunday feast guest asked, “Why is the death rate so high in India?” You replied, “The death rate is the same everywhere, 100%.”

A Sanskrit scholar, intending to slight your disciples and showcase his own learning, inquired, “Is the Lord’s name pronounced ‘Krishna,’ ‘Krushna’, or ‘Kishna’?” You deflated his pride by saying, “Don’t worry, Kṛṣṇa knows who is calling.”

An Indian gentleman declared, “I will surrender to Kṛṣṇa sooner or later.” You turned him around to the right point by saying, “Better sooner. Do you know you have a later?”

You deflected foolish or frivolous questions with sweet aplomb. When asked, “Is the world flat?” you replied, “Everywhere I walk it is,” and then, in a knowing aside to the devotees, added “Except for New Vrindāban,” (which is renowned for its rolling hills and steep hikes).

When challenged that humankind evolved from monkeys, your challenge was “And I say you are still monkeys.”

On a morning walk, the owner of several glass factories was going on and on about his business acumen and achievements. You stopped and asked him, “Where do you get the sand, the silica, the coal for your furnaces?” When the man replied, “From God,” you said, “So you are simply a thief, stealing Kṛṣṇa’s property.” The man retorted, “No, but I give something back in charity.” You gave him an inch and said, “All right, so you’re a little thief.”

The following is one of my favorite remembrances of watching you in action.

Sitting out under the covered veranda of your Devasadhan Mandir (the Detroit temple), you met the local Catholic monsignor, who arrived in an initially cocky mood. He began by trying to make the point that the devotees spent too much time endeavoring for transcendence while the Christians were more focused on helping alleviate the poverty and sufferings of this world. Out of the blue (perhaps from your days at Scottish Churches’ College) you said, “Your Jesus Christ declared, ‘The poor will always be with us.’”

Stunned to be countered with a quote from his own tradition, and recognizing that he was confronted with a worthy opponent, the priest backed up and came in from a different angle. “Your teachings are the same as ours — the goal is the same — so you don’t really have anything to offer that is not already available in Christianity.”

You asked, “Do you agree that humankind is suffering?” The priest said, “Yes,” and you asked him, “What do you think is the cause of that suffering?” The priest said, “Separation from God.” You then asked, “And what is it that is separating us from God?” The priest answered, “Sin,” at which point you struck your hand on your desk for emphasis and declared, “That is my point. Why aren’t you teaching how to lead a sinless life?”

You went on to explain that your students live by the four regulative principles and engage in pure devotional service twenty-four hours a day, thus leading “a sinless life.” At this point the tone of the conversation turned. Our visiting priest became submissive and genuinely inquisitive.

All the while you had been plying him with pieces of *sandeśa* and *burfī,* which, to his credit, the priest took enthusiastically. In a humble mood, he asked, “I see that you have so many young followers while our seminaries and churches are becoming empty. Do you have any advice for us?”

You replied, “I understand that at your services you offer one dry wafer.” (Śrīla Prabhupāda was referring to the Christian communion service.) “Add these Bengali sweets instead and you will have full attendance.” Everyone laughed, and it became clear that you had completely won the man’s heart. As he was leaving, with a *Bhagavad-gītā* under his arm, your garland around his neck, and a box of *prasādam* in his hand, he turned in the doorway and said, “I feel like I have become one of your disciples.”

As the door closed behind him, you quoted Rūpa Gosvāmīs *Upadeśāmṛta:*

A sober person who can tolerate the urge to speak, the mind’s demands, the actions of anger, and the urges of the tongue, belly, and genitals is called a *gosvāmī*and is qualified to make disciples all over the world.

Your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust recently published the *Darśana* book. Page after page displays beautiful Deities being worshiped with great devotion in practically every corner of the world. It serves as irrevocable proof that you have literally flooded the earth with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Yours is an achievement unprecedented in Vaiṣṇava history.

You once dealt with the different camps claiming that their guru is Kṛṣṇa by saying, “You say that your guru is Kṛṣṇa. This other group says that their guru is Kṛṣṇa. Either way, the standard is Kṛṣṇa. So why not just take what Kṛṣṇa Himself says in the *Bhagavad-gītā*?”

Nowadays we have some groups claiming that only their guru can give *kṛṣṇa-prema,* while others counter that “No, only our guru is revealing the true path of pure devotion.” I humbly submit that the standard to measure by is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. No honest person can deny that you were uniquely empowered by Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and the previous *ācāryas*. You are the *senapati-bhakta,* the great general in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* army whose appearance was predicted by Lord Nityānanda. Therefore, we can be confident that if we simply and sincerely follow the path you have laid out in your books, if we serve in your ISKCON institution to the best of our ability, you will bring us to the highest level of pure devotional service, and in that process we will also be able to do great good for the world.

May I always remain in your sweet service,  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you and your mission to save the world in this Kali-yuga!

Recently I have been studying Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura’s commentary on the Tenth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and I have been deeply impressed by the faithfulness to him that you have exhibited in your purports. We see the *paramparā* system living between the two of you — even though on one level there are hundreds of years separating you, still, we can see very clearly how on the transcendental platform you are living together spiritually and sharing the same perceptions and appreciations for this great literature.

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.9.20 states,

*nemaṁ viriñco na bhavo na śrīr apy aṅga-saṁśrayā*  
*prasādaṁ lebhire gopī yat tat prāpa vimuktidāt*

**“Neither Lord Brahmā, nor Lord Śiva, nor even the goddess of fortune, who is always the better half of the Supreme Lord, can obtain from the Supreme Personality of Godhead, the deliverer from this material world, such mercy as received by Mother Yaśodā.”**

In his purport to this verse Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura states,

This verse contains three negative pronouncements: *nemam, na bhavo,* and *na śrīr*. Three repetitions emphasize the exceeding absence of mercy offered to these persons (Brahmā, Śiva, and Lakṣmī). However, with the word “indeed” (*apy*) the sentence can mean that even though they received mercy, it was not to the extent of Yaśodā.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your purport you say,

In this verse there are three negative pronouncements — *na, na, na*. When anything is uttered three times — “do it, do it, do it”— one should understand that this is meant to indicate great stress on a fact. In this verse, we find *na lebhire, na lebhire, na lebhire.* Yet Mother Yaśodā is in the supermost exalted position, and thus Kṛṣṇa has become completely subordinate to her.

Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura says,

*Vimukti* here means *viśeṣa-mukti —* “special liberation,” or *prema*. The inconceivable mercy that Yaśodā obtained from Kṛṣṇa, the giver of *prema,* was never received by Brahmā, Śiva, or even Lakṣmī-devī. It is wrong to think that Yaśodā is a *sādhana-siddha* who got a boon from Brahmā in her previous lifetime as Dharā. The *prema* of Yaśodā cannot be attained as a result of Brahmās boon, for Brahmā himself prays to become a blade of grass in Vraja. Indeed, Brahmā is counted in a much, much lower class.

You say in your purport, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The word *vimuktidāt* is also significant. There are different types of liberation, such as *sāyujya, sālokya, sārūpya, sārṣṭi,* and *sāmīpya,* but *vimukti* means “special *mukti*.” When after liberation one is situated on the platform of *prema-bhakti,* one is said to have achieved *vimukti,* “special *mukti*.” Therefore the word *na* is mentioned. That exalted platform of *prema* is described by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu as *premā pum-artho mahān,* and Mother Yaśodā naturally acts in such an exalted position in loving affairs. She is therefore a *nitya-siddha* devotee, an expansion of Kṛṣṇa’s *hlādinī*potency, His potency to enjoy transcendental bliss through expansions who are special devotees (*ānanda-cinmaya-rasa-pratibhāvitābhiḥ*). Such devotees are not *sādhana-siddha*.

Even though the idea is expressed with slightly different words, still we can see the you are completely faithful to the previous *ācārya* and have perfectly presented the same conclusion.

Your purport to 10.9.20 is just a single grain of rice in a very big pot, Śrīla Prabhupāda. This same standard of absolute faithfulness is to be found universally in all your purports, and indeed in everything you ever said or did. With your own special feelings you have taken the words of Lord Kṛṣṇa, Lord Caitanya, and our previous *ācāryas* and presented them to us, pure and unchanged.

However, there is something even more than this. Not only have you presented the teachings of your predecessors faithfully, but at the same time you have added some further enrichment through your own *kṛṣṇa-prema,* following the principle of the *śuka,* or parrot.

You once explained this principle, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

Just like a ripened fruit in the tree is already very sweet, and if it is touched by the beak of the parrot, it becomes sweeter. These are natural course. If the parrot touches the fruit, he cuts little by his beak, beak. Beak, you call beak? Then it becomes still sweeter. *Śuka-mukhād drava-saṁyutam*. Similarly, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is already sublime, transcendental. And when it is spoken through the mouth of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, it becomes still sweeter, just like the fruit. The parrot is also called *śuka*. It is called *śuka-pakṣi, śuka* bird. So this comparison is given. As the fruit becomes still sweeter by the touch of the beak of the parrot, similarly, Śukadeva Gosvāmī, because it is already spoken by Vyāsadeva, ripened fruit, the experienced contribution of Vyāsadeva, all the Vedic literatures, but when it is spoken through the *paramparā*system of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, it becomes still sweeter.

So you have taken the words of the Lord and the previous *ācāryas* as they are, but then you have imparted some of your own special sweetness to them, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we have become captivated by that.

We pray at your lotus feet that we can remain a captive audience and not become distracted by the flowery words of others. Let us follow in your footsteps, and as you maintained absolute faithfulness to our previous *ācāryas,* may we develop the same faithfulness toward you.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I Never Knew a Thief Until I Met a Vaiṣṇava  
I had been going on about life in a carefree way,  
Listening carefully to what my senses had to say,  
Wondering each moment what is there to acquire,  
Jumping hopeless hoops of material desire.  
It was a late wintry night on a street called Elm,  
When youthood was not yet yoked—in fact, was at the helm—  
When a sprightly walk turned out to be full of surprise:  
A pivotal turn meant my ambition came to a demise.  
There were four men of a different species or race,  
Each garbed in pink, with paint on part of his face.  
Tails of hair graced the back of their heads;  
They played hard on cymbals ’n’ on drumheads.  
One of their free arms flashed a book that was so thick;  
The sale went so fast and rendered so slick.  
The contents and pages were so affirmatively deep;  
In contrast the world around me came to be cheap.  
Before I had a chance to breathe, the fellows were in my home.  
It was nothing fancy, no tower and no dome,  
But their presence gave it the air of a temple.  
They gave me a taste of real wealth — yes, just a sample.  
I then turned a page in your monthly magazine;  
You appeared there awesome and thoroughly clean.  
I felt my soul being robbed of its regular routine  
And contemplating renunciation and what that would mean.  
The mind was in flux whether to turn to the engine or the trunk.  
“Shall I venture the way of the artist or the monk?  
Shall it be sculpture and picture?  
Or do I turn to divine scripture?”  
I knew that I was young and wild and required an anchor.  
Your age and wisdom spelled out a world that possessed more candor.  
What was there to lose in this deadlock situation?  
Shall I accept defeat in this critical evaluation?  
I just had to say “Yes, you can steal me away,  
And rip me free from the worldly decadent display.  
Tell me that it’s a good gang to be a part of,  
This company of thieves that administer Kṛṣṇa’s love.”

Respectfully, I never knew thieves until I met these devotees, your gang of men. They stole me away.

Thank you forever.

Your servant,  
Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Your Divine Grace’s most auspicious 108thappearance year (2004–05) was a tremendous blessing for this lowly soul.

By your divine blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata and Rome) could organize in Rome, from November 11–14, 2004, the Second International Congress on Life and Its Origin: Exploration from Science and Spiritual Traditions. The congress was a humble offering to Your Divine Grace on the auspicious occasion of your 108thappearance celebrations. Many scientists, religious scholars, and other thinkers from around the world participated in the congress, and there were thirty-seven international speakers. The Vatican Academy, under the patronage of the Pope, officially participated in this congress by sending a speaker from the Vatican Academy and fifteen other scholars from the universities run by the Vatican. The venue was the historic and prestigious Protomotecca Hall, in the very heart of Rome and with a seating capacity of 250. This venue is also used by the Nobel Peace Prize Laureates every year for their annual Nobel Peace Summit under the Gorbachev Foundation. The Honorable Mayor of Rome, Mr. Walter Veltroni, was very kind and very supportive of our conference and did not charge any fee for the hall. The hall was full every day with the delegates during the conference. There was a public lecture free of charge on November 11 at the Angelicum Vatican University Hall, with a capacity of 1,500. This is the most prestigious Vatican University in Rome.

I am happy to report that the congress was received very well by all the participants. It was patronized by the Italian president, Mr. Carlo Ezelio Ciampi. In his message (see below) he congratulated the Bhaktivedanta Institute for organizing this important conference in Rome. The Honorable Mayor of Rome, Mr. Walter Veltroni, was also very impressed by the program, and he informed the Bhaktivedanta Institute office that he desired to see it happen every year in Rome and he would fully support it.

There was a lot of interest among the participants for scientific understanding of God. Most speakers spoke with great enthusiasm on different topics of the conference. His Grace Drutakarmā Prabhu, His Grace Matsyāvatāra Prabhu, Śrīmān Jānakī-Rāma Prabhu (from the Oxford Vaiṣṇava Center), and I were among the devotee speakers.

One of the highlights of the conference was the scientific discussion about the existence of God. One of the sessions was focused on theistic interpretations of empirical results. This was the first time that we introduced a discussion on the science of God during our conference. This was done in the spirit of “life comes from life,” as kindly instructed by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

The overwhelming response we received from the participating scholars is a living example of your divine vision: “Life comes from life.”

The conference began with an auspicious multireligious invocation and ended with the *Brahma-saṁhitā* prayers sung by about twenty Italian schoolchildren especially trained for this event. The beautiful chanting of prayers surcharged the whole atmosphere with devotion, so much so that all the participants, speakers, and special guests clapped for a long time in joy and appreciation. Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your pleasure I am attaching the true copy of the message (and its English translation) sent by the President of Italy appreciating the work of your Bhaktivedanta Institute.

This year during the annual Māyāpur Festival it was extremely moving and inspiring to see many of our wonderful godbrothers and godsisters together in the holy *dhāma*. It is a wonderful gift of Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly pray for your blessings in order to carry out our humble service to please you.

Your most insignificant servant,  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

From the desk of The General Secretary; Presidency of the Italian Republic

The Second World Congress on “Life and Its Origin — Exploration from Science and Various Spiritual/Religious Traditions” represents an occasion to strengthen our commitment toward life’s values of solidarity and respect for human rights.

The confrontation and dialogue at this congress must be the instruments to realize a new humanism, one that is founded on peace and integration of the diverse identities of the world’s peoples.

In this same spirit, the President of the Italian Republic expresses his profound appreciation for the Bhaktivedanta Institute and offers his cordial salutation to the illustrious speakers, to the event’s organizers, and to all the participants of the congress. To this, I too append my personal salutation for the same.

Gaetano Gifuni  
General Secretary, Presidency of the Italian Republic

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

For many years I have offered you words of praise that I did not fully mean or understand. Last year I tried to offer myself in the mood of Vāsudeva Datta to help minimize and remove some of the suffering and obstacles so many devotees have in their pursuits of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Obviously I did not mean or fully understand this offering either; nevertheless you have been so kind to allow me to serve you with greater intensity and with more concern for others.

I want to ask you for forgiveness for the number of times my false ego dominated my relationships with the devotees, and in your service. I want to thank you for giving me such wonderful godbrothers, godsisters, disciples, nieces, and nephews to associate with. I thank you for once again allowing me to serve you, but most importantly I thank you for allowing me to stay on your team in spite of the many mistakes I made. You know all the areas I have procrastinated in and been right-out dull in. So please forgive me for this also.

By the time this Vyāsa-pūja book comes out, unless you have changed your plans for me, I will already have been called out of this present body to assist you in the higher realms. I am trying to help as many devotees as I can before going, but how can I do this if you do not make me more of a puppet? So in this offering I say good bye to Johnny Boy, John Favors, Ghanaśyāma dāsa, Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, and Swami Kṛṣṇapāda, and I am fully ready to embrace the new (or old) identity you have provided for me.

This last phase was very difficult. I am that so-called spiritual warrior who is really just a beggar, full of gratitude, for I have been blessed by the best and have been too greedy to settle for anything less, but who is passing or have passed the final test only due to your unlimited mercy.

Your son and beggar,  
Bhakti-tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

In glorification of your worldwide Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, kindly accept this poem.

Countless lives in darkness,  
Gloomy nights of yore,  
Forged hopes of liberty —  
I’ve seen that road before.  
Youth, education, brotherhood —  
Never-ending consolation?  
As time has taken its toll,  
Just another hallucination.  
Being loved by someone,  
The yearning of the soul.  
Is there anyone among us?  
Anyone at all?  
O lord and master,  
What a life of grief!  
So kindly you’ve come  
To give us relief.  
Casting your merciful glance,  
You wandered the earth.  
You’ve given everything,  
Freedom from birth.  
You’ve told us  
Not to compromise—  
It’s so hard to follow,  
So hard to realize.  
Delusion is pleading,  
Handing her petition.  
With courage we shall fight,  
Pushing on your divine mission.

Your insignificant humble servant,  
A. C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhaktivijñāna Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

I am trying to write my humble offering to you in Vṛndāvana. The birds are chirping melodiously early in the morning. The sweet chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* reverberates in the air. The Yamunā is graciously flowing through the land of Vraja, her dark waters cool and inviting. The morning breeze is blowing gently, caressing the skin. The aroma of jasmine and blossoming neem trees is intoxicating. The *mahā-prasādam* from Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma is tasty and sumptuous. Even to our dull material senses Vṛndāvana is pleasing. It is only because of you that we were able to come here and experience Bhauma Vṛndāvana in its glory. To allure us from the obnoxious pleasures of the material world, you gave us this precious gift. Will we ever be able to repay you for this gift?

But being in Vṛndāvana is also painful. Here, as nowhere else, I acutely feel the pain of my own deficiency. Despite the years of endeavor, my spiritual senses are still sleeping. Celestial Vṛndāvana is still far away from my perverted conditioned perception. I do not see the palaces made of touchstone and the *ghāṭas* made of precious jewels, I do not hear the parrots chanting Kṛṣṇa’s glories, I cannot drink the honey and juice flowing down from the slopes of Govardhana. Prabodhānanda Sarasvatī prays,

May my eyes become overwhelmed with ecstasy by seeing the nectar waves of Vṛndāvana’s beauty. May my intelligence drown in the nectar ocean of Vṛndāvana’s glories. May my body become agitated by the swiftly moving currents of ecstatic bliss and thus roll about on the ground of Vṛndāvana. Falling down like a stick, may I offer my respectful obeisances to all the residents of Vṛndāvana.

I am trying to repeat this prayer, but it inflicts great pain on my heart. Will this prayer ever be answered? Or will I always remain far, far away from the celestial Vṛndāvana?

Impatient as we are, we would have long ago fallen into the abyss of despair but for your books, where you give us solace. In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 7.18–19), in your comments on the words of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura, you describe the gradual progression of our inner development. You explain that the secret of success at every stage of devotional service is attachment and love.

By attachment to the devotional service of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, one immediately comes to the ecstatic position. When he develops his love for Nityānanda Prabhu he is freed from all attachment to the material world, and at that time he becomes eligible to understand the Lord’s pastimes in Vṛndāvana. In that condition, when one develops his love for the six Gosvāmīs, he can understand the conjugal love between Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

It is the attachment to the service of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and His preaching mission that can give us ecstasy in chanting Kṛṣṇa’s holy name. It is the attachment and love toward our spiritual master, the representative of Nityānanda Prabhu, that can free us from the humiliating attachment to dead matter and thus give us entrance to the transcendental Vṛndāvana. And finally it is the attachment and dedication to the words of the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana that can give us the coveted entrance into the pastimes of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Apathy and lack of this attachment is the sole reason for our failure.

But what is this attachment? Again, it is you who by your own example teach us what real attachment means. One of your neighbors when you lived at the Rādhā-Dāmodara *mandira* in Vṛndāvana recollects,

Many times in the middle of the night, at midnight or one or two in the morning, I would hear a voice crying from inside the courtyard by Rūpa Gosvāmīs *samādhi-mandira*. That voice was calling out and crying, but I did not know what it was because I was trying to take rest. But one night, on the full moon, I heard that voice again, so I climbed up to the roof of our house and looked down into the Rādhā-Dāmodara courtyard. I saw something amazing. Śrīla Prabhupāda was sweeping the courtyard of Rūpa Gosvāmīs *samādhi*. He was bending down with a small broom and, as he swept the ground by the *samādhi,* he was crying out, “O Rūpa! O Sanātana! O Gurudeva! Please give me your mercy. Without your mercy I cannot do anything. Give me the mercy. Give me the strength that I may fulfill your orders.”

It is again you who showed us what attachment and love really mean. They are not just a pair of cheap, hypocritical words. They are the tears of the sincere heart, the long night-vigils, the sweat of your hard work for the glorification of guru, Kṛṣṇa, and the Vaiṣṇavas, and your ardent desire to fulfill the order of your spiritual master.

I came here to the Rādhā-Dāmodara *mandira* to pray to the land that you swept. In this place the famous *vraja-raja,* the dust of Vṛndāvana, the repository of *kṛṣṇa-prema,* has become even more precious because it has been mixed with the tears of your love. Here I am praying to you, “May attachment to your teachings, mission, and example one day awaken in my heart the same attachment to Śrī Guru and Kṛṣṇa that you have. May attachment to the words emanating from your lotus mouth awaken my sleeping spiritual senses and become my passkey to the ever-blissful spiritual world of Goloka-Vṛndāvana.” What else can we do but pray to you again and again for your mercy?

Begging for your causeless mercy,  
Bhakti Vijñāna Goswami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Far more than in the 60’s and 70’s when your presence graced the West, Kali, the lord of Kali-yuga, has now firmly established his outposts. Indeed, Kali’s symptoms — anxiety, insecurity, degradation, confusion, strange diseases, sensuality, intoxication, fearfulness, and mental instability — seem to define contemporary reality. That those who grow up during these times know no other standard than this lusty, angry, greed-filled “culture” is both sad and ominous. Kali must be thrilled as he envisions the *yuga’s* bleak future. Within the midst of this confusion and degradation, you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have placed your Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, with its simple message: “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.” So simple.

Uddhava also describes the path of *bhakti* in a simple way to Śrī Kṛṣṇa in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.6.46 and 48):

Simply by decorating ourselves with the garlands, fragrant oils, clothes, and ornaments that You have already enjoyed, and by eating the remnants of Your meals, we, Your servants, will indeed conquer Your illusory energy.

O greatest of mystics, although we are conditioned souls wandering on the path of fruitive work, we will certainly cross beyond the darkness of this material world simply by hearing about Your Lordship in the association of Your devotees. Thus we are always remembering and glorifying the wonderful things You do and the wonderful things You say.

And Kṛṣṇa Himself also instructs Uddhava in a similar “simple” way: “Simply take shelter of Me alone, for I am the Supreme Personality of Godhead, situated within the heart of all conditioned souls. Take shelter of Me wholeheartedly, and by My grace be free from fear in all circumstances.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.12.15)

Yet in Kali-yuga keeping faith in these processes is difficult, for Kali relentlessly crushes both idealism and hope. Observable reasons for disbelief and faithlessness continuously confront us; faithfulness appears naive.

From where, then, does faith spring in Kali-yuga? How is it conserved and cultivated? How can I maintain the faith, strength, and surrender required to move forward and ever deepen my taste in spiritual life?

It seems, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that taking shelter of your *bhakti* gifts, i.e., attentively chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa; reading, hearing, and attempting to apply the words of the *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta;* worshiping Deities with faith; serving in the potent holy *dhāmas;* associating with sincere devotees; and preaching to fulfill your order — the same basic, “simple” processes — is the only true solution to Kali-yuga’s traps. This also seems the conclusion of all *śāstra* — and the conclusion of Śukadeva Gosvāmīs instructions to Mahārāja Parīkṣit:

For a person who is suffering in the fire of countless miseries and who desires to cross the insurmountable ocean of material existence, there is no suitable boat except that of cultivating devotion to the transcendental taste for the narrations of the Supreme Personality of Godhead’s pastimes. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.4.40\]

By your blessing, may your other followers and I stay sheltered in your service by increasingly partaking of and distributing your gifts with ever-increasing taste, gratitude, and affection.

Aspiring for your service,  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

More and more I am appreciating how you are manifesting your mercy and potency through your followers. I am so unfortunate that in many cases I do not appreciate this until they leave the planet or are on the verge of leaving us. Then I lament my misfortune at not taking full advantage of their association.

It seems that every year we are experiencing the loss of some great personality.

This year we are facing the imminent departure of Śrīla Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja, one of your most enthusiastic generals. I was shocked when I heard of his advancing cancer. I had expected to have his association for many more years.

During my recent visit with him, I was able to perceive the most wonderful Vaiṣṇava qualities in him, and by his mercy I was able to realize the eternality of your service. He allowed me to converse with him for over three hours.

In spite of his being almost completely incapacitated and dependent upon his caregiver, Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja’s consciousness was completely clear, and most amazingly he was completely absorbed in the welfare of others. I have read many times how a Vaiṣṇava is “an ocean of mercy,” but this time I saw it directly with my own eyes and heard it with my ears.

He saw his infirmity as an opportunity to help others. It seems that many persons who had not contacted him in years have contacted him and in fact have taken great trouble to see him and/or speak to him. He was utilizing this opportunity to help them. He was completely focused on their welfare, being aloof from his own bodily situation.

We had some interesting realizations about our own relationship. I had expected that my visit with him would be punctuated with intervals of extreme sorrow (on my part), as I was contemplating separation from him. Instead of this, the mood was that of two friends, one of whom would be going on a long trip but who would ultimately be together again. In fact, our final goodbye was something like “See you later,” even though we understood that “later” probably meant next lifetime.

This mood was possible because we both understood that we will be serving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, life after life together. This understanding brought great joy to me and mitigated the sorrow I would usually experience in the presence of a close godbrother who was soon to pass away.

I pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I may eternally serve in the association of your dear servants like Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja, and that I always will be part of your preaching team. To be separated from them and you would be intolerable.

Your eternal servant,  
Bīr Kṛṣṇa dās Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Although my attempt to glorify you will be pitifully inadequate, I thank you for the precious opportunity. I’ll jump at any chance to attempt reciprocation for your gift of the greatest love.

You gave me the association of saintly devotees. Certainly without their friendship and succor, the path back home, back to Godhead, is fraught with roadblocks, pitfalls, and, even worse, self-delusion. With their support, the *ahaṅkāra* wondrously shrinks, like a defeated tumor. When the spoon of Prabhupādānuga-saṅga stirs the nectar of Kṛṣṇa’s glories, then the tongue, ears, heart, and mind can rejoice.

You gave me your books. Through them you speak to me, and the Lord does too. Because of your extraordinary level of *bhakti,* Kṛṣṇa cooperates fully with your literary words and illuminates their meaning deep within the heart. As the Supersoul, He teams up with you to reveal the *bhakti-mārga* to the dedicated reader. I especially relish the unparalleled *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. You wrote in its dedication: “Presented to my friends and devotees who like to read my books and who approached me to request that I render the great *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* into English.” Each turning page elicits an aching sigh: “Will the full *rasa*-saturated depths of this text ever become known to me?” The more I enter within, the more I drown. Even just a quick dip becomes a massive immersion: preaching-*tattva, bhakti-tattva, gaura-līlā, kṛṣṇa-līlā*, and *rādhā-mahā-bhāva* all dance in the reader’s head and heart.

You gave me the *mahā-mantra*. Although from my daily *japa* it is hard to believe I am chanting the same mantra that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Haridāsa Ṭhākura, and the six Gosvāmīs vibrated, I do see the ever-increasing ocean of *nāmāmṛta* just ahead.

You gave me the best goals in life, so that I could strive beneficially and thus effectively sacrifice my insignificant energies for Mahāprabhu and you. Otherwise, as the years pass, Māyās incessant plundering would render any assets and abilities useless, and I would certainly stray and fade away. Certainly a life without a sublime mission is no life at all. You gave the best lifestyle, the best career goals, and the best organization — all for the best human spiritual development.

The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is teaching people how to come to the stage of dedicating everything to the service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. . . . If whatever we do, whatever we eat, whatever we think, and whatever we plan is for the advancement of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, this is oneness. There is no difference between chanting for Kṛṣṇa consciousness and working for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On the transcendental platform, they are one. But we must be guided by the spiritual master about this oneness; we should not manufacture our own oneness. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.15.65\]

Twenty-eight years after your departure, I don’t see the slightest material improvement in the world. Information technology has proliferated to every desktop, pocket, and palm. Quasi-spirituality abounds on every bookshelf; its so-called holistic fantasies and indulgences have become a global folk religion. Meanwhile, the individual behavior of humanity continues to slide deeper into the Kali abyss, as Western pop culture, more bewildered than ever, seeks to spellbind the entire planet.

You explained that expert management means creating a situation of interest in Kṛṣṇa even when apparently there is none. Now more than ever, let us push to return your love by giving to the world the only meaningful progress: understanding Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, in a conducive, favorable ambience and setting.

Still struggling to be your unconditional servant,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

**In the Darkest Hour**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This morning, as I was listening to you speak on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, I was struck by something you said. I had heard it quoted many times before, but it seemed to take on a new meaning: “The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will go down in history as having saved mankind in its darkest hour.”

Like many of your other preachers, I have also repeated this sentence while speaking in our temples and at outreach programs, but I have often wondered how and when this will happen. Is “darkest hour” to be understood literally? As we see Kali-yuga progress, it becomes clear that mankind is indeed heading toward its darkest hour, but when that hour will arrive is anyone’s guess.

Considering the ups and downs of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, the mundane mind could easily doubt whether ISKCON would ever have enough impact on the world to save it. But the faithful never doubt your words. That you have made this statement is itself proof that this will come to pass. Perhaps in some ways, it already has.

I once heard His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja say that ISKCON’s mission is twofold: to reclaim and engage the souls who are ready now and to plant seeds in the hearts of souls who may become devotees in the future.

By your inspiration, Śrīla Prabhupāda, millions of persons around the world are connected in some way to ISKCON, many doing active service. Millions more have accepted books, *prasādam,* and the holy name, thus paving the way to their becoming devotees in the future. And your faithful servants continue to push forward Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement year after year, so that more conditioned souls may get His mercy.

Thus we can indeed know that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your glorious ISKCON have saved and will continue to save mankind.

Some years ago, I had a dream. Hundreds of persons of all nationalities, religions, and walks of life had been displaced by a natural catastrophe. Many had lost their families and were weeping. Everyone was trembling at the thought of another devastation and what it might bring.

I saw myself dressed in *sannyāsī* clothes, holding a pair of *karatālas*. The people could see that I was a monk, and one after another they came to me, desperate for words of solace and relief. I repeated your teachings, and they listened, but they wanted something more. Then I started playing the *karatālas* and taught them to chant. The *kīrtana* lasted for hours, becoming more and more intense.

Here was the relief they needed. They were fully absorbed in hearing and chanting the holy name and beginning to transcend their bodily situation. Many had closed their eyes in meditation, and others were weeping. The *kīrtana* came to its height and then ended. I awoke in tears.

But these were tears of realization. I had seen that the holy name is perfect and complete, that everything begins and ends with the chanting of Kṛṣṇa’s holy name, that anyone who takes shelter of the name will be saved in his or her darkest hour.

I therefore pray on this auspicious occasion that you let me keep company with those great souls who have a deep taste for hearing and chanting so that I may imbibe it from them and pass it on to others. Thus I will play my small role in helping fulfill your prophecy that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will go down in history as having saved mankind in its darkest hour.

Begging to remain your humble servant,  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Her Grace Mālatī dāsī tells a nice story from when we first got our land in Māyāpur and you invited your godbrothers to meet you there. You had a small thatched hut, the one that is still there (now renovated). Mālatī was cooking for you. It was difficult for her because there was almost no money. She had to struggle to get money from the treasurer, then walk with her young daughter, Sarasvatī, to the boat, haggle with the boat people so they would accept the proper fare, take the boat across to Navadvīpa, haggle with rickshaw drivers so that they too would accept the right amount, ride to the fruit and vegetable markets, haggle with the vendors there to get the right price, and then return to cook on the single portable coal burner and the single portable kerosene burner on the floor in the corner of your hut, separated from you by a plain cloth “curtain.” Since the devotees didn’t have a refrigerator then, whatever she needed she had to buy fresh daily — and thus go through the same struggles every day. Every day Mālatī was on the verge of collapsing, but then so were most of your disciples, dedicated as they were to your service in spite of all material impediments and inconveniences — living in tents, eating the local food, getting dysentery, and so on.

Mālatīs constant meditation was how to make the *prasādam* come out nice so that you would be pleased. And every day you would point out the defects in her service, so that she could improve. But she felt that you were criticizing her. And she felt that she could never get it right. Then one day she thought that she had finally managed to cook everything properly and arrange the plate just to your standard. She brought the plate of *prasādam* to you, but when she came to remove it you said, “There is too much salt”— not that there was too much salt in the food, but that there was too much salt placed on the plate with the pepper and ginger and lemon slices. So she concluded, “I can never get it right. I should find someone else who can do the service better.” Immediately she felt relieved, and she decided, “I’ll tell Śrīla Prabhupāda tomorrow.”

The next day, Mālatī heard that some of your godbrothers were coming to join you for lunch, so she assumed that she would not cook that day, because you had been under criticism from your godbrothers for keeping women in your ashrams and for having a woman cook. But you called her to tell her what items you wanted her to cook that day, as usual. She was dutiful, so she decided, “Well, I’ll just cook today, and then I’ll tell him tomorrow.” So she cooked.

When the *prasādam* was ready, Mālatī lifted up the cloth that separated the work area from your room. There she saw you sitting on the floor at the end of the room, with two of your godbrothers on either side. You appeared to her like a diamond in a gold setting, flanked as you were by your esteemed godbrothers. And she felt intimidated by the presence of such venerable Vaiṣṇavas.

Already on her knees, she pulled her sari over her head and arms so that only her hands showed, and with her knees and elbows on the floor she carried your plate of *prasādam* with her outstretched hands, practically crawling between your godbrothers to approach you, her spiritual master.

Then Your Divine Grace said to your godbrothers, “Yes, she cooks for me . . . and I criticize her. But she would slit her throat for me, and I would do the same for her.”

When she heard that, Mālatī almost collapsed on the floor. That was more than any reciprocation she could have ever hoped for or even imagined.

And what Your Divine Grace had said was true. We were ready to do anything for you, in part because we knew you would do anything for us, and in part because you taught us by your words and example that you would do anything for your spiritual master — and what the result of such service is.

On this auspicious occasion, we pray to you to give us the strength to be ready to do anything for you — and the intelligence to know what you would want us to do.

By sincere service to the spiritual master, one achieves the highest perfection.

yat-sevayā bhagavataḥ kūṭa-sthasya madhu-dviṣaḥ  
rati-rāso bhavet tīvraḥ pādayor vyasanārdanaḥ

“By serving the feet of the spiritual master, one is enabled to develop transcendental ecstasy in the service of the Personality of Godhead, who is the unchangeable enemy of the Madhu demon and whose service vanquishes one’s material distresses.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.7.19)

In his *Bhakti-sandarbha* (*Anuccheda* 244), Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī comments on this verse: “The word *tīvra* (‘intense’) hints at the special result of personal service, compared to that of mere association.” Citing verse after verse, Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī glorifies the results of associating with pure devotees (*sat-saṅga*), which is the only process that can give one the strong love that can control Kṛṣṇa. Though hearing and chanting in the association of pure devotees (*prasaṅga*) is powerful and beneficial, personal service rendered to pure devotees (*paricaryā*), combined with chanting and hearing, brings “an exceptional result” (*viśiṣṭaṁ phalam*): even greater love for Kṛṣṇa.

We pray that we will one day develop such pure, ecstatic love for you and Kṛṣṇa. Only then will all of our practices and sacrifices be completely successful — by your divine loving grace.

Aspiring to be your fully surrendered, loving servant — and the selfless servant of your dear, most beloved servants,  
Girirāj Swami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. They are the cooling shade to save us from the scorching heat of material existence.

The quality of *vādanya,* magnanimity, is most readily found in your person, and permanently instilled in your mission. That beneficence is the mainstay and beacon of my hope to become your useful servant one day.

Beyond my personal aspiration and selfish claim on your unlimited munificent gift of devotional service, you are the emblematic instrument of *kṛṣṇa-prema-pradāya te*. Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī says of Lord Caitanya,

*anarpita-carīṁ cirāt karuṇayāvatīrṇaḥ kalau*  
*samarpayitum unnatojjvala-rasāṁ sva-bhakti-śriyam*  
*hariḥ puraṭa-sundara-dyuti-kadamba-sandīpitaḥ*  
*sadā hṛdaya-kandare sphuratu vaḥśacī-nandanaḥ*

**Lord Caitanya is making available to all what no other** ***avatāra*** **has given before, something beyond even what the holy name usually bestows in other Kali-yugas: the splendorous jewellike mellow of devotional service.**

Although it is available to all, you have delivered it to their doorstep. You carefully unpackaged the inaccessible nature of pure devotional service. Then you literally served it to the unwanting and undeserving population without discrimination. Such an incomparable act of generosity has not previously been witnessed by this world. Many saints, and even *avatāras,* have come to save the masses, but only Lord Caitanya gave *kṛṣṇa-prema*. Innumerable soldiers have given their lives for others. Christ gave his for the sake of all. But only you have combined all these sacrifices into one act of mercy: your selfless offering to Kṛṣṇa.

I submit my ongoing petition that you imbue me with your causeless mercy. My prayer may seem contradictory, in that I have described you as an endless fountainhead of benefaction. However, in spite of your largesse, I remain reluctant to change. As Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura has said:

*nāṭheliho rāṅgā pāy,*  
*tomā bine ke āche āmāra*

**Please do not push me away, I have no other shelter than you.**

Your aspiring, yet still unworthy, servant,  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I offer my sincere obeisances at the lotus feet of my pure spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As we grow and mature in our knowledge of the material and spiritual worlds, one simple, central fact remains constant in our lives: it is by the great mercy, kindness, and wisdom of Śrīla Prabhupāda that we will achieve real success and happiness in life.

His greatest gift is knowledge of himself, for to know even partially the exalted nature of the Lord’s pure devotee is to know Kṛṣṇa in the most sublime way. Indeed, one cannot know Kṛṣṇa without knowing His pure devotee.

To dedicate our lives wholeheartedly to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission is the natural, satisfying response to his glories. Gratitude, prudence, bliss, and love all compel us to put aside our trivial pursuits and strive for his selfless, incomparable, contagious pleasure.

His eternal servant,  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The fact that I am still here trying to write something to glorify you is proof of your causeless mercy. I cannot begin to comprehend anything of your transcendental potencies, but I want to thank you for keeping me in the association of your followers, who are daily increasing in number. In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.5.43 you write the following:

I have traveled all over the world two or three times a year, and although I am provided comfortable places to stay wherever I go, I cannot stay anywhere for more than three days or a week. I do not mind this curse by the parents of my disciples, but now it is necessary that I stay in one place to finish another task — this translation of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. If my young disciples, especially those who have taken *sannyāsa,* take charge of traveling all over the world, it may be possible for me to transfer the curse of the parents to these young preachers.

It seems as though I have been able to fulfill at least this desire of yours. I cannot stay anywhere for very long. Your movement is growing, and the members are always calling us. Everywhere I go, devotees want to hear about my personal association with Your Divine Grace. I have to happily tell them that you kindly gave me your association in the form of your books, and that such intimate association is available to everyone. When I first joined, I often heard that you had said, “My books are better than I am.”

You have given so much, and I want to thank you. I have learned in my travels to say “Thank you” in many different languages. It is interesting. French: *merci* (“mercy”); Portuguese: *obligato* (“I am obliged”). I mean all of these things when I say “Thank you” to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

1\. You gave us yourself. You lived among us, tolerating unlimited disturbances to show us practically how to execute *bhakti-yoga*.

2\. You gave us Kṛṣṇa Himself.

a. The *mahā-mantra*

1\. Your beautiful, ecstatic *kīrtanas*

2\. *Japa* — the magic of sixteen rounds. That is not so difficult a quota, but enough that it is not too easy.

b. The *Bhagavad-gītā,* the spoken instruction of Lord Kṛṣṇa — *as it is*

c. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.* You dedicated so much energy to revealing the literary incarnation of Godhead to the world, both in print and by speaking daily on the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. I have to especially thank you for making sure that your lectures were recorded. Every time I read or listen to your lectures, I hear so many things that I want to share, and everything is always new and fresh.

d. You also gave us Kṛṣṇa in His Deity form.

e. And you gave us Kṛṣṇa in the most easily relishable and distributable form: *prasādam.*

Thank you especially for your Bhaktivedanta purports, which make all the abovementioned things available to us. Your purports are brilliant poetic lights that reveal the essential truth of Kṛṣṇa in everything.

Thank you for giving us such a great mission, where we can never feel that we have done enough. Your vision is so great and so variegated. While I struggle just to follow the basics, you are always “whipping” us to do more. You give us the challenge.

Simply we, a few members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, we are going door to door and trying to convince them: “Sir, you are not this body.” They don’t care for it. “I am . . . I am this body. I am Mr. John.”“I am Englishman,”“I am American,”“I’m Indian.”“You say ‘I’m not this body.’” So very difficult task. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, to push on, requires great patience, perseverance, and tolerance. But Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s order, *tṛṇād api sunīcena taror api sahiṣṇunā/ amāninā mānadena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*.

So those who have taken to Kṛṣṇa consciousness preaching work, they should always know that things are not going to happen so easily. *Māyā*is very, very strong. Very, very strong. But still, we have to struggle against *māyā*. It is a declaration of war with the *māyā*. *Māyā*is keeping the living entities under her control, and we are trying to save the living entity from her . . . That is the difference. *Kālo vaśī-kṛta-visṛjya-visarga-śaktiḥ*. This *śakti, visarga-śaktiḥ,* is very, very strong, but it is under the control . . . Although it is very . . . she is very, very strong, but she is under the control of Kṛṣṇa. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.22, Māyāpur, February 29, 1976\]

I thank you for struggling so blissfully and pray that I will never give up the struggle of trying to follow your sublime example.

Thank you for giving us the four regulative principles. I was a slave to unregulated life, and when I heard these rules I felt that the doors to freedom were opened.

Thank you for creating ISKCON, thereby creating shelter for so many fallen souls all over the world.

Thank you for giving us the holy *dhāmas* of Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana, and for arranging the yearly Gaura-Pūrṇimā Festival. You will be happy to know that this festival is increasing yearly. This year brought together many of your disciples who had not met each other for over twenty-five years. We spent days simply relishing memories of Your Divine Grace.

Thank you for personifying all the good qualities of a devotee. I tend to be very proud, but hearing about and remembering your life with us quickly smashes that pride. The first of the twenty-six qualities of a devotee is *kṛpālu,* “kind to everyone.” You perfectly personified that quality, especially by giving us the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Thank you for that, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The public chanting of the holy name and book distribution are the ultimate expressions of kindness, benevolence, friendliness, and compassion. Thank you for allowing me to be some small part of the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Thank you for the giving us the Pañca-tattva. From the beginning you gave us the Pañca-tattva mantra, which makes it possible for us to approach the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* and now the appearance (according to your order) of the life-size Pañca-tattva Deities in Śrīdhām Māyāpur is having an amazing effect on your ISKCON society.

I pray that I can become truly grateful and really surrender and become your dog, loyal and ready to give my life.

Your aspiring servant,  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet.”**

**“Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your dearest lotus feet. All glories to your sublime appearance day.

Upon arriving in the USA, you wrote down your realizations of how difficult the preaching in the Western countries would be:

*rajas tamo guṇe erā sabāi ācchanna*  
*bāsudeb-kathā ruci nahe se prasanna*

**“Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva. I do not know how they will be able to understand it.” (*****Markine Bhāgavata-dharma*** **3)**

Today the rampant madness of the materialists has reached unprecedented peaks, featuring a wide variety of absurd goals and delirious proclamations. One would be amused to hear such ridiculous things if it weren’t for the dangerous repercussions that they have on the lives of so many believing conditioned souls.

“We will be able to live and enjoy for up to five thousand years in the near future,” says one scientist. “Having identified the gene that causes the aging of the cells, we will not grow old anymore.”

“I will perpetuate myself eternally,” says another scientist. “By cloning the cells of my semen and injecting them into my wife, I will be born again as her (and my) son. Then I will repeat the process over and over again: I will never die!”

No knowledge of the soul whatsoever, total darkness about the laws of nature, and unfathomable distance from Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Person.

The demonic mentality has taken root in all fields of life — religious, social, political, intellectual — taking the shape of obsessive care of the body and unshakable hope for its perpetuation, along with the belief that complete freedom to enjoy the senses and mind is the platform of true happiness.

As we observe such a distressing scenario of inveterate viciousness, a tremendous sense of disheartenment and resignation rises within my immature heart.

How will the population be able to accept this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement? How will I, a tiny aspiring servant, be able to maintain my commitment to boldly preach the message of Godhead with an undeterred mind?

The answer :

*tabe jadi taba kṛpā hoy ahaitukī*  
*sakal-i sambhava hoy tumi se kautukī*

**“But I know Your causeless mercy can make everything possible because You are the most expert mystic.” (*****Markine Bhāgavata-dharma*** **4)**

Kṛṣṇa’s absolute mystic potency has indeed revealed, in the most expert way, the embodiment of His causeless mercy: A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, the founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

As Dhruva Mahārāja was able to dispel the all-enveloping magical trickery of the Yakṣas, so you are piercing through the monumental hoax enacted by the mediocre servants of Kali. You shine perpetually in the form of your instructions, which will ever triumphantly dominate this desolate plane of material existence.

I hereby confirm my implicit faith in your books as the necessary and sufficient cause for changing the hearts of the conditioned souls, and for attracting them to appreciate again the message of Vāsudeva.

Please protect your servants, the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, from the all-devouring sinful Age of Kali. In particular I pray that none of us may ever become a victim of the vainglory of thinking ourselves somewhat comparable to you in expanding the movement of Lord Caitanya, or being the authors of our devotional service.

When I was a child my grandfather had a big Alfa-Romeo car, and on the dashboard he had placed a plastic steering wheel so that I could “drive” the car. Oh, I felt like an ace racing driver! But all along he was driving, choosing where to turn, at what speed to drive, and where to go.

This is the reality of our spiritual life: every move we make, every goal we embrace, every breath we take that has some significance in Kṛṣṇa’s service has been granted by you alone.

In the material field I may now have my driver’s license and be an autonomous driver, but in the spiritual realm of devotional service you are the founder-*ācārya* of every instant of my progress, eternally.

Inconceivably, the perfect fulfillment of my aspirations as a free individual, and my total belonging to you, my eternal master, are absolutely reconciled.

*kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāro,*  
*tomāra śakati ache*  
*āmi to’ kāṅgala, ‘kṛṣṇa’ ‘kṛṣṇa’ boli’,*  
*dhāi tava pāche pāche*

**“Kṛṣṇa is yours; you have the power to give Him to me. I am simply running behind you shouting ‘Kṛṣṇa! Kṛṣṇa!’”**

Thank you also for having manifested, through a most wonderful group of dedicated devotees, the book *Darśana;* it is truly a celebration of your love for Kṛṣṇa and your mercy on the conditioned souls.

Your unworthy servant,  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

My dear spiritual master, father, and beloved bestower of truth, knowledge, and love of Godhead to the fallen and conditioned souls,
