Please accept my humbly offered obeisances to you, forever and always. You are the divine life and shelter of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, which is a beacon of light for the next ten thousand years in the Age of Kali.

This year, your gift of love to all of us seems to have taken on a deeper, more significant reality in our lives, as many of your sons and daughters have departed or are beginning to depart from this mortal world. It is actually quite wonderful to see how they have embraced your teachings, departing with a peaceful, almost joyous demeanor, remembering you and Kṛṣṇa. One godsister expressed this to me shortly before she passed: “I am glad to be prepared for this moment by the last thirty years of practice, which he \[you\] kindly instilled in our hearts. Because of him, I have no fear at all.” She was very tranquil, without anxiety, simply chanting and reading *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* in which she now discovered even deeper meanings and truths.

In some instances, the departure has become a practical demonstration of your love, along with the love developed by your dear son or daughter. That love is now being transmitted to all of us in terms of bittersweet, loving last-minute lessons. It is so intimate and intense that tears form, waiting to be released, while all the while it is also wonderful that we don’t have to be bewildered by the many pains afflicting the material body as it nears the end of its term. Your sons and daughters are entering into that experience with a mood of surrender, service, and determination similar to the mood you showed throughout your own exalted departure.

Although we have the fullest conviction that all these great souls are heading toward a sublime destination near your lotus feet, we have to admit that it deeply affects our hearts when those who have helped, guided, and loved us depart or are departing. You left us behind with your love, your teachings, and one another to carry on your mission. They are also leaving behind personal legacies to fulfill and add to your glory. But in both cases the departures are attended by a great sense of sorrow and farewell.

I meditate on what I can do for you now, in the present, praying to serve those who, without deviation, are following your orders in loving devotion. I hope to satisfy you and them and to learn from them how to serve you. I admit to the further desire of one day being personally with you and my godbrothers and godsisters in the ISKCON that you told us would exist in the spiritual world. If you would like me to do something special for you at this time, I am simply awaiting your order.

From one of the many fallen *jīvas* in this world who loves you very much,

Your eternal servant,  
Mālatī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. My offering this year is a remembrance of a conversation we once had. Sometimes I refer to you as “he” for the sake of writing. Please forgive me for this somewhat impersonal approach. This is how I might explain it to someone who wanted to know why we have a sixteen-rounds-per-day requirement. The dialogue went something like this:

“What is your definition of Kṛṣṇa?” you asked, a little playfully.

“Kṛṣṇa is God,” I answered. “He is the Supreme Being, and our duty is to worship Him.”

You looked at me hard. And I looked back at you. I could have said a lot more. But I didn’t think my talk would have been penetrating, intellectually. You didn’t ask me any more questions. I presumed I had passed the test, but probably not with high marks. You seemed concerned about my spiritual acumen. We stared at each other for a long time.

We were at Paradisio, California, in a beach house. It was July, 1967.

Then you looked me right in the eye and said, “You must chant sixty-four rounds every day.”

I went numb. *This*, I thought, *would be impossible. Why is he making such a demand? Was I so inept that I had to chant forty-eight more rounds than he had initially prescribed, than what I remembered I had actually vowed to do? Was this the remedy for my ignorance? Perhaps this was my punishment for the other day when I asked what would happen when he died.* I was in a panic.

It seemed you were singling me out because of spiritual deficiency. It seemed unjust. I had made a vow, but maybe you were starting a new standard, and maybe it wasn’t only meant for me. *That* would be a little relief, but I still couldn’t accommodate the idea of chanting for what would amount to eight hours a day.

My brain was turbulent. *How would I do it? I would have to change my entire lifestyle*. I began to think of what it would take to chant that many rounds every day. It was scary. I waited for you to take the new directive further. You would perhaps emphasize the importance of chanting and would explain how it would work. *Or he might even ask me if I thought this was OK or possible.* Dumbstruck and staring, I waited. I wasn’t thinking of any counterproposals. I was just paralyzed, waiting.

I thought of how difficult it was for me just to chant the sixteen rounds on beads each day. Sometimes I got up before sunrise in my San Francisco apartment. I tried to stand and chant with eyelids drooping, knees buckling and my mind going blank. I learned to sleep standing up. Once I fell onto the wall, and hit my head against it. I almost fell on the floor that morning.

Suddenly you said, “Then you must chant at least thirty-two rounds.”

Again silence. A little relief. My mind went to work again. I began trying to calculate. *Hmmmmm. Thirty-two, eh? That was way better than sixty-four. Maybe I could do that*. I tried to think how it would work. You had reduced the number by half. I wondered if you were testing me. You seemed to be getting more and more serious. You tilted your head back and continued to look through me. I thought maybe I could manage to double my chanting, even though I didn’t know exactly how. I was beginning to resign myself to it.

It had become a staring contest. I couldn’t talk. There was some rustling in the kitchen and a barely audible male voice talking to someone. I heard some pots clang. They were too far away to hear. Some birds warbled in a nearby tree, and the seagulls squawked continuously. Waves pounded the sand deafeningly. In the back of my mind, I was hoping you would cut the new number in half again. Maybe a pattern was being established. So I waited.

Then you said, “You must chant at least sixteen rounds per day.”

“Yes,” I answered immediately. I think I sounded like a new Marine answering in obedience to his drill instructor. It was a confirmation of a former promise. I felt comfortable with that. I felt that I could just manage sixteen on a daily basis, and that you were considerate to allow that only a fourth of your earlier demand be honored. I couldn’t believe I spoke so loudly, because right after I said “Yes” the room went silent, except for the gulls and the ocean roar. Maybe you’d been testing an idea out on me.

I figured that although sixteen rounds *was* a vow, it was a minimum and less than the ideal of sixty-four. Sixteen rounds was the minimum requirement, and that has never changed.

Your servant,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master,

As time goes by, we come to recognize more and more all the marvelous feats you have performed in this world. You have helped us understand the importance of book distribution. You have also taught us how important it is to seek the association of sincere devotees who endeavor to follow your instructions. They are true examples of practical *bhakti-yoga,* and just as you have explained in your books and classes, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is an effective and perfect process.

Humanity is suffering dreadfully from the results of how the modern society is organized, but the masses of people will eventually understand and praise your vision of how the world should be. In the long run, they will comprehend the truth behind your teachings. They will come to understand how the chaos resulting from the current system of social organization can be counteracted by the solutions you have presented.

Never in the history of Western civilization has this revelation and opportunity been given. The scientists have no idea whatsoever of the ancient Vedic civilization, and so they cannot understand how the people of that civilization had access to such sublime knowledge. Yet you have come to prove to the world through your teachings that this knowledge can be disclosed to anyone, and that anybody can become happy in both this life and the next. This is one of your great accomplishments.

Your books and other teachings clearly indicate how we should follow the spiritual master’s instructions, for if we do so, Kṛṣṇa will grant us all the necessities for propagating the *saṅkīrtana* movement. Therefore, I beg for your mercy on this very sacred day of your appearance. Although I am limited and helpless, I beg you for the shelter of your lotus feet. Please help me cross the ocean of repeated birth and death and become an instrument of your preaching mission.

Your eternal and worthless servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am a lost soul who has been rotating in the cycle of birth and death since time immemorial. You gave the example of a thirsty animal in the desert who thinks he sees an oasis in the distance and runs there, only to find it is a mirage. Again he thinks he sees another oasis and runs there, but again it turns out to be a mirage. In this way I have been taking birth in different species of life, thinking each time that “in this body I will surely find happiness,” only to be disappointed again and again.

The same thing happened many, many times even in this life. I dreamt I would find happiness and enjoyment in the material world by going to a particular place, or by being with a particular person, or by doing a particular activity, but each time I was unable to find the real happiness and satisfaction I was looking for. The reason for this, as you explained, is that it is incongruous for the soul to try to find satisfaction in matter, since spirit and matter are different kinds of energies. The soul will never be able to find the full satisfaction he is looking for anywhere in the material world, in any kind of body or by any kind of arrangement. It is simply not possible. He will find satisfaction only when he becomes reconnected with the Supreme Soul, Kṛṣṇa.

However, this idea never occurs to the conditioned souls because their intelligence is covered by the illusory energy of the Lord. In this connection you gave the example of a man living in a dark cave. He cannot see the sun, he cannot see himself, he cannot see others, and he also cannot see how to get out of the cave. However, if by good fortune someone with a lamp comes into the cave and leads him outside, then he can see the sun, he can see himself, and he can see others. You gave this analogy to show how the spiritual master enlightens the conditioned souls by bringing them out of the darkness of material existence into the sunlight of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

This analogy is most certainly applicable in my case. Prior to hearing Your Divine Grace, I tried to find material happiness in various ways, and when I ended up frustrated, I tried to follow various kinds of so-called spiritual paths, all to no avail. But when I came in contact with you — first through your books, then through your disciples and movement, and then personally — I got the opportunity for real enlightenment, the opportunity to find real satisfaction, not just theoretically but practically, as a complete way of life. I cannot say that I am following your instructions perfectly, but even imperfect following brings so much spiritual benefit and satisfaction to the soul, and by constant and appropriate practice I hope to one day attain the goal you have set for us — pure love of Kṛṣṇa and going back to His eternal spiritual abode. Although in one sense this is still a distant dream, in another sense, by your causeless mercy, we are able to make tangible progress toward this goal by following the process of devotional service you have taught us, and thus we conditioned souls can at least partially experience the genuine happiness that has been eluding us for so long, despite our endeavors to search it out in every nook and corner of this material world. At the same time, we may hopefully be able to do something useful for other conditioned souls by helping you to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

On this most auspicious day, therefore, I pray to Your Divine Grace to kindly keep me engaged in your service, since otherwise I do not see any useful purpose for my wretched existence.

Your insignificant servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

In one of your purports you write,

Everything is possible for a pure devotee because he acts under the instructions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Through His inconceivable energy, a pure devotee can perform tasks that are supposed to be very, very difficult. He can perform tasks not even previously performed by the Lord Himself.

Although you are speaking generally, about all pure devotees, we, your disciples, think this statement describes your own incredible achievement of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We give you all credit, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for attaining what was rarely contemplated before. As the years roll by, I become more and more in awe at your surrender, sacrifice, tolerance, and determination in accomplishing your mission.

In another purport you state,

The devotee always thinks of himself as instrumental for anything successfully carried out, and he declines to take credit for anything done by himself.

However, elsewhere you write,

Every plan is made by the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but He is so kind and merciful to His devotees that He wants to give the credit to His devotees who carry out His plan according to His desire.

Not only has the Lord given you credit, Śrīla Prabhupāda, but also so much glory for what in the beginning was a thankless and difficult task.

In the early years you wrote in your diary:

According to Mayapur Panjika today is the Adhivas day of Gour Purnima. Devotees at Vrindaban and Nabadwipa are enjoying the celebration. I am here alone without any devotee companion.

And from another part of your diary:

There was no response from the visitors invited to come and join Hari Kirtan this evening at 7/30 pm. But I alone executed the Kirtan ceremony with my T.R. till 10 pm. - New York 1966

This seemed to be a recurring theme in the beginning, Śrīla Prabhupāda. One time you mentioned that for days you could not go out because of snow and severe weather conditions. Normally, anyone else would have become discouraged and depressed and given up the attempt. But not you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are not an ordinary person. You are the *śaktyāveśa avatāra* of the Lord sent by Him to save us. I can understand that even the difficulties were a plan of the Lord to glorify you because we ordinary living entities become morose at the slightest inconvenience. However, your example gives us renewed enthusiasm to go on. You are so wonderful, Śrīla Prabhupāda! We are eternally in your debt.

As time went on, the Lord unfolded your *līlā* in another way, which is evident from this statement in your diary:

In the evening there was class and it was better than all previous classes. There was attendance of about twenty and the contribution was $19.00.

People were inevitably becoming attracted. It is said that a great man accomplishes great tasks but appreciates even the smallest contribution. Here is what you also wrote: “Jones presented me a copper cup. Very nice. Thanks.” And “Bob came with some flower and Sujee. I required some flour and Kṛṣṇa sent it.”

Though by now you were starting to achieve success, you always remained a humble Vaiṣṇava servant of the Lord. A conditioned soul becomes puffed up very easily, but a pure devotee’s qualities shine through no matter what the circumstance. You are a beacon in the dark night of Kali-yuga, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and those who are fortunate and intelligent will follow you. Sadly, there are many misguided people who cling to their questionable materialistic values. Generally they refuse to take up any spiritual path at all, what to speak of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

However, that can completely change if we, your followers, try to develop even a small fraction of your all-inspiring qualities.

You write in the introduction to the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

If I have any credit in this connection, it does not belong to me personally, but it is due to my eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda. If personally I have any credit in this matter, it is only that I have tried to present *Bhagavad-gītā* as it is, without any adulteration.

This should be our attitude, Śrīla Prabhupāda: if we are to have any credit, it will be when we follow in your footsteps implicitly, without adulteration.

Your worthless servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You are my eternal master. I am your eternal servant. In my service to you, I am also initiating disciples. Daily I pray to you to remain your disciple, for only by always being your servant can I act as “master” for others. For I heard you say once, in response to praise, that your only credit is that you never tried to become the master, but always remained the servant. I want to be the servant of that servant.

At this time, I beg to put before you an address I gave to my disciples on the day they gathered to honor me as the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva. Now I submit this formally to you, on this occasion, so that you may assess the quality of my discipleship.

Here it is:

“In a well-known letter describing the management he wanted to see in ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda remarked that our movement is meant for training devotees to be ‘independently thoughtful.’ I have been asked to discuss this important point.

“What does it mean to be ‘independently thoughtful’? It doesn’t mean to be whimsical—arbitrary in one’s decisions, or indulging in mental speculation.

“What is the meaning of ‘independent’ for us, for disciples? For instance, in 1976 I was requested by *Back to Godhead* magazine to interview Śrīla Prabhupāda. This was the year of the American bicentennial, and the editors wanted to publish a bicentennial issue. I was to interview Śrīla Prabhupāda for this special issue. In the course of the interview, I was talking about the Declaration of Independence, and Prabhupāda said, ‘We are declaring our dependence on Kṛṣṇa, not our independence.’ Of course, it is certainly essential to a devotee’s way of being to feel himself or herself always dependent on Kṛṣṇa, dependent on the spiritual master. Yet, on the other hand, Śrīla Prabhupāda says to be ‘independently thoughtful.’ How do you reconcile these two ideas? Some devotees have asked me about this.

“We receive knowledge and information from Kṛṣṇa, through the *śāstras,* through the spiritual master, through great devotees. *Guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra* — this is where our knowledge comes from. If we try on our own to gain knowledge in this world with our defective senses, our limited perspective, and our various kinds of mentalities, we will not be successful. Prabhupāda says that perfect knowledge comes from the perfect person — that is, Kṛṣṇa. We are not supposed to be independent of Kṛṣṇa in our knowledge and in our decision-making. When we become mature in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, we can act autonomously and, at the same time, be in perfect harmony with the instructions of Kṛṣṇa and the instructions of the spiritual master.

“The spiritual master — who conveys the messages of previous *ācāryas* and of Kṛṣṇa — is described by Śrīla Prabhupāda as being an external manifestation of the Supersoul. This means that what the spiritual master says is identical with what Kṛṣṇa is saying in the heart.

“Studying the *Bhagavad-gītā* is different from studying some mundane production of knowledge. What Kṛṣṇa is saying to Arjuna in the *Bhagavad-gītā* is really just what Kṛṣṇa is telling us from within our own hearts. Kṛṣṇa is in the heart as the Supersoul, and as such, he gives direction to the living being, direction how to make advancement in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

“In the beginning, our material contamination makes us unable to hear the Supersoul. Therefore we have to approach the spiritual master to find out what it is that Kṛṣṇa wants. We should in time gain the maturity to take the instructions of the spiritual master and make them our own.

“*Cittete koriyā aikya*, the prayer says: ‘Make my intelligence become one with the instructions of the spiritual master’ — that is the literal translation. I assimilate or internalize these instructions, instructions which are not something foreign or alien, not something coming in from the outside being imposed on me. Rather, they are the same instructions that Kṛṣṇa is giving me from within the heart.

“When I have made the teachings of the spiritual master my own, and all else is excluded, only then can I act on my own. I can think and act autonomously or independently, yet everything I say or do will be in accord with those instructions. That’s maturity in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and that is what Prabhupāda meant by ‘independently thoughtful.’ I have to be mature enough that when I am confronted with a new situation, I will be in a position to be able to receive directions from the Supersoul as to what to do.

“In this world, we are always finding ourselves in novel situations. Now, how in this situation am I able to apply Kṛṣṇa consciousness? We have the spiritual master to give us these directions. For eleven years in this movement, we had Śrīla Prabhupāda’s manifest presence. Now Śrīla Prabhupāda is no longer here in person to say, ‘Do this, do that,’ or to correct us when we make a mistake. Śrīla Prabhupāda was certainly preparing us for this time; you can see it in Prabhupāda’s career. He tried more and more to detach himself from day-to-day management, to give general principles and let his disciples learn how to apply them. He knew he wasn’t going to be around forever and that we would have to carry on without him.

“That process has to continue also with you who are my disciples. You should, each of you, become ‘independently thoughtful.’ It is not my intention to micromanage the day-to-day lives of my disciples. I wouldn’t want to do that. Sometimes it does become necessary, but that is not the best situation. It is just like when somebody is a child, parents have to guide him, but the parents expect him in time to become mature — when he is able to act on his own because the child has taken in — assimilated or internalized — the parents’ instructions. This is the general process in human life. That is what Prabhupāda wanted — for us to become mature and become ‘independently thoughtful.’

“In order to be in this position, to act properly in a mature way, we have to be following strictly the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Spiritual knowledge, or realized knowledge, has a special characteristic. For it to flow, for it to come to us from the Supersoul, we have to be in the proper condition of life. We have to be in the correct existential condition. We have to be acting in such a way that we are always trying to serve the Lord and to increase that service, to increase our attachment to Kṛṣṇa and detachment from matter.

“We should always be thinking about, and praying sincerely to know, ‘What is the next thing that I have to do to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness?’ There may be fifteen, twenty, or forty things in the list of things I have to do, but the one that I have to be concerned with most is *the next thing*. Sometimes, Kṛṣṇa lets us see far ahead, and sometimes He does not, and that’s fine, just as long as we know what to do next. If we are sincere and serious, Kṛṣṇa will always let us know what to do next. Take that step, and then He will show us the next step. What He wants us to do next always involves more and more surrender, more attachment to Him.

“If you see really far ahead, you’ll certainly gasp in utter amazement, even in fearful amazement. For it’s true: Kṛṣṇa really wants everything from us. There may well be a fear factor in contemplating this complete dependence upon Kṛṣṇa, this full surrender. Yet if we simply take the next step, then Kṛṣṇa will give us all the courage we need, all the guidance we need, all the direction we need — all facility.

“The great example of this that we have before us — and as far as I can see I’ve not encountered its equal — is the example of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s coming to the West on his spiritual master’s order. Basically all he had was his complete faith in this order and his determination to execute it. By Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, Śrīla Prabhupāda did it without any material facility. He had no money. He was seventy years old, so he did not have youthful energy. He was not in good health. He had no institutional support. Nothing. Under-capitalized from the beginning — just a few dollars. Yet, what happened? Somehow or another, in a few years, the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement was spread all over the world.

“You may wonder why Kṛṣṇa put Prabhupāda through all that. Now we can see clearly: it was to show us vividly just what Śrīla Prabhupāda’s true asset was: his complete confidence in the order of the spiritual master and the commitment to take it up.

“Prabhupāda once said that at a certain point he thought he would be a businessman and make a lot of money and then spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And then Prabhupāda remarked, ‘But Kṛṣṇa made me a business failure.’ Prabhupāda surrendered to the plan of Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa showed how with just that confidence in the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the willingness to take all kinds of risks — everything came out successful. So, this is the illustration that we have, and it is why I am very confident on this point: we have seen it work. Śrīla Prabhupāda has shown us.

“I pray to Kṛṣṇa that all of you learn this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, take advantage of everything Prabhupāda has given us, and absorb what he has to say.

“When Śyāmasundara Prabhu was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s secretary in 1971, he used to mail out a regular newsletter of Prabhupāda’s doings. When they were in Africa, Śyāmasundara quoted Prabhupāda as saying ‘I am just one person, and yet see what I have done. Now we are five hundred’ — that is how many disciples he had at the time —‘so each one of you become just like me and then see what will be done.’ This was actually Śrīla Prabhupāda’s desire. Sometimes I have heard godbrothers say, “We can’t be like Śrīla Prabhupāda.” Yet that’s precisely what Prabhupāda ordered us to become. He ordered us to become exactly like him. We may have to operate on our own scale. Yet the full surrender that we can give — that will accomplish wonders. This is the order of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

“We should also understand that along with this order comes the mercy to fulfill it. We are indebted to Śrīla Prabhupāda for having done so much to save us. We have to pay him back by doing the same thing for others. That is our obligation. Learn this Kṛṣṇa consciousness, make the instructions of the spiritual master one with your heart, and in this way give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to everyone.”

That’s what I said, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I hope you find that I represent you faithfully, and that in doing so, I help keep you present among us through *vāṇī-sevā,* devoted service to your instructions. As — in fact — you instructed.

Please bless me to remain  
Your humble servant,  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I dreamt that I was back in the Navy (a recurring dream) onboard the super-carrier U.S.S. Saratoga, but this time the commanding officer was Śrīla Prabhupāda and the shipmates were his devotees. He was giving *darśana* on one of the main decks, and hundreds of devotees had stopped their duties even though the ship was at sea, just to watch and hear him. It reminded me of the descriptions of Lord Caitanya at the home of Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura, but Śrīla Prabhupāda sat quietly and we quietly watched him. We were all blissfully idling from our regular duties. By the power of *yogamāyā*Prabhupāda recognized each of us individually, and he received our individual glances of admiration. After what seemed a long time, he broke his mood and returned to his tasks befitting a commanding officer of the ship. I was standing on the lower rung of one of the ship’s ladders, holding the hand of Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, who was on the rung above me. I said to him, “That was Śrīla Prabhupāda?” He smiled, “Yes.” But then he let my hand go and turned to his tasks. I also returned to my individual tasks.

I was a lowly enlisted man, not an officer, but satisfied with my position.

I awoke from this dream with a blissful taste after so many nightmares of returning to the Navy. I thought, “Finally, *this* was the Navy that I would *love* to return to, sailing the seas with Śrīla Prabhupāda as the commanding officer, getting a glimpse of him every day.” Sometimes we had *darśanas* and *kīrtanas* even more active than the ones we just witnessed. And sometimes, late at night, a messenger with a flashlight would wake me and say that Prabhupāda wanted to see me. I would go into his quarters, and we would have long talks about what I was typing for him as I sat closely beside him. As I sat close beside him, I could not refrain from releasing my emotional barriers, and I cried tears from my failings, which I confessed to him. He assured me to go on, and he told me wonderful things about Kṛṣṇa consciousness. On such occasions, I would leave the room reeling and so grateful to God for keeping me fixed at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Prabhupāda.

A servant and shipmate of Śrīla Prabhupāda and his disciples,  
Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Ātmanivedana Swami

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! I bow down at your lotus feet over and over.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance, I take this opportunity to thank you for saving this fallen soul from the dark well of material existence. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, took so much trouble to establish this wonderful Society, so that fallen souls like myself can get the shelter of Śrī Kṛṣṇa without great difficulty. I for one am so indebted to you that no amount of service can repay this debt. I pray that you will guide me in the right direction so I may serve your Society in a useful way. This year we have managed to get another preaching bus to expand the Bhaktivedanta Institute book distribution program. This has all been done under the able guidance of Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami. I hope that you will shower your divine mercy on us so that we can carry on expanding this very dear mission of yours.

Your humble servant of the servant,  
Ātmanivedana Swami

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet, which are the shelter of the fallen souls in this dark and perilous age. All glories to all the devotees who have been touched in heart by your loving mercy and have rendered very valuable service in your great mission.

My Prabhupāda!! Without you, what do I have? Or what would I be? I dread to think.

I look at different things wherever I go. I see South Africa, two big temples, small temples, many *nāma-haṭṭas.* I see a big Ratha-yātrā festival, I see BTG subscriptions galore. I see my Prabhupāda!!

I see *prasādam* feasts wherever I go. I see different *sabjīs,* many types of rice preps, *lāḍḍu, burfi,* many other sweets, I see *samosās, pakorās, halavā,* etc., etc. I see Prabhupāda!!

I see festivals worldwide: Gaura-pūrṇimā, Nityānanda-trayodaśī, Nṛsiṁha-caturdaśī, Janmāṣṭamī, Rādhāṣṭamī, Govardhana Pūjā, *saṅkīrtana* festivals, Vyāsa-pūjā. I see Sunday Feast festivals. I see Prabhupāda!!

I see cow protection, wonderful milk products. I see fruit and vegetables growing, beehives buzzing. I see many types of flowers growing. I see Prabhupāda!!

I see big temples all over the world, and more to come. Delhi, Vṛndāvana, Māyāpur, Bhaktivedanta Manor, Moscow, Kazakhstan, Hungary, USA. I see many temples and preaching centers. I see Prabhupāda!!

I see meeting places all over the world by the hundred, where people come to get a regular taste of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I see Prabhupāda!!

I see *Bhagavad-gītā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Nectar of Devotion,* etc., etc., in many different languages being distributed worldwide. I see Prabhupāda!!

Now while I write this attempt at an offering it suddenly occurs to me, What do I see? Do I see Prabhupāda? It really means, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you have seen us and all the fallen souls with all their needs — many that I have probably very little or no idea of.

What can I say? Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You are at all times saving us all in so many ways. All glories to you! Save me, a fallen, Your Divine Grace.

Please forgive this ignorant fool for all his offenses and stupidity. Hoping one day to really be your disciple,

Your insignificant servant, the worthless  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances under your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! You continue to be my source of inspiration and encouragement.

Somehow the *harināma-saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya is spreading more and more, despite our various limitations, shortcomings, and mistakes. This is surely due to your continued causeless mercy, which is being showered upon all the sincere devotees serving the many orders you have left us all. We understand from *śāstra* that serving the orders of the spiritual master is the only way to achieve success in spiritual life:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have left us with so many instructions. We should all endeavor to become very enthusiastic in helping to fulfill at least one of those instructions. The first half of your mission was certainly centered on distributing your wonderful translations of the vast literatures of Vedic wisdom, such as the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Nectar of Devotion,* and *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. “Books are the basis,” you taught us, and “Preaching is the essence.” And for the first phase of our preaching, most of us were indeed very heavily involved in distributing these treasured books and preaching the pure message of *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. “Distribute books, distribute books, distribute books.”

However, you also wanted us to read and understand the contents of your books. On a few occasions you directly pointed out this weakness in your otherwise very surrendered disciples: we were not reading your books sufficiently. By our reading your books, especially by our cultivating the transcendental knowledge in those literatures, our hearts would automatically become purified. Hence, “Purity is the force.” And with a cleansed heart, we would be able to apply in a practical way the teachings in those books. Hence, “Utility is the principle.” Thus the second half of your preaching mission should be centered on establishing *varṇāśrama*. “Produce food, produce food, produce food.”

A few days ago, one of your senior disciples commented that the two most important instructions he felt you left us were (1) to distribute books and (2) to establish farm communities. If our first phase was to distribute your books, then it naturally follows that the second phase, that 50% of your mission you felt had been left unaccomplished, is to establish *varṇāśrama* villages so that we can more easily demonstrate the kind of lifestyle described in your books.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am not very experienced in the whole concept of *varṇāśrama* development, but by your kind mercy I find myself with a very strong desire to offer whatever services I can to help you realize this most important and yet unfulfilled phase of your mission. You have so nicely explained how the cows are the basis of a cultured and civilized society. Without cow protection, therefore, we cannot uphold the brahminical culture, because we allow this most important servant of yours to be neglected and brutally slaughtered every single day. And without upholding the brahminical culture, we cannot hope to introduce the principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness for ourselves or for society at large.

You have thus wisely stated,

Every state and every home must endeavor to advance the cause of brahminical culture for self-purification, God consciousness for self-realization, and cow protection for getting sufficient milk and the best food to continue a perfect civilization. Cow protection means feeding the brahminical culture, which leads towards God consciousness, and thus perfection of human civilization is achieved. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.19.4\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help me and others understand how crucial are Kṛṣṇa’s cows not only to those directly involved in our farm projects, but to every single person who claims to be a Vaiṣṇava devotee. Please help every single devotee of your movement understand the utter necessity of protecting such an innocent servant of yours by somehow becoming involved in helping establish, support, or develop *varṇāśrama* village communities. How can we say we love Kṛṣṇa when we neglect His dearmost servants, the cows? It is not simply some sentimental and emotional cry, but such concern for the land and the cows, both of whom are described as two of our mothers in the *śāstras,* must be demonstrated in practical ways.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I wish to recommit myself to serving your mission to the best of my ability, for whatever days, months, or years I have left in this dwindling body. In particular, I vow to daily hear and read more regularly your kind instructions left in all of your books, classes, conversations, and interviews. I have one humble request. You know what is in my heart. Please help me fulfill this one desire so that I may somehow become a small instrument for helping to realize this second phase of your preaching mission.

Training and education will remain very essential features for introducing your novel concept of “Simple living and high thinking.” You have wisely instructed, *“Produce only what you need, and use only what you produce.”*This is indeed the formula for a quiet revolution. Please help us all understand the deep significance of these instructions so that we may begin to cultivate this way of thinking wherever we may live at present.

Your words are full of wisdom and hold the highest of secrets.

Thank you again and again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for making me a recipient of your causeless mercy. I am not at all deserving of such mercy. Please help me remain fixed in your devotional service, and please continue to shower your blessings on all your sincere disciples, granddisciples, followers, and well-wishers. May your increasing fame and unlimited glories be chanted all over the three worlds. Please always keep me under the divine shelter of your warm lotus feet.

Your humble servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

This auspicious day of the anniversary of your divine appearance in this world is a welcome opportunity to hear more of your glories as well as your instructions. We never tire of such nectar, for it is through this medium of such transcendental sound that we know you continue to guide us twenty-seven years after your departure. You have confirmed this fact in the maxim printed in the front of your first volume of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*: “The spiritual master lives forever by his divine instructions, and the follower lives with him.”

Your instructions have been well preserved in your books, recorded lectures and conversations, letters, and memories of your disciples who were fortunate enough to be close to you to receive direct instruction.

In the purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.24.14, you have explained the necessity of following the instruction of the spiritual master as follows:

The son or disciple should accept the words of his spiritual master and father without hesitation. Whatever the father and the spiritual master order should be taken without argument: “Yes.” There should be no instance in which the disciple or the son says, “This is not correct. I cannot carry it out.” When he says that, he is fallen. . . . In any case, the order of the father or the order of the spiritual master must be carried out without hesitation, with an immediate yes. There should be no argument. That is real service to the father and to the spiritual master. Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura has stated that the order of the spiritual master is the life and soul of the disciples. As a man cannot separate his life from his body, a disciple cannot separate the order of the spiritual master from his life. If a disciple follows the instruction of the spiritual master in that way, he is sure to become perfect. This is confirmed in the *Upaniṣads:* the import of Vedic instruction is revealed automatically only to one who has implicit faith in the Supreme Personality of Godhead and in his spiritual master. One may be materially considered an illiterate man, but if he has faith in the spiritual master as well as in the Supreme Personality of Godhead, then the meaning of scriptural revelation is immediately manifested before him.

The other morning I was offering *guru-pūjā*to Your Divine Grace while Umāpati Swami was standing in the *kīrtana*. As I offered the ghee lamp of five wicks, one of them was caught by a small breeze and extinguished. I was unsure whether to relight the wick immediately or continue offering the remaining lamps. After the *pūjā*was over Mahārāja remarked to me that once he had been offering *guru-pūjā*in your presence, and as he offered the ghee lamp the same thing happened to one wick. Your Divine Grace had then signaled to him to immediately relight the wick. I was happy to hear from you through the medium of my godbrother, and the next time I will know what to do.

In every situation I want to follow the instructions of Your Divine Grace, because I know that by following you I can do something worthwhile with my life.

Please keep me engaged in your service.

Your insignificant servant,  
Bhakti Vighna Vināśa Narasiṁha Mahārāja

## Bhakti Vikāśa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In Calcutta, on 16 Āśvinā 1337 (Bengali calendar, corresponding to 23 October 1930), His Divine Grace Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura spoke the following:

Our *gurudeva* was not an instructor in any subject concerned with enjoyment of this material world. Again, he was the sole unmistaking judge of all topics of this world. But I am deprived and fallen; because of my weakness, not everything Śrī Gurudeva said entered my heart. But let me have millions of tongues and millions of heads to repeat whatever entered my ear by his mercy, and a lifespan of millions of years in unlimited universes for broadcasting descriptions of his incomparable, nonharmful compassion. That will be my *guru-pūjā*. He will be satisfied, and being pleased will shower unlimited benediction, by which I will be able to broadcast descriptions of his mercy with even more millions of tongues. On that day I will get relief from the glorification of all topics concerned with this destructible illusion, and from all kinds of mundane education within the universe.

Like one offering Mandākinī to the Gaṅgā, I offer the above quote at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is fulfilling the desires of his own most worshipable *gurudeva* by preaching his message and glories throughout the universe.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be pleased with my attempt to glorify your *gurudeva*. By that attempt may you be all the more glorified, and thus grant me the benediction to be able to eternally glorify the lotus-foot dust of our *ācāryas*.

Offered with prayers for your mercy — which is indispensable for me — by the most miserable  
Bhakti Vikāsa Swami

## Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Svāmī

*vṛndāvane ramaṇa-reti prasiddha-bhūmau*  
*tatrāpi kṛṣṇa-balarāma-supāda-mule*  
*jñānaṁ paraṁ parama-kṛṣṇa-sudharmīty uktaṁ*  
*dantas tu deva prabhupāda namo namas te*

**“In the most holy land of Vṛndāvana, in Ramaṇa-reti, at the lotus feet of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, you are preaching the topmost knowledge of the Supreme Personality, Lord Kṛṣṇa, who is the fountainhead of religion. O master of the senses, O my lord, Śrīla Prabhupāda, let me offer my obeisances unto you.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’m so grateful to you for offering this lowly servant of yours a chance to grow in your ISKCON mission as an insignificant creeper there.

Under your direction you’ve somehow engaged us throughout the years to increase our dormant loving propensities to serve Kṛṣṇa and his servants’ servants. You’ve saved our lives and given them a tangible shape toward eternity. But we must further stabilize this process daily, and yearly as well. Your Vyāsa-pūjā offers just the opportunity.

Previous to our fortunate meeting with Your Divine Grace, most of us were from degraded urban backgrounds. Somehow or other, by divine arrangement, I, like all the others, were drawn to the happy *harināma-kīrtana* and the succulent Sunday Feasts of your urban preaching centers.

Following the example of your guru, then — as you continue to do now — you managed your fledgling society.

Similarly, now — as then in your presence — we follow your followers, striving to assist your preaching here. You’ve made us understand clearly that all such cities of the world are constitutionally engulfed in the modes of passion and ignorance.

Although the ignorance is so thick that most of us can hardly know the reasons for such ignorance, the bound-up *jīvas* can hardly advance spiritually and culturally in that stifling atmosphere!

To remedy this, against all odds that we would ever be able to mend our foolish ways, you always liked and encouraged devotional *gṛhastha* communities to reside happily in the sāttvic village mood — “simple living and high thinking”— based on the opulence of the land and under the protection of Kṛṣṇa’s cows.

A creeper struggles in its early life to thrive and grow. Sometimes parched by strong winds and unbearable heat, it can hardly find the proper water to quench its roots. If that fragile creeper gets help by growing in the shade of larger plants who act as loving protectors, whose own root systems are near but sufficiently distant so as to give some space for the creeper to flourish, the creeper has a significantly better chance of survival.

Over these thirty-three years of my small relationship to your mission, you have drawn me to the loving examples of many of your stalwart servants who live their lives inspired only by you. It is by the strength of their *guru-niṣṭhā* that I can continue to know you increasingly.

We glorify you and the disciplic succession with every morning’s *Gurv-aṣṭaka* prayers, yet by loving and living among your servants, I feel their blessed association to be like the big trees and bushes who give protective shade to my loving attachment to you.

As you can tell the parent’s quality by looking at the quality the child has retained, your glorification is amplified in my heart by the instructive examples of these servants of yours.

Last year your Mahānidhi Swami reminded me of one facet of the mission of your renunciant servants: “less is more.” Oh! How long will I ever struggle to understand that this is correct? It is one aspect of the multifarious moods of Lord Caitanya, the six Gosvāmīs, and their disciplic descendants.

Your Dīnabandhu dāsa always displays unswerving attachment to the service of your worshipable Lords — Gaura-Nitāi, Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, and Rādhā-Śyāmasundara — while narrating Their divine pastimes as He takes us all wandering here and there to Their pastime locations covered by the soft dust of Vraja.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you eternally sport with Kṛṣṇa and His cows. Your Rādhānātha Swami said, “You don’t get elevation to become a Vrajavāsī until you take care of the cows.”

How can we ever hope to join you eternally unless we follow your cow-herding *sevā*?

Your Kūrmarūpa dāsa astonishes me by manifesting such strong dedication to the protection of Kṛṣṇa’s cows of Vraja, who, feigning helplessness, thus offer us all the chance to advance our spiritual and material realizations in the glories of “Kṛṣṇeikera-go āśraya.” All the cowherd men of Vraja kept their health strong in Kṛṣṇa’s service due to the shelter the cows offer along with their milk, dung, and urine. Kūrmarūpa reminds me of your mood in those respects, Śrīla Prabhupāda. I’m so grateful for that mercy!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while you were physically in our presence, you taught by your example, displaying an attachment to writing your purports to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* while being simultaneously detached from the pains of an aging body.

In your absence, I therefore see your inspiration flowing and manifested before my eyes in the forms of your Nirañjana Swami, Rādhānātha Swami, and Jayādvaita Swami, Bhūrijana Prabhu and Draviḍa Prabhu, as well as countless other godbrothers and -sisters like your Daivīśaktī Dāsī and Nārāyaṇī Dāsī of Vraja.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, they all show me, in their own unique sweet ways, how to follow your footsteps in this culturally ever-degrading “digital age.”

Last year I made my *saṅkalpa* to you — to strive to be more efficient, “accepting more”*harināma* for sustenance, facilitating the increased recitation of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Only then will I automatically live on correspondingly “less” of other so-called “*arthas*” (*anarthas*?). The rise of that divine attachment will then free the *jīva* bound in this aging body from its wretched bodily deception.

I admit I still have a long way to go to meet that commitment I made. If by your grace I do get there, it will be in part because you are manifest to me in the form of your servants, who demonstrate that as long as *hari-kathā* remains on the lips and as long as resolute renunciation remains in the heart — a renunciation that follows the emanation you set in your manifest pastimes — we can be successful living and spreading Mahāprabhu’s message to the world.

Since my *saṅkalpa* remains woefully unfulfilled, I must beg at your lotus feet for an extension. I am confident that, because of their dedication to your lotus feet, you will continue to pour the shade of your daily divine mercy upon those who are my loving “larger tree” protectors.

By their mercy, I may soon attain the fruits of any *saṅkalpa* I make to you. By your and their combined mercy, this insignificant “little creeper” will get ever-rising conviction year in and year out to withstand the parching hot winds and inevitable onslaught of the invalidities of old age

May I continue to be your little creeper growing under their soothing protection.

*bhavantam evānucaran nirantaraḥ*  
*praśānta-niḥśeṣa-mano-rathāntaraḥ*  
*kadāham aikāntika-nitya-kiṅkaraḥ*  
*praharṣayiṣyāmi sanātha-jīvitam*

**“By serving You constantly, one is freed from all material desires and is completely pacified. When shall I engage as Your permanent eternal servant and always feel joyful to have such a perfect master?”**

Tridaṇḍi Bhikṣu,  
Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Swami

## Candramauli Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your life was one of teaching the world the principle of how to live a life in devotion to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Your mood of compassionate teaching was there up to your final moment. While in Vṛndāvana in your last days, you spoke to a room full of disciples and said, “Don’t think this won’t happen to you.” A powerful message to help us prepare for the inevitable. At that time most of us were young by material calculation, and perhaps we didn’t understand the full gravity of your words. Now each year many of us are witnessing the passing away of many of our godbrothers and godsisters. Hopefully, we are taking your words more seriously. But the real concern is not death or the fear of it, but rather how to use whatever time remains in the best possible way, in perfecting our devotional service.

On this anniversary of your divine appearance in this world, we celebrate our good fortune of having received your special mercy and guidance. We desire to use every remaining moment to assist you in your mission of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please awaken within me the realization of the value of every remaining moment in this life so that I may use each one to serve in the best way possible. I desire nothing else.

Your worthless servant,  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Imagine — a person like me sometimes actually looking forward to approaching people on the street! I never thought I would see the day that I would like this service (even slightly). When I first went out to do *saṅkīrtana* at Fisherman’s Wharf in San Francisco, I hated the idea of meeting so many strangers all day long. Most of the interactions were strained and uncomfortable. Then there were long periods of time that I would just be unable to even stop anyone, as if I were a ghost. Nothing about *saṅkīrtana* was easy for me: it was totally against the grain of my character. I was not an open sort of person — gregarious, outgoing, ready to meet unlimited people. What do they call that these days? A “people” person. No, not this monkey. I hated trying to get people to do something they did not seem to want to do. I got so many excuses; such a poor result at the end of the day. My brain screamed, “No! No! Stop! Stop! Anything else! Stop!!” I wanted to forget that there ever was such a thing as *saṅkīrtana*.

But I heard that *saṅkīrtana* was so pleasing to you. In the mood of drinking a strong, bitter draft of poison, I prayed that just somehow let me do *saṅkīrtana* forever no matter what, even though I dislike it so much. That was 1977. I don’t know if today I would have the grit to make such a prayer. I have thought about retracting it, but I am not sure that there is a prayer-cancellation department anywhere in Kṛṣṇa’s Universal Organization Structure. I kind of doubt it. I’ll bet it is kind of like the mafia that way, I’m sure. Once you’re in, you’re in. All sales final. Probably.

And somehow, I have always come back around to *saṅkīrtana*. Of course, in the beginning we were not really doing book distribution *saṅkīrtana*. At least where I was. Sometimes I reminisce. Was it because I would have never been able to really help people, or would not have been able to relate to such an extremely minuscule result, or would not have been able to explain anything properly (my brain was so scrambled back then—little bit better now), or maybe because I would have burned out too many innocent unsuspecting pedestrians by my super-fruitive attitude? Why was it that I never really gave books, but instead just focused on money and paraphernalia? Someday maybe I will know. On the day when I die perhaps and I will get to see the answers to all the unanswered questions I always asked about myself and my lot. It is always easy to blame others or blame circumstances, but everything happens for a purpose. Kṛṣṇa is basing our cure on our desires.

Doing *saṅkīrtana,* I looked around and saw that other devotees mainly did well and early-on acquired the knack. Not me. I was not the only one who couldn’t seem to click, but I was certainly in the minority. Even now I just creep in there on the bottom end of the spectrum. But I began to feel that this book distribution is more important for me than it is for the conditioned souls. And I began to look for the “perfect attitude” to wear while performing this service. I have learned much from others. And I have thought about what *saṅkīrtana* is and what it should be. What its goals are and how to perform it. I know that Kṛṣṇa’s mercy follows the mercy of the devotee. If I were really spiritually inclined, many, many people would be taking to the process. That we do not see. This is certainly certified proof of the extent of my spiritual inclination. But—hey, that’s OK. They are making progress and I am making progress. No need for blaming fingers. I preach this to myself when I get frustrated. It is also partly my fault that they do not take. But there is always the eternal future.

I used to think that they really did not want these books. How could they? They are all mostly Christians, isn’t it? And meat-eaters to boot. I don’t believe that any more. I have seen too much of the truly spooky nature of *saṅkīrtana*. *Saṅkīrtana* is like watching water flow uphill or watching a table float in the air at a **sīance**. Completely unnatural, downright spooky things go down. But good spooky things. Things that blatantly violate every known law of human nature. I do not believe that they do not want books anymore. Now I know: there is a heart and there is a key. You either have it or you don’t. This is the right time or it isn’t. It is up to Kṛṣṇa. But He will follow you. Huh??—Weird!!

But how to get Kṛṣṇa to give you the key? Ah—therein lies the rub. By having the “perfect attitude.” What’s in that “perfect attitude” box? A lot of interesting stuff like really wanting to help people, like not feeling better than them, like being able to take being the butt of a joke, like knowing when to move on to the next person real fast, like having a bag where you store all your daily insults, to be thrown away at the end of the day. Like good *japa,* like praying constantly, like realizing how funny we really are — and I’m sure that other *saṅkīrtana* devotees can name thousands more items. Every so often, I come across yet another item to put into my “perfect attitude” box.

What is thoroughly amazing is that lately I am actually finding *saṅkīrtana* attractive (a little bit). Please give me the compassion, determination, and knowledge to continue to evolve the “perfect attitude.” Some devotees pray before going on *saṅkīrtana* or sing *bhajans* glorifying Lord Caitanya’s compassion. I like that. I pray that the number of *saṅkīrtana* devotees may grow by your mercy. I think that there is room for many different styles of *saṅkīrtana,* and I really wish that more devotees would try to distribute books based on their own technique and realization. *Paraṁ vijayate śrī kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam*!!! One day, one day, it will all be there: me, *saṅkīrtana,* an army of book distributors, and people taking to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

**God Only Knows Where I’d Be Without You**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear beloved Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet.

Thank your very much for your unlimited causeless mercy and kind care you have shown to such a fallen soul as myself. Thank you so much for giving me the shelter and protection of your lotus feet, which allow me to go on performing devotional service. I know I do not deserve your shelter at all, but you are my savior (*patita-pāvana*), and that is why even an insignificant conditioned soul like me has the chance to become transcendental to the three modes of material nature and attain pure loving devotion to Kṛṣṇa.

On the platform of *śuddha-sattva,* you are a soul fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa and a pure and faithful follower of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Prabhupāda. Pure devotees like you own Kṛṣṇa and pure devotional service to Him and can give pure love for Kṛṣṇa to one and all. I am aware of and most grateful for all that you have done and are doing for this wretched soul, yet in fact I cannot imagine the full measure of your causeless mercy. Unfortunately, I display much ungratefulness too in that I do nothing for your pleasure. I would indeed like to please you all the time and serve you with increasing love and devotion free of material motives, but due to my offences and weak-heartedness, the results of my efforts are minute. Surely it is my failure, and I’m worried that my progress is so slow. Kindly help me so that I may not fall into Māyā, and advise me how to change and start to really perform devotional service in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Although infested with numerous disqualifications, I daily attend the temple programs as you requested—*maṅgala-ārati, tulasī-ārati,* greeting of Deities, *guru-pūjā, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, *gaura-ārati,* and *Bhagavad-gītā* class. Reading and distributing your books of newer and newer transcendental knowledge are always sources of pleasure. Recently I attended the Māyāpur-Vṛndāvana Gaura-pūrṇimā Festival 2005, where we received the blessed association of the holy *dhāmas,* your disciples, and your granddisciples. I know I am a rascal, but still I pray that the devotees will forgive all my offenses and that by their causeless clemency I will be accepted by you even if I do not deserve it.

My desire is to assist Your Divine Grace by following your instructions with body, mind, and words. Please give me your blessings so that I may continue the service of recruitment and training devotees in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and so that my material desires shall never lead me away from the society of devotees, on which I’m fully dependent and to which I am attached.

My dear Guru Mahārāja, I’m looking forward to receiving more of your causeless mercy. Please be kind to me, forgive all my offenses, and give me at least one drop from the unlimited ocean of your causeless mercy so that I can become your pure and humble servant — eternally. Only when you are satisfied with this low servant will Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa be satisfied too. My real and only happiness and wealth is service to your lotus feet — without which I am nothing. I’m sure that I can never repay my debt to you, but I humbly beg you to please engage me in devotional service and accept my clumsy attempts to serve.

Missing your pure and precious association and thanking you very much for everything,

With the deepest respect and love,  
Your worthless, eternal servant and debtor,  
Bowing down at your lotus feet,  
Offering his most respectful obeisances again and again,  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Vidvān (the Wise Sage)**

The widely accepted father of modern psychology, William James, once offered an insightful description of what it means to be wise: “The art of being wise is the art of knowing what to overlook.” In a similar manner, Oliver W. Holmes proclaimed, “A young man knows the rules, but an old man knows the exceptions.” These statements may help to highlight the unique qualifications that Śrīla Prabhupāda possessed which allowed him to spread the message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu with such astounding success.

James’ description helps us to clearly understand the difference between the states of being knowledgeable and being wise. Being wise requires not only being aware of a vast array of information, but more importantly, knowing how and when to apply that knowledge according to time and circumstances. It requires realization of the deeper principles behind their particular manifestations. It also requires expertise in dealing with the material energy, and just as important, it requires an abundance of patience and tolerance. In the words of Kant, “Wisdom is organized life.”

There are many examples of gifted persons who are extremely conscious of their surroundings and are expert in manipulating them for a particular purpose. There are also many examples of persons who are able to overlook faults and inconsistencies by blocking them out of their consciousness. But rarely do we find someone who is both so grounded in the earthly reality and simultaneously transcendental to its effects. Indeed, it could be said that Śrīla Prabhupāda is the superlative example of what it means to be “in the world but not of it.”

In Śrīla Prabhupāda we find a person who was both totally aware of his immediate environment and, at the same time, able to remain aloof to it when required. Such a person is likened in the *Gītā* to a tortoise who draws its limbs within when not required for action. Indeed, there are countless instances of Śrīla Prabhupāda even knowing the inner thoughts of his disciples and others, what to speak of being aware of external phenomena. He could also ascertain their secret desires and schemes, and he witnessed their many faults and blunders. Yet he also knew precisely when to correct and chastise — and when to look the other way and “turn the other cheek.”

For a *sādhu* absorbed in his personal *bhajana,* such selective unconsciousness may come easy, but for someone as meticulous and attentive to detail, and as eager to transform the material energy into spirit, as Śrīla Prabhupāda was, such action surely reveals a superhuman degree of wisdom. Śrīla Prabhupāda was surely not of the same quality of his surroundings and associates, so there should have been constant friction between these opposites. Prabhupāda said the same when he chastised his *sannyāsīs* in Māyāpur for not complaining about the stench emanating from the workers’ toilets around the perimeter wall. He explained that one in *sattva-guṇa* cannot tolerate the presence of *tamo-guṇa*. Yet we also find how Śrīla Prabhupāda exercised an amazing degree of patience in knowing how to gradually incorporate the rules and regulations into his society of fledging followers. Like a doctor who knows exactly what dosage of a medicine to prescribe for his ailing patients, Prabhupāda constantly prodded us forward without pushing us over the brink.

Śrīla Prabhupāda knew just how to gently encourage a young sculptor to chip away at his bad habits — like tobacco addiction — while simultaneously chipping away at three logs of wood which would soon become the cynosure of the Haight scene. He knew how to encourage his musically-inclined disciples to amplify the bliss of *kīrtana* with the use of their favored guitar instead of more traditional Bengali instruments. His liberality inspired Bk. George to combine his skill and experience with the exuberant vocals of Yamunā Devī to create a rendition of *Brahma-saṁhitā* so beautiful and moving that our Vaiṣṇava ancestors could scarcely have imagined it. Prabhupāda compassionately petitioned his cherished Divine Couple to appear and accept the simple services of his neophyte disciples who barely knew the meaning behind the sacred thread, much less the intricacies of *Arcanā-paddhati*. But he had faith that Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa would mercifully extract the spiritual fervor of these same servitors to a degree that now sets the ever-evolving standard of Deity worship for the entire Vaiṣṇava world (as evidenced in the *Darśana* book).

In short, Śrīla Prabhupāda was an *ācārya,* the wisest of all persons, in the truest sense of the term, as enunciated by himself in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 7.37): “Every *ācārya* has a specific means of propagating his spiritual movement with the aim of bringing men to Krsna consciousness. Therefore, the method of one *ācārya* may be different from that of another, but the ultimate goal is never neglected.” Again, in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 23.105), Śrīla Prabhupāda writes,

To broadcast the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, one has to learn the possibility of renunciation in terms of country, time and candidate. A candidate for Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the Western countries should be taught about the renunciation of material existence, but one would teach candidates from a country like India in a different way. The teacher (*ācārya*) has to consider time, candidate and country. He must avoid the principle of *niyamāgraha*—that is, he should not try to perform the impossible. What is possible in one country may not be possible in another. The *ācārya’s* duty is to accept the essence of devotional service.

Awestruck, one can only wonder whether Prabhupāda did in fact do the “impossible.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Seeking the forgiveness and blessings of all his followers,  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I would like to begin my homage to you by quoting a reply you gave to His Holiness Tamāl Krishna Goswami on May 13, 1977, in Hrishikesh:

**Tamāla Krishna:** Someone came this morning. He was asking that . . . He was hoping that because he had heard that you had some mystical powers, so he was hoping that you could help his situation by demonstrating some of these mystical powers.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Some magic. Our magic is already there. Throughout the whole world we are chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Is not that magic? Foreign countries, foreign religion, and they are accepting Kṛṣṇa and chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Is not that magic?

**Indian man:** It is.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** And still more magic? The world is chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Prabhupāda, you presented the highest to the lowest, inspired us to accept it, and allowed us to experience it. As you have indicated above, there is no greater display of magic than that. On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance I thus join with all the devotees to offer my deep affection and appreciation for you. You have outlined the most sublime way for us to absorb our consciousness in transcendence: the process of *saṅkīrtana*. You had us take whoever we were, in however fallen condition we were in, and offer ourselves fully for the benefit of others, giving them the treasure of the holy name. By so doing we have become an object of your mercy and have been uplifted by your compassion, tasting something very real in spiritual life. Because of your divine grace, Kṛṣṇa is now willing to personally reside in our hearts. This mystical transformation could never have been possible otherwise. You allowed us to achieve something unavailable to us by our own nature and qualities but indeed attainable by your making us an object of your mercy.

You have taught us that the goal of life is to attain *samādhi,* complete absorption in the Lord. This requires a controlled mind, something extremely difficult to master even for the greatest of yogis. But you granted us the ability to corral our very wicked and rebellious minds by giving us the joy and attraction that comes from expanding the flow of mercy by way of the glorification of the name, fame, and qualities of the Lord.

By the grace of the Lord we have become your humble servants and are now connected with you, becoming free from the egoistic conception of life. Our past associates and relatives are amazed and bewildered by the transformation we have undergone, as it is a phenomenon completely inconceivable to those who have not yet experienced your mercy. But as your devotees, we can fully understand that because you are connected with Kṛṣṇa, the master of all mystic power, you can make even the lame climb mountains, the dumb speak beautiful poetry, and the lowborn, the grief-stricken, and the hippies become happies.

I thank you with all my heart for everything you have done for me and for others. I beg you with all my heart to please let this unqualified and undeserving soul remain a recipient of your magic. And I pray with all my heart that you can continue to use this insignificant disciple of yours in whatever way you like to fulfill your goal of spreading your divine magic throughout the world.

Your aspiring servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dās Goswami

## Hanumatpresaka Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our very humble obeisance. Disqualified in many ways, we still must continue to offer our humble service to Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Nandanandana, under the shelter of your lotus feet.

We are always thinking that you must be moving in Vṛndāvana with a steady pace, going about some service for Śrī Rādhā, Śrī Hari, and the *gopīs*. We hope to keep up with you, maybe as some simple animal — a squirrel or butterfly — or maybe even as one of the particles of dust raised by your feet as you walk.

We are 90% insane, intoxicated by the attractions, repulsions, and associations of this external play of Māyā.

Detailed plans and programs of work seem like a hilarious fantasy to us. Our hope is to stay in the area of your lotus feet and then arrive at the same destination as you by the end of the day.

The famous American poet Walt Whitman once commented that life is a spiral. Again and again we encounter the same men, the same women, the same tasks and challenges, but each time a little higher, a little higher. Life is a spiral, around and around, again and again, but slowly going up. Then he asked himself, Where does this road go, this spiral road? He answered, “I don’t know, but I know that it goes someplace good.”

So we are like that.

Mostly we are looking down from minute to minute to keep from stumbling, trying to help others to keep from stumbling. The path is full of holes and edged with sharp precipices, and we have no strategy to get out of this mess.

Our only hope is to keep in the vicinity of your lotus feet as you move ahead with your steady pace, from time to time looking up as you indicate where we are going, and getting a glimpse of that goal.

That’s it.

We are fools.

We have to follow the four principles, chant good rounds, read and discuss *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and try to distribute your books and words through your purports.

Please allow us to increase our intensity in this humble endeavor until next year and become ecstatic in wonder as we see from the corners of our eyes the amazing things that pass by us as we follow in your footsteps.

Please let us continue to participate every year in the *vraja-maṇḍala-parikramā*and just have some active position in your *saṅkīrtana* party.

Without your strength we have nothing.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Nityānanda-Rāma-*kī jaya*!  
Nitāi-Gaurasundara-*kī jaya*!  
ISKCON *bhakta-vṛnda-kī jaya*.

Your servant,  
Hanumatpresaka Swami

## Indradyumna Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you!

This year I am writing my offering to you quite early — in February — because I feel most close to you today. I’m on the island of Sri Lanka, which Marco Polo called one of the most beautiful places in the world. But recently the island and its people have experienced the other side of material existence. A tsunami hit here a little more than a month ago, ravaging the coast and taking with it 33,000 lives. As I sit here I can hear in the background the wailing of women who lost husbands, parents, and children. Now this place more closely resembles the Lord’s summary of material existence in the *Bhagavad-gītā* — a temporary place full of misery and suffering.

Coming here and assisting the local devotees in their *prasādam* distribution has done much for me. Seeing such levels of devastation and suffering has made me more detached from material life. I am your *sannyāsī* disciple, and detachment is fundamental to the renounced order. But changing the color of one’s cloth can hardly uproot the strong material attachments the living entity carries birth after birth. Witnessing firsthand the cruel face of material existence is indeed helpful. Seeing the pain these people are feeling in losing their loved ones, their possessions, and their means of livelihood, I would be a hypocrite to ever again entertain desires for enjoying this material world.

Simply seeing the ephemeral nature of material life falls short of full spiritual realization. Full enlightenment means to realize Kṛṣṇa and awaken our dormant love for Him. Still, only one who is fully convinced about the reality of material life can fully devote himself to the Lord’s service. My experience here thus seems an important steppingstone toward full transcendence.

If only now is the true nature of this world becoming clear to me, how much further from pure devotional service to Kṛṣṇa must I be?
