When will I be prepared to please you in all conditions of happiness and distress, delivering your message to one and all, giving you the highest pleasure and attracting your causeless mercy?

*paraṁ gopyam api snigdhe śiṣye vācyam iti śrutiḥ*  
*tac chrūyatāṁ mahā-bhāga goloka-mahimādhunā*

**“The** ***Vedas*** **say that to a loyal disciple one may speak the confidential secret. Therefore, O most fortunate one, now please hear the glories of Goloka.” (*****Śrī Bṛhad-bhāgavatāmṛta,*** **Part Two, Chapter 1, Text 6)**

As I age, I am purposely focusing my meditation on the primary goals you gave us: to effectively preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to awaken love of God in my own heart. These goals have always been an integral part of my life, but the distractions of leftover material desires have compromised my efforts to reach them. Please help me purge these *anarthas* from my heart and make me a sharp instrument in spreading your mission throughout the world. Then, if you deem me worthy, please share with me the confidential secrets of love of God and grant me the sacred privilege of assisting you in your service in the spiritual world forever.

Your servant,  
Indradyumna Swami

## Jayādvaita Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We, your disciples, are leaving this world. Some of us have already left, the rest of us are on our way. As you taught us, everything is tested at the time of death. And by your own example you showed us how to pass the test: how to live in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and how to die the same way.

Now some of your followers are following you out of this world. Some who have left have been known to everyone, others known in smaller circles. But among them we have seen noble examples, devotees who have learned their lessons well, who have faced death bravely and free from illusion, their minds fixed on Kṛṣṇa and Your Divine Grace.

Some years ago, I was present at the departure of one devotee who had sometimes been your personal servant. He had later lost himself in Māyā; but shortly before death he came back — you retrieved him — to the association of devotees. And when death came he was clutching some mementos you had given him — a sweater, a cane — and by the grateful expression that shone through on his face we could see that at the end he was holding on to your divine mercy.

Another time, again some years ago, I was with a group of devotees called urgently to the bedside of one of your disciples, a godsister, in a hospice in London. The end was near, the doctors said: she had fallen into a coma, and now it would just be a question of hours. When we reached the hospice she was unconscious, as reported. But as soon as we arrived and started chanting she came out of her coma and asked for her beads and had us chant with her. In the presence of mere doctors and nurses she’d had nothing around her to be conscious for; in the presence of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, outer consciousness was worthwhile. She left the world that night — she had to go — but by your mercy she went with the support of devotees and the grace of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa.

Even when death ambushed Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja on the road, death caught him surprised but not unprepared: He had spent his last days immersed in *kṛṣṇa-kīrtana* and devotee association in Lord Caitanya’s holy *dhāma,* and before leaving he had told a disciple, “Please go to the Ganges and offer her my last respects.”

Mūla-prakṛti Dāsī, at the end, just wanted to remember “my Prabhupāda.”

Last year your Śrīdhara Mahārāja left in glory, his every wish fulfilled. And now, as I write, your Bhakti-tīrtha Mahārāja, sweet and bold as ever, is using his own impending death as just one last opportunity to offer you wonderful service.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when your disciples remember Kṛṣṇa and stay fixed in Kṛṣṇa’s service till the end, their example pays tribute to you, for it is you who brought them to Kṛṣṇa, who trained them and prepared them by word and deed, and made it possible for them to pass the most difficult of tests. When so many students graduate with honors, natural honor goes to their teacher.

For you that honor is still greater because these were students you had picked up from the West, from the civilization of the uncivilized, from a way of life that leads to an ignominious death. And you had led them to the perfection of life in devotional service at Lord Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet.

To Your Divine Grace, who turn death into life, I offer my obeisances again and again.

Your servant,  
Jayādvaita Swami

## Kadamba Kānana Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances again and again. All glories to Your Divine Grace! And all glories to my spiritual master, who is bringing me closer to your lotus feet!

*guru kṛṣṇa-rūpa hana śāstrera pramāṇe*  
*guru-rūpe kṛṣṇa kṛpā karena bhakta-gaṇe*

**“According to the deliberate opinion of all revealed scriptures, the spiritual master is nondifferent from Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa in the form of the spiritual master delivers His devotees.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi*** **1.45)**

So it is stated here that it is Kṛṣṇa who delivers the disciple and it is the spiritual master who acts as His representative, as a postman simply delivers a letter without alteration. The *Padma Purāṇa* states, *arcye viṣṇau śilā-dhīr guruṣu nara-matiḥ*, one should never consider the spiritual master to be an ordinary man, but everyone can see that you are special among spiritual masters, and we are certain that Kṛṣṇa also sees it like that.

To serve the servant of Kṛṣṇa is better than to serve Kṛṣṇa directly, because if the servant of Kṛṣṇa promises something, if the servant of Kṛṣṇa says, “I’ll take you to Kṛṣṇaloka,” you must go there. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda conversation, Māyāpur, 7 March 1976\]

Sometimes I think of death as the final exam, and I can just imagine how my case will be judged. Someone will say, “He did not do very well; maybe it would really be better if he would do it once again.” And others will add similar comments, and the whole situation won’t look very favorable. Just then someone will say, “Just wait a minute! This is a member of Prabhupāda’s party!” Then the authorities will wave their hand and say, “Let him go.”

We once saw another sentiment expressed in this regard by Trivikram Mahārāja in a *Bhāgavatam* class in Vṛndāvana, some time in the early eighties. Mahārāja said, “Today I want to reveal some doubts that I have kept in my heart for quite some time. I am not so sure if Prabhupāda actually went back to Godhead.” All the devotees were shocked. Then Mahārāja continued and said, “He might have gone to another universe to do it again. Therefore I am not praying to go back to Godhead; I am simply praying to go wherever Prabhupāda went.”

How could you attract and convince us sinful Westerners to leave everything and take up a totally different way of life and become so attached to you?

Clearly, because of your hard work.

They have forgotten Kṛṣṇa, they have forgotten God, and I am trying to make them Kṛṣṇa conscious. It is a very difficult job. I have to shed my blood three tons before I make one convinced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. That is my experience. I have to talk with \[them\], especially these Europeans and Americans. They do not accept anything so blindly. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda conversation, Hong Kong, 18 April 1972\]

Someone saw you for the first time and said, “I could see that the swami was not trying to impress anyone, and that impressed me.”

You could attract all of us because you never had any interest in fame but simply loved Kṛṣṇa. And the most wonderful thing is that not only did you love Kṛṣṇa, but your love is just like His, all-inclusive: you are *suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ,* the well-wisher of all living entities.

So we are worshiping Kṛṣṇa. His simple business is, as we have described, Rādhā-Mādhava, He is lover of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. *Kṛṣṇa* means “lover.” This very word *Kṛṣṇa* means “all-attractive.” You can attract by your love, not by anything else. Therefore His name is Kṛṣṇa. \[Śrīla Prabhupāda lecture, New Delhi, 16 November 1972\]

Therefore we are here today falling at your feet and begging that we may be allowed to serve you for the rest of our lives, because you have completely purchased us through your love.

Your servant,  
Kadamba Kānana Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The life of Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda is a spiritual gem with unlimited facets, all radiating effulgent transcendental bliss to every corner of the universe. I stand on the shore of the ever-expanding ocean of knowledge and devotion contained within the library of transcendental literature he munificently left for human society. And I beg for a drop of that ocean.

The *Vedas* describe that even if a person could calculate the number of atoms in the universe, he could not count the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. How, then, can such an ignorant, sinful creature as I describe the pastimes of the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a full manifestation of the unlimited mercy of the Lord? Every instant of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s life was a spiritual instruction on how to know and love Kṛṣṇa. It is indescribable. I simply pray for the mercy of Śrīla Prabhupāda for the strength and intelligence to continue.

Lord Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.11):

*ye yathā māṁ prapadyante*  
*tāṁs tathaiva bhajāmy aham*

**“As all surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly.” Later in the** ***Gītā*** **(7.14) the Lord elaborates:**

*daivī hy eṣā guṇa-mayī mama māyā duratyayā*  
*mām eva ye prapadyante māyām etāṁ taranti te*

**“This divine energy of Mine, consisting of the three modes of material nature, is difficult to overcome. But those who have surrendered unto Me can easily cross beyond it.”**

And in the final chapter (18.66) the Lord concludes:

*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁśaraṇaṁ vraja*  
*ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi māśucaḥ*

**“Abandon all varieties of religion and just surrender unto Me. I shall deliver you from all sinful reactions. Do not fear.”**

A materialist never strives for surrender. He equates surrender with suffering, something you do when you lose a war. For him, to surrender to anyone is difficult, what to speak of to God. And surrender to God is especially difficult because it demands that we relinquish our material conceptions, the very basis of life in this world. If God exists He is eternally superior and I am eternally subordinate, a thought repugnant to the materialistic mind, which is always looking for a way to achieve supremacy over others. Considering all this, I conclude that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s example of how to be successful and happy throughout life through the process of surrender was the essence of his pastimes.

The first point is that everything comes from surrender to Kṛṣṇa because as we surrender to Him He rewards us accordingly. In other words, in the process of surrender we are never the losers. Opulence, peace, freedom, knowledge, detachment, devotion, the exalted qualities of a devotee — everything comes without fear and tribulation through the process of surrender. Those who spent time with Śrīla Prabhupāda will readily testify that one astounding feature of his daily life was how effortlessly he seemed to do things amidst the most trying of circumstances. And he faced tremendous obstacles: an uncooperative family, failure of his business, breakdown of his health, theft of his manuscripts, legal battles over properties, infighting amongst his leaders, a gigantic burden of management, sabotage by his godbrothers, to name a few. Through all that Śrīla Prabhupāda remained positive and cheerful, undaunted in his affectionate remembrance of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and the instructions of his beloved Guru Mahārāja. How can we be sure of that? Because that was all he ever talked about! While working tirelessly to overcome the hindrances to spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness single-handedly, he thought and talked of Kṛṣṇa more and more; and as he did, his victory and opulence increased more and more.

Sentimentalists attribute this phenomenon to some special mystic power in Śrīla Prabhupāda or to some inherent difference between him and themselves. Burdened by this misconception, they resort to “new” philosophies or spiritual methods that infiltrate materially concocted ideas into the path of spiritual advancement in the name of dovetailing material propensities according to time and circumstance. Instead of gaining an uncompromised victory by following in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps, they take the “path of least resistance” and compromise — which usually means that their preaching stops. Śrīla Prabhupāda, however, attributed his success to something very simple: that he did not adulterate the message of Kṛṣṇa or his Guru Mahārāja. He responded to the challenges of life by remaining surrendered to the will of his spiritual master, strict in his devotional practices, and uncompromised in his preaching and cultural activities. Moreover, through all of that, he remained positive, cheerful, and undaunted, even when forced to wait for opportunities to preach. By studying the life of this liberated soul, we shall gain the courage and strength required to surrender to his example and his precepts.

The second point is that the process of surrender is ongoing — not that we surrender once and everything is finished. Śrīla Prabhupāda showed us that the call to surrender goes on to the very end. The statement “As all surrender, I reward them accordingly” implies a series of examinations, the last and most arduous of which is death. Incapacitated by infirmity in his final days, Śrīla Prabhupāda still cared for us like a loving father, concerned that each newly arriving devotee had sufficient *prasādam* and adequate accommodation. He reciprocated with affection upon seeing us, and with enthusiasm upon hearing news of any preaching success. By this indescribable display of love, Śrīla Prabhupāda prepared us for a lifetime of dedication to the moment-to-moment process of surrender, which will continue until our last breath. And with his last breath Śrīla Prabhupāda showed us exactly what to do — he chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Another point is that surrender is for everyone, regardless of his or her social position. Śrīla Prabhupāda passed through all the *āśramas;* and in each, he surrendered to Kṛṣṇa by following the instructions of *śāstra.* In his student life, he surrendered to his superiors, his father and mother, and his teachers. When the time came to marry and he wanted a prettier wife, he surrendered to the advice of his father and rejected the idea. However, even at such an early age he did not surrender blindly. He did the needful to complete his studies, satisfy his professors, and earn his degree; but he rejected their impersonal teachings.

In the *gṛhastha-āśrama,* he surrendered to his prescribed duty of maintaining the family. He followed the religious principle of working honestly, even at the expense of his business. He followed the regulative principles without deviation with his wife, remaining loyal to her and the children. And after receiving initiation from his spiritual master, he demonstrated perfect surrender to guru by vigorously preaching, despite the lack of support from family members. Guru and Gaurāṅga came first, even in his household affairs. Thus, when family life became an impediment to his devotional service, he took up the retired life of *vānaprastha.*

In the *vānaprastha-āśrama,* he performed severe austerities, foregoing even proper dress and lodging while carrying on with his spiritual duties of studying, writing, publishing, and distributing books. Finally, when his spiritual master called him to renounce, despite feeling reluctant, he once again surrendered and accepted the renounced order.

As a *sannyāsī,* he made the supreme sacrifice and renounced residence in Vṛndāvana, the most comfortable atmosphere for a *sannyāsī.* He surrendered again to the order of his spiritual master and, at great personal inconvenience, in his old age traveled and preached to save the world. And he remained steady in the discharge of his higher duties.

As the head of an expansive spiritual institution, he surrendered to doing the needful, taking full responsibility, even in the face of criticism by so-called religious authorities that he was acting in a way unbecoming a *sannyāsī*by initiating fallen Westerners and engaging women in service to the Deities. Śrīla Prabhupāda vividly demonstrated that the goal of life is to develop a surrendered attitude toward *guru, sādhu,* and *śāstra.* Indeed, he proved that surrender is for everyone as an ongoing affair.

The last point is that only by surrender can we overcome all enigmas. A surrendered soul feels no inconvenience even in trying circumstances; but a defiant person, even if he appears to be in a perfect situation externally, internally experiences the greatest hardship. We actually feel distress until we surrender. The sense of affliction comes not from surrendering to Kṛṣṇa but from the internal friction caused by the mind’s resistance to the process of surrender, which is natural for the soul.

Since Śrīla Prabhupāda’s departure, our ability to surrender has been damaged by internal politics and the fall of unsurrendered authorities. But we must always remember Śrīla Prabhupāda’s teaching that we are not surrendering to just one particular individual. We are surrendering to the religious principles enunciated by the Supreme Lord Himself and perfectly applied by the previous *ācāryas.* Without being surrendered by nature, we cannot be submissive; and only by submissive aural reception can we wake up to our original position as servants of Kṛṣṇa. Then we can actually be free from material distress.

As we examine ourselves, the members of our society, and its leaders, we may tend to compare one another with Śrīla Prabhupāda, as we knew him. Am I as humble? Is he or she as tolerant, kind, responsible, competent, and advanced? When we fall into that trap, it would behoove us to remember that we are comparing ourselves to a personage who surrendered at each stage of his life as an example for us to follow. False expectations bring disappointment. No one can live in constant disappointment. Therefore, we must abandon our false expectations and not compare ourselves to Śrīla Prabhupāda at the last stage of his life. Rather we should look to the example he set when he was in the stage of life we are in at present. After all, his whole life was a mission to teach every one of us how to be a surrendered soul at every step along the path back home, back to Godhead.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

From an eternal son,  
Keśava Bhāratī Dāsa Goswami

## Krishna dās Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet.

On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I would like to express my deep appreciation and gratitude for your continued mercy and kindness in my preaching and service to you and Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

I was born in a *brāhmaṇa* family, and from my early childhood I was made aware of the importance of the *Bhagavad-gītā*. Later, after my initiation at the age of sixteen, I was able to memorize all of the *ślokas* of the *Gītā,* and when I came to the UK in the sixties I preached the message of *Gītā*to others. Śrīla Prabhupāda, after being introduced to ISKCON and discovering how widely your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* was being distributed all over the world, I was anxious to meet you and serve you. By Kṛṣṇa’s mercy, the day did arrive when I was able to take your *darśana* and talk to you, and you asked me to commit my life to ISKCON. This I did, and I moved to Bury Place and later to Bhaktivedanta Manor, having renounced my family. I was so happy preaching from your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* and that is true to this day.

Whenever it was Gītā Jayantī day, I would always feel that it should be celebrated with greater jubilance and ceremony, the same as Janmāṣṭamī is celebrated. It is said in the *Gītā*, 18.70:

*adhyeṣyate ca ya imaṁ dharmyaṁ saṁvādam āvayoḥ*  
*jñāna-yajñena tenāham iṣṭaḥ syām iti me matiḥ*

**“And I declare that he who studies this sacred conversation of ours worships Me by his intelligence.”**

Just over eleven years ago, Gītā Jayantī day happened to arrive while I was with my disciples in South Africa. It had been raining profusely for weeks. I instructed my disciples to build a huge tent and construct several *havan kuṇḍas*. This they did. On the day of Gītā Jayantī I then recited every *śloka* of the *Gītā,* and the disciples and guests offered *ahutīs* in the *havan kuṇḍas*. To our amazement, the rain stopped for the duration of the eight-hour *yajña*. Everyone, including myself, was amazed, inspired, and enthused by this super event. Since then we have been performing this *mahā-yajña* not only in South Africa but also in many other countries, such as Botswana, UK, and Bhārata. Last year, it was performed for the first time in Mauritius.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, since that first *Gītā mahā-yajña*, this *yajña* is now being performed by many of your disciples and granddisciples throughout the world, and a great number of *Gītā*s are sponsored and distributed during that period. Without your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and without your ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the message of the *Gītā* would not have been disseminated as it has been in Kali-yuga. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your mercy and for engaging us fallen souls in Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I continue to marvel at this wonderful movement of yours and its priceless value and benefit to humanity, and although my health is failing, I remain greatly inspired by your mercy to do more and more for Kṛṣṇa. I pray for your continued mercy so that I may go on doing whatever I can in your service and in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Your humble servant  
Krishna dās Swami

## Lokanāth Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you on your one hundred and ninth Vyāsa-pūjā day.

As I am writing this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I am down here in Mauritius, a tiny island in the Indian Ocean that you blessed by your auspicious visit.

Today is Rāma-navamī. On this occasion, we think of Lord Rāma and Rāma-rājya. Mauritius, as well as India, was ruled by the British some hundred years ago. The leaders of both nations fought to make their nations independent. In Calcutta, during your youth, you were preparing to join Mahatma Gandhi’s movement to throw out the British rule.

During your first meeting with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Prabhupāda, you stated that making the nation of India independent must be the priority.

“We are a dependent country,” you said. “First India must become independent. How can we spread Indian culture if we are under British rule?”

This was a challenging question.

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī replied in a quiet and deep voice: “Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so important — so exclusively important — that it cannot wait.”

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī continued to explain that whether one power or another ruled was a temporary situation, but the eternal reality is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the real self is the spirit soul. No manmade political system, therefore, could actually help humanity.

You felt defeated. But you liked it. It was not a loss. It was an immense gain. Mahatma Gandhi and the freedom fighters continued their struggle. Mahatma Gandhi at least spoke of Rāma-rājya. He proposed that once the country was independent he would implement the principles of Rāma-rājya. But soon after independence, Mahatma Gandhi was assassinated. And the first Prime Minister of India declared that the dams, bridges, and industries would be the new temples of independent and modern India. Unfortunately, this idea smashed the vision and defeated the purpose of independence for India.

Economic development has now become the mantra and motivating force of India’s political leaders. After a country develops its economy, luxurious sense gratification follows. The leaders of India are now propagating the glories of *artha* and *kāma*. They are following only two of the five *puruṣārthas*. Unfortunately, they are forgetting *dharma* and *mokṣa*. And most lamentable is that they are forgetting *prema,* or love of Godhead.

Recently, the current prime minister of India visited Mauritius. There is a world of difference between your visit and the visit of the prime minister. In his visit there was no trace of the representation of Vedic India and its values. The prime minister’s whole focus was entirely on economic development — information technology, industrial development, and the inauguration of the Cyber City project.

The prime minister is known for being a world-renowned economist. He came to Mauritius to impose his economic agenda. In this way, he was solidifying the illusion and false perception of the people of Mauritius.

There are two economies in the world, industrial and agrarian. The agrarian economy is grounded in India’s Vedic culture and supported by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s *varṇāśrama* system.

During a visit to Mauritius in 1975, you expressed your vision for the island in a lecture attended by some of the nation’s leading citizens. You stated:

So I see in your this Mauritius land you have got enough land to produce food grains. . . . \[Then you challenged:\] I understand that instead of growing food grains you are growing sugar cane for exporting. Why? . . . You are dependent on food grains — on rice, wheat, dahl. . . . Why this attempt \[at growing sugar cane\]? You first of all grow your own eatables. And if there is time and if your population has got sufficient food grains, then you can try to grow other fruits and vegetables for exporting.

You went on to say,

I have traveled all over the world — Africa, Australia, and . . . in America also. There is so much land vacant that if we produce food grains, then we can feed ten times as much population as at the present moment. There is no question of scarcity. The whole creation is so made by Kṛṣṇa that everything is *pūrṇam,* complete.

Later you said,

I have seen in the Western countries, they are growing food grains for the animals, and the food grains are eaten by the animals, and the animal is eaten by man. . . . What are the statistics? The animal is eating food grains . . . and the same amount food grains can be eaten by so many men.

Thank you for presenting the facts. These simple, down-to-earth economic equations clearly show us the proper way of life.

The West is known for economic and industrial development, as well as for scientific advancement. India’s contribution to the world is upholding, propagating, and globalizing the principles of religion — *bhāgavata-dharma* and its essence, the knowledge of the *Bhagavad-gītā*.

You are a true *bhārata-vāsī*because you are a loyal representative of *bhāgavata-dharma* and *bhāgavata* culture.

Once, while you were in London, during a press conference a reporter asked you why you had come. You responded by explaining how the British rulers and viceroys had plundered India. They had stolen whatever they thought was valuable and taken it away to exploit, enjoy, or just display in a London museum. But, you added, the British had left the most valuable items of India behind. The reporter wanted to know what they were. You responded that the most precious thing is India’s ancient culture. *Śāstras* are our real treasure, and the holy names are our real jewels. You declared that you had come to freely deliver these most valuable goods free of cost.

On behalf of Lord Caitanya, you established this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement as the infrastructure to continue to deliver the most valuable gifts of the Indian subcontinent, Bhārata-varṣa.

So many empires have risen and fallen, come and gone. They were all based on concoctions and speculations by ambitious individuals or groups. The British were proud that the sun never set on the British Empire. So, where is that empire now?

In 1976, you wrote to all your GBCs:

My dear GBC disciples,  
Please accept my blessings. Over the past ten years I have given the framework and now we have become more than the British Empire. Even the British Empire was not as expansive as we. They had only a portion of the world, and we have not completed expanding. We must expand more and more unlimitedly. . . .

In contrast to those who established the British Empire, you as the *senapati-bhakta* commander-in-chief of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* army, continue to spread your ISKCON Society rapidly. The latest calculation is that the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement has made inroads in one hundred and sixty countries on the planet. Now wherever and whenever the sun rises, *maṅgala-ārati* is being performed. Conch shells are blowing and the *mahā-mantra* is being broadcast. This progress will not stop until the goal of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa in every country, every town, and every village is reached.

One hundred and sixty countries is an achievement of only the last forty years. In 2005 we will see the 40thanniversary of your journey to America. In another forty years, we can’t even imagine how much more expansion there will be. Most empires have a life of one hundred or two hundred years at the most. The empire you are building is destined to last for ten thousand years. You will remain known as a true ambassador of India, wherein lies the welfare of all nations around the world.

The other day here in Mauritius we had a ceremony, and five sets of Deities were welcomed by five families. During the ceremony, a thought occurred to me. I was thinking that very soon, in the next five hundred or one thousand years, all the two hundred villages of Mauritius, consisting of thousands of families, will have the original founding fathers of the *saṅkīrtana* movement as their worshipable Deity — *yuga-avatāra* Gaura-Nitāi.

Today, on Rāma-navamī, we are seeing the spread of Caitanya-rājya — the spiritual kingdom of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Your dream of Mauritius becoming the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world is swiftly becoming a reality.

Seeing the thousands in attendance on this small island of Mauritius, and seeing their association with Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement at ISKCON’S *bhāgavata-kathā*and Ratha-yātrā programs, I realize the great potential of your preaching.

*Ebe jaśa ghuṣuk tribhuvana:* “May your glories be spread throughout the three worlds.”

Your eternal servant,  
Lokanāth Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your wonderful, inimitable personality is present in your amazing words, and your words always seem to reveal more and more about Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Absolute Personality of Godhead. Your words always seem to be teaching us and answering our innermost questions at any time and place. As you explain in *Easy Journey to Other Planets,* “The spiritual master is the external manifestation of God, who is situated in everyone’s heart as Supersoul.” You are the representative of Kṛṣṇa who has came to try to save me. Despite all this, I still want to misuse my independence and avoid surrendering fully. That is my predicament. What will become of me?

My only hope is to please you and become purified before it is too late, so that Kṛṣṇa might overlook my faults and accept me. Somehow or other I have to preach, inspired by following your example and words:

Just like the members of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness Society. What is their endeavor? Their only endeavor is how this Kṛṣṇa consciousness should be spread all over the world. Why they’re endeavoring? Because Kṛṣṇa wants it. Kṛṣṇa wants it. God wants it. Why God wants it? Because we are all sons of God. We have gone to dog. Instead of going to God, we have gone to dog. Therefore God is very anxious to reclaim us. He comes therefore in person, Kṛṣṇa. He sends His son, Lord Jesus Christ, or He sends His devotee. He leaves behind Him the books like *Bhagavad-gītā* or *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* or many other such literatures. Why? To reclaim these obstinate, bewildered sons of God. They have forgotten God, and they are, instead of loving God, they are now loving dog. Therefore God is very anxious. It is natural. If a father is very rich man and he has got many sons, he wants to see the sons very happy. But if a son goes away from home and becomes crazy, does not come back home, the father is more anxious than the son. The son does not understand what is his precarious condition of life in this material world, but God understands how much in miserable condition he’s living. Threefold miserable condition of this material existence. He’s always disturbed, but still he does not wish to go back to Godhead. Therefore there is a constant endeavor on the part of God to reclaim these conditioned souls.

But the pain is more felt by God than the living creature in this conditioned life. So it is always . . . There is always endeavor on the part of God to send His representative; He comes Himself, He gives book. So anyone who takes up this endeavor on behalf of God to reclaim these conditioned souls back to Godhead, back to home, he is considered the most intimate devotee, dear devotee of the Lord. It is stated in the *Bhagavad-gītā, na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*. If you want to become very dear to Kṛṣṇa, or God, then try to take up these missionary activities. What is that? Spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kṛṣṇa will be very much pleased. \[Class on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.10, Montreal, 12 July 1968\]

Recently we held a very successful Ninth Anniversary Festival of the installation of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindahari in Kathmandu, Nepal. I saw that by the enthusiastic preaching spirit of your gemlike granddisciple, many Nepalese youth are now taking life-giving nectar from your books and instructions and dancing and chanting in glee.

*Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare*

In the early days of ISKCON, your preaching victory was also a youth preaching victory:

My students are within thirties. The oldest student I have got at the present moment, he is twenty-eight years. No, Kīrtanānanda is about thirty years old. That’s all. So, of course, I do not get any older people. That is nice, hopeful, because younger section, if they take it very seriously, then I have hopes that they will preach in future, even in my absence. And old people, if a man becomes too much accustomed to a certain limited habit, in old age it is very difficult to give it up unless one is extraordinarily intelligent. \[Interview, Los Angeles, 1 February 1968\]

Your granddisciple Pātrī Dāsa overglorified me, his spiritual uncle, at the Youth Festival program. He said that he felt just like a tiny insect hidden inside a flower being offered to Kṛṣṇa by me and his Guru Mahārāja, His Holiness Prabhaviṣṇu Swami. He meant that for many years we Prabhupāda disciples had been his preaching forerunners in Nepal and had helped lay the foundation for his success in igniting the long-awaited youth preaching explosion. I replied that I felt differently. I felt I was just an old worn-out broken devotee stumbling on the point of hopeless collapse, and that he, my spiritual nephew, had kindly paused to pick me up and inspire me. I felt he was saying in his heart:

“Come on, Uncle Mahārāja, you are not really old yet! Śrīla Prabhupāda was ten years older when he went to the USA! Don’t be hopeless and give up now! Can’t you see you have not failed? It is happening! The youth are taking it up at last! Now they need you! It is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission, and we are all his family servants! His message is ever fresh and youthful and full of preaching life! We must all pull together to please Śrīla Prabhupāda! Śrīla Prabhupāda’s family members will all get Kṛṣṇa’s mercy together and go back to Godhead! Haribol!!”

I finally said that this must be Kṛṣṇa’s plan: that I am neglecting Nepal a bit and going to Congo, in the dark continent of Africa, to help lay another ISKCON foundation so that His trained-up Gorkha (Nepalese) youth preachers (or any adventurous devotees who should please learn French) can storm over to ignite the ISKCON Central African Youth Explosion. Gaurāṅga!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please have special consideration for my degraded self! Please do not withdraw your mercy from me now!! The latest report is that now ISKCON is registered in Congo and your books are going out at last. Thank you for this chance to please you.

Your humble servant,  
Mahāvishnu Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In December 1969 I drove with several other devotees from Buffalo, New York, to a *harināma* in New York City, before going on to Boston for Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura’s disappearance day. To honor this day, Śrīla Prabhupāda was also on his way to the Boston temple, which was at that time a newly acquired two-story building. The first floor was used for ISKCON Press, the second for the temple room and altar.

When Śrīla Prabhupāda arrived at Boston airport, he was enthusiastically greeted by practically all the devotees from the east coast of the USA. Not having gone to the airport, I waited for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s arrival in the temple, along with some other devotees. When Śrīla Prabhupāda entered the temple and walked up the stairs to the temple room, his presence filled the entire atmosphere, which became like Vaikuṇṭha. Experiencing this divine energy, I had no doubt that Śrīla Prabhupāda was the empowered representative of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

As the faithful servant of his spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda was always absorbed in meditation on his Guru Mahārāja, and through him on the disciplic succession of spiritual masters, on the six Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, and on the Pañca-tattva. Moreover, in the mood of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he always felt separation from Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and Their associates in Vṛndāvana.

Lord Caitanya is saving the world by empowering His representatives. When we assist Him by desiring to use in His service whatever energies He provides, our perfection of transcendental knowledge and pure devotion will surely come. Therefore, in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda never felt any need to introduce something new, because since time immemorial the path back to the spiritual world had been well defined. In this way, by following Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps, executing his instructions, and repeating his words according to our realization, we can be assured of success for ourselves and for others.

That Śrīla Prabhupāda was the representative of the Supersoul and was empowered by Him became clear to me in the beginning of 1969, when Śrīla Prabhupāda was visiting the temple in Buffalo. During that visit, he spoke to an Indian gentleman, Dr. Lal, who had invited the devotees to open a temple there. Dr. Lal was an impersonalist and could not accept that Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa is a person. Śrīla Prabhupāda used the example of how the word of the mother is decisive in determining who our father is. Similarly, the *Vedas* are our mother, and she tells us that Lord Kṛṣṇa is our father. Unfortunately, Dr. Lal could not accept this analogy. Śrīla Prabhupāda patiently repeated the analogy, but when he saw that this gentleman remained unconvinced, he changed the subject, leaving me to wonder why he had not used some other example or argument. However, now I understand that if someone cannot understand or accept something simple, how will he ever accept or understand something more complex? Such clear logic and simple methods are characteristic of one who is in contact with the Supersoul and empowered by Him to preach.

Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s name, form, and qualities were present in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s heart, and Lord Kṛṣṇa and His associates were having their pastimes there. The entire atmosphere surrounding Śrīla Prabhupāda was therefore surcharged with an ever-flowing spiritual potency that never failed to enlighten and enliven others with transcendental knowledge and bliss. This spiritual potency manifested itself in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s keen intelligence, good qualities, and devotion, which entered into the hearts of his sincere followers, encouraging them to also serve as instruments in Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s divine *līlā* of spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness around the world.

Your aspiring servant,  
Prahlādānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

If we look into Vaiṣṇava history, there are always great luminaries who restore the preaching of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. When they leave, the world is enveloped in darkness, but Kṛṣṇa always sends persons who are like the sun, which dispels the darkness, and like the stormy winds, which blow away the clouds covering the sun. Such great stalwart devotees carry their own spiritual master in their hearts, and in this way they are infused with transcendental potency.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 22.65), these great devotees, who have absolute faith in Kṛṣṇa and their spiritual master, are described:

*śāstra-yuktye sunipuṇa, dṛḍha-śraddhā yāṅra*  
*‘uttama-adhikārī sei tāraye saṁsāra*

**“One who is expert in logic, argument, and the revealed scriptures and who has firm faith in Kṛṣṇa is classified as a topmost devotee. He can deliver the whole world.”**

Such a devotee is very expert in argument and logic and has *dṛḍha-śraddhā,* firm faith and confidence in Kṛṣṇa. As a result, he can deliver the whole world (*tāraye saṁsāra*).

We know that Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī trained three students who had this wonderful ability of the *uttama-adhikārīs*: Śyāmānanda, who delivered Orissa, Śrīnivāsa, who delivered Bengal, and Narottama, who went to Manipur and Assam. After Mahāprabhu’s disappearance, these three devotees were particularly empowered to carry on the spiritual inheritance of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the six Gosvāmīs.

By the influence of time, the pure teachings and also the transcendental potency gradually disappeared, and the world sank into darkness. When Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura appeared, he was disappointed that although the Indians have very favorable cultural *saṁskāras*, they were turning largely to Western ways. But he carried this potency in his heart, and soon the pure teachings of Mahāprabhu were spread. By Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s intense desire, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura appeared, who carried the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu forward even after the disappearance of his illustrious father. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was very keen on spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the West, so he sent his disciples out to preach, but they did not meet with so much success.

Then one day you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, offered your obeisances to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. You were still in the kneeling position when he enjoined you to spread the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura had recognized you as the person who would carry the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu to the Western shores: Kṛṣṇa had sent the right person.

Today, on your divine appearance day, we are extremely happy and grateful to know you as such an extraordinary transcendental torchbearer. Your glories have become accepted by all.

On this divine appearance day of yours, I stand before you with a humble prayer:

By your inconceivable mercy, you have somehow entered more and more into my rebellious heart. The sincere desire to please you through following your divine instructions to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the unfortunate souls has become more and more established in my heart. But over the years, this proud disciple of yours has seen without any illusion that he is incapable of spreading this mission alone. Oh, how much I would like to catch hold of your divine lotus feet to become an empowered instrument! At the same time, I can see that I am not yet free from the contaminations of material existence. In fact, I see myself as very weak and in need of your mercy and guidance. Please somehow take mercy on me and overlook my bad qualities. Please allow me to serve you, and please instill whatever I lack in my heart. Time is running short: Please let me become your loving, surrendered servant.

Thanking you very much for your patience and your extraordinary mercy,  
Your humble and most unqualified servant,  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Smita Krishna Swami

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You left us a big house, a big task. You said that we at least should maintain what you had given. By your grace and Kṛṣṇa’s mercy things are expanding. You have many sincere followers, through whom your mercy manifests. In some places there is not so much expansion. To maintain requires *sattva-guṇa*. I fear *tamo-guṇa*. Things come and things go.

The story in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* about the astrologer Sarvajña, who visited a poor man in his house, comes to my mind. You are like the astrologer giving us the information about the hidden treasure. You have also given us the information how to get to that treasure. At the same time, I feel as if you are the father who has left us with a big house, but I might not understand that there is a hidden treasure. Or, I know you left a treasure, but I dig on the wrong side. *Karma, jñāna,* and *yoga* do not bring the desired result. *Bhakti* is the path, the method, and the goal. *Bhakti* means *bhakti* for Kṛṣṇa.

You teach us that Kṛṣṇa is never alone. Along with Kṛṣṇa there is Rādhārāṇī, the personification of *bhakti* and His internal pleasure potency. With Him are also His various other energies, including the living entities. We used to say that if there is love of Kṛṣṇa there is also love for His parts and parcels. Then our *bhakti* would be complete.

You are the guru and I am the student. Whether I am a good student I do not know, but I am still your student and will so remain. Sometimes I feel your presence and I wonder if it is real. The experience is real — that is enough. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your guidance and your presence.

Your attempting servant,  
Smita Krishna Swami

## Śubhag Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

When I reflect, I find how you dedicated everything in your possession to fulfill the desires of the Lord and your spiritual master. Sometimes the Lord appears Himself, and at other times He sends his associates to bring back the fallen souls to the spiritual world.

In this age, the people in general are most fallen. To save them, the Lord appeared in His most munificent forms, Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda Prabhu. It all happened over five hundred years ago. Then Lord Caitanya asked Lord Nityānanda and Haridāsa Ṭhākura to deliver the most fallen and sinful brothers Jagāi and Mādhāi.

Now the whole world is full of Jagāis and Mādhāis. Now you have come. You are a pure devotee. A pure devotee is *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī*. You deeply felt compassion for them all. So the Lord especially selected a very rare associate of His to fulfill His mission. When the Lord especially favors someone, He takes away all his wealth. He did the same with you. So you renounced the world. You took the *sannyāsa* order under the direction of your Guru Mahārāja. Then you went to Vṛndāvana. You dived deep and studied all the six Gosvāmīs’ literature, and the essential works of these most valuable treasures were translated by you and distributed throughout the world, and thus you saved millions of most fallen and sinful souls.

When you lived in Vṛndāvana you were especially inspired and blessed by Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Dāmodara and Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī. They were very pleased with your prayers and humble service. So you worked very hard. You had almost nothing; you lived in a very humble way. You lived like the six Gosvāmīs. Thus you taught us how to live an ideal life of a Vaiṣṇava.

You took great risk, and at the age of seventy you sailed for the USA. When you landed there, you didn’t know where to go or where to reside. You had nothing in your pocket for your sustenance. You said, “I took the word of my spiritual master seriously and tried to implement it to the best of my capacity and all sincerity. As a result, the Lord, His associates, the previous *ācāryas,* and my beloved Guru Mahārāja have blessed me, and this movement has been established.”

In a lecture on the disappearance day of your Guru Mahārāja, you related your experiences to your disciples, and they were very moved. You poured out your heart’s feelings for your Guru Mahārāja:

By Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement, we came in contact. I was born in a different family, my Guru Mahārāja was born in a different family. Who knew that I will come to his protection? Who knew that I would come in America? Who knew that you American boys will come to me? These are all Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement. We cannot understand how things are taking place.

Your voice choked up. You couldn’t speak. What can we say about your loving feeling for both your spiritual master and your disciples?

Please bestow your mercy so that we can follow in your footprints with all sincerity and seriousness.

Begging to remain your humble servant eternally,  
Your servant,  
Subhāg Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisance.

By good fortune and the passing of time, we are able to see more and more clearly how exalted your position is, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The gurus have had their chance to establish themselves during the last so many years, but now we can see practically what is their position and what is your position.

I remember one of the original gurus wrote in 1980, “Isn’t it wonderful that previously there was only one but now there are eleven.” And others told us the “guru is one,” putting themselves on a level with you. As it is said, “Familiarity breeds contempt.” Now, as the years go by and we serve in separation, everyone is forced to acknowledge what should have been so apparent to us all from the beginning. You are not ordinary, even when compared to the recognized pure devotees of our tradition, what to speak of in comparison with us.

Still, with all of this knowledge and passage of time, I have not become really humble: still the desire for name and fame, for honor, haunts me. Why is it so difficult to put aside these illusions of grandeur and to honestly accept my position as your humble servant? I know intellectually that this desire for honor is due to false ego connected to the mode of passion, but still my foolish mind hankers for it. Please punish me so that this desire will be purged from my psyche. I am such a low rascal that I can’t learn in any way except by being forced. Therefore I humbly request you not to spare me the full brunt of your heaviest chastisement. Only then will there be a chance for me to be of some value to you. Praying for your mercy in this way, I beg to remain at your lotus feet.

Your servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umapati Swami

**A Bewildered Child**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Every year about this time, when I write my Vyāsa-pūjā offering, my mind wanders back to the early days at 26 Second Avenue. Today I have been thinking about Śrīla Prabhupāda’s routine before the evening program.

Śrīla Prabhupāda would come out of the shower and put on *tilaka*. This much I understood. Then he would wrap his sacred thread around his thumb and say a mantra. What it was, I didn’t know. Then he would turn and face his little table-altar with the pictures of his Guru Mahārāja and the Pañca-tattva. He would light some incense and offer it to the pictures while he rang a bell and said another mantra. “The swami is saying Bells,” we would say.

Today, I look back and laugh at my bewilderment, but what strikes me is that when I look back on these days, I see them in the same perspective as my early childhood. I see myself looking at Śrīla Prabhupāda in the same way that I remember myself as a dumbfounded little child watching the adults do things I did not quite understand.

I see things this way as I look back, but I did not see them this way at the time. As a child and as a new devotee, I thought I knew what was happening. Didn’t we all? So now I have to wonder: Years from now, when I look back on today, will I see myself as the same dumbfounded little child?

The question has taken on a new importance because more and more I hear devotees lightly dismissing many of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instructions as meant for neophytes and spiritual infants. We don’t have to worry so much about what Śrīla Prabhupāda said anymore because we have grown into big devotees.

Consider Śrīla Prabhupāda’s instruction not to go outside of ISKCON. “When you are a child, your father may tell you not to cross the street,” say the big devotees, “but when you grow up you do not have to worry about that.”

Am I really grown up now? I thought I was grown up in 1966, and I remember thinking I had finally hit the big time in 1941, on my fourth birthday. Cute, huh?

I thought I knew the score when I took to the path of unrestricted sense gratification. Oh yes, I knew it all, didn’t I? But I surely would have died a stupid death long ago if Śrīla Prabhupāda had not intervened and saved me.

So I wonder, Have I ever *not* been a bewildered child? But I can’t think of a time.

Back in 1941, I might have thought I could cross a busy street by myself, but my father would remind me: “Take my hand.”

Now I am faced with trying to cross the banyan forest of the material world. Am I big enough to do it by myself now? Am I big enough to find my way out of this dense forest where no one can find the beginning or the end? I might think so, but years from now, when I look back, will I see that I was still a bewildered child?

Śrīla Prabhupāda is holding out his hand. “Take it,” he tells me, but I cannot reach all the way up to Śrīla Prabhupāda’s hand. I can just about make it to his lotus feet.

But that’s okay. I’ll hang on there. May Śrīla Prabhupāda be merciful and drag this bewildered child back home. Otherwise, I’ll never find my way.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

As the passage of time diminishes the vitality of everything in this world, a darkening backdrop of material nature highlights more and more the eternal illumination emanating from remembrance of you.

Like spiritual gems continually washing ashore from the ocean of nectar, ever-fresh realizations of your glories and pastimes continue to refresh our spirits and lure our hearts to the distant homeland where you await our return.

As I hear how your Gurudeva was imploring you to cross the ocean and deliver us, the fallen souls of the Western world, my heart finds assurance that all along, even before you left India, your Gurudeva and you were hearing and reciprocating our prayers and aspirations.

Indeed, your momentous journey to the West signifies the fruition of a long history of sacrifice, born of the compassion of the *paramparā,* culminating in the fulfillment of Śrīmān Mahāprabhu’s prediction that the holy name would spread to every town and village in the world.

In the setting of gems of remembrance of you, there is a crest jewel which is most effulgent — a beacon of hope in a darkening world.

It was Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, the most blessed event in 1972, and you smiled so lovingly upon the cows and their herdsmen as you ascended the hill to join Their Lordships in accepting our worship. Afterwards, on that sacred hilltop, as we honored *prasādam* in a picnic with you, it was as if a verse of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* suddenly came to life in a dynamic visualization:

*kṛṣṇe sva-dhāmopagate dharma-jñānādibhiḥ saha*  
*kalau naṣṭa-dṛśām eṣa purāṇārko ‘dhunoditaḥ*

**“This** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **is as brilliant as the sun, and it has arisen just after the departure of Lord Kṛṣṇa to His own abode, accompanied by religion, knowledge, etc. Persons who have lost their vision due to the dense darkness of ignorance in the Age of Kali shall get light from this** ***Purāṇa*****.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.43)**

Truly you personified the *Bhāgavatam,* revealed the book *Bhāgavatam,* and brought it to life in an inconceivable manner. It was as if the dark clouds of Kali parted for a while, allowing the light and love of Goloka Vṛndāvana to shine through, illuminating New Vrindāvan as nondifferent from original Vṛndāvana, awarding living visualization, realization, and revelation of your words.

So deeply did this pastime illumine our world, right down to the depths of my heart, that it continues to dispel the darkness that ensued after your disappearance pastime.

Gradually I am coming to terms with your leaving this world, seeing how it enkindled a fire of separation, leaving our heart to blaze in the years of darkness due to separation from you.

Seeing that your disappearance was not your final pastime, but one of a series of emerging gems, I accept it now and offer it a place of honor among the other jewels of remembrance.

Today, remembrance of you came in a most special way. I add that jewel to my collection, and it lightens and brightens my way through dark and difficult times, revealing your footsteps as they ever guide our path to the land where walking turns to dancing, communication to singing — where Kṛṣṇa’s flute is ever calling, and time is conspicuous by its absence.

Your servant in separation,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your purity in devotional services to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga with unflinching faith in their order! Your purity is the force of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. It is still breathing because of your purity of purpose in creating it. And you are very much attached to it and running it still, because it is ordained by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and subsequent *ācāryas*. You have firm faith in their words, and that is why all the Vedic truths are revealed to you.

You have been cautiously watching what is happening in your movement from up there. Now, with *mahā-rathīs* like TKG, Gaura Govinda Mahārāja, and Śrīdhara Mahārāja at your side, you won’t allow your movement to stagnate. Even though you wanted your spiritual sons and daughters to do their best in sacrificing their lives for service to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, you gladly accepted whatever contributions they could offer you. I am inspired by them to do something for your pleasure. But I am weak and fumbling. I seek their mercy and try to cooperate with their desires. At least I can do that much to please you, because you said that our love for you would be shown by how we cooperate after your departure. Therefore I accept my weakness and my fumbling attempts in the performance of my service and cultivate a sincere desire to become stronger all around so I can serve you better. This is my heartfelt prayer to you on this auspicious day of your appearance.

I realize that there are many sincere disciples of yours, both inside and outside your movement, who over the years have been serving externally and internally in this mood, and you have been magnanimously guiding them and mercifully keeping them alive in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, exactly like the Supersoul. Therefore, I too have high hopes of receiving your mercy.

Your weak and fumbling servant,  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Abidjan

Dearest eternal grandfather,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, who are the dearmost ambassador of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the time of year has come again when the entire world pours out its heart at your divine lotus feet as we celebrate your divine Vyāsa-pūjā 2005.

Dear eternal grandfather, the world has not realized one percent of the wonders and treasures Your Divine Grace gave mankind. As we continue to study your books, we find that everything we need to go back home, back to Godhead, has been giving to us by you, O *jagad-guru,* savior of the whole world. We few lucky ones on this ISKCON boat, of which Your Divine Grace is the captain, pray to you to please bestow your divine mercy upon us so that the waves of this ocean of material miseries do not sweep any of us out of your divine boat, which is taking us all back home, back to Godhead. Please give us a fearless heart as we go to every town and village preaching the message of Lord Caitanya.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your eternally indebted grandchildren at the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple in Abidjan, Ivory Coast, West Africa.  
(written by Kuśa dāsa)

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this 109th anniversary of your appearance day! Hare Kṛṣṇa.

For some inconceivable reason you appeared in Calcutta and made that city the most blessed city in the world. You performed your childhood Ratha-yātrā there and went to Motilal Seal High School and Scottish Churches’ College and purified all these places. While always contemplating how to spread the mission of your Guru Mahārāja, you set the example of a householder Vaiṣṇava. As Lord Caitanya told Raghunātha dāsa,

*prabhura śikṣāte teṅho nija-ghare yāya*  
*markaṭa-vairāgya chāḍi’ hailā‘viṣayi-prāya’*

**“Instead of becoming a so-called renunciant, Raghunātha dāsa, following the instructions of the Lord, returned home and played exactly like a pounds-and-shillings man.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya*** **6.14)**

*bhitare vairāgya, bāhire kare sarva-karma*  
*dekhiyā ta’ mātā-pitāra ānandita mana*

**“Raghunātha dāsa was inwardly completely renounced, even in family life, but he did not express his renunciation externally. Instead, he acted just like an ordinary businessman. Seeing this, his father and mother were satisfied.”**

In the same way, you outwardly remained in householder life while at every moment simply devising different plans to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your pastimes in householder life were just like those of Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura or Śrīvāsa Ṭhākura or Advaita Ācārya. In 1896 Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, desiring to fulfill Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s prediction that the holy name of the Lord would spread to every town and village in the world, sent his book *Sri Chaitanya Mahaprabhu: His Life and Precepts* to McGill University, in Montreal, Canada. By Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement you appeared in the same year to fulfill this prophecy. In fact, by the grace of all these great Vaiṣṇavas you were able to take *sannyāsa* exactly when required, and then you surpassed all other *sannyāsīs* in the preaching field.

Even though all these facts are obvious, the envious still cannot see them, just as the owl cannot see the sun. As the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Ādi* 3.84–86) says concerning the divinity of Lord Caitanya:

*bhāgavata, bhārata-śāstra, āgama, purāṇa*  
*caitanya-kṛṣṇa-avatāre prakaṭa pramāṇa*

**“*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*** **the** ***Mahābhārata,*** **the** ***Purāṇas,*** **and other Vedic literatures all give evidence to prove that Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu is the incarnation of Kṛṣṇa.**

*pratyakṣe dekhaha nānā prakaṭa prabhāva*  
*alaukika karma, alaukika anubhāva*

**“One can also directly see Lord Caitanya’s manifest influence in His uncommon deeds and uncommon Kṛṣṇa conscious realization.**

*dekhiyā nā dekhe yata abhaktera gaṇa*  
*ulūke nā dekhe yena sūryera kiraṇa*

**“But faithless unbelievers do not see what is clearly evident, just as owls do not see the rays of the sun.”**

It was my great good fortune to personally see you and hear you. Once in Bombay you told me how you had faced so much difficulty in order to preach. You said, “I practically walked through fire to spread this mission.” When we look back at all the difficulties you faced, it appears that if you hadn’t faced and overcome every single one of them, the whole Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement wouldn’t have developed. Like Prahlāda Mahārāja, you went through so many tests. Like Lord Śiva, you drank poison to save the world. Like Lord Caitanya, you sacrificed your comforts and your family’s comforts to save us. You are a perfect example of *yukta-vairāgya* as taught by Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī and as mentioned by Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya in his verse about Lord Caitanya beginning *vairāgya-vidyā-nija-bhakti-yoga*.

The twentieth-century world was absorbed in materialistic progress and sense gratification. Māyā made more strides in one century than in thousands of prior years. And yet you were never affected by the *māyā*of the twentieth century.

In reference to your mood of preaching, we find this passage in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 7.128–30:

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**\[Lord Caitanya told Kūrma the** ***brāhmaṇa:*****\] “Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.”**

This is the sublime mission of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Many people come and inquire whether they have to give up family life to join the Society, but that is not our mission. One can remain comfortably in his residence. We simply request everyone to chant the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare. If one is a little literate and can read *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* that is so much the better. These works are now available in an English translation and are done very authoritatively to appeal to all classes of men. Instead of living engrossed in material activities, people throughout the world should take advantage of this movement and chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* at home with their families. One should also refrain from sinful activities — illicit sex, meat-eating, gambling, and intoxication. Out of these four items, illicit sex is very sinful. Every person must get married. Every woman especially must get married. If the women outnumber the men, some men can accept more than one wife. In that way there will be no prostitution in society. If men can marry more than one wife, illicit sex life will be stopped. One can also produce many nice preparations to offer Kṛṣṇa — grain, fruit, flowers, and milk. Why should one indulge in unnecessary meat-eating and maintain horrible slaughterhouses? What is the use of smoking and drinking tea and coffee? People are already intoxicated by material enjoyment, and if they indulge in further intoxication, what chance is there for self-realization? Similarly, one should not partake in gambling and unnecessarily agitate the mind. The real purpose of human life is to attain the spiritual platform and return to Godhead. That is the *summum bonum* of spiritual realization. The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is trying to elevate human society to the perfection of life by pursuing the method described by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His advice to the *brāhmaṇa* Kūrma. That is, one should stay at home, chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra,* and preach the instructions of Kṛṣṇa as they are given in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam.*

TEXT 129

*kabhu nā bādhibe tomāra viṣaya-taraṅga*  
*punarapi ei ṭhāñi pābe mora saṅga”*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu further advised the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **Kūrma, “If you follow this instruction, your materialistic life at home will not obstruct your spiritual advancement. Indeed, if you follow these regulative principles, we will again meet here, or, rather, you will never lose My company.”**

This is an opportunity for everyone. If one simply follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, under the guidance of His representative, and chants the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra, teaching everyone as far as possible the same principle, the contamination of the materialistic way of life will not even touch him. It does not matter whether one lives in a holy place like Vṛndāvana, Navadvīpa, or Jagannātha Purī or in the midst of European cities, where the materialistic way of life is very prominent. If a devotee follows the instructions of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, he lives in the company of the Lord. Wherever he lives, he converts that place into Vṛndāvana and Navadvīpa. This means that materialism cannot touch him. This is the secret of success for one advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

TEXT 130

*ei mata yāṅra ghare kare prabhu bhikṣā*  
*sei aiche kahe, tāṅre karāya ei śikṣā*

**At whosever house Śrī Caitanya accepted His alms by taking** ***prasādam,*** **He would convert the dwellers to His** ***saṅkīrtana*** **movement and advise them just as He advised the** ***brāhmaṇa*** **named Kūrma.**

This is so amazing. You almost repeated the same process five hundred years later, and you got exactly the results promised by the Lord. Your strong faith in the process of preaching is the source of all auspiciousness. Preaching is *saṅkīrtana* and is always victorious. *Paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam*.

My greatest inspiration in all situations is “Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma,” the prayer you wrote at the Boston Pier as you arrived in the USA.

My dear Lord Kṛṣṇa, You are so kind upon this useless soul, but I do not know why You have brought me here. Now You can do whatever You like with me. But I guess You have some business here; otherwise why would You bring me to this terrible place? Most of the population here is covered by the material modes of ignorance and passion. Absorbed in material life, they think themselves very happy and satisfied, and therefore they have no taste for the transcendental message of Vāsudeva \[Kṛṣṇa\]. I do not know how they will be able to understand it. . . .

How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own.

Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure, as you like. . . . O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if you have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.

Here you show extraordinary humility, enthusiasm, determination, and a clear idea of your mission, which could be called “mission impossible.” This poem will eternally be our inspiration.

When Sanātana Gosvāmī approached Lord Caitanya for instruction, he did so with the same humility you showed in your prayer and was empowered by the Lord, just as you were. This pastime is related in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 20.103–107:

*‘sādhya’-‘sādhana’-tattva puchite nā jāni*  
*kṛpā kari’ saba tattva kaha ta’ āpani“*

**\[Sanātana Gosvāmī addressed Lord Caitanya:\] “Actually I do not know how to inquire about the goal of life and the process for obtaining it. Being merciful upon me, please explain all these truths.”**

*prabhu kahe,—“kṛṣṇa-kṛpā tomāte pūrṇa haya*  
*saba tattva jāna, tomāra nāhi tāpa-traya*

**Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “Lord Kṛṣṇa has bestowed His full mercy upon you so that all these things are known to you. For you, the threefold miseries certainly do not exist.**

*kṛṣṇa-śakti dhara tumi, jāna tattva-bhāva*  
*jāni’ dārḍhya lāgi’ puche,—sādhura svabhāva*

**“Since you possess Lord Kṛṣṇa’s potency, you certainly know these things. However, it is the nature of a** ***sādhu*** **to inquire. Although he knows these things, the** ***sādhu*** **inquires for the sake of strictness.**

*acirād eva sarvārthaḥ sidhyaty eṣām abhīpsitaḥ*  
*sad-dharmasyāvabodhāya yeṣāṁ nirbandhinī matiḥ*

**“Those who are eager to awaken their spiritual consciousness and who thus have unflinching, undeviated intelligence certainly attain the desired goal of life very soon.’**

*yogya-pātra hao tumi bhakti pravartāite*  
*krame saba tattva śuna, kahiye tomāte*

**“You are fit to propagate the cult of devotional service. Therefore gradually hear all the truths about it from Me. I shall tell you about them.”**

Undoubtedly, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you perfectly understand Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s inner heart, and only you can bestow these inner meanings of the scriptures.

You are the embodiment of this verse from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.3.21):

*tasmād guruṁ prapadyeta jijñāsuḥśreya uttamam*  
*śābde pare ca niṣṇātaṁ brahmaṇy upaśamāśrayam*

**“Therefore any person who seriously desires real happiness must seek a bona fide spiritual master and take shelter of him by initiation. The qualification of the bona fide guru is that he has realized the conclusions of the scriptures by deliberation and is able to convince others of these conclusions. Such a great personality, who has taken shelter of the Supreme Godhead, leaving aside all material considerations, should be understood to be a bona fide spiritual master.”**

You are actually the sum total of all the demigods — *sarva-deva-mayo guruḥ*. So by worshiping you on this auspicious day, we worship all the demigods. And since you are *sākṣād* Hari, we also worship Kṛṣṇa by worshiping you. When Lord Caitanya told Sanātana Gosvāmī, “Kṛṣṇa is very kind upon you,” Sanātana replied, “I don’t know Kṛṣṇa. I know only that You are my savior.” In the same way, we don’t know Kṛṣṇa or Caitanya Mahāprabhu. We only know you as our eternal master and savior. Kṛṣṇa is yours, and you have the power to deliver Him: *kṛṣṇa se tomāra, kṛṣṇa dite pāra, tomāra śakati āche*. Please give us the shade of your lotus feet. Please give us the six good qualities, please remove the six types of faults, and please give us the six types of *sat-saṅga*.

Thank you very much.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON Ahmedabad, India.

## Almviks Gard

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, the savior of us all, on this auspicious day of your appearance!

I looked into one of Satsvarūpa Mahārāja’s books today and found an inspiring poem. I want to share a few lines from that poem:

The history of our movement — it’s about compassion.  
Compassion costs, hurts, takes us beyond ourselves,  
our voices become poetic — because we care.  
It’s the opposite of selfish and self-absorbed,  
although we say compassion begins with showing care for ourselves.  
It just doesn’t end there.

Compassion isn’t *only* giving people spiritual knowledge  
by selling them a book or telling them about Kṛṣṇa.  
I mean, that’s the end of it, but it includes plain old help — food, money, *care*  
for people — and not only for the fools “out there” but our *own* people too.

It means while delivering meals to a woman whose baby lies in the hospital,  
or sharing confidences while someone struggles with themselves,  
we apply the greatest balm to any person in any condition — the balm of devotional service to Bhagavān — to reach out with that.

These words made me remember stories from the “old times” ISKCON in New York. Stories of how you showered your immense compassion on your first disciples. You cared for them, cooked for them, listened to them, taught them patiently, gave them all your love, time, energy, attention — like a loving father. In return, out of love for you, they developed the desire to please you and serve you. It was a sweet exchange. This exchange of loving service among devotees is actually what keeps a spiritual movement alive. We might speak great learned words and engage in “important,” big preaching projects, but if we don’t know how to relate to one another and serve one another in a loving way like soft-hearted Vaiṣṇavas, our big programs will not convince anybody.

We live here as in a little village on our farm project in the cold climate of northern Scandinavia. We have realized what is keeping our community alive: our personal relationships and our caring for each other. And, of course, our personal relationship with and service to our presiding Deities, Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva. We are Kṛṣṇa’s family. You were very concerned about this point, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda: that we learn how to cooperate nicely in your service, in our service to Kṛṣṇa.

I feel very blessed. It is actually a privilege to live so secluded, out in the forest, in pure nature, and peacefully worship the Deities and associate with devotees. This is possible only by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are all very indebted to you. Please bless us with a spark of your unlimited enthusiasm and sincerity so that we may succeed in our task of maintaining and developing this project so it may reach its full potential.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your humble servants at New Chandrodaya Mandir, Almviks Gard, Sweden.  
(written by Aṣṭasiddhi-devī dāsī)

## Amravati

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your divine lotus feet on this most auspicious event of the 109th anniversary of your appearance.

All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!
