This price is simply the intense desire for it, and you taught us how to attain this desire. It is through selfless service to Kṛṣṇa, the spiritual master, and the Vaiṣṇavas, rendered out of love.

As you say:

The Spiritual Master is never without his followers, so to serve the Spiritual Master also means to be the servant of his disciples. When you want to serve the king, you must also serve his minister, secretary, and everyone who serves him. And to serve his servants may please him more than to serve the king personally. So the Spiritual Master is not alone. He is always with his entourage. We are not impersonalists. We take care of every part of the whole, as much as one should take care of his hat as well as his shoes. \[Letter to Śivānanda dāsa, 23 January 1969\]

“We are not impersonalists; we have to take care of every part of the whole.” By this we understand that we have to take care of your house — Prabhupāda’s House — so that everyone who has gotten your mercy and has read your books will feel welcome when he comes to the temple seeking shelter from the cold iron tongs of Kali-yuga. We must be anxious about spreading the *saṅkīrtana* mission so that every living entity has a chance to achieve shelter at Lord Caitanya’s lotus feet and be enlivened by His loving glance. You have given all of us this chance, and you have also given us the sweet duty to give this chance to others, and this obligation we feel makes us serve you.

At present here in Kiev, the construction of the temple where we are worshiping Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi is going on, and the second stage of construction is almost finished. We are hosting from two hundred to six hundred people during programs and festivals, trying to take care of your guests. We have a spiritual academy for studying the *śāstras,* as you desired. Now we have four courses in deep Vaiṣṇava education. Lots of devotees go out on the streets and to people’s homes, preaching and distributing your books and performing *harināma-saṅkīrtana* as well. They dance on the streets of the city, chanting the holy name and distributing *prasādam*. We have an ambitious Food for Life program also, so that many unfortunate people get their basic necessities — food (*prasādam*) and sometimes medicine, and even clothes for spiritual practice. Our preaching program directed toward students and intellectuals has started. We are establishing more and more centers here in Ukraine; there are now twenty-five temples and still more *nāma-haṭṭas*.

We could go on describing our activities here, but there would be no end. You know everything that we haven’t been able to describe, because you are the source of this service. All this is the manifestation of your mercy and of the power of your representatives. Thank you very much. None of us could even imagine that we would see such a transformation, even in our most courageous dreams. Indeed, your power is unlimited. Your mercy has made Lord Kṛṣṇa and His eternal abode available. Since we will remain your servants forever, we would like to ask you to give us still more mercy. This year we want to invite to our temple ŚrīŚyāmasundara and His eternal beloved, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī. You are the most intimate of Their associates, so without your mercy They will not come to accept our service. Therefore we pray to you to please invite Their Lordships here. Please make us qualified to worship Them so that all the devotees may drown in the ocean of bliss. We promise that we will continue to serve Their Lordships throughout our lives, along with you and your mission. We have to always keep in mind the verse you quoted from the *Gītā-māhātmya* in your introduction to the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*:

*ekaṁśāstraṁ devakī-putra-gītam*  
*eko devo devakī-putra eva*  
*eko mantras tasya nāmāni yāni*  
*karmāpy ekaṁ tasya devasya sevā*

**“There need be only one holy scripture — the divine** ***Gītā*****sung by Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa; only one worshipable Lord — Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa; only one mantra — His holy names; and only one duty — devotional service unto that Supreme Worshipable Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.”**

Then “the Kṛṣṇa sun” will rise and sanctify all the living entities.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please let us always remain eager to engage in your service.

Your servants in the ISKCON temple in Kiev, Ukraine.

## KwaZulu Natal

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**At Your Lotus Feet on Vyāsa-pūjā Day**

Vyāsa-pūjā celebrations, immersed in love, celebration, and appreciation.  
To you who radiate in pure love and dedication,  
These humble and fallen servants pray with faltering but sincere devotion.  
May blooming mercy and spiritual energy upon us all thee bestow,  
And all of your glory crown every hour;  
Appreciated, esteemed, loved, and admired, your merit certainly globally known,  
And devotees worldwide admire you as their very own Prabhupāda,  
The boldest preacher by far,  
Whilst we, various meditations of thee constantly envision,  
Distracted with the confusion of materialism, management, and achievement vain,  
Our restless consciousness tugs at our changeful yet stubborn brain,  
With mixed ideas of Kṛṣṇa conscious delight and desire pain;  
Often thy *bhakti* and service our morning dreams employ  
In the glorious dawn of your great visionary joy;  
With rigid reason your service quickly inspires in a very nice way  
Palatial temples, *prasādam* a-mile, books abound, festivals flowing,  
Rādhā-Rādhānāth the all-true joy, a *kīrtan* sway  
In pleasing madness and some minute *bhakti* ease,  
We fondly cherish our beloved disease  
Of service and surrender by body and mind,  
At least no weakness in the choice we find,  
ISKCON, *sādhu,* guru, Vaiṣṇava,  
*Saṅga,* and *śāstra* of the true kind.  
In artificial interaction we stray,  
Independent enthusiastic trifle,  
*Aparādha* at such ease,  
Tolerance a need, loyalty the creed!  
To follow you AS IT IS . . .  
So soothed to softness by our omniscient grandfather guru,  
Especially today the genuine merit of a Vyāsa-pūjā so true;  
Certainly losing our hearts to this saving De,  
Your wisdom, love, and mercy-moves  
Are such a virtue . . . Oh, so true! Goloka itself approves!  
A soul superior to each Vaiṣṇava’s view,  
Great, steady, gentle, generous, and true.  
How we regret our trifling hours past  
And look with sorrow over the dreary waste!  
In false and lofty pursuits, vanity bestowed,  
The perfect image of needless pride  
Through calamities, over obstacles, and offenses we stray,  
A tiresome dull uncomfortable way,  
And after toiling long through manufactured independence  
To reach Your Divine Grace and be ever so dependent,  
for the mercy you so easily eternally share,  
Yet savor the spiritual rewards with little care.  
At thy lotus feet, where all the world will surely be saved,  
Uniting us all, your plan unfolds.  
Spiritual embassies in an African wild,  
The real rainbow nation, spiritually bold,  
The Vaiṣṇava family of A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami.  
Your mercy we seek, your shelter we need,  
And your fine example we embrace.  
All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Begging your mercy,  
Your servants at ISKCON KwaZulu Natal, South Africa.

## Lampung

My dear grandfather spiritual master, Gurudeva,

Please accept my humble obeisances unto your holy lotus feet, you who have distributed the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* all over the Eastern and Western world, thus delivering and uplifting all fallen souls like me.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I think it is not possible for me to repay your kindness. But I can try to do what you are teaching me in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I thank you very much for always showering your mercy upon us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you came to this world to save all the fallen souls. In this life, before I read your books I did not know the goal of my life, but by your mercy — through books like your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and others — you have opened my eyes to see the actual world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you brought the shining lamp of knowledge to destroy the darkness born of ignorance. By your mercy you established temples, *gurukulas,* farms, Vaiṣṇava villages, like our Gītā Nāgarī Baru Village Project, home of the Rādhā-Madana Gopāl temple, and the Youth Hostel, Prahlāda & Kuntī-devī Ashram in Bandar Lampung, Indonesia.

Please don’t stop pouring your blessings on us; we always hope for your mercy.

*Jaya Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! *Vande guroḥśrī-caraṇāravindam*. *Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your devotees at the Youth Hostel, Prahlāda and Kuntī-devī Ashram, Lampung, Indonesia.  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa)

## Lenasia (South Africa)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace! All glories to your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā celebration!

Here in Johannesburg we feel blessed by your shelter  
As we continue to try to serve you, our divine master.  
By your mercy we have an opportunity to study your books  
And also distribute *prasādam* we cook.  
Festivals you gave us we embrace,  
Constantly praying for the blessings of Your Divine Grace.  
To follow your instructions nicely is our aim.  
May we spread your teachings and more conditioned souls reclaim.  
To increase all BTG subscriptions is our desire,  
And thus to raise our country’s spiritual awareness higher.  
To keep our temple clean we shall strive,  
To honor your desires we wish to remain alive.  
To live separate from your wish is never our plan,  
But to build strong spiritual relationships amidst the devotee clan.  
To serve your followers will bring us pleasure.  
We pray for your blessings to fulfill our spiritual desires.  
May all our forthcoming book marathons be of increased success,  
May the lessons from your books we properly digest,  
May all our attempts in keeping good *sādhana* bring us strength,  
May we all be able to extend ourselves to serve you at great length.  
We pray that we may be able to serve until our last breath,  
And to continue to serve you even after death.  
May we always remain chaste in service to your mission,  
And be always engaged in service to you with submission.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Nitāi–Gaura-Hari Mandir, ISKCON Lenasia, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## London (Soho)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you and your wonderful International Society for Krishna Consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are turning the cold, stone, steel-framed hearts of the people of Kali-yuga into pools of spiritual bliss. You are changing illiterate fools into erudite Vedic scholars. You are making the crows of this world lose their taste for garbage and, induced by you, want to taste the sweet mango nectar of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Their feathers miraculously begin to turn white, their necks grow longer, and they gradually attain the form of swans and fly gracefully back home, back to Godhead. All glories to you.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when you were asked on British television, “Do you have a concept of hell in your religion?”, you immediately replied, “Yes, London is hell.”

You elaborated, “Any country which does not get the full facility of sunshine is to be considered condemned.” London is hell. Not conception. Always dark, moist, drizzly, no sunshine. Cloudy, misty. Simply big, big buildings. That’s all.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are living in the very center of hell, a place where sin, lust, godlessness, greed, and vanity are amplified unlimitedly, and where the air (if one can call it that) is barely breathable. But we do not mind; we actually like it here. You explain, “A devotee of the Lord is not even afraid of hell. He goes to preach the glories of the Lord everywhere — even in hell — because there is no distinction between heaven and hell for a devotee.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the devotees at the Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Temple in London are endeavoring to preach as you instructed. Through book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, *harināma*, and preaching to guests, many people are benefiting from Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and the devotees themselves become ecstatic and advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the last year we have been endeavoring to improve our service to you. We have completely refurbished Govinda’s Restaurant and rejuvenated the front of the building, including beautiful new signs for the temple and the restaurant. We are now decorating the temple room, which is a huge job and is almost finished. Once every month, members of the congregation (sometimes as many as thirty devotees) go out on Mahā-Saturday book distribution, which has resulted in many books being distributed and enabled more devotees to taste the nectar of distributing your books. *Harināmas* are now improving and becoming more organized and regulated. We are putting more effort into greeting and preaching to guests. There are generally about twenty new people coming to the temple every day, and we are doing our best to make sure that they are well looked after.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you never aim small; you want the whole world to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. The vision of this temple is “London flooded with Kṛṣṇa consciousness!” When London, by your extraordinary mercy, is flooded with Kṛṣṇa consciousness, not only will the 7.6 million residents benefit, the whole world will surely change. London receives about 30 million visitors every year from all corners of the globe. People from communist countries such as China and North Korea, Islamic countries such as Saudi Arabia and Iran, as well as people from Africa get the chance to hear the holy names, receive one of your books, meet a devotee, and taste *prasādam*, even though they may not get the chance to do so in their own countries.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please invest your full potency within us to practice and preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to distribute millions of your books, to spread the chanting of the holy name of Kṛṣṇa all over London (the world), and to change everyone’s diet in London to consist only of Kṛṣṇa *prasādam*.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is only right that Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara (your favorite Deities, we’ve heard) should have a temple that at least compares with St. Paul’s Cathedral, the Houses of Parliament, or Buckingham Palace. This would attract millions of people to the lotus feet of Their Lordships, the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, and your divine instructions. Please empower us to fulfill this dream and thus further enhance your already unlimited glories.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at ISKCON London (Soho), UK.  
(written by Subal dāsa)

## London (south)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We would like to humbly offer a few words to express our appreciation to you. On this most blessed occasion, we recall with awe your great determination in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the world.

You carried within your heart the orders of your spiritual master to spread the teachings of Lord Caitanya beyond the borders of Bhārata. It is certainly a daunting task, but your total surrender unto Śrī Kṛṣṇa ensured your success. You had full faith in Śrī Kṛṣṇa and relied fully on Him. This is expressed in your historic prayer “Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma,” where you wrote, “O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance, O Lord, make me dance as You like.”

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, this past year Śrī Kṛṣṇa has been especially merciful to us, and we pray that this experience strengthens our faith in Him while we seek refuge and inspiration from Your Divine Grace to carry on serving within this wonderful movement of yours.

Your servants at ISKCON South London, UK.  
(written by Gomtī-devī dāsī)

## Malaga

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet. In the name of the ISCKON center of Malaga, Villa Varṣāṇā, we humbly present this offering to you, collectively begging for some drops of the ocean of your unlimited compassion.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the hearts of the Vaiṣṇavas are bleeding in separation from you, from your luminous personality, and from your profound and complete teachings and their application in the form of the favorable circumstances you have created for the upliftment of the consciousness of all living entities. Our hope for the future is your plan for the establishment of an alternative society according to the *varṇāśrama* system, with Kṛṣṇa in the center.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because of the contamination of Kali, the earthly planet has become very ill. The hearts of the Vaiṣṇavas are bleeding to see the state of the world. Still, in many areas the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya has spread dynamically. Many souls have been touched by the grace of Nitāi and Gaurāṅga through the agency of Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for our purification, we beg with all the strength of our collective hearts for your mercy, upon which we are so dependent. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we need you once more, more than ever. Following in the footsteps of Śrī Advaita Ācārya, we call for help. Please intercede for us with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. May He send you back into the hearts of your followers to rejuvenate our spirit so that we may fight the war against the illusory energy. By your mercy, this impossible task will be accomplished. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please come back to earth to save the world.

Crawling underneath the shade of your lotus feet for safety, we chant your name, which is sweeter than nectar — founder-*ācārya* of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness His Divine Grace Abhay Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, Jagat Guru, Patita Pāvana-*kī jaya*!!

Your humble servants in the ISKCON temple in Malaga, Spain.

## Manokwari (Indonesia)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Allow us to offer our humble obeisances to our spiritual master, to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, and to all the devotees, because it is due to their mercy that we still have a chance to perform devotional service.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are so merciful that even though we are unqualified and full of ignorance, you still allow us to do devotional services. You are even more merciful than Kṛṣṇa Himself.

We are here in Manokwari, Papua, in eastern Indonesia, trying to help spread your mission, although we don’t have any power to do so.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, please be merciful to us so that our attempt to accomplish your mission may continue and be successful.

In closing, we only ask for your affection and your forgiveness for our mistakes.

Your servants at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Manohara temple in Manokwari, Papua, Indonesia.  
(written by Śuci dasa)

## Markham/ Richmond Hill

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this day, this most blessed event,  
We must thank Kṛṣṇa for you that he sent.  
There are not enough words to glorify or describe  
What you have done — we are not qualified.  
But we certainly want to attempt  
To praise you even if we are not eloquent.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day,  
It is at your lotus feet that we wish to forever stay.  
For you have given the true meaning  
Of what it is to be a human being.  
You have made us so fortunate:  
From materialists to devotees you did create.  
If only the entire world could see  
That they also have this same wonderful opportunity.  
You showed us that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is not to be followed just sometimes,  
But is to become part of our entire lifetime.  
You were Kṛṣṇa’s calm answer in this material storm;  
It was the sole reason that you were born.  
And you were definitely put to the test;  
You proved you could do it even through so much duress.  
It was your spiritual master you pleased,  
Since you followed his order exactly as received.  
You taught us the proper way to live,  
The ultimate gift you did give.  
You wanted everyone to join,  
To make more Kṛṣṇa devotees — that was the point.  
You were so merciful and so kind,  
You helped us remove Māyās strong web that binds.  
It is in you that we have faith,  
For everyone else is just commonplace.  
You answered each and every question,  
Leaving no room for interpretation or aberration.  
You imparted Lord Caitanya’s teachings  
With so much reverence and feelings.  
By Kṛṣṇa’s grace and your command,  
You built so many temples across the land.  
You published so many books  
That are now read in every town, cranny, and nook.  
It is at your lotus feet that we seek shelter,  
Because we know there is nothing better.  
You taught us that Kṛṣṇa is omnipotent,  
And for that you are due the utmost compliment.  
We think of you with such affection;  
For us, your wishes are our motivation.  
You are certainly like a touchstone  
That guides us to achieve spiritual milestones.  
You showed us that spiritual life is what we should really treasure,  
That material gains will not result in any pleasure.  
You taught us that we should pursue spiritual activities  
Instead of going after materialistic opportunities.  
You blessed so many with your divine grace;  
You were able to do it anyplace.  
You effectively used Vedic evidence  
To refute fake gurus’ nonsense.  
Through your teachings and transcendental mission,  
You proved that you were the next in the disciplic succession.  
It will certainly be remembered in all of history  
That you are the real guru for the centuries.  
You brought us out of this material darkness,  
When we were struggling to gain material happiness.  
You fulfilled Lord Caitanya’s prediction  
By following your spiritual master’s order with conviction.  
By your order we know what to do:  
Our divine mission is to simply follow you.  
You taught us that in this material existence  
We must from temptation keep our distance.  
Across all the continents you displayed Lord Jagannātha’s smile  
By creating the Ratha-yātrā parade on major city streets for miles.  
You have presented Kṛṣṇa consciousness so scientifically  
That even scientists become devotees so terrifically.  
You created temples that are in the desert of Māyā like an oasis,  
Showing everyone that, for success, Kṛṣṇa must be the basis.  
You told us that we must have a bona fide spiritual master  
To get back to Godhead and avoid disaster.  
Because everyone’s salvation was your prayer,  
You asked that Lord Caitanya’s teachings be preached everywhere.  
You spread the Vedic message as it is,  
Without modification, interpretation, or guesses.  
You worked so hard on preaching through translation  
That it is no wonder you are remembered with such adulation.  
To save each and every spirit soul:  
That is your wish and your goal.  
You came to America by the *Jaladuta* all alone;  
Now you have created a spiritual ship to take us home.  
You showed us what is true knowledge,  
Proving that all material gossip and information is just garbage.  
We are so fortunate that you made us find  
Something so sweet to clean our heart and mind.  
So far from India you came,  
To create devotees of Kṛṣṇa and spread the holy name.  
You wanted Kṛṣṇa to be a household word,  
And by your grace, in every town and city, His name is heard.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our spiritual master,  
The one who has provided us with all the answers.  
You brought us out of darkness and into the light  
By making us think of Kṛṣṇa day and night.  
It was you who were the cause  
Of telling us what are Kṛṣṇa’s laws.  
You preached to us time and time again,  
Trying to clear our minds and make us sane.  
You never for a moment sat still,  
Knowing it was Lord Caitanya’s desire you must fulfill.  
You came to give us spiritual direction,  
And from material life give us protection.  
We wish to never let you and Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa out of our sight;  
We will try to not let Māyā take over and win this fight.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for everything you have done;  
Thank you for saving us from this material condition.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep us forever at your lotus feet,  
For without you and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, our lives would be incomplete.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! *Jaya* Gurudeva!

Your humble servants in Markham/Richmond Hill, Canada.  
(written by Mīnākṣī-devī dāsī)

## Miami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

Just thirty short years ago, in 1975, the world knew ISKCON as a powerful and rapidly expanding movement. You had already made “Hare Kṛṣṇa” a household word. The demonic forces in the world were feeling the force of your preaching. But compared to then, it appears that your movement has become splintered and divided. Many have given up spiritual life altogether. Many have joined other groups or accepted other philosophies. Many of your temples are nearly empty, and the standards of Deity worship have been reduced to adjust for the change. In this condition, we naturally point fingers of blame at others for ruining your movement and our own spiritual lives. We lament that this would not have happened if you had remained with us.

Although by Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement you have brought an end to your earthly pastimes, you are with us eternally in the form of your instructions. In your books you gave us everything we need to keep your movement strong and ourselves spiritually fit. You foresaw and forewarned us in your books of the problems that we face today. For example, in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 19.160, you quote your spiritual master, saying, “The unwanted creepers have been described by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. He states that if one hears and chants without trying to give up offenses, he becomes materially attached to sense gratification.”

This point is extremely important and all too often overlooked. We daily recite the ten offenses to the holy name in all your temples. We state that we must give up the ten offenses to quickly achieve the desired goal of loving Kṛṣṇa. But how many of us conscientiously endeavor to give up those offenses?

The ten offenses are all based on various kinds of gross and subtle sense gratification. Our failure to endeavor to give up those offenses means that we want to maintain our desires for sense gratification. Spiritual life is dynamic, and if we do not make a serious endeavor to give up our offenses, we will be forced by the material energy to increase our material attachments instead. We cannot be lazy in spiritual life and expect to achieve the desired result. Our material attachments to sense gratification are compared to the unwanted creepers that choke the *bhakti-latā,* the creeper of devotion.

If we study the forms the unwanted creeper may take, we will see that practically all the problems we face within ISKCON today can be traced back to our failure, both individually and as a whole, to try sufficiently to free ourselves from the ten offenses to chanting the holy names. What that means is that if we change our attitude and seriously work to give up all offenses, this movement can again become the dynamic spiritual force it was during your physical presence.

We need to expertly distinguish between the *bhakti-latā* creeper and the unwanted creepers so that we do not accidentally cultivate the unwanted creepers. Making that distinction is not always easy, as you explain in the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā* 19.159, purport:

One should be very careful to avoid all these unwanted things. Sometimes these unwanted creepers look exactly like the *bhakti* creeper. They appear to be of the same size and the same species when they are packed together with the *bhakti* creeper, but in spite of this, the creepers are called *upaśākhā* \[weeds\]. A pure devotee can distinguish between the *bhakti* creeper and a mundane creeper, and he is very alert to distinguish them and keep them separate.

The best way for us to protect our *bhakti-latā* is to always remain under your shelter within The International Society for Krishna Consciousness. We have firm faith that if we follow your instructions in detail we will again witness the dynamic expansion we experienced during your physical presence. This movement is not lost by any means. We do not need a new *ācārya*. We only need to follow your instructions. You once said, “My books will guide the entire human civilization for the next ten thousand years.” In 1973 you even told us that “One day scholars will note how this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement saved the world in its darkest hour.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us become totally serious about chanting the Lord’s holy names without offense so that we can purify our lives and properly assist you in spreading this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world.

Your humble servants,  
The devotees at ISKCON Miami, Florida, USA.

## Mumbai (Chowpatty)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, the devotees at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir in the Chowpatty district of Mumbai, are your grandchildren, and we have special love and affection for you. We feel the reciprocation of your similar feelings for us. Your mercy and affection have resulted in our being able to accomplish a significant upgrade of the temple under the personal and pristine supervision of His Holiness Rādhānātha Swami. As a token of our affection for you, we have started the Bhaktivedanta Academy for Culture and Education to spread your wonderful teachings, not only to devotees but also to others who are interested. We feel that your maximum effort was spent in preparing your divine teachings in the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and other books, and as a token of our appreciation, we have taken the step of building this academy to honor your memory and try to fulfill your instructions to preach. You yourself told us that “Books are the basis, preaching is the essence, utility is the principle, and purity is the force.”

Based on these principles and teachings, and under the benevolence and kind guidance of His Holiness Rādhānātha Swami, we have had some initial success at the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir complex. We are now growing beyond our wildest imagination, with more and more devotees joining and the temple room just appearing too small for the number of people who attend various lectures, *kīrtanas,* festivals, etc.

Since it was you who envisioned our temple in 1971, we beg you to give us more *sevā* so that we can further expand under your most munificent mercy. We are now in a situation where the soul of your body, ISKCON, is looking to us, but we know that we are really not so capable. For the temple renovation, for example, we felt we did not have the financial strength to fulfill our plans, but miraculously you provided everything because you have a very strong connection, through the *paramparā,* with Lord Caitanya and Lord Kṛṣṇa. All the opulence seems to flow through this *paramparā.*

So we just beg for the transcendental blessing of more opportunities to serve the citizens of South Mumbai, particularly the younger generation, who are taking tremendous interest in Kṛṣṇa consciousness through our youth festivals, such as Prerana (the Festival of Inspiration, for boys) and Chetana (the Festival of Consciousness, for girls) and other preaching activities. By your mercy, we have also a very cooperative and wonderful relationship with our elder brothers at Juhu Beach (the devotees at the Rādhā-Rāsabihārī temple) and younger brothers at Mira Road (the devotees at the Rādhā-Giridhārī temple); we know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that such cooperation is also your strong desire.

We beg that you may continue to kindly shower your blessings on us. Our only prayer is that we may serve you continuously without offense or stoppage of any kind.

Your servants at ISKCON’s Rādhā-Gopīnātha Mandir in the Chowpatty district of Mumbai, India.  
(written by Śrī Nāthjī dāsa)

## Nagpur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please allow us to place our tiny hearts at the dust of your delicate lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! It is a matter of great pride for us to see that all over the world your 109th appearance anniversary is being celebrated with great fervor and enthusiasm.

We are grateful to you for making our lives fortunate enough so that we dare to avail ourselves of the opportunity to glorify you at the culmination of the year of special celebrations following your 108th appearance anniversary. This has been a year of attempts to please Your Divine Grace, and now is the time for Your Divine Grace and the whole *guru-paramparā* to appraise our presumptuous attempts. Here is our preaching report for your transcendental pleasure: ISKCON Youth Forum (IYF) Girls Youth Festival Pragati. A 54-member IYF team participated in the IYF Prerana Youth Festival at Pune. We tried to “boil the milk” at a yoga retreat for IYF boys of the region and the members of the temple management board.

The *saṅkalpa:*

To commence the Bhakti-vriksha-cell preaching program and thereby assimilate 108 new families into ISKCON Nagpur before next year’s Vyāsa-pūjā.  
To welcome 108 youths into IYF at a second Youth Festival and host a total of at least 300 youths over 2 days at Nagpur.  
To ensure that at least one road in Nagpur is named after Your Divine Grace.  
To build a temporary structure and occupy the land offered for the Nagpur temple, which you desired should be built.  
To ensure a spiritually stout temple management board.  
To ensure a well-trained set of temple devotees, IYF, and congregation members.  
To ensure that at least one well-known VIP will become our flag-bearer.  
For all members of the temple management board, to read at least one book on your life.  
To hold classes on *Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta* for a week.  
To hold a one-day nonstop *japa* program.  
To develop *mātājīs* as a potent preaching weapon through cooking classes and home programs.  
To hold seminars on vegetarianism, stress management, etc., for the city’s elite.  
To commence corporate preaching before your next Vyāsa-pūjā.  
To reconcile with as many devotees as possible who are now unfavorable to the temple.  
To establish the highest standards of Deity worship for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha.  
To build a *vyāsāsana* for you.  
To increase the number of temple devotees.  
To achieve a target of five thousand *Bhagavad-gītās* sold during the December marathon.  
To invite 108 elite VIPs of the city and present them with your books on your Vyāsa-pūjā.  
To train 108 of the children attending the Prahlāda School to chant the *mahā-mantra* regularly, and then to present them to you for your pleasure on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.  
To achieve a target of 108 new life members by next year’s Vyāsa-pūjā.  
To establish Nagpur as a seat for VIHE/MIHE courses.  
To stage a one-day festival of spiritual dramas.  
To organize a grand gala “Hare Kṛṣṇa Night” for fund-raising.  
To flood Nagpur with *Back to Godhead* magazines and your books.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we feel that instead of emotional offerings we should give you the spiritual results of our attempts at *bhakti*. *Yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo . . .*

Our spiritual future lies in trying our best to please you. Please always keep us at your lotus feet in the service of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra, and the *guru-paramparā*. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we beg your mercy so that we can be enthusiastic in our endeavors to please you throughout the year.

Aspiring to be your good servants,  
Your devotees at the Hare Kṛṣṇa temple in Nagpur, India.

## New Badarikāśrama (Denver)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance,  
I’m praying that your instructions I’ll take to heart with strict adherence.  
Although I like to pride myself as being in control and cool,  
By your causeless mercy I want to be your tool and not some dancing fool.  
Your words were like a thunderbolt when you said,  
“How can I teach you if you can’t remember?”  
Your heart so full of love, just like your touch,  
Was so soft and tender.  
Lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya:  
Just one moment around you, we pray to be your eternal toy.  
Tirelessly traveling, translating, and teaching,  
You worked for our encouragement.  
Following your example of preaching,  
We find our real nourishment.  
“The servant of the devotee is My devotee”  
That’s the Lord’s stance.  
Though I don’t deserve it, please, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Give me another chance.  
Your every movement and gesture was so entrancing;  
When you stopped your morning walk in Māyāpur and motioned,  
We couldn’t help but start dancing.  
I was watching how the ants were crawling across your lotus feet—  
If I could only achieve such a transcendental treat!  
Looking at your golden form with scent of sandalwood,  
We were distributing books in San Francisco when  
You turned and, smiling, there you stood.  
Looking so lovingly, you told Guru dāsa the new *sannyāsī:*  
“Now you’re free.”  
To be engaged in Kṛṣṇa’s service is the only reality.  
In New Vrindāvan you tasted and distributed the Lord’s plate;  
The position of the pure devotee we can’t fully estimate.  
You sipped milk with a spoon and bit into a *gulābjāmun.*  
When I put out my hand and you dropped it in,  
I knew something special was acomin’.  
In New York you appreciated the comfort and ideal facility.  
Coming out of the elevator, you said, “I want to fight to the last breath.” That’s intensity.  
Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us your mercy  
So we don’t become fatuous and practice out of courtesy.  
In Vṛndāvana you gave a class in Hindi that you wanted your disciples to be attending.  
Though we didn’t understand, you saw our desire to serve Kṛṣṇa, your determination unbending.  
Our desire, Śrīla Prabhupāda, is that what you’ve given we can preserve.  
But because of my envious mentality, I’m driven to disserve.  
Your vision of us is as servants of Kṛṣṇa, not bones and blood.  
In New Vrindāvan you turned and laughed in a Jeep as we all ran behind, falling and covered in mud.  
The fact is we are stuck in this crud,  
But you have made available to the world the Caitanya flood.  
In Dallas, after Paramahaṁsa’s *kīrtan,* you thanked him and patted him on the head.  
Without your touch in our lives, we are as good as dead.  
You distributed cookies to the kids and teased them at times.  
By getting your remnants one is relieved of untold crimes.  
I’ll stop here, as I know you must be tired.  
But please don’t forget this soul, who to be your servant does aspire.

Your servants at New Badarikāśrama, Denver, USA.  
(written by Naikātmā dāsa)

## New Biharvan (Idaho)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you! All glories to Śrī Śrī Gaura and Nitāi, whose *saṅkīrtana* movement you amazingly propagate throughout the world!

In the *Śrī Nityānanda-caritāmṛta* we read that Jagāi and Mādhāi were so demoniac and contaminated that people in Nadia would take a bath in the Gaṅgā, with their clothes on, to purify themselves even if they happened to just see these two brothers. When Lord Nityānanda saw the fallen Jagāi and Mādhāi, he desired to deliver them and make them so pure that instead of taking a bath people would become purified just by seeing them.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your godbrothers have called you the incarnation of Lord Nityānanda’s inconceivable and unlimited mercy. We can see why. Distributing the mercy of Lord Nityānanda, you have delivered thousands of Jagāis and Mādhāis all over the world. You delivered the *mlecchas* of the Western world, whom you found in the gutters of New York’s Bowery, who were not allowed in the temples in India, and whom even their own governments had rejected as useless untouchables. You purified them with the holy names of Hare, Kṛṣṇa, and Rāma, and today the world becomes purified by their presence.

In this horrible Age of Kali, Mother Gaṅgā gets overburdened by the numerous sins of the people who bathe in her waters. Ages ago, when Mahārāja Bhagīratha prayed to Mother Gaṅgā to come down to Earth to deliver his 60,000 forefathers, Mother Gaṅgā put forth two conditions: (1) Someone would have to take the force of the water when she came down from the heavenly planets to Earth, and (2) someone would have to take the sins that she would accumulate when people bathed in her waters.

Mahārāja Bhagīratha fulfilled both these conditions. He prayed to Lord Śiva, who agreed to receive Mother Gaṅgā on his head and absorb her impact. And for the second condition, Bhagīratha told Mother Gaṅgā that the pure devotees would take the sins when they bathed in her waters. Mother Gaṅgā agreed, and we are blessed with her presence. However, at the beginning of Kali-yuga there was one slight problem. In Kali-yuga everyone is born a *śūdra,* and there are very few saintly people left, so who would absorb the sins from Mother Gaṅgā? The *Brahma-vaivarta Purāṇa* describes that Mother Gaṅgā asked Lord Brahmā this question. Lord Brahmā replied that devotees from the West would travel to Bhāratavarṣa, and when they bathed in her waters they would take her sins.

But who would make these Westerners into devotees? The people of the West were accustomed to meat-eating and intoxication and all kinds of other unspeakable sins. This task was done by you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Armed with the detergent of the *mahā-mantra* and Lord Nityānanda’s unlimited mercy, you tirelessly preached all over the world and turned the Westerners (and a lot of Easterners) into pure devotees who are now taking away the burden of Mother Gaṅgā.

In Boise, we continue to try to be your instruments. Last year, Lord Jagannātha’s Ratha-yātrā was very successful. Nearly ten thousand people on the streets received His merciful glance as the *ratha* moved smoothly through the July 4thparade. Every year Lord Jagannātha lets us know of His presence. This time He had the local firemen, with their ten-thousand-gallon water-trucks, wash the street right in front of the *ratha* and arrange for a cool breeze to blow for the devotees. By the efforts of Chandra Prabhu, the preaching in the jail is gradually expanding. Now we have prison programs twice a month, and some prisoners are chanting eight to sixteen rounds daily and reading your books. The congregation enthusiastically sponsors Sunday Feasts and celebrates festivals. For the first time we had the Janmāṣṭamī Festival outside, on the temple grounds. Almost five hundred people from all walks of life came for the festival, and the local weekly carried a beautiful portrait of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Baṅkebihārī. The holy name is having an impact. More people have started chanting — some are chanting four rounds, some eight rounds, some sixteen rounds. Last summer, April was initiated as Amṛta-devī dāsī. Two newly born girls in the congregation were named Surabhi and Sugopi. Jayanta and Prathana made sure that Surabhi was born on the auspicious day of Śrīla Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura’s appearance anniversary. Sugopi’s parents, Srinavas and Sangeetha, were elated to see her born on your holy disappearance day.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us with a few drops of your unlimited mercy so we can serve you and be better instruments in your preaching mission.

Begging for your mercy,  
The devotees in New Biharvan, Boise, Idaho, USA.  
(written by Anantarūpa dāsa)

## New Delhi (Noida)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. Hare Kṛṣṇa! All glories to your lotus feet! All glories to your Vyāsa-pūjā day! All glories to your dear Guru Mahārāja, His Divine Grace Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda, whose order was your life and soul! All glories to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu for empowering His sincere devotee Śrīla A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda to accomplish His mission and fulfill His prediction! All glories to the revolutionary movement you formally started in 1966, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness!

Because of your *bhakti*, Kṛṣṇa’s glories traversed the earth and were lavished upon it. Even Kali-yuga wretches could perceive the most attractive and powerful secret of the cosmos — devotional service. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you had a vision to open temples of Lord Kṛṣṇa in every village and town of the world, so that love of Godhead would be distributed to the conditioned souls, who are greatly suffering due to their forgetfulness of their real identity.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you gave us seven main purposes (visions) of ISKCON. You wanted ISKCON to systematically educate people in general to live a sinless life. ISKCON Noida has a project to educate people in the spiritual knowledge. The Bhaktivedanta Academy for Spiritual Science (BASS) is our humble offering at your lotus feet. It is an attempt to please you by inviting conditioned souls to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and dance.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, whatever little we know, we have learned from you through your books and your representatives. At Noida we are trying to render a tiny service at your lotus feet by propagating Lord Caitanya’s message to the masses, following your footsteps. Keeping in mind your inclination toward “educating the so-called educated people,” we are building a unique educational center in Noida.

We are very pleased to inform you that on 20 March 2005, by your blessings, we were able to vacate our small rented place and move into ISKCON’S own building. This will be a four-story building when completed. Two floors will be dedicated for BASS, to deliver spiritual education in a very organized way in the form of short-term and long-term courses. One of your strong followers, Śrīmān Vedavyāsa Prabhu (from Delhi) has sponsored the whole floor that will house BASS, and Śrīmān Ballabha Prasad Aggarwal (Noida’s leading industrialist) has sponsored the land for ISKCON Noida. Śrīla Prabhupāda, kindly bless these two souls so that they can remain in Kṛṣṇa’s service life after life. We are very fortunate to have some sincere devotees personally trained by you to guide us through this project, namely His Holiness Lokanāth Swami and His Holiness Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami. His Holiness Devāmṛta Swami, Braja Bihārī Prabhu, Bṛhat Mṛdaṅga Prabhu, Devāmṛta Prabhu, and many other expert devotees have committed their services to this center to deliver quality education with their expertise in preaching, teaching, managing, etc.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very happy to bring to your attention the fact that, on 3 April 2005 we inaugurated Krishna Jayanti Park behind our temple here in Noida. We often heard from your disciples that you wanted Krishna Jayanti Park near Delhi.

ISKCON Noida organized Hare Krishna Utsav 2005 to celebrate the opening of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir, the new temple. The festival started on the eve of Lord Caitanya’s appearance day, 25 March 2005, and concluded on 3April 2005 with Lord Jagannātha’s Ratha-yātrā. Every evening there were special discourses on Śrī Caitanya’s *līlā* that attracted hundreds of people to the new temple. These discourses explained the importance of providing spiritual education to the masses in the Age of Kali.

This year Lord Jagannātha’s Ratha-yātrā was the biggest-ever *yātrā*in the town, breaking all previous records for opulence, crowd size, book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, preaching, and especially for attracting many souls to chant the holy names of the Lord together. Thousands of people attended the festival and rendered various services to the Lord of the universe. Donors arranged a very nice reception for the Lord at different places on the route, offering *ārati,* fruits, flowers, and many other gifts to Lord Jagannātha, Lord Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī. During the Ratha-yātrā procession there was plenty of *prasādam* distribution from Jagannātha’s cart. Book distribution went on simultaneously. Also, at the end of the *yātrā* there was a huge sumptuous feast waiting for devotees at the new temple.

The ISKCON Youth Forum (IYF) was the highlight of the festival. The youth took up all the key services and performed them with utmost care and responsibility. IYF Noida grew from ten to fifty devotees in the last six months. The media were quite favorable to ISKCON Noida, with all the leading newspapers covering the whole festival. One noteworthy picture from the Ratha-yātrā showed devotees from various nations dancing together chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra.* Seeing this picture, we couldn’t help thinking of your statement that ISKCON was the real United Nations, the only organization able to bring about peace globally.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have a grand project ahead — to complete the construction of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir and the Bhaktivedanta Academy for Spiritual Science. We do not have the knowledge or resources required for this glorious project, but we do have a strong desire to complete this project by Kṛṣṇa Janmāṣṭamī 2006, for your pleasure.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please send us sincere souls to help us complete the project on time. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you once said that your Guru Mahārāja mercifully sent so many nice devotees to assist you in fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s prediction. So kindly bestow similar blessings upon us, who are total fools but who are willing to render some little service in your mission of spreading the holy name of the Lord.

Your humble servants,  
The devotees at ISKCON Noida, home of the Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandir and the Bhaktivedanta Academy for Spiritual Science, in New Delhi, India.

## New Dvārakā

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We, the residents of New Dvārakā-dhāma, offer our most humble obeisances to you. Being eternally indebted to you, we prostrate ourselves in the dust of your lotus feet.

On the occasion of the 109thanniversary of your appearance in this world, we are especially grateful to you for instructing us, and showing us by your practical example, how to take advantage of the opportunities afforded by this human form of life.

As conditioned souls born in the degraded Kali-yuga, we are driven by the modes of passion and ignorance. You, however, saw us as forgetful servants of Kṛṣṇa and convinced us that we could be purified, first to the mode of goodness and then beyond. You state in your commentary on the *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.18):

There is an opportunity for men in the modes of ignorance and passion to be elevated to the mode of goodness, and that system is called Kṛṣṇa consciousness. But one who does not take advantage of this opportunity will certainly continue in the lower modes.

You repeatedly reminded us to not waste this human form that we had attained. The following statement typifies this exhortation:

It is said that this human form of life is a good boat by which one can cross the ocean of nescience. In the human form of life one can obtain the guidance of a good navigator, the spiritual master. One also gets a favorable wind by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa, and that wind is the instructions of Kṛṣṇa. The human body is the boat, the instructions of Lord Kṛṣṇa are the favorable winds, and the spiritual master is the navigator. The spiritual master knows well how to adjust the sails to catch the winds favorably and steer the boat to its destination. If, however, one does not take advantage of this opportunity, one wastes the human form of life. Wasting time and life in this way is the same as committing suicide. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.23.28, purport\]

You gave further elucidation on your position as spiritual master by saying:

The spiritual master is considered to be the outward expression of the Supersoul. God is so kind that He is within, giving you education, provided you are prepared to hear. And from without, He sends His representative. So both ways we are benefited. We have to take advantage of this opportunity. From without, we have got these books, the spiritual master, the saintly persons, so many. Friends, books. And from within, Kṛṣṇa, the Supersoul. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 12.2.1, San Francisco, 18 March 1968\]

You also wrote:

As for *saṁskāra,* or reformation, this is possible by good association, for by good association one develops good habits, and habit becomes second nature. Therefore, *bhakta-sane vāsa:* let people have the chance to live with *bhaktas*. Then their habits will change. In the human form of life one has this chance, but as Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura sings, *hari hari viphale janama goṅāinu:* if one fails to take advantage of this opportunity, one’s human life is wasted. We are therefore trying to save human society from degradation and actually elevate people to the higher nature. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.13.53, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may heed your words and not waste our lives.

Knowing well the defects of conditioned souls, you warned us to be on guard against laziness and the propensity to cheat:

Every Vaiṣṇava is dependent on Kṛṣṇa, and Kṛṣṇa is ready to supply all of life’s necessities, provided a Vaiṣṇava follows the principles set forth by the spiritual master. There are certainly many householders in our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. They join the movement and live in the Society’s centers, but if they take advantage of this opportunity and do not work but live at the expense of the movement, eating *prasādam* and simply sleeping, they place themselves in a very dangerous position. It is therefore advised that *gṛhasthas* should not live in the temple. They must live outside the temple and maintain themselves. Of course, if the *gṛhasthas* are fully engaged in the Lord’s service according to the directions of the authorities, there is no harm in their living in a temple. In any case, a temple should not be a place to eat and sleep. A temple manager should be very careful about these things. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta*, *Madhya* 24.266, purport\]

In New Dvārakā we try hard to avoid the pitfalls of laziness and cheating by working vigorously to push on your programs of Deity worship, book distribution, *prasādam* distribution, and *harināma* for your pleasure. These programs were repeatedly stressed by you:

So my advice to you, I am old man. So even I may not return, you shall continue this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. This is eternal, and I shall request you to keep the standard as I have already given you the program. The Deity worship, the *kīrtana,* the street *saṅkīrtana,* distribution of literature, books. You should carry on this program with great enthusiasm. That is my request. \[Purport to Nṛsiṁha prayers, Los Angeles, 2 August 1970\]

Under the able guidance of stalwart senior devotees such as Svavāsa dāsa, Sura dāsa, and Bṛghupati dāsa, New Dvārakā is the perennial book distribution leader in North America. And the Deity worship in this temple is performed at a very high standard. In addition, we try to give attention to the other areas of service that you mentioned. In a personal letter to a disciple, you stressed the distribution of *prasādam:*

By *prasādam* distribution you will do service to the Lord, and the public will be pleased upon you as well. Gradually increase the number of guests, and don’t deny anyone. Continue in this way: Whatever is there, distribute equally—it may not be very sumptuous, but no one should be denied equal portion. \[Letter to Girirāja dāsa, 12 April 72\]

Through Govinda’s Buffet, Food for Life, festivals, and college campus distribution, we try hard to put *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* in the hands of the public.

In the following quote you indicated that ISKCON temples should be maintained as spiritual hospitals, where people can be cured of the disease of material consciousness:

Just like take, for example, hospital. There are hundreds and thousands of hospitals, but where is the hospital for treating the spirit soul? They have no knowledge. They have no knowledge even, what to speak of hospital. This is the hospital. This Kṛṣṇa consciousness temple is the hospital for the diseased spirit soul. And everyone is diseased. Come to this hospital. We shall take care of you and cure your material disease. \[Town Hall Lecture, Auckland, 14 April 1972\]

Of course, the doctors (devotees) in ISKCON’s spiritual hospitals have to remain free from material contamination to effectively help others. The recommended vaccine is the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, as you point out in this comment:

One can chant Hare Kṛṣṇa anywhere. Children, for example, also chant and dance. It is not at all difficult. While walking, our students take their beads with them and chant. Where is the loss? But the gain is very great, for by chanting we associate with Kṛṣṇa personally. Suppose we were to associate personally with the President. How proud we would feel. “Oh, I am with the President.” So should we not feel very much proud if we were to associate with the supreme president, who is able to create many millions of presidents like those of this world? This chanting is our opportunity to do so. Therefore Caitanya Mahāprabhu says, *etādṛśī tava kṛpā bhagavan mamāpi:* “My dear Lord, You are so kind to me that You are always prepared to give me Your association.”*Durdaivam īdṛśam ihājani nānurāgaḥ:* “But I am so unfortunate that I do not take advantage of this opportunity.” \[*Teachings of Queen Kuntī,* Chapter 13\]

We try to increase our attachment to chanting and attract others to Śrī Harināma through street chanting, *nāma-haṭṭa* programs, and large and small festivals. We’ve also established a temple *bhajan* band that concentrates on outreach to yoga societies and other religious groups.

We pray that by your continued blessings upon us we shall remain steadfast in service to the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa, Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, who are the original equal-opportunity employers. May we, by Their mercy, manage the New Dvārakā temple to meet your stated goals for your centers, which you mention here:

The human life is meant for understanding Kṛṣṇa. There is no other business. If you simply stick to this business, your life is successful. Our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is meant for that purpose. We are opening so many centers so that the people of the world may take advantage of this opportunity and understand Kṛṣṇa and make his life successful. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 7.9.32, Māyāpur, 10 March 1976\]

Your aspiring servants in New Dvārakā-dhāma, Los Angeles, California, USA.  
(written by Arcita dāsa)

## New Goloka (North Carolina)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

With compassion, sweetness, and indescribable beauty, Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī and Śrī Golokānanda daily broadcast their mercy and love to your servants here in New Goloka, North Carolina. By your grace and the mercy of your dedicated followers, over the years this community of your servants has grown and prospered in devotion to the mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Our focus here, the secret of success, is a firm emphasis on personal spiritual practice and purity that then nourishes effective outreach. You explain the importance of steady practice in your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 5.29:

Kṛṣṇa consciousness brings one into spiritual life even while one is within the jurisdiction of matter, for it is an arousing of spiritual existence by practice in the material world. . . . The Lord is not partial toward anyone. Everything depends on one’s practical performance of duties in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, which helps one control the senses in every respect and conquer the influence of desire and anger. And one who stands fast in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, controlling the abovementioned passions, remains factually in the transcendental stage, or *brahma-nirvāṇa*.

We beg your continued grace so that this branch of the tree of your mission will continue to thrive and bloom with the invigorating fragrance of purity and dedication. May we always remain connected to you not only in feeling but also in the daily, sustained actions of *bhakti*. May spiritual strength, devoid of *kāma* and *rāga,* continue to nourish us through our dedication to following your instructions. May our dedication to your instructions be indelibly engraved in our hearts like stone carvings that last for millions of years.

When we constantly receive the flowing life of potent purity, anchored on the stone of fixed determination, then we can give to others what you have kindly given us. By this preaching, our tiny, brief lives have meaning in the never-ending quest to repay our debt to you. Please accept our offerings of our lives and service, however full of faults and deficiencies. Please keep us always under your shelter.

Your servants at New Goloka, Hillsborough, North Carolina, USA.  
(written by Ūrmilā-devī dāsī)

## New Govardhana (San Diego)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your transcendental lotus feet. We, the assembled devotees of the San Diego *yātrā,* beg to present this humble offering on your appearance day.

Our only shelter is to be situated in service at your lotus feet, always remembering the unlimited kindness and love you have bestowed upon us. Your appearance day is another opportunity to absorb ourselves in thoughts of you and engage body, heart, mind, and intelligence in your loving service.

Daily we see and hear of so many of your devoted, elevated Vaiṣṇava followers executing selfless service, with their energy focused on you, our most beloved spiritual master. All of this magnifies your profound greatness, which reflects on the thousands of your disciples and granddisciples throughout the world.

Some devotees read your books many hours a day, while others constantly preach the glories of Kṛṣṇa’s holy names. Some illustrious Vaiṣṇavas are recognized by many for their heroic service, while others not so well known (but just as reverential) have displayed profound devotion while performing the ultimate service and undergoing the utmost test of their lives — leaving the material body. How could we ever do all this on our own? There is just no other explanation but that you have mercifully provided the spiritual strength for all these devotees to cling to the protective shade of your lotus feet.

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“By the mercy of the guru, even a dumb man can become the greatest orator, and even a lame man can cross mountains. I offer my obeisances to the guru, the deliverer of the fallen.”**

In your books you have said countless times that this material world is a dangerous place. Even mundane literatures reveal all the catastrophes that take place here. Death is sure, you tirelessly pointed out. That is one gift we are guaranteed material existence will offer us.

By your grace you have given us Kṛṣṇa and His holy names, and you have shown us that He is the Supreme Person and not impersonal. You sometimes told the story of the *brāhmaṇa* who, while studying the *Bhagavad-gītā,* came across the statement where Kṛṣṇa says that He personally comes to deliver His devotees. The *brāhmaṇa* scratched out “personally,” thinking that Kṛṣṇa comes through His energies. Shortly afterwards, his wife heard a knock at the door, and when she opened it she saw a young boy who was scratched and bleeding, with tattered clothes. When she asked what had happened, the boy said, “Your husband has done this to me.” When her husband came home, she relayed the entire incident to him, and he was horrified, realizing the blunder he had committed — doubting that Kṛṣṇa is absolute and is nondifferent from His name, fame, and paraphernalia.

You taught us how Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Person, won over only by sincere devotion, and how He is most pleased when we lovingly serve the servant of the servant of the servant of the devotees. And you also always showed your genuine personal care for all, such as during one of your pastimes in Bombay. There had been much trouble with the demonic landlord who had sold us the land in Juhu. He was threatening to remove the temple Deities from the land by Monday if there was no regular building to house Them. Your devoted friend Mr. Sethi, who loved you, was the head of a construction company. When he heard what was about to happen, he rounded up many of his men on Sunday night. Even though it was incessantly raining, they erected a sturdy, secure building for Their Lordships, thus thwarting the demon’s intentions.

Some time later Mr. Sethi got very sick and couldn’t make it to the temple. He didn’t go for days. Nobody called on him to see how he was doing. After a few days went by, he exclaimed to his wife how no one had inquired or cared about his well-being. All of sudden there was a knock on their door. When his wife opened it, she saw you standing there, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You had come to check up on Mr. Sethi. You said, “Do you think I don’t care?” Such is your unlimited kindness. Oh, how you are able to touch so deeply the souls of so many!

We are eternally indebted to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and beg to remain forever in your loving service.  
Your servants in New Govardhana, San Diego, California, USA.

## New Govardhana (Australia)

**I Only Look to You**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
There is life and there is death,  
And I have spent eons hovering between the two.  
But this time round,  
I cry out for your guidance.  
I only look to you.  
Somewhere between darkness and light I have loitered too long.  
But you show the way.  
I don’t care for life or death.  
I only look to you.  
You show what is right and what is wrong.  
How I must act and how I should not.  
Yet only I can choose.  
Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Don’t leave again.  
I only look to you.  
In this final life . . .  
I will give up all thoughts of gratification.  
I will reject the allurements that are all around me.  
And I give my self to your service.  
Actually, I am yours.  
I’m your eternal servant . . .  
And I only look to you.  
But somewhere soon,  
Between life and death,  
The time will come again,  
When the world dissolves before my eyes.  
And what appears to be, is not so.  
But I take your hand,  
And touchstone turns,  
Confirms within  
And you lift my eyes . . .  
“O my Lord!  
My dear Lord!”  
We only look to You.

Your eternal servants at ISKCON New Govardhana, Murwillumbah, Australia.  
(written by Veṇugopāla dāsa)

## New Jagannatha Puri (Berkeley)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your beautiful, transcendental lotus feet.

You are the beloved, intimate associate of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. You are an eternally liberated soul, forever situated beyond the three modes of material nature. Your pure heart shines with ecstatic love for Lord Kṛṣṇa and for all living beings. You are the perfect disciple of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura. Not only did you impeccably carry out his order to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness in English-speaking countries, but by your intense dedication and spiritual potency you continue to spread Lord Kṛṣṇa’s message all over the world.

In performing your transcendental *līlā,* you so kindly blessed the San Francisco Bay Area, including the small, picturesque city of Berkeley, with your auspicious presence. Devotees who love you can feel your presence here even to this day.

In the 1960’s, in a small apartment building on Frederick Street in San Francisco, you patiently explained Lord Kṛṣṇa’s sublime philosophy to the confused but innocent young men and women of that day, many of whom still remain your dedicated disciples. By Lord Kṛṣṇa’s arrangement this building has been cleaned, renovated, and freshly painted by the current owner. When devotees walk or drive by this holy place, they at once experience fond memories of you. Nearby, on Haight Street, in 1967, you enacted the first Ratha-yātrā festival in the West.

In San Francisco’s Golden Gate Park, you performed sweet and wonderful pastimes. There you chanted Hare Kṛṣṇa with such devotion and ecstasy that the flower children, sitting on and strolling beneath Hippie Hill, joined in, singing and dancing in a joyous circle, hands held together, playing various musical instruments and dressed in the colorful garb of the day. Because your love and compassion shone forth so brilliantly, you spontaneously attracted a generation of youths whose slogan then was “Don’t trust anyone over thirty!”

In the early 1970’s, you energized a grand Ratha-yātrā festival in Golden Gate Park, attended by thousands — a festival that, under your guidance, is still being conducted every year by your loyal, hard-working devotees. During the festival, you stood up on the Ratha-yātrā cart, your hands raised jubilantly in the air, causing the devotees chanting in *kīrtana* to experience a wonderful spiritual happiness, expressed vividly on their happy, smiling faces.

Stowe Lake, located in the park, was sanctified by your soft lotus feet and transcendental presence as you walked and talked with your disciples there. Devotees who visit this *tīrtha,* even now, feel great inner peace in the fond remembrance of you.

Across the Bay Bridge, in your temple on Stuart Street in Berkeley, ISKCON’s first Deities — Their Lordships Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā— stand majestically on their altar, flanked on their left by the most sweet and charming Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gokulānanda and on their right by beautiful Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. Under your direction, Their Lordships continue to be gorgeously dressed, decorated, and worshiped with devotion by the many devotees who, over the years, have developed a personal relationship with them.

A short walk up the street from the temple is the materially prestigious Berkeley campus of the University of California, which you also sanctified by your strolls on its grounds. Here devotees still chant and dance in *harināma-saṅkīrtana* and distribute your books and *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. Every year on Gaura-pūrṇimā, university students and local residents are given the glorious *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi ad They ride on Their lovely palanquin surrounded by large numbers of ecstatic devotees.

A few paces away from Gaura-Nitāi’s altar in your Berkeley temple stands the original *vyāsāsana* on which you sat. On this seat, your grave yet beautiful *mūrti,* in which you are personally present, is served, worshiped, and adored by thousands. As the years go by, your fame and the devotees’ love and admiration for you continue to grow. This is only befitting, considering the inestimable value of the spiritual gifts you gave to the world. It was in this temple that you made the famous statement that still resonates: “I will never die. I shall live from my books and you will utilize.”

Your Divine Grace wanted the devotees to use the building for offering advanced degrees:

**Bahulāśva:** This is a union of various theological schools. So now in Berkeley we have this very nice building, and we can become a member of this Graduate Theological Union. I have been working with Dr. Judah for about one year, and we’ve talked about this before. And he said that he will sponsor us.

**Prabhupāda:** So I shall take it?

**Bahulāśva:** Yes, he said that he will sponsor us in this union, and then our devotees can study just your books. Maybe they must take an English course, and that would be all we couldn’t offer. Then everything else, they can study from your books.

**Prabhupāda:** Some way or other, if they read my books, they will be benefited. There is no doubt about it.

**Bahulāśva:** Yes. Then they can get a degree recognized by the state of California so they can teach in universities all over the whole country.

**Prabhupāda:** That I want. Do it. We want to give degrees, at least B.A., M.A., and Ph.D., according to the advancement of knowledge. And that will be very much beneficial to your country. Then America will be saved from disaster and it will be the leader. The country will be leader of the whole world. Take this advantage.

By your own transcendental will, the Bhaktivedanta Institute’s Western world headquarters is also here, and by your mercy the sincere devotees there are pushing forward the difficult task of making scientific presentations based on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* all over North America. We have been able to fulfill your desire to grant degrees at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai, and eventually, if you still desire, we will also offer them in Berkeley. Working up to that goal, we invited many scholars, such as Professors Walter Freeman, Don Howard, and Edward MacKinnon, to visit. In addition, the BI devotees also presented two papers in two international scientific conferences, one in San Francisco and another in Canada.

We feel so very fortunate, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to live in a place that was beautified by your divine *līlā* and where memories of you are so easily aroused. Although we are insignificant *jīva* souls trying to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness in the face of so many material conditions, we are hopeful that we will one day attain love for Kṛṣṇa and see you face to face. This hope is nourished by our understanding and faith that you are our eternal spiritual master, father, savior, friend, and ever well-wisher. You are our life and soul, and our hearts’ comfort lies in knowing that you love each and every one of us.

On this holiest of days, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to you to continue to protect and guide us. We also pray that you purify our hearts so that we may work together cooperatively to help one another advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness and give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Please keep us eternally situated in a tiny corner of your beautiful lotuslike heart.

Your servants at ISKCON New Jagannatha Puri Dhāma, Berkeley, California, USA.

## New Nabdwip (Honolulu)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, our founder-*ācārya* and captain of the ship to cross over the dangerous ocean of Kali.

Since the Hawaiian Islands are so remote, farther from any major land mass than any other islands on earth, we may wonder why you named your Honolulu temple New Nabdwip. But just a glance at the *saṅkīrtana* opportunity here tells the story. The whole world comes to Hawaii to try to enjoy life. And if they are lucky enough to hear *harināma*, receive your wonderful books, and take *prasādam,* they really do.

Over the years, Hawaii has been a little infamous for sweeping devotees off the ship, back into the vast *bhava-sindhu.* Fortunately, even a little service done for you never goes in vain, and for those who stay aboard, the gales of time only make the spirit stronger.

Older devotees who visit us remark that New Nabdwip is doing as well or better today than it was while you walked among us, translating the *Bhāgavatam* and the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* beneath the swaying palms. For the last two years, for example, ours has been the leading small temple in North American book distribution. For every distributor touching people’s lives with your books, there are at least five devotees “behind the scenes” making that all possible. To thank you and praise you on your sacred appearance day, I approached some of the guests and crew aboard your ship this past Gaura-Pūrṇimā, to listen to what they had to say.

Since the autumn of 2003, Tejaprakash Prabhu has served you well as temple president, markedly improving the temple’s appearance and performance. Just this week, for example, he expanded our Govinda’s Restaurant cooking to cater to the students at the University of Hawaii. Tejaprakash:

As Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura was the answer to Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s prayer that the Lord send someone from his personal staff to help him revive the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, so our Prabhupāda was also sent by Lord Caitanya to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. Today we rededicate our lives to serving you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who provide all of your followers with such amazing and absolute shelter.

It is said that “behind every great man there is a great woman.” That certainly fits Tejaprakash’s good wife, Lakṣmaṇā Mātājī. Lakṣmaṇā:

It is simply amazing what Śrīla Prabhupāda has done — established temples with gorgeous Deity worship all over the world, saved countless numbers of people and given meaning to their lives. What a great pure devotee he is! I am so thankful to be included among his followers. How grateful and determined we should be to make him the center of our lives.

Five years ago, Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇāmṛta Mātājī and Gokulshyām Prabhu came with their two daughters from Fiji to serve you at New Nabdwip. Gokulshyām is our head cook, both for the temple and for our Govinda’s Restaurant. The “kitchen religion” tests its cooks to the max, but Gokul is as steady as a blue flame, his good wife right behind him. Kṛṣṇāmṛta:

From just a brief airport-transit meeting with Vāsudeva Prabhu in Fiji, Śrīla Prabhupāda planted a Kṛṣṇa conscious seed that gave us gorgeous temples and hundreds of devotees. I miss my *dīkṣā-guru* Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja, but serving in Hawaii, I feel so protected by all the direct disciples of Prabhupāda here. From Prabhupāda’s rooms I can see the whole Tulasī garden, all the flowers we offer to the Deities, the great banyan tree in the back, the devotees chanting. I have no desire to go anywhere else, not to tour or even go to the ocean. Why waste my time? Everything is here. And the people who come to our restaurant feel that. The peace and happiness they couldn’t find half-naked at Waikiki they find here at Kṛṣṇa’s place. That is Prabhupāda’s mercy. He is our founder-*ācārya,* and we must remember that and serve him every single moment.

A U.S. Army soldier I met when I came to Hawaii four years ago, Śrīla Prabhupāda, has now become your granddisciple. After I taught Jearold Hersey *Bhakti-śāstrī,* he received initiation from your Bhakti Tīrtha Mahārāja as Mahāratha dāsa. Now he’s married and making lifelong plans to serve your mission in communications. Mahāratha:

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have boldly initiated a media onslaught to make me Kṛṣṇa conscious. Love resonates from everything you have given, and especially through your servant Bhakti Tīrtha Swami.
