He has initiated me into your school of transcendental communication. Please help me build a network through which I can share all you have given me. Please help me to not be a fan but a loving student-teacher in your school.

Jayagaursundara and Kaumodaki Prabhus are Gujaratis, granddisciples of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who came to our Gaura-pūrṇimā festival all the way from their home in Baltimore, Maryland. Jayagaursundara:

Śrīla Prabhupāda came to America to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness; I came to enjoy. I am a scientist; Prabhupāda would blast people like me. If I had stayed in India, I would not have become a devotee. My wife and I were worshiping Kṛṣṇa, but we did not really understand who Kṛṣṇa was. We found out when we came to America, by Prabhupāda’s mercy. He came to Baltimore. I just missed him, but whenever I hear his voice, I feel like I’ve always known him.

Several Indian devotees serve at New Nabdwip, Śrīla Prabhupāda, worshiping the Pañca-tattva, and some distribute your books, fulfilling Mahāprabhu’s mandate that everyone born in Bhāratavarṣa spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. Ādideva Prabhu from Mumbai came to your movement through the scientific preaching of your Bhaktivedanta Institute. Ādideva:

Two things about Śrīla Prabhupāda especially stand out for me: how he could attract the most sophisticated people, like the scientists, and also accommodate and elevate even the most degraded people, like the hippies, to Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Bhakti Svarūpa Dāmodara Mahārāja brought me to Kṛṣṇa through the door of science. I was an impersonalist and was impressed by how the Bhaktivedanta Institute could reconcile science and religion. How great Prabhupāda is!

Kṛṣṇa-kīrtana Prabhu serves as our head *pūjārī*for Śrī-Śrī Pañca-tattva:

Śrīla Prabhupāda could speak and relate to anyone, from heads of state to outcastes, and remain the same pure devotee of Kṛṣṇa. He was so determined to give everyone Kṛṣṇa consciousness, despite all odds against him: old age, no support, being a foreigner alone, setbacks, heart attacks. And because he’s a transcendental personality, beyond the limits of time and space, he is fully present in his *mūrti*. Just like his room upstairs, where his writing *mūrti* sits. It’s not different from the temple room. He’s fully present, fully available for us, if we have the eyes of love to see.

Time and space press in, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as I recall the many devotees who glorified you that Gaura-Pūrṇimā day: our gardener-preacher Rakṣaṇa Prabhu, relishing the simplicity of the service you have given him: planting, cultivating, and offering Hawaii’s beautiful flowers to the Lord; Bhayahari Prabhu, recalling how transcendentally regal you were — the Supreme Personality of Servitor Godhead — and how stern you became when he laughed once during an initiation ceremony: “Do not laugh; it is not a joke”; your veteran disciple Daśaratha Prabhu, visiting from Arizona, remembering how you wanted us to build a new temple beneath our eastern palms, and how your godbrother Purī Mahārāja said we were doubly blessed: first by Mahāprabhu, second by Your Divine Grace; our lady Ann Davis, who said so much with her hands and her eyes and her words about how present you are in her life, and about the life you have given her serving Śrīmatī Tulasī Devī; and your very own Gurukṛpa Prabhu, telling how you weren’t conditioned like us, wrote through the night, slept only a few hours during the day, caught your *dhotī*in midair once when it blew off during a windy morning walk, and stunned elderly tourists from America’s heartland with your radiant spiritual demeanor: “sweet, sweet.” So much to tell, so little time and space.

I’ll close with a story by a young woman who was only four years old when you last visited New Nabdwip in 1976. Recalling Your Divine Grace, her face lit up like a golden Maui sunrise. Rāsa Rādhā Dāsī:

Śrīla Prabhupāda cared so much for us children. We would shower flowers and Ganges water on him from the balcony outside as he entered the temple. Once during *guru-pūjā,* Prabhupāda gave me a cookie. When I ran out, all the mothers wanted a piece and told me to go back and get some more. So I ran back in and stuck out my hand, but Prabhupāda noticed me and held up a finger to say, “Only one.” I was so shy I was devastated, but then he gave me the most beautiful rose, and I was so happy.

Another time when Prabhupāda was sitting on the *vyāsāsana,* everyone was giving him so many garlands. He was putting them on and taking them off. I wanted to give him one, but I was too small to reach him, so a *brahmacārī* picked me up to give the garland. He was trying to help me, but I was thinking he shouldn’t be touching me because I’m female, so I started kicking him, right in the wrong spot. Poor *brahmacārī*. Prabhupāda saw this and he was laughing so hard. I was really mad. I couldn’t believe Prabhupāda was laughing. I finally got the garland on him, got down to pay obeisances, and Prabhupāda was still laughing. I was so mad, but the *brahmacārī* picked me up again and brought me to Prabhupāda, who piled all these garlands on me and melted my heart with the most wonderful smile, and everyone cried, “*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!”

*Jaya,* indeed, Śrīla Prabhupāda. All victory, all glories to you, our beloved founder-*ācārya*! You live forever in your divine instructions, and your followers live with you. Please accept this humble garland of grateful praises, for it is in the cooperative *saṅga* of your faithful followers that your full mercy alights. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

The servant of your servants in New Nabdwip, Honolulu, Hawaii, USA.  
(written by Sureśvara dāsa)

## New Nīlācala (Philadelphia)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble and heartfelt obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace on this glorious day!

Forty years ago you selflessly set out for the Western countries to give us the precious gift of knowledge of Kṛṣṇa. Our lives, and in fact this world, has forever been changed. By your grace, people from all countries and walks of life are reading your books, rendering service to the Lord, and chanting the Lord’s holy names. Your mission to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness has not stopped with your departure, nor will it end with your direct disciples’ departure. By your teachings and the example set by your unwavering disciples, countless numbers of aspiring devotees from the next generation are sincerely striving to take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness and to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others. Even though they may never have met you in person, those within the next generation, your granddisciples, affectionately and reverentially embrace you as their ever well-wishing grandfather and steadfast exemplar of their highest spiritual aspirations. This is sure proof that the potency of your dedication to your own spiritual master is being successfully passed down through the generations, and that your mission still continues today.

Currently at New Nīlācala-dhāma, the next generation is being encouraged and guided by your disciples to take on stronger leadership positions in preaching, congregational development, and management. This joint effort of senior and junior members of ISKCON has successfully resulted in a heightened sense of enthusiasm and cooperation to practically improve and spiritually enrich the Philadelphia community in accordance with your instructions. Groups such as the Temple Support Committee are working to enliven fellowship within the community, to promote preaching programs, and to increase communication between temple leadership and the congregation. The local Pāṇḍava Sena Youth Group is enthusiastically engaging young people in attractive Kṛṣṇa conscious college preaching programs and youth activities. Additionally, several of your disciples and granddisciples have joined together to revive the Ratha-yātrā in Philadelphia so that thousands can once again receive the mercy of Lord Jagannātha.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in light of these ongoing efforts, we humbly ask for your blessings that our community continues to grow with the active guidance of your disciples and the enthusiasm of the next generation, in accordance with your vision. May we, in your name, foster this temple to become not only a place of worship but also a center of active preaching and genuine spiritual development for everyone who takes shelter of New Nīlācala-dhāma. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please give us the necessary intelligence and enthusiasm to become Kṛṣṇa conscious ourselves and to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness across and through multitudes of generations.

Your eternal servants at New Nīlācala Dhāma, in Philadelphia, Pennsylvania, USA.

## New Ramaṇa-reti

Dear Srila Prabhupada,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On the battlefield,  
even the ordinary person can become a hero.  
The villager, the doctor, the student, the thief—  
history plays not its role nor imposes its own force,  
the simple choice to follow  
the commands of the general  
initiates the probable course.  
Prabhupāda, O saffron-clad general,  
both in action and prediction,  
in your right hand the banner of Gaurāṅgadev  
tightly gripped,  
you launched a full-scale assault  
on the fortress of Māyā and her associates.  
It was not a covert operation  
but a full frontal confrontation  
on Māyās most crucial and strategic point  
of holding.  
With your arsenal of transcendental books  
and the weapon of the holy name  
you strode, head held high,  
into Māyās stronghold, NYC,  
and claimed strategic hearts.  
Master strategist,  
under your command  
your newly found soldiers spread  
throughout the land —  
Boston, San Francisco, Montreal, Los Angeles,  
London, Paris, Calcutta, Bombay, Delhi,  
Moscow, Frankfort, Berlin, Rome, Caracas, Nairobi —  
and established outposts of knowledge  
from which the holy name was launched.  
In the battle soldiers were lost,  
and your heart felt the pain;  
many joined,  
recruits were gained,  
and inch by inch  
the juggernaut progressed -—  
north, south, east, and west,  
across every boundary and obstacle,  
far across Māyās plain.  
The war rages on  
externally, and internally  
in the battlefield of the heart.  
And we stand, hands folded,  
praying you continue to impart  
the strength, determination,  
enthusiasm, and concentration  
to follow under your loyal command,  
no matter what the demand,  
no matter what the demand.

Your loyal warriors and servants,  
The residents of New Ramaṇa Reti, ISKCON’s rural community in Alachua, Florida, USA.

## New Remuṇā (Toronto)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all your devotees here in New Remuṇā Dhāma, ISKCON Toronto, I offer my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet, and to our Vaiṣṇava disciplic succession.

You once stated that your disease was that you could not do anything small. In Toronto we experience the power of your prayers when we see the immense size of Śrī Śrī Rādhā Kṣīracorā-Gopīnātha’s temple and North America’s largest and most well-attended Ratha-yātrā festival. The sincere devotees here preserve the loving feeling of devotional service you taught us by your example and words:

Here is Kṛṣṇa personally present. We have to receive Him very nicely. Here is a chance to serve Him. We have to give Him nice food, nice dress. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Vṛndāvana, 24 September 1976\]

The Deity worship continues to go on here, with six offerings a day, and with a loyal following of several hundred people who come every Sunday. Today Bhaktimārga Mahārāja will initiate several more devotees as *brāhmaṇas* to help facilitate the Deity service and preaching.

The negligence of others’ devotion was your gain in acquiring this large church, as well as other major temple buildings around the world:

In Western countries also the churches are being closed — big, big churches. . . . Nobody comes. . . . We have purchased several churches because it is now useless. . . . In Toronto that \[church\] recently we have purchased. Big, big church. But they would not sell us. . . . The priest said that “I shall set fire in this church, still I shall not give to Bhaktivedanta Swami” (laughter). \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class, Vṛndāvana, 24 September 1976\]

In that same lecture you warned us to be mindful that we don’t suffer the same plight faced by the Christians:

As soon as you will lose the spirit of service, this temple will become a big *go-down,* that’s all. No more temple. So we have to maintain that service spirit. . . . Who can worship Kṛṣṇa? When there is *bhāva,* loving feeling. . . . A show cannot go on many days. Show will be finished very soon.

You commented on how the temples in Vṛndāvana are becoming broken. But Bhaktimārga Swami, Mother Subuddhi, Arjunanātha Prabhu, and the temple council are making sure that renovations go on all year long.

At your arrival address on 17 June 1976, you said,

So we have come here in Toronto to open this center to give this enlightenment to everyone. It is not meant for a particular nation, particular religious system. It is a science. . . . These instructions are not meant for East or West. It is meant for everyone. . . . This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is to educate people how to become wise, honest, truthful, believer in God. . . . This is the platform of united nations, or united living beings. And we are not simply talking. This science is being discussed in . . . fifty-four books. These books are selling very nicely. \[Kṛṣṇa consciousness\] is not sentiment, it is a science. So we request the people of this nice city, Toronto, to take advantage of this movement. Come here, read our books, make your questions solved, and you’ll be all happy. It doesn’t matter whether you are Canadian or Indian.

Please bless us to increase our devotion to please you with more service.

Your servants at New Remuṇā-dhāma, ISKCON Toronto, Canada.  
(written by Gaura dāsa)

## New Tālavan (Mississippi)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

You are the most exalted of self-realized souls. Since you are always auspicious, I wish that you be auspicious for our community. You are worshipable by virtue of the all-perfect instructions you give. By expanding the Lord’s transcendental vibrations, you have revealed the actual meaning of everything, and you have bestowed the topmost of all benedictions and answered all of these questions: Who are we? Where have we come from? Where are we going? What is the purpose of life? Who is God and what is our relationship with Him? How do we become qualified to meet Him? Where does He live and what is His name?

Every year at this time we are faced with the question of how the unqualified can glorify the most qualified and glorious. How can someone born in ignorance know someone in full knowledge, a fully self-realized soul? How can those with amnesia remember and the unfortunate become fortunate? How can the hellish become heavenly, the ungodly godly? How can the unfriendly become friendly, the resentful grateful? How can someone with blunt material senses understand or reach transcendence, and how can the imperfect know perfection? How can the greedy and selfish become generous, the inauspicious auspicious? How can the uncontrolled senses be controlled, and the fearful become fearless? How can someone serve who does not have a service attitude and only wants to enjoy? How can someone impure know purity, how can the arrogant become humble, and the disrespectful learn respect? How can the dishonest become honest, the insane sane, and the angry peaceful? How can the unhappy become happy, and how does a materialist become spiritual? How does the envious become nonenvious, and the rebel learn cooperation? How do you turn lust to love and sow the seeds of love of God in a heart as barren as an alkaline field?

Impossible. But let us try anyway, by your causeless mercy. This was your mood in coming to the West, Śrīla Prabhupāda, as you related in this famous talk you gave on your Guru Mahārāja’s appearance day in Atlanta in 1975:

This movement was started in 1965 from New York. And I was not very much hopeful because it is very difficult task, just opposite the European and Western culture. . . . When I was on the ship at Boston port, Commonwealth port, I was thinking that “I have come here. I do not know what is the purpose because how the people will accept this movement? They are differently educated, and as soon as I will say, ‘So, my dear sir, you have to give up meat-eating and illicit sex, and no intoxication and gambling,’ they will say, ‘Please go home.’” \[laughter\] Because that was the experience of one of my godbrothers. He went to London, and he had the opportunity to talk with one big man, Marquis of Zetland. . . . So he proposed to my godbrother, “Whether you can make me a *brāhmaṇa*?” So my Godbrother proposed, “Yes, we can make anyone *brāhmaṇa* provided you follow this principle: no illicit sex, no intoxication, no meat-eating, no gambling.” So that Lord Zetland immediately replied, “Impossible.” \[laughter\] So I was thinking that “I will propose something which is impossible. Anyway, let me try.”

It is only by your causeless mercy that we can practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Our only qualification for coming in contact with Your Divine Grace was that we had no qualifications, and because we were so fallen we qualified for the Lord’s special mercy mission. To distribute love of God freely to the most fallen in the most fallen age was simple, but not easy, for you, the most empowered devotee of the most munificent incarnation, Lord Caitanya, the golden *avatāra,* whose process is the chanting of the holy names of God, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

We all pray that we may know how to pray, how to speak, how to desire, and how to cooperate with one another so that we may do something right in assisting you in this mission of spreading love of God. Or, at least let us not get in the way or cause a disturbance to your mission. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for distributing this causeless mercy and doing the impossible!

Your servants at the New Tālavan rural community in Mississippi, USA.  
(written by Gokularañjana dāsa)

## New Varṣāṇā (Auckland)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You still come in dreams . . .

You were sitting on the floor, which was covered with a clean white sheet, surrounded by your disciples. The room was full. Leaning back on a large bolster, you looked effulgent.

The discussion was on how to finish decorating the new building. Everyone was giving their ideas, and this was going on for some time with no conclusion.

I was in the far corner when I suddenly got up and gave an idea of how it could be done. Then you looked at me and exclaimed, “Very good! This will finish everything very nicely,” which made me feel very satisfied.

I then went to sit back down, this time next to you. As I was sitting I touched your lotus feet, and you looked at me with one of those Śrīla Prabhupāda smiles, the ones that made us melt on the spot. The ones that made us feel wanted and cared for, the ones where we felt that you were pleased with our service . . . just like Kṛṣṇa’s smiles, the ones we read about in the *Kṛṣṇa* book, and how they affect the residents of Vṛndāvana. So your smile has an amazing effect on us all.

Then still in a joyful mood, I woke up. I reflected on how you always drop in, not at any particular time, just when you feel like it. And that keeps me going, your association. Thinking of you has always kept me going. I don’t feel that I’m doing anything different from other devotees; in fact, I feel that I’m the last person who should have your association since I’m such a rascal.

But in reflecting, I think that at least I am your rascal and I have a master, a great one at that, who remains my ever well-wisher through the ups and downs of material existence.

How can I complain? How can I not keep going on my way back to Godhead? What an amazing service you do and have for Kṛṣṇa, bringing back the fallen. I can’t wait to see you again.

With love and affection,  
Your servants at New Varṣāṇā, ISKCON’s temple community in Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Kalasaṁvara dāsa)

## New Vraja-dhāma (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances! All glories to you!

Thank you for coming to the Western world and bringing us the message of Lord Caitanya! Thank you for translating *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for us and bringing us the holy names of the Lord! Thank you for sending us our spiritual master, His Holiness Śivarāma Swami, so that he may guide us in spiritual life. Thank you for New Vraja-dhāma, the spiritual abode, where we can practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Thank you for Śrī-Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, our Deities, whom we can worship day by day. Also, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us a mission: on the one hand to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness to others, and on the other to establish *varṇāśrama* here in Hungary in the Age of Kali.

Today we live in a world where people have completely forgotten not only God, Kṛṣṇa, but also the simple way of making a living. People work day and night to get money; in this way they serve false masters who just exploit them.

*kathañcana smṛte yasmin duṣkaraṁ sukaraṁ bhavet*  
*vismṛte viparītaṁ syāt śrī-caitanyaṁ namāmi tam*

**“Things that are very difficult to do become easy to execute if one somehow or other simply remembers Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. But if one does not remember Him, even easy things become very difficult. To this Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu I offer my respectful obeisances.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi-līlā*** **14.1)**

If one remembers Lord Caitanya, the most difficult thing, going back to the spiritual world, becomes possible, whereas if one forgets Him, even the easiest thing, just making a living, becomes very difficult.

The aim of New Vraja-dhāma, with the service of the presiding Deities, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in the center, is to establish a *daivī-varṇāśrama* community. The members of this community, while following Vaiṣṇava culture, pursuing self-sufficient farming, and bringing up their children to live a spiritual life, practice the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness with the goal of attaining pure love of God and inspiring others to follow their example.

Only by your grace will we able to carry out this mission.

Once you said that *varṇāśrama* means that we can make our living from cultivating the land and protecting cows. The scriptures emphasize how important the cow is for human society and for spiritual progress. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted ISKCON to show the world how to live simply on the land, depending on the cows and the bulls and, of course, Kṛṣṇa. Ultimately you envisioned introducing the *varṇāśrama* system in the West, starting with the ISKCON farms. You emphasized that the guiding principle of life should be “simple living and high thinking,” and this principle will ensure that people are engaged according to their natural propensities, with Kṛṣṇa in the center.

Living a simple life in Kṛṣṇa consciousness is a serious challenge for us, since our upbringing has made us very much attached to the achievements of modernization: running water, telephones, cars, and so on. That is complicated life. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, give your mercy to us so that we can live a simple life! We are trying to become independent of so-called civilization in more and more aspects. Now we produce half our food, and we are trying to increase the proportion of it. We have found that the more we give up using machines, cars, vans, tractors, etc., the more we need to use the strength of the bulls. If we need the strength of the bulls, then we will automatically protect them, and thus cow protection will not be just a sacred thing but an integral part of our everyday life as well as a natural necessity.

We experimented to learn how to produce our own detergent, and now we are able to make a high-quality strong detergent from ashes. We would like to develop to the point where we can build houses of locally available raw materials and can make our own clothes from local plants. This is a big challenge, though a few decades or centuries ago it was absolutely natural for everyone in every part of the world.

Today, modern technology has forced more and more people to eat food that barely merits the name. Milk sold in the shops just barely comes from cows, crops are treated with large amounts of chemicals, fruits are losing their taste, vegetables do not see the moon, all bodies of water are becoming more and more polluted, and it is predicted that clean drinking water will soon be more expensive than petrol. Large amounts of seasoning and artificial flavors and coloring are added to most food people eat, so that they seem genuine, but in fact they are not. We live in the world of names:

*ataḥ kavir nāmasu yāvad arthaḥ*  
*syād apramatto vyavasāya-buddhiḥ*  
*siddhe ’nyathārthe na yateta tatra*  
*pariśramaṁ tatra samīkṣamāṇaḥ*

**“For this reason the enlightened person should endeavor only for the minimum necessities of life while in the world of names. He should be intelligently fixed and never endeavor for unwanted things, being competent to perceive practically that all such endeavors are merely hard labor for nothing.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **2.2.3)**

And in the purport you say:

Those who are captivated by the illusory advancement of material civilization (following the way of phantasmagoria) are certainly madmen. The whole material creation is a jugglery of names only; in fact, it is nothing but a bewildering creation of matter like earth, water, and fire.

To get out of this world of names, by your grace we can take shelter of the holy names of the Lord. So that we will have plenty of time to sing the holy names of the Lord, please help us establish a self-sufficient farming community here in Hungary. Enable us to make our living by the grace of the land, the cows, and, of course, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, and also enable us to teach others to do the same.

Your insignificant servants at the New Vraja-dhāma farming community in Hungary.

## New Vraja (Khaśadeśa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Mission Impossible**

Our dear grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda, in the last part of the preface to *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* you speak of our mission: “Our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is teaching the whole world this central point \[to satisfy the senses of the Lord\].” In the last chapter of the *Gītā,* you stress this point again: one must become a devotee of Kṛṣṇa, and the essence of all religion is to surrender unto Kṛṣṇa (*sarva-dharmān parityajya mām ekaṁśaraṇaṁ vraja*).

The task of teaching the whole world to surrender unto Kṛṣṇa seems impossible, full of insurmountable obstacles. Before you went to America, some of your godbrothers tried to preach in the Western countries, but none of them succeeded. They all said it was impossible to transform the *yavanas* and *mlecchas* into Vaiṣṇavas, because nobody in the Western world could follow the pure principles of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

But you did it. You accomplished the impossible task of transforming thousands of *yavanas* and *mlecchas* into Kṛṣṇa’s devotees.

Today, there are many wonderful ISKCON centers and blissful devotees all over the world. Here, in a place thousands of miles away from the birthplace of Lord Caitanya, we are chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* and reading the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Our dear grandfather, you not only accomplished a “mission impossible” on behalf of your Guru Mahārāja but also bestowed a potency like your own on your sincere followers. Simply by the mercy of your pure representatives, we have gotten the chance to learn how to obtain the perfection of human life — surrendering unto Kṛṣṇa.

And it is all by your mercy.

When we think about your incredible achievements, we wonder how to carry on your legacy successfully, how to execute the impossible task you have left for us.

Your task ahead is very huge, but it will be quite simple and easy if you simply do as I am doing. \[Letter to Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami, 9 July 1972\]

So we have to do what you have done. You preached *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. And what is the secret of your success? You yourself told us:

If I have any credit in this connection, it does not belong to me personally, but it is due to my eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda. \[*Bhagavad-gītā,* preface\]

If we follow in your footsteps, our success is also guaranteed. The trick in accomplishing this impossible mission, then, is to get your mercy by following the instructions of our own Guru Mahārājas. Before we surrender to Kṛṣṇa, we have to willingly surrender ourselves to His devotees.

You said that “*impossible* is a word found in a fool’s dictionary.” Thus we are forced to accept your extraordinary mercy. Such is the ecstatic mood needed to execute our “mission impossible.”

All glories to you, our dear grandfather!

The devotees in New Vraja, Khaśadeśa.

## New Vrindaban

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Vrindāban Vrindāban all covered with snow,  
Where did all your *surabhi* cows go?  
The green scenic pastures are covered with ice,  
To live in Vrindāban is not a cheap price.

This is a stanza from a beautiful poem written by a New Vrindāban Brijbāsī, Hlādinī-devī dāsī. She, like so many others, Śrīla Prabhupāda, found the beauty of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental abode here in your New Vrindāban-dhāma. They found your gift of transcendental reality superimposed on the hills of West Virginia.

Over the years there has been a huge array of souls who have spent time here serving Your Divine Grace in a diversity of ways, and we have been blessed by and have benefited from the association of all of them. These are the souls you have plucked from the garden of Māyā to offer to Rādhā-Vrindābanchandra. This is your beautiful bouquet of devotees, all unique and distinctive. Well done, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Well done.

For the benefit of souls like us, you spent your years establishing places where we could find the manifestation of Kṛṣṇa’s pastimes. For this we thank you. What would have happened to us if you had not taken the trouble to come to our shores to give us spiritual life? The processes you gave us to reach Godhead and feel His association — hearing, chanting, remembering, worshiping, serving, and associating with like-minded devotees — are all manifest in these holy places still, and we can fortunately take advantage of them. We know you taught us these processes so we could make advancement out of the mire of material existence, and all we have to do is follow your lead to the best of our ability. You are the kind master who loves us, and it is to our benefit to follow you.

As time passes, many of us seem to drift further away from the strict process of pure devotional service you gave us, and we realize that your personal manifestation was our strengthening, attractive oasis that inspired and nourished us on our “march toward the Personality of Godhead.” We hope to have the opportunity to have your association again at some time in the future.

So here we all are still, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in New Vrindāban, doing our best and hoping that you are pleased with our endeavors and accomplishments. Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet and forgive our many offenses. All glories to you!

From your loving inmates of New Vrindāban, West Virginia, USA.

## New York (Brooklyn)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

***“*****I offer my respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa on this earth, having taken shelter at His lotus feet. Our respectful obeisances are unto you, O spiritual master, servant of Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī. You are kindly preaching the message of Lord Caitanyadeva and delivering the Western countries, which are filled with impersonalism and voidism.”**

*yathā kāñcanatāṁ yāti kāṁsyaṁ rasa-vidhānataḥ*  
*tathā dīkṣā-vidhānena dvijatvaṁ jāyate nṛṇām*

**“Just as bell metal is transformed into gold when mixed with mercury in an alchemical process, so one who is properly trained and initiated by a bona fide spiritual master immediately becomes a** ***brāhmaṇa*****.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your desire, so many wonderful, most beautiful, famous, and all-attractive Deities have been established for worship in many important cities of the world. Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Londonīśvara and Rādhā-Gokulānanda in London, Rādhā-Parisīśvara in Paris, Rādhā-Śyāmasundara in Vṛndāvana-dhāma, Rādhā-Mādhava in Māyāpur-dhāma, Rādhā-Rāsabihārī in Mumbai, Rukmiṇī-Dvārakādhīśa in Los Angeles, Rādhā-Kālachandjī in Dallas, Rādhā-Vṛndāvanacandra in New Vṛndāvana, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govindajī in New York City, and many, many more. *Arcana-siddhi* has become a real possibility for many of your sincere students, rendering *bhakti* to their beloved *iṣṭa-devas,* the all-attractive Śrī Govinda, Gopīnātha, and Madana-mohana. In your letters you often stressed the importance of sincerely serving the Deity. Here is one such statement:

If one attains perfection in Deity worship, that is called Arcana Siddhi. Arcana Siddhi means simply by Deity worship one goes back to Godhead, immediately after this life. \[Letter to Himavatī-devī dāsī, 18 March 1969\]

Realized love of Kṛṣṇa is the topmost goal, made achievable by your mercy, available for all your disciples to strive for. From early each morning until late every night you provide us with the opportunity to realize our love for Kṛṣṇa by engagement in the high standards of Deity worship. *Maṅgala-ārati*, chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* performing *pūjā, śṛṅgāra,* hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā,* preaching, giving your books to others, teaching the secret of the *Gītā* to the devotees — all are aspects of Deity *sevā,* and when they’re offered sincerely, with *bhakti,* with love and devotion to Kṛṣṇa, they become little drops of love that fill our consciousness with realization. When enough little drops of love for Kṛṣṇa fill us up, our consciousness overflows with *ānanda,* the bliss of *prema,* and by your mercy we achieve the topmost goal of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You express it so nicely in a letter:

So if you carry on your service to Radha and Krishna according to the prescribed rules, your love of Krishna will increase more and more; this you are seeing practically by the Grace of Krishna. . . . We are following the prescribed method of Temple worship, as it is the effective program for developing the highest perfection of pure love of Krishna. \[Letter to Yamunā-devī dāsī, 16 January 1970\]

Peace and enthusiasm result from sincere service to Your Divine Grace and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa *vigraha*. The latter includes cleanliness and punctuality, dressing and decorating the Deity, offering palatable *bhoga* prepared with *bhakti —* love and devotion, the most essential ingredient — and offering sweet-scented articles, especially sweet, fragrant flowers, e.g., gardenia, rose, jasmine, kadamba, champak, narcissus, and tube rose (there are so many varieties of fragrant flowers for pleasing Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa). You were asked once what pleases you the most, and your answer — “When I see that my disciple is learning to love Kṛṣṇa”— is an inspiration. You want us to take the most special care of your beloved Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa Deities, and you know that by serving Kṛṣṇa with *bhakti,* love and pure devotion, we become enriched. Therefore you directed us:

The Deity must be very nicely worshiped, dressed, and fed, etc. In so doing you will always be peaceful and enlivened. \[Letter to Balavanta dāsa, 8 July 1976\]

Preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness and worshiping the Deity go side by side; hearing and chanting is the life and soul of deity worship. If we serve you sincerely, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we elevate our care for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa to the platform of *śuddha-bhakti,* by that pure *sevā*of love and devotion all those fortunate enough to get *darśana* with the all-attractive couple Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa will be mesmerized in a trance of *bhakti,* and they will forget about all so-called worldly beauty. Māyā cannot stand in the presence of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you saved us from the inebriating clutches of Māyā, especially by directing us to serve Kṛṣṇa with *bhakti.* When we follow your directions, our real love and devotion blossom, entwined with the mercy and love of Lord Kṛṣṇa in His all-attractive Deity form. Millions of aspiring *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas* are being attracted by the beautiful Deities you established in ISKCON. You wanted us to perfect Deity worship, knowing that perfect Deity worship would be an effective preaching tool. Your Divine Grace wrote:

So many people are coming to the New Delhi Temple because of the nice Deity worship. This is very good. Keep the standard of Deity worship very nicely. \[Letter to Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Goswami, 11 July 1976\]

Beautifying the heart and feeling transcendental bliss as we decorate Lord Kṛṣṇa with gorgeous clothes, sparkling jewels, and colorful and sweet fragrant flowers, we simultaneously adorn our heart with the qualities of a devotee. This principle is stated by Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī in his commentary on a verse in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (11.3.5): “\[In this verse the word\] *ātma-pratyoditaiḥ* indicates that by surrendering unto the Lord, who is the reservoir of all good qualities, the pure devotees become similarly endowed with godly qualities.” Our goal is targeted: we aim first to please Your Divine Grace, and by so doing we are cent percent confident we will please our Lords, Śrī Rādhā and Śrī Kṛṣṇa, we will chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and dance in ecstasy in this world, and by the potency of your wonderful mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we shall chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and dance in ecstasy in the spiritual world also.

You inspire us with your encouraging words, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

I am pleased that you are decorating your temple nicely, and the more you decorate the temple beautifully, the more your heart will be beautiful. The example is that the more you decorate the original, the more the reflection automatically is decorated. So our heart is the reflection of the Original Consciousness, Krsna, and the more Krsna and His paraphernalia are decorated, the more this will be reflected in our heart and we will feel transcendental bliss. \[Letter to Upendra dāsa, 27 October 1969\]

Studying the *Vedas* is the duty of every *brāhmaṇa,* and realizing the imports of the *Vedas* is achieved by *bhakti,* pure devotional service, to the *arcā-vigraha* of Lord Kṛṣṇa. You wanted to create a society of first-class *brāhmaṇas* in ISKCON, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You stated as much in Detroit in 1976, when you told two Catholic Priests, “We are trying to create a society of *brāhmaṇas*.” A *brāhmaṇa* engages in Deity worship and teaches others how to worship the Deity of Kṛṣṇa, studies the *Vedas* and teaches the *Vedas* to devotees, and accepts charity and gives charity to others. If all ISKCON *brāhmaṇas* would rigidly adhere to these brahminical occupations, ISKCON would grow and be in the best of spiritual health. Deity worship is the key that unlocks the door leading to *bhakti* and devotional success, as you so nicely express:

Anyone who engages in the worship of the Deity, or the form of the Lord in the temple, is understood to be studying all the *Vedas* twenty-four hours a day. Simply by decorating the Deities of the Lord, Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, in the temple, one very minutely studies the injunctions of the *Vedas*. Even a neophyte devotee who simply engages in the worship of the Deity is understood to be in direct touch with the purport of Vedic knowledge. As confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā*(15.15), *vedaiś ca sarvair aham eva vedyaḥ:* the purport of the *Vedas* is to understand Him, Kṛṣṇa. One who worships and serves Kṛṣṇa directly has understood the truths of the *Vedas*. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.7.46, purport\]

Thank you so much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for establishing an all-pervasive network of *bhakti* Deity *sevā,* love and devotion, in so many locations all around the world. Your Divine Grace made it easy in this most difficult Age of Kali to find a Vaikuṇṭha-dhāma, an ISKCON temple, in which to perform our humble and insignificant devotional *sevā*to the all-attractive Śrī Rādhā and Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*śrī-guro paramānanda premānanda phala-prada*  
*vrajānanda pradānanda sevayāṁ mā niyojaya*

**“My dear spiritual master, who give the fruit of the highest bliss — the bliss of love of Godhead — please engage me in the blissful service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who bestows bliss upon the land of Vraja.”**

We pray at your lotus feet to remain your menial servants forever, and we pray to remain  
Your insignificant beggars at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda Mandira, Brooklyn, New York City, New York, USA.  
(written by Rāmabhadra dāsa)

## New York (26 Second Avenue)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement!

May your blessings always be with us.

Here in the sacred room of 26 Second Avenue we feel safe. Sitting at your lotus feet and taking *darśana* of Your Divine Grace is our solace and inspiration.

Although most of the devotees here never met you in person, still, we feel very fortunate to call this *tīrtha,* where you began your inconceivable pastime of saving humanity, our home.

Home means the place of our origin and shelter. You are both father and mother to all humanity, and we feel very grateful to live so close to you. Your presence emanates a warmth and love that cannot be found in this material world. *Golokera prema-dhana, harināma-saṅkīrtana*. You brought the love of our eternal home in Goloka Vṛndāvana to the Lower East Side of New York City. No wonder we are so attached to this little storefront, where you revealed this treasure for the first time and for all time to come.

There is only one problem: We are just a handful of devotees, but you wanted thousands and millions of people to receive your most precious gift. That was your express desire in your prayers on the *Jaladuta,* and that was the driving force during your pastimes on this earth.

Here in New York the discrepancy between your desires and our daily reality is particularly apparent. Living in close quarters with millions of people who have never experienced a taste of *prema-bhakti* and are therefore running madly after Māyās allurements makes it very difficult to be satisfied and just go on with business as usual.

The very purpose of your coming here was to create a society exclusively dedicated to giving shelter to millions of people, but where are these people now?

How can we possibly think that you are satisfied with us?

On this most auspicious day of your appearance in this material world, I fall at your feet and beg you, on behalf of all the devotees in the New York area, to kindly use us in your most important mission. Invest in us a spark of your blazing desire to share Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

There is no other means of salvation for any of your followers. To the degree that we are absorbed in assisting you in your service to Guru and Gaurāṅga and attract millions of *jīvas* to the *saṅkīrtana* movement, to the same degree we will find our taste for the holy name and service to the Vaiṣṇavas increasing.

You have the power to bestow this taste for service to your lotus feet, and I just wanted to let you know that we want it.

This might sound arrogant and presumptuous, but how could I ask for anything else? You destroyed all our hopes for happiness in this material world and for liberation and revealed the most elevated state of consciousness — Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And even though we know that we are the most unqualified, still, how could we not ask for this boon? You took everything else away.

We are your servants, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Kindly engage us in your service.

Your servants at 26 Second Avenue, New York City, New York, USA.  
(written by Yajña Puruṣa dāsa)

## Osijek (Croatia)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious occasion of your appearance day, let us try to glorify the greatest *ācārya* of modern times, the *ācārya* for the Golden Age. But how can we, the unknown little devotees from the wild and uncivilized Balkan area, glorify Your Divine Grace, who descended from the spiritual sky, who are the resident of the Vaikuṇṭha world? There is a monumental contrast here, between the greatest resident of the spiritual world and the lowest residents of the material world. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are really a *patita-pāvana* of the highest rank!

The very fact that the voice of barbarians and *mlecchas* like us can be heard in the assembly glorifying you speaks for its self: This is your glory! This is your great achievement!

Kirātas, Khaśas, Pulindas, Pulkaśas, Yavanas, and all other *mlecchas* are taking to this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. This is your glory!

Nobody else except you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all around the world — nobody else could do it! This is the sign of the most advanced, *uttama* devotee, as Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura taught us. So nobody else can claim the title of world *ācārya*. There was no *ācārya* like you in the past, and there will be no *ācārya* of such a magnitude as you in the future, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Wretched and cursed as we are, we were not fortunate to meet you in person, to see you and be blessed by your aristocratic glance. In those great moments of history we were filling our diapers in deep ignorance, spending valuable time uselessly serving our wicked mind, preoccupied with childish games.

What a waste, what a pity! How unfortunate we were to be on this planet in those times but to be separated from your divine glance.

Decades have passed, witnessing the massive failure of ideologies and the legalization of degradation in all spheres of modern society. Even so-called religionists have no power to discriminate right from wrong, wrong from right. But where the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness shines, there is no darkness. You are spreading this light, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your books are everywhere. Your sincere followers — disciples and granddisciples — are still on the street, distributing them, following your example. As you sacrificed everything to distribute your *Back to Godhead* magazine on the streets of Delhi, so your sincere followers are making great sacrifices to distribute your transcendental literature to everybody and anybody on the streets of the world’s cities. This is your mercy, this is your care for all the conditioned *jīvas* of the world. Sometimes our faith in you may waiver, and sometimes we may even deny your will, but none of that can change the fact, dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you came to save us all and sacrificed everything to do it.

Please, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us with good intelligence and vigor to become and stay your faithful followers. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, smash us and correct us if we start to speculate and deviate from the clear path you have mercifully shown us. We have no other hope than to stay simple servants of Your Divine Grace.

Your servants at ISKCON Osijek, Croatia.  
(written by Divya-prabandha dāsa)

## Palangkaraya (Borneo)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

We, the devotees of Palangkaraya and Lalitā Sevā Kuñja, offer our most respectful obeisances to Oṁ Viṣṇupāda A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who is bringing the greatest pleasure to all the fallen souls by spreading the teachings of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu all over the world. Being fully surrendered to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, he has spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the Western countries, which were deeply covered by ignorance, and he has made the people of the West become his devotees and disciples. All glories to Oṁ Viṣṇupāda A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who has opened our hearts, which were fully covered by the darkness of the material world, and who has given us the light of true spiritual science, Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

We pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who has been patiently saving us from this great ocean of suffering and giving us this topmost gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

All glories to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who never stopped spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, till the very end of his life.

The depth and breadth of the ocean could not shake Śrīla Prabhupāda’s determination to carry out Lord Caitanya’s mission. The darkness of Kali-yuga could not slacken his resolve to introduce Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the atheists.

To this day, Śrīla Prabhupāda remains alive in his books and helps us understand who Kṛṣṇa is and who we are.

We pray that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s fame will be spread throughout all three worlds. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

From the devotees in Palangkaraya and Lalitā Sevā Kuñja, Tangkiling, Borneo.  
(written by Arjuna dāsa)

## Paris

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances onto the dust of your lotus feet.

This Vyāsa-pūjā marks the end of the year of special celebrations commemorating your 108thappearance anniversary in 2004. The number 108 reminds us of the leading *gopīs* who surrendered themselves to Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, but it also reinforces our feelings of how special you are: You established the chanting of the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra* in the West.

You delivered the teachings of Caitanya Mahāprabhu by traveling all over the world to establish ISKCON, the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, which you said is your very body. We fortunately have access to your message through your books and other instructions, which are available to all now and will be for all time.

All us devotees in France try to serve you at our utmost capacity, and we all take shelter in what you yourself said was your body, ISKCON. In the mood of separation, we try to offer the Deities the best standard of worship.

Our only prayer is to become more humble than a blade of grass and more tolerant than a tree. Thank you for inspiring us to progress on the path of devotional service, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and avoid offenses to the holy name. By your mercy, may forgiveness be triumphant as we humbly serve Your Divine Grace in the mood of separation. We wish to remain your faithful servants in whatever manner possible.

Your humble servants at the ISKCON temple in Paris, France.  
(written by Śatarūpā-devī dāsī)

## Pecs (Hungary)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my respectful obeisances. All glories to you on this special day!

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Your role is great in the mission of Lord Caitanya. By your behavior and teachings, from your appearance until the moment of your departure, you showed your followers and everyone else who somehow had the opportunity to associate with you the example of a pure devotee.

Although we did not have the opportunity to experience the magic of your personal association, still we can feel your greatness through your disciples, written teachings, and recordings. From these we can understand how you accepted your spiritual master’s instruction in your heart, how much you desired to fulfill his wish, and how determined you were to accomplish your mission. We can also see how many disciples you had and how many temples and centers you opened. You have spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world, just as Lord Caitanya predicted. You have created an oasis in Kali-yuga for people who are thirsty for spiritual guidance. Finally, you left this world teaching your disciples by your behavior and words; even in the last days, in the midst of bodily distress, you were always remembering Kṛṣṇa.

We can still experience the results of your achievement. Your disciples are delivering your teachings, and in this way we also have the opportunity to take part in your mission.

We can see how Kali-yuga makes people miserable. Still they do not readily accept the help we can offer. Your greatness is even more palpable, therefore, because you alone attracted so many people. You said that purity is the force, which means that we have to be much purer and advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness to achieve substantial results in the preaching field.

Please bless us so that your mission becomes the most important thing in our lives. Please give us your mercy so that we can realize this soon and so that we can preach in this spirit.

Your servants in Pecs, Hungary.  
(written by Bhaktin Cani)

## Perth

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

The suburbs of Perth and the beaches you walked have not changed very much since you visited here some thirty years ago. However, we now have a fair-sized temple surrounded by flower gardens, trees, and lawns; a nearby block of land that provides Gaura-Nitāi with fresh organic vegetables; and a downtown restaurant that makes a tidy profit. The devotee community has its ups and downs, but generally a friendly co-operative spirit pervades. In comparison to many other parts of the world, we have it very easy here, with practically no political, environmental, financial, or social stresses. Time moves slowly in this isolated part of the world.

But time has taken its toll on us. Those fortunate young disciples who served you in Perth thirty years ago are now on the verge of old age. This first generation of devotees has grown up in your ISKCON, and thus we can all look back over our lives and compare the persons we are now with who we might have been. In this part of the world, with no external pressures of material deprivation or distress to force us to surrender, we live with the burden of a personal freedom to choose to serve Kṛṣṇa or Māyā. When there are so many allurements for which to leave, what keeps us here?

It is simply you and your legacy alone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that keeps us here. We stay because of your sweetness, your purity, your intelligence, your wit, your smile. The way you rubbed your nose, smelt a flower, popped *prasādam* into your mouth. Your walking with head held high, paying your obeisances before the Deities, working at your desk late at night. We stay because you established that special uniqueness of *sādhu-saṅga,* the enduring philosophical quest, the framework for healthy sustainable growth in our lives. And finally maybe, just maybe, if we stay right to the end, we might get a chance to associate with you again in the spiritual world — or wherever you choose to go. All by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in ISKCON’s Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi temple in Perth, Australia.  
(written by Prahlāda Bhakta dāsa)

## Port Elizabeth

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances to your lotus feet.

On this most auspicious event of your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā we pray to you to always shower your mercy upon all of us here in Port Elizabeth. By the gentle and loving guidance of His Holiness Bhakti Caitanya Swami, we are trying to restart a small preaching center in this friendly city. Thank you for sending us Mahārāja, who is very devoted to you and to the mission. Thank you for sending other dear sons and grandsons of yours as well, such as His Holiness Kadamba Kānana Swami and other Mahārājas, who come to inspire us so much.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please help us establish a temple here in Port Elizabeth where the Lord may reside. Gaurasundara Prabhu and his wife, Vṛndā-devī dāsī, and I, Śvetadvīpa dāsa, are begging you again and again for your causeless mercy to be able to assist your mission here in Port Elizabeth. Please enlighten us so we may do everything properly and serve all the Vaiṣṇavas without any offenses. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Port Elizabeth, South Africa.

## Portland

Our dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace! All glories to this most wonderful day!

To feel separation from the Spiritual Master or Kṛṣṇa is very good position. That means one who is in pure love with Kṛṣṇa and His Representative, Spiritual Master, he thinks always of Them. And this thinking process is Kṛṣṇa consciousness. If we can think always of Kṛṣṇa even in separation, that is Kṛṣṇa Consciousness. And in the absolute platform, there is no difference of separation and meeting. The separation is also meeting, rather in separation one relishes the loving relationship more tasty. So don’t be disappointed that you are separated from me, I am also always thinking of you how you are making progress there. \[Letter to Kṛṣṇa dāsa, 21 October 1968\]

Today we are especially inclined to reflect on all that you give us. Our debt to you grows daily. We pray that you will allow us to continue to distribute the knowledge that is our inheritance from you, to serve you in a pleasing manner, to understand you to the best of our ability, to love you purely, to protect your legacy, and to never let you slip from our thoughts.

What can we really offer you? When we compare ourselves to other centers around the world, our paucity of assets is apparent. While many of your disciples and devotees spread your message of love very gloriously throughout the world, a small number of devotees attempt to share this message in Portland, Oregon. Many devotees maintain magnificent Vedic temples, hold large festivals, and perform all kinds of other wonderful services due to your inspiration and guidance. But in Portland we hold simple programs by inviting people to our center to chant the Lord’s holy names and accept His mercy in the form of *prasādam*, and in this way we introduce them to your spiritual family.

What we share with the devotees at other centers around the world is a deep love and appreciation for you and all that you have done. Your pure teachings enable us to attract many people to this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement despite our own shortcomings. Our attempts to serve you are nothing in comparison to the sacrifices you made, but they are all we are able to offer. If we can play any part in assisting you in your mission, then everything is a success.

Please continue to bless us.

Your servants in Portland, Oregon, USA.

## Pretoria

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Our dearmost beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Hare Krsna! All glories to your lotus feet! All glories to your ISKCON, which you have created for our eternal benefit. This movement and your devotees are our only shelter; they have saved us from the ocean of birth and death and total degradation into animal species.

Thank you so very much for all you have done for our benefit, all the struggles you undertook and obstacles you crossed for our sake, all the love you showed — absolute selflessness!

ISKCON is an ocean of unlimited service — service to the devotees, service to those who are not yet devotees. It is only by service that we can attain the lotus feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Nityānanda.

Please give us the intelligence to understand how we are dependent on you and the *paramparā*. Intelligence and ability are all supplied by the Lord and the mercy of the Vaiṣṇavas.

Here in the capital city of South Africa, Pretoria, there are many opportunities. We’ve made solid headway in establishing ourselves here by purchasing a nice house in a very good area where many foreign embassies are located. Our sign out front announces “The Embassy of the Spiritual World.”

We have a vibrant community of devotees of all cultural backgrounds that is full of life, with devotees living inside and outside the temple. Here we are famous for ecstatic, sometimes wild *kīrtanas* where people really dance and jump and chant enthusiastically. The congregation cooks ecstatic feasts full of variety for the Sunday programs. Many young enthusiastic African *bhaktas* really want to preach in Africa.

The simplicity and lack of decadence and degradation in many parts of Africa make it a wonderfully promising place to get the full mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. The sweetness and personal warmth of many of the devotees constitute a great treasure that we are very thankful for.

His Holiness Devāmṛta Swami was amazed at the lack of derangement in young students at the university and felt the opportunity for preaching was most favorable.

We have much to be thankful for. We pray to stay on the move, focused and alive in your mission. May we dive into the flow of nectar that is available in your mission. Please do with us whatever you like.

Hare Krsna!

Your aspiring servants at ISKCON Pretoria, South Africa.

## Pune (India)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine self!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the sole spiritual savior in this soul-killing modern civilization. In the pathetically misdirected modern educational system, a student goes through years of torturous study without coming to know even the basic facts of life — that he is not the body or that there exists a God who loves him. Everything that he learns makes him addicted to drinking the poison of sense gratification as the only goal of life.

It is in such an age of tragic spiritual ignorance that you freely and vigorously distributed the priceless wealth of transcendental knowledge all over the world. “Poverty,” you would say, “means poverty of knowledge.” Your life proved your bold proclamation. You walked onto Boston’s Commonwealth Pier alone and penniless. In just twelve years you created for the world an incredible legacy — a global organization comprising over 108 temples, an awesome library of over seventy volumes of philosophical masterpieces, and an international community of happy devotees dedicated to living according to the Vedic knowledge you so tirelessly taught with your every word and action. Śrīla Prabhupāda, your achievements are stupendous and your gifts matchless.

You often said that you were trying to create a class of *brāhmaṇas* who could lead the world away from its suicidal course. In your final pastimes you also said that the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement would spread through organization and intelligence. With this in mind, we at Pune have focused on organizing a systematic education and training program, especially for the youth. A majority of the most intelligent Indian youths pursue careers in engineering. Therefore we have concentrated on preaching to the engineering youth, first in Pune and then in eminent educational institutions all over India. At present several hundred students all over India are being trained by the Bhaktivedanta Academy for Culture and Education (BACE) to become committed and competent preachers, managers, and leaders in ISKCON. Over thirty of these highly educated youths have dedicated their lives to carrying forward your timeless message.

Keeping your mood and mission in mind, we emphasize one particular aspect of your instructions every year. Last year we concentrated on enriching the śāstric knowledge of the *brahmacārīs,* who comprise the BACE faculty, by having Bhakti Śāstrī courses conducted for them. This year we focused on the training of the BACE faculty so that they could increase their repertoire of weapons while fighting the preaching war against Māyā. Equipped with the knowledge, skills, and values acquired from various Vaiṣṇava training and education courses, we hope and pray that our services to you will increase in both quality and quantity. With your blessings, we will soon be offering Bhakti Śāstrī courses in Pune.

This year we were also blessed with the opportunity to host the first-ever National Youth Festival, organized to celebrate your 108th appearance anniversary in 2004, under the inspiration of His Holiness Lokanāth Swami Mahārāja. By your causelessly mercy the festival was a memorable success, with over four thousand youths from all over India eagerly hearing and assimilating your message, as spoken by a galaxy of over a dozen of your illustrious sons. The success of the festival has stimulated youth preaching in many other parts of India, thus paving the way for many, many more intelligent youths to be delivered by your message and mission.

As youth preaching is picking up everywhere, we are finding ourselves under-equipped to properly serve the hundreds of youths and dozens of youth preachers coming to Pune for education and training. We hope that we will be able to provide them with better accommodations and other facilities in a new temple, which we pray will be built in the near future. On this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we seek your blessings and mercy so that we may be able to continue and expand our humble services to your lotus feet.

Your servants at ISKCON’s Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kuñjabihārī Mandir in Pune, India.

## Rāḍhadesh

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to you, the long-awaited *senapati-bhakta* in the great *saṅkīrtana*-army of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu!

As this offering is being written, there is an anxious stirring in many corners of the world’s population. After decades of powerful, uplifting leadership in the Roman Catholic Church, the Pope has passed away. He was adored by multitudes, even by the many who were at odds with his unflinching religious principles. It seems he has been blessed with the quality of *dhīrādhīra-jana-priya,* which Your Divine Grace, like the Vṛndāvana Gosvāmīs, most perfectly exemplified, making you simultaneously dear to the gentle and to those afflicted with unrestricted desires for material enjoyment.

During your all-too-short visit to this earth, you emanated the qualities revered by all persons seeking a higher station in life. What to speak of devotees appreciating your unlimited divine merits, even worldly philosophers will find in you the essence of their cherished ideals to a superlative degree — and beyond. You have revealed to us “the best of all possible worlds” by demonstrating the very “best use of a bad bargain,” and you unveiled the real meaning of the categorical imperative in the nutshell verses of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Because you are connected heart and soul with the “self-sufficient philosopher who has been awarding everyone’s desire since time immemorial,” your company is always available to us when we sincerely desire it. You teach us how to inquire appropriately, and then you answer our relevant inquiries from all angles of vision. According to requirement, reassurance is given, chastisement is meted out, and eyes are fitted with proper “spectacles.” Even as Arjuna was given divine vision at Kurukṣetra, we learn to see how everything, “both phenomenal and numinous,” exists in relation to Lord Kṛṣṇa. Being so blessed, we discover that anything we perceive, internally or externally, serves to confirm whatever realization we have been able to assimilate from the invincible reservoir of transcendental, blissful knowledge in which you are inviting us to sport. If someone says that others have similar experiences in their given or chosen ideologies and life-views, we know that faith is developed according to inclinations that vary according to association — material, spiritual, or a mixture of both.

Now that Lord Kṛṣṇa is gradually withdrawing your heroic commanders to engage them elsewhere in your divine service, we who are still awaiting our departure are being forcibly drawn to deeper realization concerning the rarity of such great souls. “If we are fortunate, or if we are fortunate to meet another fortunate devotee, then we can understand what is Kṛṣṇa.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* lecture, Bombay, 18 February 1974) Their meeting with you, both in union and separation, is committed and pure — now they will again receive your *darśana*. Surely you will bring them to Lord Kṛṣṇa and His entourage. Under your guidance they will continue to engage in the kind of sublime service that is the hallmark of their supreme good fortune. We pray to be ever-increasingly molded by your devoted, enlightened disciples and followers, the dust of whose feet is far more valuable and substantial than any material or spiritual attainment.

With the variety of projects thriving here in the Radhadesh community, we are continually blessed by the association of many such great souls. We pray to not take this fortune for granted but to properly honor it and sincerely follow their good example and guidance in order to improve our devotional service. May this community be a jewel that transcendentally brightens the lives of many, many souls, thus giving you the kind of satisfaction that makes our lives worthwhile.

The servants of your servants in Radhadesh, Belgium.

## Seattle

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Today we celebrate the culmination of the special festivities commemorating your 108thappearance anniversary one year ago. What an auspicious occasion this Vyāsa-pūjā is! *Āvirbhava-mahotsava*, the auspicious descent of Śrī Gurudeva!

Since the beginning of the year we have staged several Prabhupāda festivals, beginning with a gala pandal program in Mumbai (Chowpatty) in February. Then, in March, we celebrated in Māyāpur with a fantastic bathing of the Śrī Pañca-tattva Deities and a special Prabhupāda family reunion. We also met in Vṛndāvana. From the lips of godbrothers and -sisters we heard hundreds of testimonials about your glorious activities. Some disciples spoke at length about their initial meetings with you; some spoke about your worldwide preaching prowess; some spoke about your frequent-flyer pastimes; some spoke about you in Russia or Africa; and some spoke about your passing away in Vṛndāvana. Upon hearing these testimonials, sometimes we laughed, sometimes we cried, and sometimes we were stunned into silence.

Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda! How charmingly, mysteriously, and indeed pedagogically you reciprocated with each and every one of your disciples. How you personally greeted us, smiled at us, spoke appropriate words, glanced at us, or even sometimes ignored us. Like a great statesman, a great yogi, a great seminal prophet, a great physician, a great professor, and a great general, you led your people. And just as a great man knows how to reciprocate properly with higher-ups, equals, and underlings, you also expertly dealt with everyone around you. You saw us as we were, or, as many godbrothers and godsisters intimated, you saw right through us. But how you saw us — or indeed even now see us — very much depends on how we saw you then and see you now! Kṛṣṇa says in the *Gītā,* “As they surrender unto Me, I reward them accordingly.” Similarly, as we surrender unto you, you also reward us accordingly. And how we see you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, depends very much on how we hear you. “Seeing through the ear” is your dictum.

At the Mumbai celebration, an enormous photo exhibit featuring well over a hundred temples was staged in the main exhibition arena at Cross Maidan. Meant to stimulate interest in the current ISKCON projects worldwide, the theme of the exhibit was “What are we doing worldwide for Śrīla Prabhupāda in the first decade of the twenty-first century?” Among temples we saw various buildings ranging from traditional Vedic-style *mandiras* to converted churches, old dance halls, Victorian mansions, Bavarian castles, *sādhu’s* huts, and pedestrian addresses in urban row houses. Small snapshots blown up to gigantic proportions featured people larger than life, and the glowing Kodachrome hues suggested an unearthly vibrancy. Attended by tens of thousands of attentive visitors, it was an immensely successful exhibit. And what was the most impressive feature of the show? Was it the Fisher Mansion in Detroit? Florence’s Villa Vṛndāvana? The Palace of Gold in West Virginia or the exotic farms in South America? In fact, neither a fancy villa on the Cote d’Azure nor a bohemian loft in Paris nor a castle in France can ever suggest per se the treasure and spiritual ambiance of the happy devotee inhabitants of such quarters. Looking at the photo exhibit again, the gopi-dotted children running on the lawns of the Bhaktivedanta Manor appeared like cherubs in a Vedic play! The temple devotees in Hare Krishna Land performing common services such as cooking, sewing, or shopping for vegetables appeared like blissful servants of a benign king. The traditionally-clad *brahmacārīs* dancing in the streets of Heidelberg appeared like celestials celebrating the arrival of gods and goddesses. The householders of New Goloka’s Hillsborough farm glowed in the performance of their daily duties. The *sannyāsīs* in Poland appeared like aristocratic envoys from a medieval Indian empery. The ladies worldwide appeared chaste and virtuous. The young girls universally appeared to inspire an entire army of spiritual welfare workers, and the young boys looked like sacred heroes from a “Thousand and One Nights” in Goloka Vṛndāvana. Even the guests visiting our centers appeared bright, effulgent, and confident in their newly-found environment of Vaikuṇṭha Land. Significantly, the cows and calves and other animals featured in the exhibit were blissfully portrayed as citizens of a propitious empire.

We did not see any pictures of you in any of the traditional poses so dear to us: leaning on your cane and looking out over the green fields of New Vṛndāvana; sitting on a *vyāsāsana* in any of the hundred ISKCON centers worldwide that existed during your lifetime; or sitting on a simple muslin-covered pillow and bolster, against a wall, looking pensive, stern, amused, or simply incomprehensibly ecstatic. But in the thousands of square feet of printed celluloid illustrating contemporary ISKCON projects and events, *you* were shining there above all! The smiling faces of our children simply reflect the efficacy of Kṛṣṇa conscious child-raising methodologies, which you taught divinely ad hoc to neophyte teachers. The faces of the temple devotees reflect the beauty of your instruction as per working for the Lord. The *brahmacārīs* reflect the moon of chastity and discipline that you instilled. The *sannyāsīs* reflect the wisdom of your teachings and the joy of sharing love of God. The householders reflect the freedom and responsibility of maintaining Kṛṣṇa conscious families. The chaste ladies reflect the splendor, purity, and submission of Gāndhārī, Sāvitrī or Anasūyā, and the guests reflect the newfound wonder amidst your family of effulgent devotees. Cows, calves, and other creatures simply reflect the security of living in a violence-free environment of universal brotherhood that you taught as an adjunct to love of God.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the communities you inspired sparkle like gems in the ISKCON chain of good fortune. Blissful generations of the young and old are following the *sanātana-dharma* that you taught so expertly to your disciples in the confidence of intimate settings and through the public galleries of your copious literature. Because you most expertly reflect the sun and moon of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi in every facet of your life and teachings, we are now bright-faced recipients of Their mercy. Because of your brilliant example as the **crṝme** de la **crṝme** *paramahaṁsa* in this current age, we have become decent people interested in the laws of love of God. Because of your spotless following of your *gurudeva,* we have been able to accept you as the guiding principle in our lives. Because of your inestimable faith in Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, we believe in and serve the Deity. Simply by hearing from you we are seeing you in every facet of our lives. Simply by listening to you we are achieving your *darśana*. Again, how do we see you? Through the submissive ear of hearing your *kathā*. Through the submissive ear of following the *paramparā*. Through the submissive ear of listening to the heart of hearts, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* via your Bhaktivedanta purports. And foremost by chanting that holy name you gave so liberally.

At the time of this writing your devotee community here in Seattle (Sammamish) is actively engaged in preparing a new temple building for Śrī Śrī RādhāNīla-Mādhava. We hope to commence the actual construction in 2006. Of course, this building is very necessary, as we are currently housed in a very small facility. But, as we mentioned above, just as a castle does not necessarily make a home, a physical temple structure does not necessarily create a spiritual community. You said that devotees can live under a tree. But because you are the community builder for the entire world, you are inspiring us to become transcendentally motivated municipal workers in your metropolis. Because of your divine good will to all peoples of the world, we are learning to work as a spiritual unit. Because of your insistence on spreading the message of Gaurāṅga, *kṛṣṇa-prema,* we are able to lovingly plan and execute a challenging project such as the building of a palace for the Lord of our heart. Because of your boldness in preaching, we are humbly requesting our people to come forward and push Satya-yuga harmony in an age designed for quarrel. You are our goodwill ambassador. You are our architect. You are the designer of our heart. You are the programmer of our future.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda! May you bless our humble endeavors.

Your servants in the Seattle yātrā, Washington, USA.  
(written by Rādhikā-devī dāsī)

## Silicon Valley

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We are here today praying again for your mercy as we celebrate this most auspicious day of your appearance.
