You’ve written in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.7.20:

The path of liberation, as recommended by all authorities, is to serve the *mahātmā* transcendentalists. . . . Even if one has no assets of favorable austerity, if he nevertheless takes shelter of the *mahātmās,* who are engaged in chanting and hearing the glories of the Lord, he is sure to make progress on the path back home, back to Godhead.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest *mahātmā* and transcendentalist. Lord Kṛṣṇa says that a *mahātmā* is one who has taken full shelter of the spiritual energy.

*mahātmānas tu māṁ pārtha daivīṁ prakṛtim āśritāḥ*  
*bhajanty ananya-manaso jñātvā bhūtādim avyayam*

**“O son of Pṛthā, those who are not deluded, the great souls, are under the protection of the divine nature. They are fully engaged in devotional service because they know Me as the Supreme Personality of Godhead, original and inexhaustible” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **9.13)**

Your life follows this description. Taking shelter of your spiritual master’s instructions, you abandoned everything — family, friends, and country. You begged passage on a cargo ship and came to the United States at an advanced age, with no material support, taking sole shelter of Kṛṣṇa.

Another symptom of the *mahātmā* transcendentalist described in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is that he never fears any condition of life:

*nārāyaṇa-parāḥ sarve na kutaścana bibhyati*  
*svargāpavarga-narakeṣv api tulyārtha-darśinaḥ*

**“Devotees solely engaged in the devotional service of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Nārāyaṇa, never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation, and the hellish planets are all the same, for such devotees are interested only in the service of the Lord. (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **6.17.28)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, for preaching you fearlessly moved about the world, accepting residence in dangerous and degraded neighborhoods like New York’s Bowery, or in lavish dwellings like Bhaktivedanta Manor. Similarly, you taught us that any place where pure devotees were chanting Kṛṣṇa’s glories was a favorable place to live. And conversely, you said that even the most opulent palaces were hellish and undesirable if inhabited by those averse to Kṛṣṇa.

Another sign of a *mahātmā* transcendentalist according to Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* is that he is free from the material conception of life. He sees everyone — a *brāhmaṇa,* a cow, an elephant, a dog, and a dog-eater — all with equal vision.

In this regard, you are the greatest transcendentalist. Seeing every living being as part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa, you patiently fanned the spark of Kṛṣṇa consciousness within everyone. You made no material calculation as to who was fit or unfit to receive your inspiration. Out of generosity (another symptom of a *mahātmā*) you patiently took time to explain the basic principles of spiritual life even to the very ignorant.

Once a girl in New York told you that she heard buzzing and saw a red light whenever she chanted. You told her, “Just keep on chanting; it will clear up.”

You painstakingly explained to another youngster that he would be forced to become old and warned that this would be a miserable condition. The boy challenged, “I find it not miserable.” You tolerated his impudence and preached on.

Furthermore, you methodically dismantled the arguments of yogis who claimed that God is impersonal. You spoke to police chiefs, cardinals, reporters, musicians, businessmen, parents, poets, doctors, professors, students, derelicts, and dropouts, giving each the perfect answer, explanation, or insight to bring each one closer to Kṛṣṇa.

How many hundreds and thousands of questions did you answer? How many thousands of letters did you write? How many hundreds of thousands of miles did you travel, all while translating, writing, and publishing volumes of transcendental literature for the welfare of your disciples and the people of the world?

Contemplating the sheer volume of your accomplishments, we must say that you are the greatest of all the *mahātmā* transcendentalists. You are Kṛṣṇa’s empowered agent who has come to this world to widely distribute the mercy of the Lord.

But even beyond these qualities, a *mahātmā* transcendentalist can be identified by his sweetness, purifying quality, and transparency. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* says that in these ways a pure devotee is like water. Flowing water creates an attractive and alluring vibration, it purifies whatever it touches, and it is transparent.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we find all these qualities in you. Your words captivate our minds with their sweetness and clarity, and they purify our desires. And through your books, into which you’ve poured your very essence, you are revealing Kṛṣṇa to millions of readers around the world.

Therefore today and every day we eagerly direct our worship to your safe and sanctifying lotus feet. And we pray — especially on this Vyāsa-pūjā day — for the strength to go on enthusiastically practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness while distributing the holy name, *kṛṣṇa-prasādam,* and your transcendental books.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at ISKCON of Silicon Valley, California, USA.  
(written by Vaiśeṣika dāsa)

## Siṁhācalam (Germany)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

All glories to you and your own beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda!

On your auspicious appearance day, which is bringing about unlimited good fortune for this entire earth planet, we would like to take the opportunity to express our deepest and heartfelt thanks to Your Divine Grace, as well as to provide you with a brief report on your Siṁhācalam farm project in Germany.

Among all historical spiritual personalities we can conceive of, you are by far the most fascinating and extraordinary. No other spiritual teacher — except perhaps for *mahājanas* like Nārada Muni — can be compared to you. Within practically no time — a little more than a decade — you established the spiritually most powerful, authentic, and revolutionary spiritual movement on earth! Your preaching was so pure, so uncompromising, that sometimes even your close followers and disciples were scared by your boldness. You fully lived and preached according to the premises taught by your own wonderful Guru Mahārāja, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Prabhupāda:

1\. Books are the basis.

2\. Purity is the force.

3\. Preaching is the essence.

4\. Utility is the principle.

Without wanting to appear impudent, we sometimes feel like adding a fifth principle to this list:

5\. Devotion to your lotus feet is the key!

To use a rather mundane technological analogy: Most “providers” of God consciousness nowadays, i.e., gurus and other religious proponents, are offering only a “narrowband” connection to God, in exchange for which they are usually demanding lots of money from their followers. But Your Divine Grace — like Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda — is generously and freely offering to each and every person the blissful “broadband” access to Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

Unfortunately, most of us did not meet you personally. But, being members of your spiritual family, we have access to your spiritual legacy in the form of your books, your countless established wonderful temples, farms, and other projects, and association with your faithful disciples. In fact, we eagerly take every opportunity to obtain their consoling association and never get tired of listening to their relishable narrations about the pastimes of Your Divine Grace.

Among all the projects inaugurated by Your Divine Grace, farm communities were given utmost importance, not only in view of possible future world crises, but also due to being the very foundation of brahminical and Vaiṣṇava culture. You conveyed the same message, in a nutshell, in your famous slogan “Simple living, high thinking.”

Your Siṁhācalam farm community currently consists of about twenty permanent residents, mostly *gṛhasthas,* and about sixty regularly donating congregational members. Since last year we have noticed that especially devotee couples are getting increasingly interested in moving their residence to Siṁhācalam, among them mainly *brāhmaṇas* (*pūjārīs* and cooks), but also farmers.

When accepting responsibility for the Siṁhācalam management three years ago, my wife and I had to face vast financial debts from the past. Thus this oldest German ISKCON project (the Deity of Lord Narasiṁhadeva was installed in 1982) was on the verge of being sold. But eventually, by your mercy and with the help of our congregational donors, all those debts have been successfully amortized, and the future looks somewhat bright.

We are now in the process of creating an economic base for our community, carefully considering different plans. In particular, we want to put our energy mainly into the following three projects: (1) Development of our organic agriculture up to the point of self-sufficiency and sale of any surplus; (2) *prasādam* production and distribution in various ways; (3) conducting public classes and seminars on topics related to Kṛṣṇa consciousness and Vedic culture, e.g., *Āyurveda*.

Please let us continuously be engaged in the loving service of the Lord and thus play a useful part in your transcendental mission.

Finally, we want to thank you so much for enabling us to achieve the association of and devotional service to Śrī Śrī Prahlāda–Lakṣmī-Narasiṁhadeva!

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Śrī Śrī Prahlāda–Lakṣmī-Narasiṁhadeva-*kī jaya*!

Your eternally grateful servants at Siṁhācalam, Germany.

## Soweto (South Africa)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace. All glories to Śrī Śrī Nitāi–Gaura-Hari. All glories to Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā. And all glories to Śrī Pañca-tattva.

Each and every year is an opportunity for me to surrender more to your lotus feet. Each and every day is an opportunity for me to serve you more. Each and every minute and second is an opportunity for me to try to be practical in my dealings with your servants and aspiring servants. Today, as I sit here in Soweto, I ponder on what good fortune it is to be part of this wonderful spiritual organization known as ISKCON. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for bringing this movement to us. May your glories and accomplishments be known throughout the world. May your books be the guides for humanity for the rest of Kali-yuga.

Last year I wrote to you expressing a desire to get a vehicle, a pickup van for our activities in Soweto, more specifically for Food for Life. Last year, shortly after writing to you, on 14 May 2004, by the mercy of your beloved servant His Holiness Bhakti Cāru Swami Mahārāja, we were able to get a brand new pickup van and ISUZU KB 250D long-wheelbase bakkie. Since then our activities have strengthened and Food for Life has increased. Now we are feeding six primary schools daily with 1,500 plates of *prasādam* on Tuesdays, Wednesdays, and Thursdays. On Saturdays we sometimes distribute an additional thousand plates to the squatter camps. In all we distribute around 5,500 plates per week. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for responding so quickly in giving us this vehicle. This means a lot to us in Soweto. This has made our preaching in Soweto much simpler and easier.

This ISKCON movement is very personal. The world is full of impersonalism, and you came to destroy just that. You taught us that Kṛṣṇa is a person and that Kṛṣṇa’s eternal associates are also persons, and that if treated as persons they will reciprocate personally. Now I can see that believing in you and following your instructions have strengthened my attachment to your lotus feet. How you reciprocate with certain individuals and guide them in their spiritual lives inspires me and makes me want to surrender.

Please guide me and give me good intelligence so that I may be a worthy servant of your servants and so I may lead those under my care to your lotus feet. Please guide me so that I may be a good example to those I care for in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Sometimes I feel incapable and unqualified to be in charge and strongly feel I should be just a follower. But I guess you have some plan for me, since there is no one to take over the responsibilities. There is so much to be done, so much responsibility, and this makes me feel unqualified. But with you and my Guru Mahārāja at my side, I feel I can go on.

This year we are about to start building a temple for the pleasure of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Mahārāṇī, Gaura-Nitāi, and Your Divine Grace. Please guide us and inspire us to achieve this. By your mercy everything is possible.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, once more for being there for us through your *vāṇī* (instructions). Thank you for everything you have done for us. Thank you for giving us the three main programs of ISKCON, namely, book distribution, *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and Food for Life. Thank you for building a house in which the whole world can live peacefully — ISKCON.

May your glories and accomplishments be known throughout the three worlds. May your books be the guides for the next ten thousand years. Thank you, thank you, thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

The aspiring servants of your servants at ISKCON Soweto, South Africa.  
(written by Mahāprabhu dāsa)

## Stockholm

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances.

All glories to Your Divine Grace on this auspicious occasion of the 2005 celebration of your transcendental appearance day. As ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya* and our pre-eminent *śikṣā-guru* and (for some) *dīkṣā-guru,* you will permanently receive honor from us as our main connection to the Brahma-Madhva-Gauḍīya-sampradāya. Your accomplishment in spreading the *saṅkīrtana* movement is unparalleled. Our allegiance to you is our only authorization to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Everything is in your books. If something is not in your books, it is worth knowing only in connection with your teachings. It is in our self-interest to know your message, limitless as it is, to the best of our carefully considered understanding. Our guideline is the particular trademark that you stamped on the message of Lord Caitanya and the previous *ācāryas*. The association of your followers in ISKCON under the GBC is our real home.

You kindly visited Stockholm in September 1973 for one week. The first Swedish temple had recently been acquired at that time, and you were pleased to preside over the new *yātrā*here in this northernmost region. You lectured at the temple from the Fifth Canto of the *Bhāgavatam*: “All these ‘isms’ are sense gratification”; “Modern leaders will force you to work like dogs and hogs and asses”; “Liberation: service to great souls”; and more. You also gave a wonderful lecture on the meaning of the *Gurv-aṣṭaka* prayers. You had many outside engagements as well. For example, you spoke to an auditorium of students at Uppsala University. At that time the Swedes were very politically oriented to protesting the Vietnam War. Knowing that, you challengingly launched into a description of the four divisions of society. Some of the audience misunderstood you, but the devotees clearly heard that you wanted to present Kṛṣṇa consciousness purely, without compromise. You showed us how to depend on Kṛṣṇa and be fearless in serving His order to explain the most confidential knowledge to the devotees.

ISKCON Stockholm has progressed steadily in spite of many setbacks over the last thirty-two years. This year we installed *vijaya-mūrtis* of the Pañca-tattva. We keep up an on-going development of our understanding of *guru-tattva,* and quite a few of the congregation are presently awakening their faith to the point where they are prepared to take the step of initiation from some of your disciples who serve in that capacity. Govinda’s Restaurant continues as one of the oldest and most popular vegetarian restaurants in the city. Daily morning and evening programs, the Sunday Feast, theatrical performances — all are steadily maintained. School groups come regularly, sometimes from distant places. For years Tapasya Prabhu has been cultivating the academic community, and at the moment he is awaiting response in the magazine of the national teacher’s association to his recent article challenging the assumptions of a self-appointed “cult expert” concerning your movement. The *gṛhastha* families are growing and appear peacefully but firmly committed to the responsibility of their ashram, despite the unfavorable climate in society at large. At festival times we see so many visiting devotees that we are wonder where they all come from. We are, however, confident that you are keeping track and taking responsibility for us all in accordance with our various degrees of surrender.

We are convinced that you have created our good fortune despite tsunamis and imminent catastrophes. We are eternally indebted to you. Our hearts belong to you, and we are proving it by cooperating to push on together.

Please bless us: let our intelligence be conducted spontaneously by your desire and pleasure. That will be our perfection.

The servants of Śrīla Prabhupāda and Gaura-Nitāi at ISKCON Stockholm, Sweden.

## Sydney

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Since your departure from this mortal plane, ISKCON has struggled on many levels. Through numerous adversities, setbacks, and challenges we have been forced to grow up, to learn much. Unfortunately, in exchange for the experience we have also lost much. We have lost many of our leaders, many of our devotees, on certain levels much of our self-belief, and, for better or worse, a great deal of our former individual and collective innocence.

You left when we were very young. Even materially we were extremely inexperienced, and spiritually we were literally babes in the woods. Although we must accept everything as part of Lord Caitanya’s transcendental plan, the last twenty-eight years have been very difficult. Nevertheless, the legacy of your example and the ideals of Kṛṣṇa consciousness you imparted have sustained us throughout, and your mission and organization, if somewhat bruised and battered, remains intact; the future seems positive, our mood ever optimistic.

Still, as a Society we miss the complete integrity of your character and your vast spiritual wisdom. As an organization we lament the absence of your empowered leadership and your dynamic spiritual vision. As an individual we miss having your hand to hold and your smile to look forward too. So we remain, caught between our lament for times past, our discontent with the present, and our mixed sentiments regarding the future. On reflection, it may sound much like any normal life, yet the singular difference, of course, remains you, the great transcendental pivot of our lives, our eternal spiritual master, our father, our founder-*ācārya,* our Śrīla Prabhupāda.

On this anniversary of your appearance we ask you to please keep us close to you, please go on sustaining us always, and please never let us go. We have literally invested our whole life in you; please bless, please protect, and please uplift us with your nectarean vision.

Your humble servants at ISKCON Sydney, Australia.

## Togo (West Africa)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please kindly accept my prostrated obeisance at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya, which is like a benedictory moon bringing relief to our afflicted souls.

Lord Kṛṣṇa states in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (4.9):

*janma karma ca me divyam evaṁ yo vetti tattvataḥ*  
*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ‘rjuna*

**“One who knows the transcendental nature of My appearance and activities does not, upon leaving the body, take his birth again in this material world, but attains My eternal abode, O Arjuna.”**

The transcendental nature of the appearance of Lord Kṛṣṇa and that of His pure devotees is indeed *divyam*. One whose vision of Kṛṣṇa is limited to a particular form cannot really understand the truth about His appearance. Although there are many transcendental forms of the Lord, they are still one and the same. In the same way, one has to understand that same truth about the appearance of the guru.

The guru appears in order to teach the transcendental message of Godhead according to time, place, and culture. Thus the guru also comes in different forms, and yet they are still the same representative of the Supreme Person. It is certain that one who understands this *guru-tattva* will never be bereft of *guru-bhakti* and -*śakti,* though his guru may no longer be present in his physical form.

Why am I reflecting on this issue of *guru-tattva* on this day of your appearance? Because day by day the movement that Your Divine Grace left behind is getting older, and so are your disciples who have worked so hard to continue your legacy. It was just last year that the devotee community mourned the glorious passing away of one of your illustrious sons, His Holiness Śrīdhara Mahārāja, in Māyāpur. Just barely a year later, we are again in the throes of losing yet another one of your fearless, dynamic, and veteran generals, His Holiness Bhakti Tīrtha Swami.

Reflecting on what to do when one loses his spiritual master, I realize that the first thing is to be able to see Śrī Gurudeva in everyone. This is like seeing Kṛṣṇa in everything. What is the result of such equal vision? Lord Kṛṣṇa Himself declares (*Bhagavad-gītā* 4.35):

*yaj jñātvā na punar moham evaṁ yāsyasi pāṇḍava*  
*yena bhūtāny aśeṣāṇi drakṣyasy ātmany atho mayi*

**“Having obtained real knowledge from a self-realized soul, you will never fall again into such illusion, for by this knowledge you will see that all living beings are but part of the Supreme, or, in other words, that they are Mine.”**

One has to understand this fact with conviction, although it is incomprehensible to mundane scholars and empiric philosophers. As stated in the *Vedas* (*Puruṣa-bodhinī Upaniṣad*):

*eko devo nitya-līlānurakto*  
*bhakta-vyāpī hṛdy antar-ātmā*

**“The one Supreme Personality of Godhead is eternally engaged in many, many transcendental forms in relationships with His unalloyed devotees.”**

Only with such vision can we continue in devotional service with just the same spirit of surrender and unalloyed devotion with which we served the previous *ācāryas*. Your true disciples, irrespective of their position in the movement, are those who continue to serve ISKCON as your body, and who are prepared to render service to anyone who identifies positively with your legacy in the form of book distribution, Food for Life, college preaching, Nāma-haṭṭa, etc.

Without such vision we run the risk of getting fragmented by sectarian views, bigotry, and other forms of segregation, which are symptoms of the Age of Quarrel, Kali-yuga.

Our prayer unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this day of your appearance is that you infuse us with this understanding that guru is one, that the *śakti* of the whole Vaiṣṇava *paramparā* is with whoever purely represents the teachings of Lord Kṛṣṇa in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

Though our link with you and Kṛṣṇa, His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, has been called to join you in your eternal *līlā,* we nevertheless want to continue with the same inspiration, enthusiasm, and determination we had when he was still physically present. We pray that we can continue to serve ISKCON in the same spirit with which His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami served until his last breath.

It is true that one measure of a great personality is the quality of his followers. The greatness of your disciples, our stalwart leaders and gurus — His Holiness Tamāl Kṛṣṇa Goswami, His Holiness Śrīdhara Mahārāja, His Holiness Bhakti-tīrtha Swami, and many others who are either still serving or have been called to join you — is proof of your touchstone qualities. You have transformed into rare gems many souls who came in contact with you. We pray that we can also become living proofs of your greatness, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

At present your glory continues to spread in Africa, as thousands of souls are receiving your mercy daily in the forms of *prasādam* distribution (especially Food for Life), *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and book distribution. In Togo, where we have just launched Food for Life, people are suddenly expressing such untold happiness and appreciating the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Everyone participating in this service — the donors, the volunteer cooks, the servers, and the consumers of the *prasādam* — are all experiencing bliss. It is just indescribable how all the programs you established are helping conditioned souls to once again discover their link with the infinite source of bliss, Svayaṁ Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your granddisciples and Food for Life donors in Togo, West Africa.  
(written by Varāha dāsa)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances on this most auspicious day of your appearance.

Today we remember your determination in establishing this great movement for reviving the lost consciousness of the conditioned souls in this material world. Your ISKCON was started with only your books and the holy name. When you landed in New York, you had no money except for 40 Indian rupees (about $5 then) and the $20 you had received for a set of your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatams* from Captain Pandia of the *Jaladuta*. How fortunate he was to be the first recipient (outside India) of your powerful message! (We wonder where those 200 sets of books are now?)

You entered America and stayed in Butler, Pennsylvania, with the Agarwal family for awhile. Soon you went to New York and started to preach the *Bhagavad-gītā,* the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Gradually people started to join you. What great determination you had! You didn’t change the message. You had a great vision — a worldwide society of devotees joined together by the chanting of *harināma-saṅkīrtana*. And what great success you achieved in such a short time! We pray that your pastimes will always remain fresh in our minds.

Since 14th November 1977 many things have happened, and many devotees have come and gone, but your position as founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON will remain eternally fixed. By remembering your pastimes and instructions, we can become successful and rejoin you and Kṛṣṇa in Goloka Vṛndāvana.

Your servants in Tokyo, Japan.

## Tyumen (Russia)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance please accept my humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

I am happy to get the opportunity to express my gratitude to you. Your transcendental movement is spreading all over the world and inspiring everyone. Your devotees are becoming more serious and responsible preachers. Intelligent people are becoming interested in the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness because they see that it can solve their personal problems as well as those of the whole civilization. You have unlimited compassion for the fallen souls. Your sincere followers spread transcendental knowledge to the most remote and dark regions of the planet. By the wonderful service of your sincere servants, especially my beloved Guru Mahārāja, Jayapatāka Swami, the mercy of the Pañca-tattva is pouring forth from the Māyāpur Candrodaya Mandir. With the installation of the Pañca-tattva your movement has gotten new strength and inspiration, and many new people are being attracted all over the world.

Here in Tyumen — “Prabhupāda-deśa — a new president has taken responsibility for the Vaiṣṇava community. Senior devotees are concentrating their attention on service to your mission. Some of them visited Māyāpur during the Gaura-pūrṇimā festival, where they prayed, “Dear Lord! Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda! Please give us the mercy of service unto you! What can we do for your satisfaction?” Realizing the secrets of real renunciation, your followers take active social positions in society to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As a result, intelligent people — students, teachers at the university, and other leaders of Tyumen society — are becoming attracted to the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your instructions give us unlimited opportunity to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, to give the holy name practically to everyone. Vaiṣṇava relations become stronger, the spirit of service is higher. Following your instructions, the devotees get more taste from the service. We nicely associate at *bhakti-vṛkṣa* meetings (we have four groups here in Tyumen). In the temple of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi the devotees attend *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class every morning. We edit the community newspaper, and it inspires our Vaiṣṇava relations. We all accept the good fortune of following your instructions, the good fortune to be your followers and get the opportunity to help you in your service to Lord Caitanya. We have firm faith in your instructions, and we try to deepen our connection with you. We are thankful to you for your mercy. We are made of your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we depend completely on your mercy.

On this most auspicious day of your appearance we beg you, Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please accept us as your servants and let us be useful to you as you want!

The devotees in Tyumen, Russia.  
(written by Vasusreṣṭha dāsa)

## Udaipur (India)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

This year you have revealed more mercy to us, mercy that is always available by reading your books. In India many false *sādhus* are increasing their followers by impersonal philosophy. This age is very dark. In the Eleventh Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* the sages praise this Kali Age because in this age, just by chanting the holy name of the Lord, one can gain liberation from birth and death and go back to the spiritual world.

Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda constantly danced in ecstasy while vibrating the holy name. You are so affectionate to the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa that since time immemorial you have been giving Kṛṣṇa pleasure. You are so dear to the Lord on this planet — *kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale —* that you came to this world to deliver everyone. Demigods praise your glories, witnessing your pure *bhakti* for the Lord. Your are the greatest teacher of pure *bhakti-yoga*. The results of your preaching will last forever. The fortunate souls who have come in touch with your glorious ISKCON movement are deeply indebted to you because out of your great mercy and concern for them you delivered pure *bhakti*. The solution to all problems in spiritual life is to distribute your transcendental books throughout one’s life. You are so expert in preaching that you are converting the most difficult cases in human society. In Tripura state there is so much need to spread your glories and the elevated sacred scriptures you translated. Then all these miserable materialists can be turned into jewel-like devotees and serve your most valuable lotus feet. All glories to your voice, to your intelligence, and to your miraculous preaching of the essence of all the sacred scriptures!

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless this most fallen and stubborn disciple of yours by giving him *yogaḥ karmasu kauśalam,* the skill in distributing more and more of your priceless and most elevated books.

ISKCON BBT founder-*ācārya* Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!!

All glories unto you! We eternally wish to become more dear to you and your sincere followers, who eternally serve your sweet lotus feet.

The devotees in Udaipur, India.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Urbana (Illinois)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda

“A” is for the *ācārya* extraordinaire  
that he was, is, and shall always be.  
“B” is for his Bhaktivedanta purports,  
which empower the world to see.  
“C” is for his compassion,  
unbounded by time or space.  
“D” is for his devotion,  
which is certainly not commonplace.  
“E” is for Caitanya’s emissary  
cloaked in human dress.  
“F” is for the fortune he bestows  
upon man and even less.  
“G” is for his generosity  
in booking us a ticket home.  
“H” is for his humility,  
which melts our hearts of stone.  
“I” is for his ingenuity  
in preaching to modern man.  
“J” is for the joy he spreads  
as he travels from land to land.  
“K” is for the knowledge  
with which he opens our eyes.  
“L” is for his love,  
which pleasantly takes us by surprise.  
“M” is for his mercy,  
more abundant than autumnal rains.  
“N” is for the nectar he injects  
into our dull and lifeless brains.  
“O” is for the opportunity  
to serve his lotus feet.  
“P” is for his preaching work,  
with which no one can compete.  
“Q” is for his qualities,  
brighter than any flame.  
“R” is for his renunciation  
of any claim to fame.  
“S” is for the sacrifices he makes  
so that we may live with God.  
“T” is for his tolerance,  
unwavering despite all odds.  
“U” is for his uniqueness;  
indeed he can be replaced by none.  
“V” is for his victory –  
surely he’s a ray of the Kṛṣṇa sun.  
“W” is for his wisdom and wit,  
by which he defeats false claims.  
“X” is for the x-stasy he feels  
as he chants the holy names.  
“Y” is for the yearning he has  
for us to heed Caitanya’s call.  
And “Z” is for his eternal zeal  
to save us — one and all.

Humbly submitted as an offering to His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda by His disciples and followers at the Krishna Center in Urbana, Illinois, USA.  
(written by Dānakeli-devī dāsī)

## Vancouver

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we, your followers in New Gokula-dhāma, Vancouver, wish to offer you our very best heartfelt appreciations on this happy occasion of the anniversary of your sacred appearance in this world.

As children of Kali-yuga, we possess so many faults — too innumerable for us to even attempt to imagine, let alone overcome. Yet, by your mercy and your example we now have hope that we may surmount them, and for this hope alone we are truly indebted to Your Divine Grace. Fresh from the land of the *dvipada-paśus,* we have crawled to find your lotus feet. So humbly you came among us, teaching the ancient path of perfection, and still we seem to lack the ability to follow. With what little sincerity we can muster we must now cry out to you, knowing that you, in your patience, will see in our meager effort the first steps of a spiritual infant. We have heard it said, “By the grace of Kṛṣṇa one gets a bona fide guru, and by the grace of guru one gets Kṛṣṇa.” Again, your mercy is ever present; we need only become fit receptacles to receive it. Let it be so.

The devotees at ISKCON Vancouver, Canada.  
(written by Rohita dāsa)

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa (Italy)

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, which are the source of unlimited mercy. I put my head under those lotus feet and pray with all my heart to always be a worthy receptacle of that mercy.

Whenever I am asked to write a letter in praise of my spiritual master, mixed sentiments of honor and pleasure, shyness and incompetence, overcome my mind. Every year, as soon as a new Vyāsa-pūjā book is distributed in our temples and I am finally able to get hold of a copy, I eagerly read the offerings of your dear disciples, and I am amazed at their beautiful expressions of spiritual realizations and deep feelings for Your Divine Grace. Will I also be able to disclose some meaningful thoughts for my dear father and master and, after getting rid of my immature mentality, squeeze out something fertile from my barren heart and mind?

Once more, as I sit in front of a white page, I am forced to take stock of the year that has just passed. In New York in April of 1966 you said, “If you are steady either in success or in failure, that is called yoga. Don’t mind for the success or failure. Do act on behalf of the Supreme Lord. And if you are steady in that position, then your working in spiritual platform is successful.” Actually, what I have been trying to do year after year is to offer you my desire to do my duty with your instructions always present in my mind, and sometimes I am meeting with success and sometimes failing, but since you are still keeping me in your service, I guess you graciously accept the very few good things I am doing by your mercy and overlook the many wrong ones. Therefore with pleasure I would like to inform you that this year we have completed the translation of all the volumes of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in the Italian language, and the Twelfth Canto is going to the printer soon after I write this homage. Everyone can remember how excited you were whenever a book was published in any language in any part of the world, and how a broad smile would illuminate your face when a new publication was offered to you. I would also like to present to your attention another publication that came out this year: the *Darśana* book, a prestigious attempt to present a photo-anthology of the Deities that you personally installed all over the world and that were so much dear to you, together with those Deities installed after your departure.

I have no merit in these achievements, and without the association and help of your faithful disciples I would feel lost and empty. Your mercy is the key that opens all my files. Without it the spiritual domain would be impenetrable, but with your mercy even a person as engrossed as I am in material attachments can appreciate the beauty of the Deities and Their opulent worship. Similarly, the words of the *Bhāgavatam* are far beyond my comprehension, but by your mercy I feel delighted when I hear them. I am lazy and weak, but one statement I can honestly and boldly make without a doubt: A crumb of your mercy has touched me and is keeping me alive, infusing me with the necessary strength to go on with my duty, with the determination to be part of your mission, with the courage to overcome inevitable difficulties, and with the hope to make it in this lifetime.

Like that grain of rice thrown by Lord Nityānanda that made Advaita Ācārya dance in ecstasy, a crumb of your mercy is making me dance in *kīrtana,* is making me chant sixteen rounds every day, is making me enjoy the company of your devotees, is making me follow the regulative principles, and is making me eager to hear transcendental sounds. Isn’t that astonishing? Isn’t that a miracle? Gradually I am getting cured of the worst sickness, the material disease, simply by your words full of love. In fact, you had so much fatherly affection for each one of us, your sons and daughters, that you gave us the proper treatment to facilitate our complete recovery. Without ever forcing anyone, you suggested we apply our nature, talents, and temperament in Kṛṣṇa’s service. You didn’t impose anything but just invited us to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and offer to the Lord whatever was in our capacities. You appreciated every little effort we made to serve you, and you enjoyed accompanying us in our clumsy attempts to follow you. You were always ready to direct, correct, and sustain us when we tripped, fell, or went astray. Like a father, you were always present, forgiving our endless mistakes and encouraging our flickering enthusiasm, always ready to water our tender devotional creeper with your nectarean association and pleased to see it growing day after day into a stronger plant.

Personally, on many occasions I felt your supporting hand sustaining me and taking me across tumultuous times. I never felt alone, never thought, “It’s too difficult; I must quit and go where it’s easer.” No, I could never give up your merciful hand, not even for a moment, because you saved my life from suicide, you saved my heart from drawing into futilities, you saved my mind from complete chaos, you saved my sight from total darkness, you saved my speech from inconsistency, and above all you saved my human time. Therefore my life, heart, mind, speech, sight, and time belong to you, my dear father and master, and I surrender them at your lotus feet. Please continue to engage me fruitfully in your service and make this weak daughter of yours a worthy recipient of your mercy.

The devotees who are faithfully serving Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission at Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Ramaṇa Temple and Govinda’s at Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Milan, Italy.  
(written by Āli-Kṛṣṇa-devī dāsī)

## Vṛndāvana

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When Śrī Kṛṣṇa sent you to this material world,  
He placed you in a family of devotees.  
As a child you imitated the worship of the Deities,  
And your father spent lavishly to distribute *prasādam* for your festivals.  
This preliminary foundation of devotion  
Was built upon by Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.  
He drew from you service sublime.  
You accomplished what no other disciple could achieve.  
You understood your spiritual master’s inner desire  
To see Lord Caitanya’s mission spread worldwide.  
Like the sparrow and the ocean, you succeeded in  
Your mission against insurmountable odds.  
Your success is incomparable and unfathomable,  
Ever-expanding with magnitude immeasurable.  
You masterminded a spiritual revolution  
That has reached to every corner of the planet.

A powerful devotee of the Lord is, by the grace of the Lord, more than the Lord Himself. . . . The Lord is so merciful upon His pure devotee that He presents His beloved devotee as more powerful than Himself. . . . Therefore, not only is a devotee of the Lord more powerful than the Lord, but also worship of the devotee is considered more effective than direct worship of the Lord (*mad-bhakta-pūjābhyadhikā*). \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 2.4.10, purport\]

The father likes to see the son become more famous than himself. The Lord declares very emphatically that worship of His devotee is more valuable than the worship of the Lord Himself. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.9.25, purport\]

On this most auspicious day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we worship you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, offering to you a glimpse of your own transcendental prowess. Commemorating the thirtieth anniversary of the grand opening of Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarām Mandir, we honor you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with thirty of your principal transcendental achievements.

1\. You brought Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the West.

2\. You uplifted unlimited fallen souls and brought them to Kṛṣṇa.

3\. You established temples in every major city of the world.

4\. You published over sixty books on Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

5\. You initiated approximately five thousand disciples.

6\. You instituted formal Deity worship worldwide.

7\. You created and trained qualified *brāhmaṇa* Vaiṣṇavas.

8\. You traveled and preached throughout the world thirteen times.

9\. You spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in more than fifty languages.

10\. You inspired your disciples to risk their lives for preaching.

11\. You established the Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā worldwide.

12\. You established traditional *gurukulas* to train the youth.

13\. You established massive *prasādam* distribution for spiritual welfare.

14\. You established *varṇāśrama* communities and farms.

15\. You popularized cow protection and opened *gośālās* worldwide.

16\. You brought the world *saṅkīrtana* movement back to India.

17\. You revitalized India’s dormant Vedic culture.

18\. You established major international centers in India.

19\. You established annual pilgrimages to Māyāpur and Vṛndāvana.

20\. You sent your disciples on secret missions to preach.

21\. You convinced the scientific community of the existence of God.

22\. You replaced material communism with spiritual communism.

23\. You succeeded in bringing *harināma* to every town and village.

24\. You fulfilled Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s prophecy of an *adbhuta* Māyāpur temple.

25\. You fulfilled Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura order beyond all expectations.

26\. You fulfilled your promise to build a temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Rāsabihārī.

27\. You fulfilled your desire to build a Kṛṣṇa-Balarām temple in Vṛndāvana.

28\. You preached Kṛṣṇa consciousness up to your final breath.

29\. You attained sublime *samādhi* in the holy land of Vṛndāvana.

30\. Your fame continues to expand throughout the world.

As fire is kindled from wood by another fire, the divine consciousness of man can similarly be kindled by another divine grace. His Divine Grace the spiritual master can kindle the spiritual fire from the wood-like living entity by imparting proper spiritual messages injected through the receptive ear. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.32, purport\]

We offer our insignificant lives at your lotus feet. We pray for your divine grace in our lives. Please allow us to assist you in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Though we have no qualification, our lives have become glorious by contact with your lotus feet. You have kindled the spiritual fire of devotional service in our hearts through your divine instructions. We dedicate our lives to your instructions and pray for your continued guidance eternally.

Your servants at ISKCON Vṛndāvana, India.

## Wiesbaden

Dear A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda, founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON,

Please accept our most humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have built a house where everybody can live. Please let us understand your greatness. You are the well-wisher and savior of the world, for you have given us the holy name: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

Thank you for letting us take part in your movement. Please help us become purified so we can bring as many people as possible into contact with you. May we lose the taste for this material world. May we become real devotees and never forget you. May their Lordships eternally be the center of our lives. Please engage us in devotional service and let us taste the eternal ocean of *bhakti* and love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. May we become humble and be able to cry out “Hare Kṛṣṇa!” as a small child cries out for help.

Your example and shelter give us hope that we will one day be able to understand the spiritual world. Please grant us your divine vision.

Please take us back home, back to Godhead, and do whatever you like with us.

Your humble servants at the ISKCON community in Wiesbaden, Germany.

## Yogyakarta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. On this very auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please allow us, the devotees at Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Ashram in Yogyakarta, Indonesia, to again and again offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. We are very insignificant, but due to your causeless mercy we have been given a chance to take part in your mission to spread the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa.

You often instructed us that the youth, in particular university students, would be the best recipients of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy. Yogyakarta is well-known as “the student city” of Indonesia because it is a learning center with more than one hundred colleges and universities spread all over the city. Students from all parts of Indonesia come here to get a good education. In addition, Yogyakarta is also well-known as a cultural center where many features of the ancient Vedic tradition and culture are still being practiced by most of the native people. This is true of the Javanese people in general. The world-famous ancient Hindu temple Prambanan, with its historical *Rāmāyaṇa* story engraved in the temple mural, is situated very close to Yogyakarta. It has become an icon for the wonderful architectural Hindu achievements of the past.

Such an ideal situation inspires us to slowly and carefully introduce the Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy to the student academic community. Under the guidance of His Holiness Bhakti Rāghava Swami and Śrīmān Gaura Maṇḍala Bhūmi Prabhu, as well as other senior devotees, we have established a Kṛṣṇa conscious student hostel that serves as both an ashram and a temple. Now we have a separate hostel for male and female students, a prayer hall with Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi as our main Deities, a *kuṭira* for visiting *sannyāsīs,* a Bhaktivedanta Library and Tape Ministry, and other facilities. Because most of us are full-time students, we have a condensed morning and evening program that we follow every day. We chose the name Nārāyaṇa Smṛti Ashram, which in essence has the same meaning as Kṛṣṇa Conscious Ashram.

By your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, more and more students are gradually becoming interested in our philosophy and way of life. Please give us the intelligence to know how keep this seed of *bhakti* growing stronger and stronger in their hearts, so that they may take up the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness more seriously.

In addition to the student hostel facilities, we are also developing a small farm, named Surabhi Sevā Kuñja, located about fifteen kilometers north of Yogyakarta. We now have two cows with three full-time *brahmacārīs* staying on the farm. Of course, we still have so many things to learn about how to cultivate the land so that we will gradually develop self-sufficiency.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is all due to your mercy that gradually things are developing here in Yogyakarta. Please always inspire us to continuously engage in your service by trying our best to spread the mission of Lord Caitanya, distributing the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa to all the people of Indonesia.

Your humble servants in Yogyakarta, Indonesia.  
(written by Sūrya Locana dāsa)

## Zurich

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to the dust of your lotus feet!

We are very happy to have a grandfather like you. We are also very happy to announce to you that this year we will install a *cakra* on the roof of your temple in **Zūrich**. By your blessings this project will be successful and the *cakra* will protect your temple from all kinds of inauspiciousness. With united energies the *cakra* was manufactured, and with united energies we will be able to present it at the end of May to all the inhabitants of **Zūrich**. It will give a sign to the people of **Zūrich** that this house belongs to Kṛṣṇa and his servants.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we really want to ask your blessings so that we will be able to carry on your mission here in Switzerland. We went through many ups and downs, and we hope that in the future we can steadily continue to serve your divine instructions.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, always be our guide so that we can be instruments in your merciful hands. Please let us always remember how many difficulties you went through to spread the mission of Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu, so that we will be able to face the difficulties that are surely coming and unite at the shelter of your lotus feet.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are very much unqualified, but we hope that with your blessings we can carry on and please you and all the devotees.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your servants at ISKCON **Zurich**, Switzerland.

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Bhava-roga or Bhavauṣadhi  
Life just seems so hard  
From attachment that has soured.  
Old age is approaching like a railroad train.  
Acts we were so sure  
Were the prescription for the cure —  
Diseased in matter, where’s the gain?  
Where you’ve gone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, take me with you please,  
To the place beyond birth, death, old age, and disease.  
Wasted life that was spent  
On this body’s monthly rent,  
With the eviction notice clearly on the wall.  
Back in the womb we go  
For another round of *bhava-rog,*  
Caught in the repetition like a bouncing ball.  
Where you’ve gone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, take me with you please,  
To the place beyond birth, death, old age, and disease.  
Your books are here for all to hear,  
Your purports the ecstasy we all hold dear.  
*Śravaṇam* is the start of spiritual life.  
*Kṛṣṇa-kīrtan* emanates from your lips,  
Dissolving *kāma, krodha,* and all the six whips,  
Nectar so thick you can cut it with a knife.  
Where you’ve gone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, take me with you please,  
To where *śravaṇam* and *kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ*never cease.  
Just let us remember your words sublime,  
Your service, your pastimes beyond space and time,  
Fulfilling the craving from life after life.  
The *bhavauṣadhi* is free to take,  
Medicine for all good souls who want to wake  
From the slumber that creates only pain and strife.  
Where you’ve gone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, take me with you please,  
To where *śravaṇam* and *kīrtanaṁ viṣṇoḥ*never cease.

Your servants at the North American division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Sura dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

It is the inherent characteristic of the common man to habitually strive for personal advantages over his fellow men. As such, it is rare to find a person who would readily share with others what he has, without any ulterior motive. Yet you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, openly reveal the highest truths in your books for everyone's genuine welfare. This is remarkable.

We see that learned people often speak a language that pleases them (and if one doesn’t understand their language, it makes them feel even better). But the style of writing we find in your books is very clear and straightforward. It is not an academic scholarly language, it is not Gauḍīya-Vaiṣṇava Sanskrit punditry, it is not the *rāgānuga* flowery style, and it is not any obscure Vedic idiom. Without any of these, you just speak the language everyone can understand. This shows your greatness.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, today, on the anniversary of your appearance, we are worshiping you directly. We hope that you are pleased by our effort. But you also have taught us that the spiritual master becomes even more pleased when he sees that his disciples have understood his mission and are sincerely trying to execute it. One of your greatest missions is the publishing of the books you have translated and commentated. We are dedicated and pleased to assist in the publishing of your books, which are works of true compassion and generosity for the whole of mankind.

As Ravīndra Svarūpa Prabhu has told us, the memo Śyāmasundara Prabhu sent out in 1971 included your now-famous summary of ISKCON: “Books are the basis; purity is the force; preaching is the essence; utility is the principle.” You had then remarked, “I am one person, and see what I have done. Now we are five hundred; each one of you become just like me and imagine what could happen.”

Of course, we can never become like you. But from your statement we can understand your mission in its proper context. You want us to become Kṛṣṇa conscious ourselves by strictly following the directions you have given us, and you want us to alleviate the suffering of humanity by propagating the teachings of Lord Caitanya, especially by massive distribution of Kṛṣṇa conscious literature.

No doubt, if we, the devotees, become happy and united in spirit and commitment, we will accomplish miracles in preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the world. A fulfilled and satisfied devotee is our greatest asset. That’s what people primarily look at: Whether we are more happy and fulfilled individually than they are, and whether we have a stronger unity and fellowship among one another than they have.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please bless us so that we may become increasingly empowered to help make your movement an example of pure devotional service. We know that millions of people, as well as governments and influential personalities, have read your books. They have heard the message. They are looking at us. They are waiting for us to do it right. When we are ready, the world will be ready.

In the meantime, please help us publish and distribute hundreds of millions of your books.

Your humble servants at the North European division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Ratnabhūṣaṇa dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Western Pacific)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Words always seem to be such a paltry way to describe and praise your glories, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You who were personally sent to this world by the all-powerful, all-merciful Supreme Lord Himself — how can words possibly describe or explain who you really are? You whose accomplishments are so innumerable and profound — what words can possibly encompass the breadth of your reach through space and time? You who many of us saw exhibiting indescribable and overwhelming spiritual symptoms, even as you worked to suppress them — how can words describe such a perfect swanlike soul as yourself, who so compassionately descended to walk among us, so close that we could touch you?

Answer: They can’t hope to. There are simply no words that will ever come close to properly describing or explaining you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Still we make an attempt to realize some tiny aspect of your beyond-words divinity by thinking of you and then writing some thoughtful words in praise of you and your world-changing devotional service to the Lord. What else can we do?

A whole lot, according to Your Divine Grace! Action is more powerful than words, and you always pushed us to express our devotion by accomplishing something tangible in the service of the Lord. Something that will last. Temples, devotees, books, and more. If we can somehow, someday, simply follow in your footsteps and actually accomplish some infinitesimal spiritual good in this lifetime, then we will have properly reaped the harvest of your brilliantly divine presence and ever-merciful association.

May we report that, by your divine grace, the first Burmese *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* is currently at the press, as well as the Farsi *Perfection of Yoga*. The Arabic *Science of Self-realization* and Japanese *Perfection of Yoga* will also be coming out soon, and we want to steadily produce new titles of your books in all eleven languages of our BBT division. But those words just written will manifest into action only by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please gift us with the intelligence to grab onto that mercy tightly, and to act in such a way that you are pleased enough to smile, broadly.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants at the Western Pacific division of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Academic Outreach Preaching (Lagos)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, ISKCON’s founder-*ācārya*!

We of the second generation feel very much obligated to you as our spiritual grandfather because, as we understand from the traditional African culture, the grandfather is even more merciful than the father. With that in mind, we feel committed to helping to spread your teachings around the globe. In this connection, we have been showing how Vedic philosophy offers solutions to social, economic, and political issues. On this, your appearance day, I offer you a summary of my programs from March 17–26, 2005.

I flew to Washington, D.C., on 17 March 2005 from Lagos. From there I traveled to North Carolina to participate in the annual conference of the North Carolina Political Science Association (NCPSA), held this year March 18–19. My hosts were Professor Andrew H. Ziegler, Jr., and Professor Frank Trapp, president of the NCPSA. Both were very happy that I had come to participate in their conference. While the three of us rode in a car to the conference at the University of North Carolina in Pembroke, they asked many questions and I explained things to their hearts’ content. They knew I was a priest of the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Professor Trapp said that when he was in college one of his professors had arranged for a devotee to speak to the students.

When I arrived at the conference hall, Professors Ziegler and Trapp told their colleagues that one of the panelists was a priest from the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. After asking some questions about my paper, Professor Ziegler requested a copy of it, entitled Synergic Leadership and the Role of Belief Systems in National Transformation, which I supplied. He gave me his business card and requested that we keep in touch. Just before the conference began, one professor walked up to me and said, “Vāsudeva, you are welcome.” After I had presented my paper, the audience asked several pertinent questions, which I answered to their satisfaction. Later several of the attendees came forward to express their appreciation.

Next it was off to the South Western Social Sciences Association at the University of Southern Mississippi, in Long Beach. There I presented a paper at a conference titled “Politics and Ethics: An Interdisciplinary Exploration of Contemporary Issues.” My paper was entitled “Politics or Caring Thinking for Economic and Social Reconstruction in Nigeria.” As soon as I entered the conference hall, a Professor Robert Press greeted me and said they had been expecting me and immediately invited to present my paper. He said he’d been to Nigeria and wondered if the name “Dāsa” is Nigerian. Taking advantage of this opportunity, I explained that every Hare Kṛṣṇa takes on such a Vedic name after “baptism” (initiation). After he asked how I came to be associated with the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, I explained everything. After my presentation, the attendees asked some very nice questions, and by Guru and Kṛṣṇa’s grace I answered them all to their satisfaction.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have done so much for us! We beg you to empower us to be able to plant the seeds of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in academia.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

The most unworthy servants of your servants at Academic Outreach Preaching in Lagos, Nigeria.  
(written by Vāsudeva dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We, your servants at *Back to Godhead,* often hear from devotees that BTG gives them a sense of security. For them, it is a continual reminder of your legacy. They are happy to see that it keeps coming out year after year, even in times when your Society faces challenges from both within and without.

ISKCON’s membership has grown in both size and variety since you were here with us, and interest in Kṛṣṇa consciousness covers a wide spectrum. BTG has many longtime readers who have made the magazine a cherished part of their life. And we find a special appreciation among devotees, new and old, who are unable to live close to temples or other devotees. They tell us that BTG is their lifeline.

During your years with us, you were always concerned about *Back to Godhead,* the magazine you once described as the beginning of your spiritual life. We pray that just as you guided the former editors of BTG with your direct instructions, you guide us now within our hearts.

Your humble servants,  
The Back to Godhead Staff.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You had the vision to follow the orders of your spiritual master when it seemed like an impossible task. The first order was to preach the message of Lord Caitanya in the English language in the Western countries. The second order was to print books. You said that you were simply following blindly the orders of your spiritual master and that by sticking fast to this course of action all success followed. This simple prescription for making advancement is a necessity, and this Vyāsa-pūjā day is the annual reminder to all your disciples and followers to follow in your footsteps.

You encountered many obstacles along the path to the fulfillment of this vision. We are inspired by your words in this lecture:

When I took *sannyāsa,* when I was living alone, I was feeling very bitter. I, sometimes I was thinking, “Whether I have done wrong by accepting?” So when I was publishing this *Back to Godhead* from Delhi, one day one bull thrashed me, and I fell down on the footpath and I got severe injury. I was alone. So I was thinking, “What is this?” So I had very, days of very tribulations, but it was all meant for good. So don’t be afraid of tribulations. You see? Go forward. Kṛṣṇa will give you protection. That is Kṛṣṇa’s promise in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. *Kaunteya pratijānīhi na me bhaktaḥ praṇaśyati:* “Kaunteya, My dear son of Kuntī, Arjuna, you can declare throughout the whole world that My devotees will never be vanquished. You can declare that.” \[Lecture, New York City, 17 April 1969\]

There have been many instances around the world where your devotees have encountered opposition in the form of hostile government officials or unscrupulous cheaters or other misfortunes. Sometimes setbacks come in the form of diseases like cancer or heart attacks or automobile accidents. You rightly preach that this material world is *duḥkhālayaṁ aśāśvatam,* temporary and full of miseries. Your analysis of these phenomena is just according to *śāstra,* and you are revealing to us the perfection of *śāstra-cakṣus*, the eye for seeing the Absolute Truth.

Beyond this world, your vision and revelation are showing us the spiritual world. Your translations and purports give us the view of the pastimes of Kṛṣṇa and his incarnations strictly according to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* we are given the intimate details and dealings of Lord Caitanya and His associates. As fallen as we may be due to our past impious activities and birth in this Kali-yuga, by your mercy we can immerse ourselves like the sages of Naimiṣāraṇya:

*vayaṁ tu na vitṛpyāma uttama-śloka-vikrame*  
*yac-chṛṇvatāṁ rasa-jñānāṁ svādu svādu pade pade*

**“We never tire of hearing the transcendental pastimes of the Personality of Godhead, who is glorified by hymns and prayers. Those who have developed a taste for transcendental relationships with Him relish hearing of His pastimes at every moment.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.1.19)**

We are so fortunate that because of your establishment of ISKCON a place exists where we can immerse ourselves in this hearing and chanting process. We are not alone. We can have the association of so many devotees who are sincere in following your instructions, reading your books, and delivering their message to others.

Many times you declared that “Everything is in my books.” At the same time you instructed your disciples to write books. What would be the purpose of such writing? The answer is in the following two letters:

You must all study very scrutinizingly all of the books so that when the need arises you can repeat in your own words their purport. Also I will be very pleased if you contribute articles to BTG. By writing regularly, what you read will become realized. As much as possible read, chant, and preach. This is our life and soul. If we keep to this simple formula then there is no doubt that we will be victorious wherever we go and very soon we shall become the only religion in the world. \[Letter to Hridayānanda dāsa Goswami, 6 January 1972\]

It appears that you have good talent for writing, so kindly continue to develop in this way and write more and more of your realizations based upon our books. There is no need to concoct anything new. You simply have to study carefully our books and then in your own words try to express what you have read. This will automatically make you a very successful preacher. \[Letter to Dāsānudāsa dāsa, 13 May 1977\]

Your instructions help us overcome all doubts and setbacks. By your establishment of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness and its publishing arm, The Bhaktivedanta Book Trust, you have given us everything we need for success in our individual spiritual life and for preaching the message of Lord Caitanya to others.

On this day we pray to Kṛṣṇa to make these instructions of yours “our life and soul.”

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your humble servants at The Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace! All glories to your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

Bearing the legacy of your divine master,  
Name and fame you were never after.  
Through illness and challenges, resolute in purpose,  
Writing purports out of mercy to save us.  
Respect was never from you a demand;  
You earned it, however, by your pure command.  
Your purity made us our faith in you to repose,  
Impersonalism is what you came to oppose.  
To share the teachings was your emphasis.  
Hence you taught “books are the basis.”  
Not just to sell them but to study diligently;  
You implored us to hear and chant the message attentively.  
The message you taught is our only shelter.  
It lives on even after your departure,  
Providing shelter for the next ten thousand years,  
Protecting us from the greatest fears.  
To spread these teachings is our aim,  
Every town and village being our domain.  
Through structured courses and regular classes,  
Your teachings forms our syllabuses.  
We bow and pray on this auspicious occasion  
To practice and preach your teachings in devotee association.  
We submit our lives to be used as instruments,  
Following in your footsteps to spread the *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Preaching report: Structured courses in Vaiṣṇava Etiquette, *The Science of Self-Realization, The Spiritual Master and the Disciple, Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, The Nectar of Devotion, The Nectar of Instruction, Śrī Īśopaniṣad,* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* have been maintained and started in new areas. By your mercy, in the last year we have held classes in Durban, Guadalajara, Guyana, Houston, Johannesburg, London, Mauritius, Mexico City, Montreal, New Delhi, Pietermaritzburg, Pune, Trinidad, Toronto, and Vancouver. We pray that by your blessings we may increase our service to you eternally.

Your servants at The Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, Johannesburg, South Africa.  
(written by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta College

Beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, we offer our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

In your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.22, you write:

Advanced people are eager to understand the Absolute Truth through the medium of science, and therefore a great scientist should endeavor to prove the existence of the Lord on a scientific basis. Similarly, philosophical speculations should be utilized to establish the Supreme Truth as sentient and all-powerful. Similarly, all other branches of knowledge should always be engaged in the service of the Lord. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* also the same is affirmed. All “knowledge” not engaged in the service of the Lord is but nescience. Real utilization of advanced knowledge is to establish the glories of the Lord, and that is the real import. Scientific knowledge engaged in the service of the Lord and all similar activities are all factually *hari-kīrtana,* or glorification of the Lord.

It is in this spirit of distributing *hari-kīrtana* all over the world that we are working together to develop your Bhaktivedanta College and systematically educating devotees so that they can offer a significant contribution to this transcendental purpose.

By the divine desire of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu and your inspirational blessings, the project is developing well. Already during the second year the college obtained official accreditation and the number of students and the study program are expanding. Many competent devotees are coming forward to offer their dedicated services, and there are prospects for opening branches in Māyāpur and North America.

Still, as some wise devotees have expressed, it is an ambitious project and we are just in our beginning stages. We need your divine inspiration and guidance to consolidate and succeed. We are in the mood of Śrīmatī Kuntī as she prays, “As the name and fame of a particular body is finished with the disappearance of the living spirit, similarly if You do not look upon us, all our fame and activities, along with the Pāṇḍavas and Yadus, will end at once.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you, as the empowered representative of Kṛṣṇa, are the living spirit that keeps ISKCON and its various transcendental projects and activities alive. Only if you grant us your glance of mercy and spiritual strength will we be able to accomplish something meaningful.

We take this opportunity to pray at your lotus feet so that Bhaktivedanta College can play an important role in your International Society for Krishna Consciousness as it strives to fulfill its mission of spreading the holy name of the Lord to every town and village of the world.

We pray that our efforts are pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

Your servants working with Bhaktivedanta College in Radhadesh (Belgium) and abroad.  
(written by Yadunandana dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you!

April 15,2005. Springtime in Vṛndāvana. The flowers are blooming, the peacocks are calling, and the evenings are stretching and being filled with the sweet sounds of many bells, gongs, and the singing of devotees worshiping Kṛṣṇa in the temples.

It is the growing season. Everything is flourishing.

Thirty years ago, during this spring month, on April 20th, you opened your Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma temple in Vṛndāvana. You planted it around the *tamāl* tree, in the middle of Ramaṇa-reti. Its roots have reached deep, and its spiritual canopy shelters many.

Next to it you planted a school. In a letter in 1968, you wrote, “We have to grow children to become the head of the human society and there is great need for this.”

How do you grow children? They seem to grow on their own, and often outside of our dreams for them. And sometimes they grow in the wrong way. How do we grow them right, toward loving devotional service to Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet?

I have learned from you that the prime ingredient for growth is love, because that is what you had for all of your disciples. I read about it in your letters, I see it in your talks and movements on video, I hear it in your voice on tapes. When your disciples speak about you their faces light up, their voices change, tears often come to their eyes. The listeners could listen forever.

The more I grow in age and wisdom, the more I know just how extraordinary a person you were. How unfortunate that I never met you while you were here! But also how fortunate I am! For you left your books and disciples to guide me and unlimited service to engage me. I am blessed. Truly.

I pray that all the students in your school in Vṛndāvana (180 this year and growing!) have their hearts, minds, and intelligence captured by your love. I pray that they will grow, under your instructions, to be fine young men, the future heads of society, who will assist you in your efforts to awaken the lost and lonely conditioned souls.

The staff and students at the Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School in Vṛndāvana, India.  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvana-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Berkeley and Mumbai)

Most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our repeated humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

No less a person than Sūta Gosvāmī says in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (1.2.2), regarding Śukadeva Gosvāmī: “Let me offer my respectful obeisances unto that great sage, who can enter the hearts of all.”

As an especially empowered *ācārya* of the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *paramparā,* whose mission is to deliver *all* the fallen conditioned souls in this Kali-yuga, you are also certainly likewise able to enter into the hearts of all living entities and infuse the core of their consciousness with *kṛṣṇa-prema*. But this will occur only to the extent that they are willing to reciprocate with you by following your instructions and rendering loving devotional service to Kṛṣṇa within your International Society for Krishna Consciousness. Certainly all your sincere followers in ISKCON are being directly guided by you. We feel it personally in our lives. Otherwise, how could we, with our tiny brains, feeble intellects, and near-absent devotion, understand and work toward the goal of establishing the scientific knowledge contained in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the welfare of all? *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* fully describes the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the nature of the *jīvas, and also the nature of matter*. You have set up ISKCON to help you extensively propagate authoritative knowledge about souls and their eternal loving relationship with Śrī Kṛṣṇa, while you have established the Bhaktivedanta Institute to disseminate the *Bhāgavatam’s* knowledge of matter, according to the modern scientific way.

**Dr. Sharma:** And there is a lot of scientific data in our ancient scriptures which I told them that if they go through from their point of view they will find lot of things in *Purāṇas,* in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* in many Vedic—

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** No, apart from *Purāṇas* and Vedic literatures, scientifically it is the modern science, according to their way, we can convince. *Purāṇas* you may not believe the authority. I may not believe. But scientific truth has to be accepted. \[Room Conversation, Bombay, 31 March 1977\]

“According to their way” means we have to extract and develop the scientific content in *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* that pertains to matter and present it in appropriate technical language to meet contemporary standards of theoretical and experimental science. Thus you even said that BI presentations can avoid explicitly mentioning the spiritual connection entirely:
