**Rūpānuga:** So the title of the journal is *Sa-vijñānam, Sa-vijñāna*.

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** “Scientific Knowledge”

**Rūpānuga:** “In Scientific Knowledge”

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** “In”? This “in”?

**Rūpānuga:** That’s what Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “In Scientific Knowledge.” I was wondering if this, is this, *sa,* mean “in”?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** No, *sa* means “with.”

**Rūpānuga:** “With scientific.” Would it be improper to say *Vijñāna* only? Would that be improper? I was thinking in terms of making the title as short as possible. *Vijñānam* or *Sa-vijñānam,* both nice, but I was thinking . . .

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** *Sa-vijñāna* is more important. *Vijñānam* means theoretical. And *Sa-vijñāna* means practical.

**Svarūpa Dāmodara:** So in parenthesis we write, “In Scientific Knowledge”?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** That you say. Or you can write, “Spiritual Science.” No. That will not ring for them. They will not understand “Spiritual Science.” \[Conversation, Washington, D.C., 8 July 1976\]

You also said this science means practical technology:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** I can propose some theory, “It is like that.” But that is not science. Science means observation and experiment. That is science. You observe how the rules are working, and when you practically bring them into experiment, then it is science. If you simply theorize, that is not science. Mental speculation. It has no benefit. You can speculate, constructing a castle in the air. That is not a very good thing. *You should present something which will benefit the people, and practical. That is science.* \[(Emphasis added.) Conversation, Perth, 9 May 1975\]

This is certainly an extraordinarily difficult task — one that cannot even be conceived — yet by your mercy and your divine guidance in the hearts of your sincere followers, we have very recently been able to form an experimental initiative group at the BI in Mumbai to start practically applying the theoretical advances we have made so far (by your mercy and guidance) in quantum physics. It seems possible to apply this knowledge to develop new curative drugs within allopathic medicine! In the very first meeting of our experimental group, one of the members, who is the head of a multimillion-dollar multinational life sciences laboratory in Mumbai, commented that the approach we are taking in regards to quantum theory can certainly lead to the development of new techniques for designing medicines. Just a few days before the meeting, we had not even conceived of such a breakthrough, and thus we felt your guiding hand through all these discussions. Since the structure-based design of new curative drugs is a multibillion dollar effort around the world, one can only be immensely awed by your vision.

You have explained that the importance of systematically establishing the knowledge about matter on sound *Bhāgavata* principles is not a mere preaching technique. It is a very essential part of Lord Caitanya’s plan for the spiritualization of the whole world. In ancient times the sages knew not only spiritual knowledge but also material knowledge, by which they could help people fulfill their material needs in accordance with Vedic principles. As a result, when the sages gave theistic knowledge on the spiritual level, the people accepted those teachings as well. In modern times people get their material needs fulfilled by the atheistic scientists, and thus by and by such scientists’ propaganda about the nature of life and origin of the universe is also accepted widely. Your Divine Grace tells how the Communists spread atheism:

The Communists make propaganda in the villages by asking people to go to church and pray for bread. So the innocent people pray as usual, “O God, give us our daily bread.” When the people come out of the church, the Communists ask, “Have you gotten bread?”

“No, sir,” they reply.

“All right,” the Communists say. “Ask us.”

Then the people say, “O Communist friends, give us bread.”

The Communist friends, of course, have brought a whole truckload of bread, and they say, “Take as much as you like. Now, who is better — the Communists or your God?”

Because the people are not very intelligent, they reply, “Oh, you are better.” \[*Teachings of Queen Kuntī,* Chapter Nine\]

Thus, in modern times (as in ancient times), if any religion merely presents spiritual knowledge but doesn’t teach from its scriptures how to achieve material well-being also, leaving material well-being to the atheistic scientists, then such a religion cannot expect to change the views of the larger population, who are materialistically inclined. The people will remain under the influence of the atheistic propaganda. The uniqueness of ISKCON in comparison to other religious institutions is that the Vedic literature you have provided us gives a detailed description not just of God and the soul, which all other religions also give, to varying degrees, but also of matter, which no other religion gives. You have taught that the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas, following Lord Caitanya, accept the reality of matter. By contrast, even the traditions that have come out of the original Vedic teachings, such as Jainism, Buddhism, and Advaita Vedānta, deny the Supreme Personality of Godhead and end up ultimately denying the reality of matter too. Thus, in the realm of meeting the challenge of modern science, ISKCON is the only religious institution in the world that does not have to dialog with science but can actually offer an alternative material science. You pointed this out in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34: “This verse is the potential basis of great scientific research work.” How amazingly prescient you were!

Establishing such *bhāgavata* scientific knowledge of matter within modern science will also help devotees better understand the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (7.2) the Lord says:

jñānaṁ te ’haṁ sa-vijñānam idaṁ vakṣyāmy aśeṣataḥ  
yaj jñātvā neha bhūyo ’nyaj jñātavyam avaśiṣyate

“I shall now declare unto you in full this knowledge, both phenomenal and numinous. This being known, nothing further shall remain for you to know.”

Kṛṣṇa then begins by *first* describing various levels of *matter:*

bhūmir āpo ’nalo vāyuḥ khaṁ mano buddhir eva ca  
ahaṅkāra itīyaṁ me bhinnā prakṛtir aṣṭadhā

“Earth, water, fire, air, ether, mind, intelligence, and false ego — all together these eight constitute My separated material energies.”

In your own inimitable style, which is, as ever, full of potent transcendental realizations, in another of your books you explain the importance, even for devotees, of understanding matter according to the *Bhāgavatam’s* point of view:

Kṛṣṇa says that out of many millions of people, one may understand Him. But, by chanting of this Hare Kṛṣṇa, as introduced by Lord Caitanya — chanting and dancing — you can understand Kṛṣṇa within a very short time. *Knowledge begins not from Kṛṣṇa, but from things which we are accustomed to see every day.* \[(Emphasis added.) *Kṛṣṇa consciousness: The Topmost Yoga System*, Chapter Five\]

You then go on to explain the various kinds of sciences possible using the eight elements of matter. This has direct connection to your vision for the BI, since in modern physics there is a theory known as quantum theory that is applicable to the macroscopic world we see around us, not merely to the atomic realm. Yet for nearly one hundred years scientists have not been able to figure out how to apply quantum theory to the macroscopic world. Recently, scientists around the world have begun to accept your Bhaktivedanta Institute’s solution of this problem, based on the notion of five gross elements.

By your mercy, the BI’s M.S./Ph.D. program, which teaches all this knowledge at a very advanced level to general students, is progressing nicely. Graduates from your M.S. program have been accepted at such distinguished universities as Harvard, Leeds, and Utrecht (Holland). Among the further signs that your M.S. program is fast receiving ever-increasing international recognition is the fact that this year we have already received six overseas applications from students from distinguished institutions around the world, in such countries as Mexico, Bulgaria, Germany, and the United States.

Also, Your Divine Grace has arranged for ten papers from scientists at the BI to be published in leading scientific journals this year alone.

The Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai and Berkeley has reached a stage where it is going to require enormous resources in the form of building space, *lakṣmī,* and manpower. We humble hope and pray that you will make all this available as and when you see fit, and that you will continue to protect us in devotional service and inspire us even though we are very foolish and virtually useless instruments in your hands.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Berkeley, California, and Mumbai, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata)

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

“When the scientist, philosopher, medical man will get the opportunity to study systematically what is God, . . . then only there will be paradise.” —Śrīla Prabhupāda

Most worshipable Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your unlimited blessings, the scientific research about God is increasingly gaining momentum throughout the world. There are large numbers of scientists, groups, and institutions — both scientific and religious — from around the world that are participating in this wonderful work. Many books and journals are being published, and a number of conferences are being organized every month on science and spirituality. The Bhaktivedanta Institute is also making its humble and sincere attempt at bringing the Vedāntic conception taught by Your Divine Grace to the attention of scientists, scholars, students, and many interested people. While the scholars in India are happy to learn their traditional Vedic conception about the modern scientific theories, the Western scholars are surprised to learn the breadth and depth of the concepts covered in the Vedic literature.

In order to fulfill your divine instructions to make scientific presentations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in colleges and universities around the world, and by your unlimited blessings, our two buses — “Bhaktivedanta Institute on Wheels”— are touring all over India. Devotees in these buses are reaching numerous colleges and universities in India and presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness and distributing books.

In the last year we have brought out the following publications:

1\. *Savijñānam: Scientific Exploration for a Spiritual Paradigm, Journal of the Bhaktivedanta Institute,* Vol. 2

2\. *Seven Nobel Laureates on Science and Spirituality*

3\. *Vedānta and Science Series: Life and Origin of the Universe*

4\. *Science, Spirituality and the Nature of Reality: A Conversation between Sir Roger Penrose and Dr. T. D. Singh (Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami)*

5\. *Life and Spiritual Evolution*

6\. *Essays on Science and Spirituality*

7\. *Savijñānam: Scientific Exploration for a Spiritual Paradigm, Journal of the Bhaktivedanta Institute,* Vol. 3, (at the press)

8\. *Towards a Culture of Harmony and Peace* (at the press; has contributions from as many as nine Nobel Laureates, the President of India, and the Prime Minister of India)

Our humble effort through the buses and publications is very much appreciated by the scientists, students, and scholars. To network and further nurture the interest generated among them by our projects, we have started Bhaktivedanta Institute’s Scholars’ and Students’ Nexus at Universities (BISNU). There has been a good response, even at premiere institutions, where both students and faculty have shown much interest in becoming members of BISNU and starting BISNU units in their regions.

In order to fulfill your divine instructions, the Bhaktivedanta Institute organized The Second International Congress on Life and Its Origin: Exploration from Science and Spiritual Traditions, in Rome, November 11–14, 2004. The congress was a humble offering to Your Divine Grace as part of the special celebrations commemorating your 108thappearance anniversary last year. By your unlimited blessings, the conference was a grand success, attracting the appreciation from the President of Italy, the Mayor of Rome, all the participants, and the general public.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious occasion of your appearance day we seek your unlimited blessings and guidance so we may increase our humble efforts to please you.

The Bhaktivedanta Institute Members, Kolkata, India.

## Bhaktivedanta Student Center

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*śrī-caitanya-mano-'bhīṣṭaṁ sthāpitaṁ yena bhū-tale*  
*svayaṁ rūpaḥ kadā mahyaṁ dadāti sva-padāntikam*

Our dearest spiritual grandfather Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our *koṭi koṭi daṇḍavats* at Your Divine Grace’s divine lotus feet, where all other masters eternally take shelter. By your mercy we have the opportunity to be delivered by the most munificent Lords — Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and the Pañca-tattva.

We are most unqualified to try to understand and describe the love you have invested in this movement, ISKCON. You built ISKCON with your great love for the Supreme Personality of Godhead (Lord Kṛṣṇa), your spiritual master, and humanity, which you strove to deliver from darkness and ignorance. Your love is ever increasing, even though all of us in this material condition are unable to fully relish your love or perceive its greatness.

None of us in this simple, new place ever meet Your Divine Grace in person, but we understand and feel your presence in our service. Thus you have been the motivating factor for us to continue our involvement in ISKCON, even though there are many hurdles within and without.

With this in mind, we try to offer whatever service we can according to how you taught your disciples. We have met many of your disciples, and each of them reflects some of your qualities. Seeing your disciples’ purity helps us continue preaching according to the principles taught by Your Divine Grace. Collectively, we see your presence in our activities and receive your guidance daily; in this way we try to follow your principle of “unity in diversity” and your instruction to show our love for you by cooperating with one another.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you showed us this path of devotional service, all of us are indebted to Your Divine Grace eternally and unlimitedly. We are obliged to follow in your footsteps unconditionally. Every year when we write the Vyāsa-pūjā offering, we feel connected to you more intimately and thus become more qualified as Prabhupādānugas. Yearly we strengthen our connection with you in this way so that we will not be carried away by any opposing elements or by our tendency to act independently and lord it over the material realm, a tendency that is very subtle yet strong.

We do not want to fail you by diverting our energy from continuous preaching and book distribution. We humbly beg Your Divine Grace to empower us so that our attempts at service will be successful. We have no other shelter but your lotus feet. Please petition the Supreme Lord for a very special concession for us so that we may serve in this movement eternally without interruption. Let us please Your Divine Grace in our efforts so that everyone will be pleased.

Even though we are unqualified, out of duty and our ardent desire to obtain your causeless mercy and eternal blessings, we strove to overcome all obstacles and start this student center during the special year just past, commemorating your 108thappearance anniversary in 2004.

At Navadvīpa Baru, which means “New Navadvīpa” in our Malay language, we are inviting Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva to reside here and deliver from birth and death all the students and intellectuals at the local university. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please remove all impediments so we will be able to install replicas of Śrīdhām Māyāpur’s Pañca-tattva Deities within a year.

At present, since we have limited manpower, we distribute on campus only two hundred packets of *prasādam* twice a month. This is happening by Your Divine Grace’s special blessing, since we are in a Muslim country and the officials therefore rigidly scrutinize everything we do on campus. Please give us the strength and determination to continue preaching to the students until the end of our lives, since we believe such preaching greatly pleases Your Divine Grace, who were always eager to give Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the youth.

Let us not wither in the face of challenges as we execute our duty. You are our knight in shining armor who dispel all the darkness of ignorance, which is simply increasing with the so-called advancement of the modern so-called civilization of science and technology. Your Divine Grace guided us, is guiding us, and will guide us in our efforts to penetrate the hearts of the scientists and philosophers at this university, the Universiti Sains Malaysia. Any success we have will come due to our distribution of Your Divine Grace’s powerful books and Śrī Jagannātha’s *prasādam,* which cut swiftly through the dense ignorance of sense gratification pervading our surroundings.

We humbly take our leave from Your Divine Grace with the firm conviction that we are listed in Your Divine Grace’s super-special mercy journal. The thought of Your Divine Grace will blossom eternally in our hearts. Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

All glories, all glories to your divine 109thappearance-day *mahotsava*!!

*Jaya nitya-līlā-praviṣṭa oṁ viṣṇu-pāda paramahaṁsa parivrajakācārya aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedanta Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda-kī jaya*!!

The devotees at Navadvīpa Baru, the Bhaktivedanta Student Center in Penang, Malaysia.  
(written by Caitanya Hari dāsa)

## Brighton Food for All Center

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

“Well,” says Joe, in his broadest cockney, his salty sea-dog face wrinkled in a wide grin, “you’ve got to have a laugh, haven’t you?”

Joe is all of seventy-six years young, a hardened drinker and local character who lives alone in sheltered housing across the road from our place. He’s an early riser, up by five a.m., which is how I first noticed him, taking his rubbish to the bin across the street. He’s always gadding about the neighborhood, stick in one hand, bottle in the other, always chatting with the passers-by. Everybody loves him round here.

A couple of months after we moved into the little storefront (which is a whole transcendental story in itself) Joe introduced himself when we bumped into each other on the street. I was surprised that he stopped me. I wasn’t at all expecting him to be favorable toward the Hare Kṛṣṇas. He didn’t really know who we were, but he had heard about what we were up to at the center.

“What you people do is very kind” he said. “Feeding those people. Well, they need looking after, don’t they?”

A couple of days later he came in during evening *prasādam* and called me over secretively.

“Here you are,” he whispered hoarsely into my ear, shoving a crumpled tenner into my hand. “Put that in your box.” A tenner, by the way, is a lot of money for an old man on a state pension.

Joe started to pop in on his way to and fro most days, all deep laughter and rich humor, but always very respectful and wanting to help. He brings things we can use and cuts jokes with us, always ready to surgically remove our false egos and prejudices in the most stunning of ways that make us love him more every time. We got to know a bit about him, how he had lived on the streets for five years until age seventy, how his family had broken apart. And he started to come upstairs once or twice with some of the students and homeless guys and characters we serve *prasādam* to, to hear the *kīrtana,* though he wouldn’t stay for the class. He’s very fond of the *mṛdaṅga*. And of *halavā*. And of your smiling face. He would always look at your picture on the wall downstairs, the one with you looking to your right, your saffron chaddar wrapped elegantly around your head to keep out the chill of a morning walk. Your smile beams from ear to ear, pure and childlike, your face and shoulders filling the picture frame, triumphantly dominating the atmosphere of the room.

“He’s beautiful,” Joe said to Bhakta Pete one day. “Have you got a picture I could have?”

“We’ll try and get one for you, Joe,” Pete said, mentioning it to me later on. I made a mental note to try and look for something in Dharaṇīs shop when I next went there, not really taking it seriously. We had three pictures of you around the place in our little center, but I was very attached to them, and it didn’t occur to me for a minute to let Joe have one of them, or even that he was *really* all that serious about whether I got him one or not in the first place.

One picture that we had in the center was a larger-than-lifesize print of you holding your *daṇḍa,* looking fatherly and formidable, which we had put at the top of the stairs leading to the temple room. It was a heavy poster, and once a month or so the sticky-tack would loosen and the poster would land on the floor, requiring me to guiltily put you back up. Ever since Joe asked for you, however, this pastime became a daily event. I was beginning to wonder if you weren’t trying to tell me something . . .

“I’ve got something, Joe, but it might be a bit too big,” I said, feeling confident that Joe would reject my offer and that I could buy myself more time and hold on to you. Anyway, it’s not such a nice place to send you, with Joe being a meat-eater and a drinker and a smoker and everything.

“That’d be just right,” said Joe, looking up the stairs at you. “I’ll put him up on the wall by my door.”

So I huffed quietly and rolled you up in as dignified a way as I could and brought you across the road, and Joe stuck you to his wall with masking tape. I felt a little uncomfortable about it all, but Joe was ecstatic.

“He’s beautiful,” he said, smiling beautifully, admiring you from two paces back.

“Look after him, won’t you, Joe?” I said, a little concerned.

“Don’t you worry,” he said, “ I’ll look after him.”

And you looked contented enough, Prabhupāda, so I left you both there and went back to the center and stared at the empty space on the wall at the top of the stairs, wondering what to do.

Then the next day Joe popped his head round the door.

“How’s Prabhupada?” I asked.

“Come and have a look at this,” he said.

And he took us across the road and opened the door to his apartment. There you were, radiant and resplendent, surrounded by large bouquets of plastic flowers stuck at a distance on either side of your face with brown tape, a makeshift aura somewhat in the manner of a Roman emperor’s laurel wreath. You looked incredibly regal, your stern but compassionate gaze enhanced unlimitedly by the imperceptible smile that danced on the corners of your mouth, and enhanced again beyond all calculation by Joe’s innocent but wonderful act of love for you. The look on your face told me it was all Kṛṣṇa’s intention. My jaw dropped. It was a complete surprise.

“That’s beautiful, Joe!” I said, somewhat amazed. Joe didn’t know the first thing about devotional service, or so I thought. He certainly didn’t know about garlands and flowers. He didn’t know about honoring the spiritual master. He didn’t even really know what a spiritual master was. Inspired devotion without even a single instruction from anyone.

“Let me get my camera,” I said.

At your lotus feet I offer this meager and insufficient account of just one of your pastimes so that still more of the continuing glories of your eternal mercy might in some way be broadcast to the devotees.

You’re the greatest, Prabhupada. A hundred and nine years on and still totally cool. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Your devotees at the Brighton Food for All Center, UK.

## Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

The sacred Vedic literature is likened to a wish-fulfilling tree (*kalpa-taru*), since it is believed capable of satisfying all aspirations for both terrestrial and celestial happiness. Those who turn to the *Vedas* with honesty, faith, and goodwill attain satisfaction of all their desires, be they for riches, beauty, renunciation, power, fame, knowledge, or even liberation (*mokṣa*).

Among all that’s desirable, what satisfies most is the experience of love.

The more that love is freed from contamination (*tamas* and *rajas*), the more it is satisfying and long-lasting.

Among all experiences of love, the supreme one, which satisfies completely and forever, is the experience of love for God (*bhakti*).

Among all experiences of love for God, the one in which God is an active and creative partner in the loving relationship is the most appealing and ecstatic.

Every experience of divine love has its own peculiar spiritual taste (*rasa*). In the Vaiṣṇava tradition, the personal relationship with Kṛṣṇa in the mode of *bhakti* is said to be the most elevated and satisfying, capable of fulfilling all the soul’s aspirations and of causing it to feel permanently and supremely happy (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.2.5: *yenātmā suprasīdati; Nārada-bhakti-sūtra* 60: *paramānanda-rūpāt*).

Among all experiences of divine love, participating in Kṛṣṇa’s childhood pastimes in Vṛndāvana is my greatest aspiration.

With wonder and gratitude, I reflect upon the innumerable spiritual gifts I have received from you throughout the years. More and more, I appreciate how, due to your divine mercy, my awakening was instantaneous and yet slow and gradual. I can feel the irrationality of such a statement and I apologize for it, but I can’t but note that this has happened simultaneously *in* and *out* of time, *in* and *out* of me, my will, and my possibility of change. Certainly, your mercy has been essential to start in me a renovating, rebirthing process and, along the way, to protect and orient it. Thank you from the bottom of my heart.

At our first meeting you told me, “Study the *Bhagavad-gītā* and become a devotee.” At the beginning and for years I believed that the most elevated goal and the most precious gifts of spiritual realization were the acquisition of transcendental knowledge of reality and the development of an ethical conduct coherent with the spiritual dimension, as expressed in practical devotional service offered to you and Śrī Kṛṣṇa. In my view, proof of this was that out of that experience I gained vibrant joy and ever-increasing inspiration.

I gradually realized that striving to awaken and develop the latent devotional-spiritual qualities in others was an integral part of *bhakti-sevā,* and also of the very process of my own awakening. I recognized that this experience was profoundly suitable, precious, and productive of enlightenment and joy.

Later, I started to get an inkling of God’s presence in all relationships — in relationships with humans and with the world at large, even with so-called natural events; gradually, also the relationship with the sacred scriptures has taken on more elevated meanings and tastes, especially in those passages dealing with the loving relationships between God and His devotees in Vṛndāvana.

I have gradually started to feel attracted to Kṛṣṇa’s adventures in Vṛndāvana. I desire to become qualified to participate in this special *rasa* with His playmates as a servant, and I set this objective above all other possible aims human life allows one to attain.

As for my becoming qualified to enter Kṛṣṇa’s *vraja-līlā,* it appears impossible simply by my own efforts: without the intervention of your divine mercy, this aspiration of mine would certainly remain just a pious desire. My *sādhana*, even if it were constant and rigorous, would be insufficient to reach the goal of love with God. This ideal necessarily implies the joyous participation of the partner (Kṛṣṇa), and if He were not to consent to participate, the process would remain barren of results.

As the *śāstras* teach, the guru’s mercy is essential for receiving Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. This is true in more than a technical sense. In the *Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad* (6.23) it is stated that he who has the same love for the guru and for God obtains spiritual realization (*brahma-bhūta*). As the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.54) further teaches, only by attaining this *brahma-bhūta* stage can one obtain the highest goal: pure love for God.

Since the love we are talking about is free of egotism, if the heart is not free from *anarthas* we are able neither to love nor to appreciate the love we are receiving.

God reciprocates when we love Him with that quality of love.

In truth, even before then He loves us, but because we are chained to our inborn egoistic tendencies, we are not able to love or realize His love.

The pathways of divine love open up to him who agrees to be educated to love.

Without your teachings and your divine mercy, without your constant and exemplary life model of the perfect *bhakta,* never would it have been possible for me to understand spiritual reality or move one single step on the pathways of love.

I am infinitely grateful to you for your life lessons, which satisfy the intellect and heart.

I am infinitely grateful to you for your accomplishments, which confer a sense of reality to love, which otherwise is abstract sentimentalism.

I am infinitely grateful to you for your devotees, wonderful and precious companions on the spiritual journey along the pathway of *bhakti*.

I am infinitely grateful to you for giving us the family of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, without which we would all be castaways in the terrific ocean of Kali.

I am infinitely grateful to you for giving me an inextinguishable source of inspiration through the example of your pure devotional life, essential to direct me and make me firmly proceed toward the perfection of *bhakti:* always living in pure love for God.

With the desire to always better serve all creatures, your devotees, you, and Bhagavān Śrī Kṛṣṇa, I ask you to give me the strength to faithfully, joyfully, and hopefully face all the difficulties and crises that human life presents to everyone, including devotees.

With gratitude,  
Your servants at Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta, Italy.  
(written by Matsya Avatār dāsa)

## The Druze Project

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our humblest obeisances at your lotus feet and at the feet of all your Vaiṣṇava disciples who carry on your great mission throughout the world.

We wish to thank you from the bottom of our hearts for this great movement of self-understanding and self-liberation, which attempts to spread your endless compassion and knowledge throughout the world. We in the Druze Project of Israel have learned so much from our interaction with this Middle Eastern culture that in many ways parallels the Vedic one. Over the past nine years many, many men and women among them have been amazed and deeply moved to learn of our movement and these parallel beliefs. And where we are *not* parallel, they have been very patient to hear our suggestions from the *śāstras* and to discuss matters politely. We have made many friends.

Although we cannot take our preaching and projects with the Druze to the next level without at least several more men, or several more *gṛhastha* couples, and so must therefore devote ourselves to other services for the time being, we will not give up on this most promising preaching. Rather, we will pray constantly for the mercy of Lord Kṛṣṇa and you, his dearmost representative, and our spiritual masters, who so lovingly serve you, so that these devotees will find their way to this wonderful service that could open more doors for your books to the Arabic-speaking world. And we will continue our efforts to translate more of your books into that language, such as the splendid *Science of Self-Realization,* which is ready for printing in Arabic now and which has inspired so many to become devotees. We are confident that other devotees will join us to assure that this amazing preaching opportunity does not die. Your endless mercy will assure this, we know. We cannot fully express our gratitude for your constant inspiration, but we hope to have honestly tried in these heartfelt thoughts, realizing that the most genuine attempt is in trying to increase the depth of our devotional service. All glories to you on this day, on which even the demigods and Lord Kṛṣṇa rejoice.

With love and utmost respect,  
The servants of your servants at The Druze Project, Osafia, Israel.

## Festival of India (Denmark)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet. Thank you for giving us the matchless gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In trying to reciprocate with this great mercy of yours, we are putting on the Festival of India in Denmark with the aim of giving out the mercy of Lord Caitanya to everyone. At last year’s festival four thousand to five thousand people participated, and we distributed around twelve hundred plates of *kṛṣṇa-prasādam*. It was a most wonderful experience for everybody. Many people chanted and danced in ecstasy, enjoyed *prasādam,* and took home your transcendental books afterwards.

You came to the West to introduce the ancient Vaiṣṇava culture of India, and you often emphasized that the mission of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is to revolutionize the whole world by a cultural conquest. In one letter your wrote, “I want that many such festivals should be held in every city.”

One of the participants last year wrote:

I would like to express my gratitude for your organizing Festival of India. The only way I can express myself is: *it was the happiest day of my life*. I have been through *lots* of years of my life, but I have never felt like this before. The whole day I was standing or sitting on the ground or dancing before the stage. I do not know where the time flew. I got it for *free*. I did not even lift one finger. And that can only be due to the mercy of all the devotees of Srila Prabhupada. For the first time I realized how merciful Lord Caitanya is. I have always heard from the devotees about this, but never understood exactly what it meant. Please convey my heartfelt thankfulness to all the devotees who were instrumental in making this day so special.

With eternal gratitude,  
Mrs. Nalini matu

We humbly request you to consider us worthy of more of your special compassion. Please continue to give us the opportunity for purifying the dirt from our hearts by participating in Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement.

Your aspiring servants on the Festival of India crew in Denmark.  
(written by Rūpa Mañjarī dāsa and Gaurabhāvana dāsa)

## Festival of India (USA)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet!

Traveling with the Festival of India opens one’s eyes to how much suffering there is in the world due to material conditioning and sense enjoyment. It shows how urgently we need to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness to every man, woman, and child, irrespective or birth, status, or religion. You set the example of being compassionate to everyone simply by offering everyone the chance to render any amount of devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa. By your mercy we have been able to offer service opportunities to countless people in dozens of cities — from the clapping bystanders on New York’s 5th Avenue to the homeless residents of Golden Gate Park happily enjoying *prasādam.* By providing us with these experiences you taught us that Kṛṣṇa notices every small act of devotional service.

One of the wonderful aspects of Kṛṣṇa consciousness is that it works whether you like it or not. The best example is the devout Christians who come year after year to the Los Angeles Ratha-yātrā parade dressed in cow costumes and carrying large signs warning that hell awaits us all. Little do they know that by hearing the transcendental vibrations of the holy names and by seeing the magnificent forms of Lord Jagannātha, Baladeva, Subhadrā, and you, they are in fact being purified and are being allowed to serve Kṛṣṇa in either this life or the lives to come.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to allow us to serve you and grant us strength and steadfastness in spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants on the Festival of India crew in the USA.  
(written by Rasācārya dāsa)

## Food for Life Global

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On behalf of all your *prasādam* distributors, please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am writing this homage from Śrī-dhāma Māyāpur, the place where it all began — both the *saṅkīrtana* movement and ISKCON’s mass *prasādam* distribution program, most commonly known as Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life.

It has been nine years since my last pilgrimage to Māyāpur, and I feel there is a special significance to my return. Having immersed myself in mundane responsibilities these last nine years, the sense of coming “home” to my roots is tangible. This morning I cried uncontrollably at the *samādhi* of Tamāl Krishna Goswami, being all of a sudden overwhelmed by a sense of helplessness and an emptiness in my heart.

Yesterday hundreds of ISKCON devotees descended upon Shantipur to partake in the annual mass *prasādam* distribution festival in celebration of the disappearance of Mādhavendra Purī, eagerly pursuing Śrī Advaita Ācārya’s benediction that anyone participating in this celebration would receive Govinda *prema*! Once again I felt a strong connection to my roots, this time to my responsibility as director of Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, being an omniscient *sādhu,* you know as well as I do that the phrase “Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life” is really just a variation of the phrase “*prasādam* distribution.” From the very beginning of my involvement in Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life, I have made it clear that *prasādam* distribution is for everyone, not just poor people. In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* you write, “*Everyone should be given a chance to take prasādam* and thus be induced to chant the holy names Hare Kṛṣṇa and also dance in ecstasy” (emphasis mine). It is this one statement that has fueled my passion for *prasādam* distribution all these years.

When I meditate on your words “Everyone should be given a chance . . . ”, they also remind me of your oft-stated request to the devotees to “somehow or other spread Kṛṣṇa Consciousness.” You encouraged creative thinking. You demanded we use our individual talents in Kṛṣṇa’s service. You wanted a society of free-thinking, innovative Vaiṣṇavas. At the same time, however, you stressed the importance of maintaining traditions, not changing things, and the need to stick to the principles. Quite a challenge. Yet we feel that, by your mercy, Food for Life is gradually succeeding on all fronts.

The recent tsunami relief conducted by ISKCON Śrī Lanka and Food for Life Global is a case in point. More than 250,000 *prasādam* meals were distributed, *kīrtana* was performed for the distressed survivors, and over $100,000 in donations was raised from the public to support these efforts, as well as another ISKCON project called Gokulam: Bhaktivedanta Children’s Home. The orphanage has a reputation of being the best in all of Śrī Lanka. The one hundred children sheltered there receive three *prasādam* meals daily, clean accommodations, a full education, and the motherly love of your dear disciple Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī and her assistant caretakers. On top of this, Food for Life Global received applications from over five hundred volunteers to help with the tsunami relief, including doctors, nurses, lawyers, engineers, soldiers, firefighters, teachers, pilots, and even a boat captain! Indeed, the response was unprecedented in the history of ISKCON. Never before had so many professionally trained nondevotees offered to volunteer for an ISKCON project. Eventually twenty were selected, and all of them performed wonderful devotional service as Food for Life volunteers, working alongside ISKCON-initiated *brāhmaṇas*. It was another example of how Food for Life can be a medium for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness, whether the person is taking *prasādam,* supporting *prasādam* distribution projects, or actually performing devotional service.

Our Food for Life program is one of the many ways in which devotees can give their association to the nondevotees, especially those who would ordinarily be difficult to approach through other methods. In this regard there is a very nice instruction from Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura, followed by your comments about the power of *sādhu-saṅga*:

\[Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura explains that\] in the scriptures, devotional service and pious activity are considered fortunate. Pious activities can be divided into three categories: pious activities that awaken one’s dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness are called *bhakty-unmukhī sukṛti,* pious activities that bestow material opulence are called *bhogonmukhī sukṛti,* and pious activities that enable the living entity to merge into the existence of the Supreme are called *mokṣonmukhī sukṛti*. These last two awards of pious activity are not actually fortunate. Pious activities are fortunate when they help one become Kṛṣṇa conscious. The good fortune of *bhakty-unmukhī* is attainable only when one comes in contact with a devotee. *By associating with a devotee willingly or unwillingly, one advances in devotional service, and thus one’s dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness is awakened.* \[(emphasis mine) *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 22.45, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the personification of Vaiṣṇava compassion, traveling all over the world to give your *saṅga* to the most fallen souls. Through the Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life program we have personally witnessed the most wretched, hopeless, and materialistic people become purified and touched by Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Back in 1991 I wrote a poem describing the transcendental nature of this program. Today, I would like to share it with you:

A Vaiṣṇava’s compassion knows no limit, it is said,  
Oceanic and sweet, distinguished like gold, not lead.  
Such compassion you’ll not find in the mundane welfare realm;  
It emanates from the heart and has the ability to overwhelm,  
To dispel dryness and insecurity from the hearts of homeless souls,  
To instill faith in the faithless and establish spiritual goals.  
Although externally ours appears like many other missions,  
The essence we’re preserving through transcendental vision.  
Simply saving shirts (or bodies) while neglecting the swimmer (the soul)  
Is a waste of valuable time, we’re emphatically told!  
Our aim is to go deeper, our service to personalize,  
to become expert in both realms, complete welfare to combine!  
No need to be fanatical; our mission is foretold:  
“In every town and village inundate the young, the poor, and the old!”

For myself and thousands of others engaged in *prasādam* distribution, the effectiveness of the Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life program is unquestionable. It gives millions of people every year a chance to take *prasādam,* it makes people favorable to ISKCON, it opens the door for introducing Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy and culture, it engages people directly in devotional service, and it helps to purify the sinful eating habits of the public.

My initial motivation to get involved in the Food for Life project came from reading a letter of yours:

\[The\] mass of people take direction from their leaders, so if the leaders of present-day society can be persuaded of our beneficial working for the human welfare, and if they help us somehow to spread what they have learned from us to the people in general, that is the point to be considered. \[Letter to Dāmodara dāsa, 9 January 1973\]

For me, Food for Life represented an opportunity to connect to these opinion leaders, and by your mercy, Food for Life devotees all over the world have been quite successful in doing just this by way of many exemplary Food for Life projects.

Some in particular deserve your attention. Rūpa Raghunātha dāsa, director of the Vṛndāvana Food for Life project, has been instrumental in not only expanding *prasādam* distribution in the holy *dhāma,* but in improving the environment, bringing clean water to remote villages, planting trees, protecting cows, and even educating some of the poorest children in India. The Food for Life Sāndīpani Muni School currently cares for more than four hundred disadvantaged children, providing them with *prasādam* meals, uniforms, education, Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and a bright future. The efforts of Rūpa Raghunātha and his team of eighty staff members are being noticed by leaders from all parts of the world. More importantly, they are glorifying Vṛndāvana with these Food for Life-inspired projects, something I am sure Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī is very pleased with.

In Sri Lanka, a hidden jewel of ISKCON’s many outreach programs is Gokulam: The Bhaktivedanta Children’s Home, founded by Nandarāṇī-devī dāsī. There over a hundred orphans receive three nutritious *prasādam* meals daily, along with a full education, sports, dance classes, singing, art, and even yoga. Food for Life Global decided to adopt this program during the tsunami disaster, and for good reason. As I saw the children sitting happily at their little blue wooden desks, their attention fixed on the teacher, I had to keep reminding myself that this was not an ISKCON *gurukula* but an orphanage with a fully accredited international school. All the children were orphans. Some were actually war orphans, having suffered the traumatic experience of seeing their parents killed in front of them! And yet here they were, bright-eyed, happy, and adorned with Vaiṣṇava *tilaka,* studying to be good Sri Lankan citizens, obviously very satisfied in the warm loving embrace of this exceptional children’s home.

Another incredible preaching project very much under the radar is the Bengali village Food for Life project conducted by Mahāśṛṅga dāsa from Poland. In the mid-nineties he established himself as a leader in Food for Life with his almost superhuman efforts in Warsaw, where he would cook for a thousand people in a kitchen no bigger than a car, help serve the meals out, and then pack up and distribute three hundred books the same afternoon! Nine years ago he decided to move to Māyāpur-dhāma. Always looking for a challenge, he single-handedly began cooking and serving hundreds of villagers around the Māyāpur area. Soon he was feeding thousands. Today the program continues with the help of the village people themselves, who all absolutely adore Mahāśṛṅga. He now talks to them in fluent Bengali and leads a *kīrtana* through the jungle every day before *prasādam* is served. What is even more amazing is that this program has operated without any financial assistance from the Māyāpur temple. “I meditate on Prabhupāda’s wish that no one within ten miles of our temple should go hungry,” he explains, “and Kṛṣṇa sends me all the money I need. It is really not that amazing. Kṛṣṇa provides for all his children.”

Reminiscing about my day at Shantipur, I enjoyed hearing a story narrated by Śacīnandana Swami describing how you once visited Shantipur as a *gṛhastha* and then again as a *sannyāsī* the day before you were to leave for America. I found it fascinating that of all the places you could have visited, you decided to spend your last day in Shantipur, begging for the mercy of Lord Gaurāṅga, Nityānanda Prabhu, and Śrī Advaita Ācārya at the same place where the latter initiated massive *prasādam* distribution to the public in celebration of the passing of his *gurudeva*. I could now understand where you got your passion for *prasāda-sevā*.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am begging for your mercy to expand *prasādam* distribution all over the world so that everyone gets a chance to take *prasadam.* So profuse is Food for Life’s *prasādam* distribution now that every second of the day someone is taking *prasādam,* yet still I will not be satisfied, nor I’m sure will you, until everyone in the world is daily being nicely nourished, spiritually and materially, solely by *prasādam*.

Thank you for inspiring us in this wonderful service to Lord Caitanya.

Your servants at Hare Kṛṣṇa Food for Life.  
(written by Priyavrata dāsa)  
Food for Life Global

## Hare Kṛṣṇa School (Auckland)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Gifts to the World**

Who is this person who established ISKCON temples for transcendental association all over the world? Who is this person who provided us with windows to the spiritual world?

Prabhupāda, our *śaraṇāgati***.**

Who is this person who penned the pages of the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* which has sold over seventeen million copies in over thirty languages? Who is this person who so carefully crafted the purports to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* for the benefit of humanity?

Prabhupāda, the most outstanding Vaiṣṇava author of our times, the person *bhāgavata*.

Who is this person who has brought to fruition the prediction of Nityānanda Prabhu to inundate the world with the chanting of the holy names? Who is this person who traveled around and around the world in old age, to every town and village of the globe, spreading the mission of Caitanya Mahāprabhu?

Prabhupāda, the *senapati-bhakta*.

Who is this person who taught us how to live, how to clean ourselves, how to cook and eat sanctified foods offered to the Lord? Who is this person who introduced the Vaiṣṇava calendar of festivals throughout the world?

Prabhupāda, the *jagad-guru***.**

Who is this person who concisely extracted the essence of the Vedic literatures and distilled them for easy assimilation, free from the jargon and pretentiousness of so many other scholars, religionists, and devotees?

Prabhupāda, the Bhaktivedānta of Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇavas.

Who is this person that has provided us with guaranteed routines and regulations designed to uplift the soul and transform faithful adherents to the transcendental realm, to the position of *viśuddha-sattva*?

Prabhupāda, the transcendental touchstone.

Who is this person we get to bathe and dress each morning? Who is this person we glorify with the words of the *Guru-vandanā*as a part of our daily *sādhana*?

Prabhupāda, the master at whose feet all masters sit.

Who is this person who continues to touch, move, and inspire millions of people even after his departure from this material world?

Prabhupāda, our eternal guide and master.

Who is this person who was honored by the leaders of Indian society for his pioneering work in popularizing the Vedic culture throughout the world?

Prabhupāda, the great transcendental emissary of the spiritual world.

Who is this person who installed the transcendental *vigraha* of Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa in all corners of the globe?

Prabhupāda, the perfect *pūjārī* for the Divine Couple.

Who is this person who tirelessly spoke to enlighten thousands of reporters, academics, politicians, and skeptics about the sanctity of the human form of life?

Prabhupāda, the supreme scholar and logician.

Who is this person who never felt intimidated by external considerations and fearlessly represented his Guru Mahārāja in preaching the message of Caitanya Mahāprabhu?

Prabhupāda, the *siṁha-guru*.

Who is this person who gave to our children a perfect system of education?

Prabhupāda, the greatest of all educators.

Who is this person that shared with the world the esoteric teachings of the Gosvāmīs of Vṛndāvana, and who has taught the world the real import of renunciation, humility, and tolerance?

Prabhupāda, the pure *rūpānuga* from Vṛndāvana-dhāma.

Who is this person who sang so devotionally the Gauḍīya Vaiṣṇava *bhajans* of the previous *ācāryas*? Who is this person who inspired lively and endlessly energetic *kīrtanas* for the glorification of the Lord?

Prabhupāda, the great *kīrtanīyā*.

Who is this person who preached tirelessly in colleges and universities around the world?

Prabhupāda, the compassionate scholar of the *Vedas*.

Who is this person who revealed the holy *dhāma* to the world?

Prabhupāda, the moving *tīrtha*.

Who is this person who distributed *kṛṣṇa-prasādam* to the world?

Prabhupāda, the bringer of mercy.

Who is this person who established farming communities around the world?

Prabhupāda, the great champion of “simple living and high thinking.”

Who is this person who installed Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī here in Auckland, along with Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā Devī?

Prabhupāda, the *patita-pāvana* guru.

Who is this person who has stolen our hearts and is directly guiding us to higher realms of existence through such theft?

Prabhupāda, our ever well-wisher.

Who is this person that we stand before today and beg for one thing, that we might become, one day in the not too distant future, his sincere followers?

His Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

His name is destined to become a household name even more well-known than those of the great thinkers and shapers of modern consciousness. He has done more for the modern world than any single person could dream of in the past, present, or future.

Śrīla Prabhupāda gave us everything. Let us remember the insurmountable debt we owe him, and let us serve in his mission to help bring about a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.

The devotees at Hare Kṛṣṇa School, Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Prāṇa dāsa)

## Hare Kṛṣṇa Seminary of Theology and Philosophy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have presented us with a great legacy of transcendental literature.

**Books Are the Basis**

Your books are the basis for spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness in this misguided civilization of Kali-Yuga. Considering how carefully and tirelessly you wrote them and the importance you gave to their distribution, we feel even more inspired to study your books deeply and teach them to others.

Here at our seminary at Campina Grande, Brazil, we have humbly attempted to provide facilities for those devotees who wish to systematically study your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Nectar of Devotion, Nectar of Instruction,* and *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*. For this purpose we are encouraging devotees from Brazil and abroad to take a year off and join us for an intense program of study and *sādhu-saṅga,* under the guidance of your disciple His Holiness Dhanvantari Swami.

**Preaching Is the Essence**

Education in ISKCON can be understood as a kind of preaching specifically directed toward the new generation of devotees. Proper training promotes a healthy spiritual growth and enables one to serve better. With this idea in mind, our desire is to extend the opportunity of Bhakti-śāstrī studies to as many devotees as possible. Naturally this will also automatically benefit the preaching to the external public. Furthermore, as Kṛṣṇa reveals in the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.68), to be engaged in this kind of preaching is extremely auspicious:

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*

**“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me.”**

**Purity Is the Force**

It is certainly a great challenge and responsibility for all of us, as your followers, to preserve the purity of the Vaiṣṇava tradition and transfer it intact to the following generations. In fact, only pure devotees can do it. But how can we elevate ourselves to such a position? As teachers, we experience that to teach the *ślokas* and philosophy of the *Bhagavad-gītā* is not as difficult as to teach values and provoke a positive change in the student’s heart. Similarly, in our personal cultivation of devotional service, we notice that knowledge comes much easier than realization. *The Nectar of Devotion* explains that only by association with pure devotees can a neophyte be elevated. Therefore we are trying to provide the best association for all students here, which includes your special association through the careful and constant contact with your books.

**Utility Is the Principle**

In the second chapter of *The Nectar of Devotion,* quoting the *Padma Purāṇa,* you write, “A person who is engaged in devotional service in full Kṛṣṇa consciousness is to be understood to be doing the best service to the whole world and to be pleasing everyone in the world.” Your own life is the perfect example of this principle of practical application of the Vaiṣṇava philosophy. As your servants, we also desire to act in the same way. Therefore, on this auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray that our endeavors may somehow or other be useful for the fulfillment of your most cherished desire: that pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness may flourish all over the world.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!!

Your servants at the Hare Kṛṣṇa Seminary of Theology and Philosophy in Campina Grande, Brazil.  
(written by Gurusevānanda dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Moving to Greener Pastures**

The sun’s warmth changes the earth  
To bring green, luscious grass to birth.  
The cold, dark winter is over,  
Again the cows will eat fresh clover.  
From the hilltop barns the cows descend,  
Eager to see the full streams bend.  
Old Vraja, the king, leads the way,  
Not a defiant word the cows say.  
Noble Vraja’s concern is the herd,  
Riding on his back, a bluebird.  
They pass through the red gate  
To be together without hate.  
Kicking their hooves in the air,  
Jumping, and chasing on a dare,  
New to the pasture and not so calm,  
Young oxen are Kṛṣṇa and Balarām.  
The pasture meets the pale blue sky.  
Green grasses sway with a sigh.  
Warm sunny days bring pleasure.  
But winter we do not treasure.  
Beloved cows move on, move on,  
To the transcendental land beyond.  
Where Kṛṣṇa a cowherd was born,  
His youth spent herding cows each morn,  
Deep in fragrant Vṛndāvana’s grove,  
Oxcarts Kṛṣṇa and the cowherds drove,  
Enjoying eternal pleasure in the glen,  
His red, yellow cows and some that blend.  
The cows and Kṛṣṇa love each other.  
Each calf has an adoring mother.  
Their cow-barns palaces of gold,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, to us you told.  
To protect cows gives a higher taste  
To see Lord Kṛṣṇa with great haste.  
Secret knowledge only for a few.  
Thank you for this point of view.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, with you our bond  
To the cows, of whom Kṛṣṇa is fond.  
You taught us the joys of loving cows.  
To you our repeated, respectful bows.

Your servants at the International Society for Cow Protection.

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your holy lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

There has been no greater advocate for the bound conditioned souls than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. No philosopher has explained real freedom more completely, more eloquently than you. *Bhakti te mukti:* Devotion is real freedom, and you spent a lifetime teaching the conditioned *jīvas* how to break the chains draped from their shoulders and how to snap the shackles of the heart.

You have opened our eyes and are allowing us the freedom to walk the path back home. I’m sure you would be amused by popular advertisements. In a lecture you said, “They purchase Kool \[cigarettes\]. This is called *māyā*. He’s smoking fire, and he’s becoming cool. \[*laughter*\]” Today they say real freedom is “No psoriasis!” or “No migraine headaches!” or “a low interest mortgage loan.” Real “freedom.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we found our freedom in serving your movement, a movement you say deserves respect. As the dogs bark, the caravan moves on and has the whole world dancing in full freedom! Among so many other things, you wrote thousands of letters to devotees and aspiring devotees. As you wrote in one letter, “I have got always time to answer the letters of sincere souls because my life is dedicated for their service. So you are always welcome to send your inquiries. And I shall always try to enlighten you to my best capacity.”

We are trying to follow in your footsteps by keeping the spiritual growth of thousands of inmates alive through correspondence. In our ISKCON Prison Ministry Freedom Newsletter, we explore the meaning of freedom, and inmates who study your books contribute many excellent essays.

We recently received this letter:

Dear Candrasekhara,  
Jai Nrsimhadeva! All glories to Srila Prabhupāda and the Sri Krsna Sankirtan!

Well, I am stagnating in county jail, my thoughts are lavished with images of Srila Prabhupada, though and I must say I am in awe of the mercy bestowed upon me despite my fallen state. After japa, I sat on my cot in lotus position, I see mental images of Prabhupada banging a gong as he leads kirtan, followed by images of him walking along a seashore. A tinge of jealousy touches my heart because blessed devotees who came before me had been allowed the mercy of moving association with our acarya, while I am so unfortunate that I have been born unable to serve him personally.

Though I have only heard Prabhupāda’s voice on tapes, he chants with me in the morning. I am delighted to have his voice resound so clear in my heart and mind. The bhajans I have heard him sing and the lessons I have heard him teach linger in my memory like the most beautiful moments I have experienced. A tear flows down my cheeks as I realize I am removed from serving Srila Prabhupada because of my sin and can only pray to someday serve his devotees.

O Chandra, let us glorify our acarya together! Let’s serve Srila Prabhupada joyfully. I am his servant. Please accept my association and devotion to Srila Prabhupada. He has brought the light of the Bhagavata to me. Soon I will be transferred to state prison. Though I am devoid of direct association with Vaisnavas, Sri Krsna will bless me with better conditions than this jail. I will be able to order some books from Vedic Resource, such as the Prabhupada-lilamrta, which I began to nourish my soul with when I was still in Wisconsin. I am very fond of Satsvarupa Goswami, having perused his poetry that Bhakta Kevin sent me years ago, as well as pieces in BTG. My prayer is that the teachings of Prabhupada, carried on by truly self-realized souls, can have their well-deserved place in the education of humanity. It is a shame that I only realize this now as I dangle from the lifeline of prema bhakti.

I realize that there is no success without Prabhupada. Thank you for this chance to be of service to the blessed Lord, Sri Krsna-Govinda, and the disciplic succession from Sri Sri Gaura-Nitai. All glory and adoration upon the lotus feet of Srila Prabhupada.

Your servant,  
Bhakta Tim Shipley  
Lufkin, TX

This letter is exceptional, and yet it is not exceptional, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We receive letters like this every month. You are still offering freedom to anyone willing to accept your guidance. Śrīla Prabhupāda, like the six Gosvāmīs, you are *dhīrādhīra-jana-priyau,* dear to both the gentle and the ruffians.

This morning I read from your *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. I only had time to read for a few minutes, so I just read a few pages, the first ten pages of *Ādi-līlā*, Chapter 14. In those ten pages there was the original Bengali text, roman transliterations, synonyms, translations, and a few short purports. So it didn’t take long to read those ten pages. Yet after I had put the book down, I thought about what I had just read and was amazed. Those few short purports were not ordinary purports that one might expect to find in a religious classic. They were not even the kind of scholarly purports that a Vaiṣṇava might expect to find in the *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. They were Bhaktivedanta purports! Wonderful, glorious, profound, ecstatic transcendental bliss of devotional service. In those few short purports you shattered the illusion of the Māyāvādīs, smashed the false pride of the scientists, destroyed the misconceptions of the followers of false incarnations, increased the faith of the devotees, established the personal conception of the Absolute Truth, gave transcendental instructions to your followers, encouraged the devotion of the Vaiṣṇavas, glorified Lord Caitanya, and proudly praised your Hare Kṛṣṇa movement!! Here is an excerpt from text 19:

**“In the future this child will protect and maintain all the world. For this reason He is to be called Viśvambhara.”**

The *Caitanya-bhāgavata* confirms that Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, by His birth, has made the whole world peaceful, as in the past Nārāyaṇa protected this earth in His incarnation as Varāha. Because of His protecting and maintaining this world in the present Kali-yuga, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is known as Viśvambhara, which refers to one who feeds the entire world. The movement inaugurated by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu when He was present five hundred years ago is again being propagated all over the world, and factually we are seeing its practical results. People are being saved, protected, and maintained by this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement. Thousands of followers, especially Western youths, are taking part in this Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, and how safe and happy they feel can be understood from the expressions of gratitude in their hundreds and thousands of letters.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we here at ISKCON Prison Ministry are also receiving hundreds and thousands of letters from prisoners expressing gratitude. And what are they grateful for? What are they deeply and fervently appreciating? You, Śrīla Prabhupāda! Yes. Because they have your books! These prisoners are the absolute lowest of the low. Criminals, rapists, child molesters, murderers. But now they have hope. You have given them that hope, Śrīla Prabhupāda. And it is all coming from your books. From your wonderful Bhaktivedanta purports.

*Ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanam*. This is the process of devotional service. Anyone can be purified by following the principles of devotional service in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, for Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so powerful that it can purify even the dirtiest class of men and transform them into the topmost Vaiṣṇavas. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s movement aims at this purpose. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.18.43, purport\]

Śrīla Prabhupāda, thank you for allowing us to live in your house, ISKCON, and preach. It is a great transcendental adventure. We won’t leave the shelter of your house due to difficulties. We will follow the GBC because it is your desire, and we will co-operate within your institution, one that is benevolent and dynamic with obscure ISKCON angels serving the Deities and humanity by spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. We are proud to live in your house, and we invite the whole world to enjoy the same freedom. Although we are all recidivists in Māyās criminal court, we attain freedom very easily when we remember you. People are looking for freedom everywhere, Śrīla Prabhupāda. They are looking for freedom in skin creams and temporal relationships. Śrīla Prabhupāda, they should be looking for you.

On behalf of the IPM preachers and inmates in America, India, and worldwide, we thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because we have found freedom and redemption in you.

Your fortunate servants at ISKCON Prison Ministry.  
(written by Candraśekhara dāsa)

## ISKCON Resolve

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

There are so many gifts that you have given us. You have given us instructions to shelter us and help us gain understanding. You have given us the example of your life to inspire us and offer us hope. You have given us the holy name to purify us and grant us the direct association of Kṛṣṇa. You made the holy *dhāmas* available so that we can realize the truth and vitality of *bhakti*. And you created a society where we could take advantage of these and all the gifts you so generously and selflessly offered us.

For the magnitude of your largesse we owe you a great deal. Yet humble gratitude is not enough. An abiding appreciation for you, your sacrifice, and your mercy is a good foundation for our spiritual lives, but it is not nearly enough. There is much that you taught that I am unable to grasp or apply in my own life. Repeatedly I struggle with the most basic concepts of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Like the wasted effort of the elephant bathing and then immediately spraying himself with dirt, I purify my consciousness only to soon contaminate it again with some seemingly important diversion. Again and again I fall for Māyā’s same old tricks.

Despite my shortcomings, there is one conviction that I have been able to firmly grasp: the importance of devotee association. Rūpa Gosvāmī wrote that he would rather be encaged with a tiger than devoid of Vaiṣṇava association, and Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura would rather be an ant in the association of devotees than Lord Brahmā without it. You sometimes said that your purpose in creating ISKCON was to give people the opportunity to associate with devotees. I have seen that whatever weaknesses or faults one has, as long as one is able to remain respectful toward devotees, Kṛṣṇa allows him to remain in the association of His devotees. If, on the other hand, someone is disrespectful and offensive toward devotees, Kṛṣṇa does not allow that person to stay in their association. Whatever valuable service he has performed or admirable qualities he has attained, he soon becomes unable to associate with *kṛṣṇa-bhaktas*.

Although by nature I am often contrary or skeptical, I naturally seek to avoid conflicts. Disagreement and fighting often make me uncomfortable, especially if I am directly involved. It especially breaks my heart to see devotees fighting. Nevertheless, we live in Kali-Yuga. It should come as no surprise that devotees sometimes do not get along. However, for those involved, this awareness does not make conflicts between devotees any less frustrating or disheartening to see. As much as it pains me to see devotees not getting along, I have a strong desire to see people cooperate, even more so when those people are devotees.

You never shied away from doing what needed to be done. Taking the mission of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura as your life and soul, you accepted Kṛṣṇa’s pleasure as your sole criterion in deciding what to do. Your personal pleasure was not a factor. And look what you accomplished!

My service for ISKCON*Resolve* involves getting right in the middle of devotees’ conflicts. It is often not easy to see or hear this discord. But if I can help, it is worth it. Devotees are by nature sincere and want to work together to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. If I can assist in this way, then I feel fortunate. I also feel fortunate that I am able to work with such wonderful devotees on this project. We pray that you bless our efforts and help us help devotees manifest their love for you by increased cooperation.

With our heads at your feet,  
Your servants at ISKCONResolve.  
(written by Jai Nitāi dāsa)

## ISKCON Women’s Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my and humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Beloved Father, I have been given the undeserved privilege of attempting to write a suitable offering in honor of your holy and glorious Vyāsa-pūjā day. With a pretense of humility I tried to protest, but in truth I am afraid that my feeble efforts will reveal me for the impostor that I am. As if anyone has been fooled all these years. Often I suspect that my initiation was an accident, Śrīla Prabhupāda. While I am eternally grateful that you accepted me, it had to be a mistake. I am so unqualified as a disciple, it is difficult to feel that you could be pleased with me in any way. Yet, by your mercy, I do not feel rejected. My fortunate position is one of congregation member, a well-wisher of your society of Vaiṣṇavas, a friend of the *bhaktas,* who kindly engage me in your service. I am a pretender in any other capacity. Occasionally, there are moments of clarity and I am so grateful for all that you have given, and still give today. At these moments I have a tiny understanding of the breadth of your magnanimity, a glimpse of your causeless mercy that lifts my heart, inspiring renewed enthusiasm for whatever insignificant service I can offer.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, understanding my fallen and wretched condition, you know that I have prayed for guidance and inspiration. In the hope of purification only, I write, knowing that my words sound with the dull thud of a lead weight. Still, by the mercy of the assembled devotees, I hopefully aspire to serve you.

How does one express in a few well-chosen words enough gratitude for the continuing mercy of a lifetime? Your skillful touch shook us from slumber and pointed the way to freedom. The knowledge that you have so freely offered does not prevent the adversities here in the prison house, but prepares us for the inevitable onslaught. Over the years, as each predicted suffering comes to pass, your mercy is the only shelter in the storm. We pray that we may constantly remember your transcendental message with faith and conviction.

How does one encompass in a few well-chosen words ever-expanding appreciation for the sublime artistry of Kṛṣṇa’s transcendental emissary? In years too short, with a few deft strokes, the picture was complete. Your expert hand outlined the structure of our eternal shelter, the opportunity to develop devotion to Śrī Kṛṣṇa without tinges of *karma* or *jñāna*. We pray for the eyes to see it, the heart to appreciate it, the strength to protect it, and the gratitude that offers the same shelter to others.

How does one articulate in a few choice words the increasing admiration for your faultless and unerring ability to touch the hearts of so many diverse individuals? O dearest master, how simple you made it seem, how difficult it has been to emulate! No one felt rejected by your glance; everyone felt encouraged to reach for the highest goal, *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*. By your kindness only, we are no longer orphans loitering in the streets. We pray for the compassion to see every living entity as Kṛṣṇa’s beloved, including ourselves.

How can one put into words the growing respect for your age-defying sacrifices of comfort and peace to carry Lord Caitanya’s mercy to the savage cultures of the West? You were blessed with a simple life in Vṛndāvana — Gokul and Golok, a jewel to be treasured. As exuberant youth, we did not fully appreciate the extent of your surrender. With the creaking bones and painful joints of maturity, we can only marvel at your determination and selfless sacrifice to save the conditioned souls lost in Māyās embrace. We pray for the enthusiasm to serve you with our every breath.

O glorious master, you expertly presented the Vedic teachings so that even the most fallen could understand the eternal goal. Among hard-core members of “the Me generation,” you firmly established Kṛṣṇa as the center and enchanted the ruffians into His service. Who could be more competent at interpreting Vedic principles than you, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You had all the Vedic conclusions at your disposal, you came to America with a mission, and you established that mission according to your vision, introducing the geneuine Vedic version. No one, however inconsequential, felt oppressed or discouraged in your care.

Heartfelt compassion, a concerned attitude for the suffering of others, is indicative of growing spiritual maturity. As aspiring Vaiṣṇavas, as your faithful servants, we must encourage one another and revere one another as fellow travelers on this spiritual journey that you inspired each of us to take. O benevolent master, you are compassion personified. No one is insignificant in your eyes. A slight to the least among us is a slight to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Because of the nature of this place, all will take blows here during the course of a lifetime. Material energy is expert in this regard. As your servants, we pray not to add to the suffering of others by careless or mean-spirited treatment. You have taught, and the material energy has corroborated, that everything can be taken away in an instant. The only thing that cannot be taken from us is the mercy of the holy name and the compassion of the Vaiṣṇavas. Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray to remember this as we pray for compassion — as we pray to receive it and pray to exhibit it.

Twenty years ago, in a Vyāsa-pūjā offering, I likened your mercy to a tightrope spanning the treacherous chasm of the material world. I prayed to keep your face in view, knowing that only by your mercy would I be surefooted enough to cross this narrow band. Now, after so many mistakes, I understand how treacherous this material world can be. Countless times I have slipped into the canyons below. Now I understand that your mercy is not a tightrope but a broad highway that many saints traverse, in whose company even this dunderheaded, clumsy fool has some hope of staying on the path. This is the superlative shelter of ISKCON.

I am awed by the wisdom and power of the many men and women who humbly and faithfully walk this path, which you, in your infinite mercy, have paved. Certainly, none of their voices should be silenced but should rather be incorporated into the shelter you envisioned. I am eternally grateful for those who are making the selfless effort to strengthen and stabilize this shelter — grateful for myself, my sisters, my daughters, and for all of humanity who have yet to hear of Lord Caitanya and His mercy.

Some of us struggle with the constant reminders that we are in a woman’s body. We pray for the strength, certainty, and realization that we are not our temporary bodies but eternal spirit souls. O beneficent father, in your infinite wisdom and mercy you have invited us to take shelter in the house you built. By our eternal connection to you, we all have a room there. Some rooms are small and simple, some are grand and prestigious, but even a small corner in your house is preferable to any palace without your presence. We pray for your continued protection and guidance in our efforts to retain our rooms, decorate our rooms with spiritual qualities, and invite others into those rooms of refuge.

You have established this society solely to propagate the mercy of Lord Caitanya. Great were your countless sacrifices to ensure that all living entities would have the opportunity to imbibe that mercy.
