Eternal master, as your faithful followers we must not limit the opportunities of any living entity to serve Kṛṣṇa. It is our duty to remain servants of your Vedic vision. In my unqualified condition, I am not seeking opportunities to serve in the capacity of giving class or leading a *kīrtana*, but others are. Others who are qualified by realization, skill, and desire are in some places being discouraged in devotional service simply because they are female. This neglect strikes a discordant note in the eternal *kīrtana* of Lord Caitanya and our society. With you as our guide, may we ensure that all living entities are limited only by their capacities and skills, not by fear or legislation. This is the legitimate Vedic principle, the one that you established, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Our society should reflect your compassionate and inclusive demeanor. Your message was as simple and sublime as it was broad and deep: everyone is part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa and should serve Him. Those who felt inspired to use whatever skills or assets they had in Kṛṣṇa’s service were encouraged to do so. This is genuine protection; this is by definition the transcendental religious principle.

Many mistakes have been made in our zeal to expand your merciful mission to inundate the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Every endeavor in the material world is covered with some fault, and as a family we have endured the growing pains of a pioneer organization. As maturing disciples, we pray to keep the bruises of the past in perspective. Please bless us with the humility to remember those past mistakes. May we remember them, not because we want to maintain some bitterness in our hearts, but because they are a path we do not care to tread a second, third, and fourth time. We must remember so that, as a society — a society that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, had great hope for — we do not make the same offenses again.

Beloved master, we only know the true meaning of love because you loved us so truly. Well-chosen words do not mean anything if we do not love others as you love us. ISKCON is not a few buildings, a power structure, or a business model. ISKCON is your heart. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are more merciful than Kṛṣṇa himself. While the Supreme Lord travels in the heart of each living entity struggling in the material world, only His devotees retain a place in his heart. Yet you have invited us, and the world at large, into your heart. With no qualifications to call our own, we have been invited into the heart of Kṛṣṇa’s pure devotee, and we will never leave that safe haven. We have a home, an eternal home in the chambers of your munificent heart. I don’t believe we can leave it, any more than we can leave the heart of a loving parent. If we treat everyone with the same love and compassion we received from you, we have understood the true shelter of ISKCON.

O divine master, your exemplary management has taught us that respect and submission, just as surrender, service, and love, can’t be legislated, but naturally flow from a grateful heart. We pray that our gratitude remains dynamic, increasing each day as we understand more and more the depth of your transcendental gift.

We pray for the constant understanding that ISKCON is a reflection and manifestation of your love. As such, we cannot withdraw from its radiance, just as we cannot refuse to receive the sun. Some will use the sun’s energy for less-than-admirable purposes, but the sun is there, shining for all. Some will get a sunburn or create havoc for others, and some will grow crops or feel the warmth of the sunshine after a long, cold winter. We pray for the compassion to use the sunshine of your love to warm our hearts and strengthen our resolve to spread your message of love far and wide, and in the family of ISKCON as well. You have included all of us; we are all part of ISKCON. If we maintain loving connections with one another, shelter one another, encourage one another, share this journey with one another, we are fulfilling the vision you had for ISKCON, a shelter for the world.

Beloved father, bless us so that our well-chosen words become grounded in transcendental reality. Bless us that we may constantly remember your inspiration and reflect the selfless love that shines so brightly in your person. May we continue to find shelter and strength in your encouraging glance, to understand and reflect your love, mercy, and compassion as the moon reflects the light of the sun.

Falling as an insignificant and eternally grateful speck of dust at your lotus feet,  
Your faithful servants at ISKCON Women’s Ministry.  
(written by Kāntī-devī dāsī)

## Journal of Vaishnava Studies

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

My obeisances unto you. All glories to your eternal love for Kṛṣṇa and to your sacred memory!

My service involves the world of modern scholars, and, somewhat surprisingly, this gives me a deep appreciation for you and your teachings. Let me explain. I am constantly reminded that you always gave us absolute truth, directly from Kṛṣṇa, not hypotheses and speculations innumerable. I am repeatedly amazed at how modern scholarship, at times, appears to be just the opposite, diametrically opposed to your purity, certainty, and clarity.

That being said, I will be the first to admit that scholarship has its place and, when properly directed, can serve to inform people about technical nuances of a given subject. But there are also times when scholarship seems to lead to dead ends. Take, for example, what scholars know of “Hindu” origins. They speak of an Indus Valley Civilization. For almost one hundred years now, they have been uncovering relics ranging from the alleged remains of Dvārakā, off the Arabian Sea, to pottery and ruins from an era so ancient, they say, that time loses meaning.

Despite such finds, they admit that they know virtually nothing about this ancient civilization. They also admit not knowing who the nomadic wanderers were who entered India from the northwest millennia ago, but they claim to be certain that such people did indeed wander into Bhāratavarṣa at that time. This refers to their famous Āryan Invasion Theory, recently dismantled by the students and disciples of yesteryear’s scholars, the very ones who initially propounded this theory.

Both masters and students in the world of mundane scholarship hypothesize a language — Proto-Indo-European — that these ancient Āryan people supposedly spoke, but we are given no evidence of this language, other than the fact that numerous languages seem to come from Sanskrit, which they claim might possibly be a later version of this proposed language. And speaking of languages, modern scholars have unearthed a plethora of ancient seals in the Indus Valley region, but, despite some seventy-five years of poking and theorizing, the language on these seals remains indecipherable — no one has a clue as to what they are trying to say.

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have revealed truths about ancient India — nay, about the origin of life, and more, its essential purpose — that cannot be approached by means of the archaic artifacts mentioned above. You have given us *sanātana-dharma,* the Perennial Philosophy, which transcends the limits of India’s geographical boundaries, and which allows us entrance into a world beyond time. From this vantage point, it becomes clear that no amount of mental wrangling will ever truly reveal the reality behind the so-called Indus Valley Civilization, the Āryan Invasion Theory, or the undeciphered language on the Indus seals. At best, such remains of a civilization long lost, when finally understood, will only support what you have already revealed to us. At worst, they will lead to more confusion and tentative half-truths. If we’re lucky, they will fall somewhere in the middle, offering hints of Vedic culture. Obviously, after all this time, they will offer nothing conclusive.

On this auspicious day, then, I would like to applaud your ability to give us something conclusive, something that doesn’t depend upon our meager powers of speculation and the imperfection of our sense perception. I applaud your ability to give us the Absolute Truth, something that could never be awarded by archeologists, anthropologists, or the countless academicians who still labor to unearth the secrets of ancient India. While many of these researchers may uncover important finds along the way, the essence has been delivered by Your Divine Grace, and it will always remain so. For this reason, we shout, Obeisances unto you, and to the entire disciplic succession! You give us reality, not theories.

Your loving servants at The Journal of Vaishnava Studies.  
(written by Satyarāja dāsa)

## Padayātrā Worldwide

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us such a nice service, and thank you for keeping us always in the association of devotees of Kṛṣṇa. On this most blessed day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, it gives us great honor to help you propagate the message of Godhead.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, despite our shortcomings, by the grace of all the devotees and Guru and Gaurāṅga, many people have been benefited through the Deity worship, *saṅkīrtana,* and other activities of Padayātrā Worldwide.

We are confident that these activities will create a revolution and that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread all over the world.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants at Padayātrā Worldwide.

## Padayātrā North America

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I’m writing from the roads of Mexico, near Veracruz State. For the last three years I have had the desire to write to you, but somehow on the road the time factor and communications facilities were not favorable. Last year I thought, “Maybe next year I will have more things to offer you.”

Today, Gaura-pūrṇimā, marks five thousand kilometers of walking, chanting, and sharing Kṛṣṇa consciousness with others. From the industrial areas of the northeastern United States to the southern states where fundamentalist Christians predominate, to the dangerous areas of Alabama and Louisiana where signs of racism still exist, to the desertlike plains of Texas, to the tropical coasts of Mexico, your Padayātrā has been welcomed, impacting with the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu places where people never knew about Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The results are wonderful! We have visited around seven hundred villages and towns in our effort to fulfill the prophecy of Lord Caitanya. We have been spontaneously invited to schools, town centers, plazas, markets, cowboy associations, and interfaith missions to share our message. Literally thousands of people have accepted Kṛṣṇa conscious literature — your wonderful books — and taken *prasādam*. Many newspapers have published accurate accounts of our activities, with pictures of the Deities, and we have also appeared on televised news.

We have seen some people take to the practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness by chanting the holy name. People in villages often come forward just by hearing from their neighbors that a holy pilgrimage has come. They bring flowers to the Deities and want to touch Their feet. They try to participate in “the spiritual chanting.” They bring their families, they bring *bhoga* (cheese, milk, fruits), and one person asked, “Where are They \[Gaura-Nitāi\] taking you?”

In the schools the youth are wildly curious and inquire about everything — the culture, the program, the message.

To live on Padayātrā is to live by your mercy, in your association. To be on the road means to have a festival everywhere we go. We don’t need to do anything except keep moving, and just as ants come to sugar, *jīvas* come to Padayātrā to receive mercy and hear the holy name.

Nothing is lacking, and your presence is the most beautiful form of reciprocation with our endeavors.

All this is to your credit. You have faith in the program and let us participate in this eternal pastime, and even if the facilities are minimal — for example, no trucks, no advance party, only the wagon, the animals, and, most important, Their Lordships and your presence — still the success is predominant. All glories to your vision! And all glories to your mercy upon us and those living entities receiving the seeds of love along the way!

It’s easy to be thankful when things are good, but even better is to thank you for being with us through all the difficulties we have passed — those heavy moments like in North Carolina when a car hit us, or the four months we spent in a semi-arid region of Texas, or the danger of being exposed to people who want to exploit us, or more recently the passing away of Kāna (one of our four-legged Padayātrīs) in front of the Deities, leaving us by the side of the road on the coast of Mexico with no idea how to proceed.

If the glorious moments when all goes well are sweet, these difficulties are even sweeter, because in them you have showed your support and confirmed our faith that if we preach, everything will be there, even the solutions to problems that seem impossibly difficult. The mystical arrangements are always there.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for being with us. It is our desire to take you not only to Vṛndāvana but around the five continents in this simple Padayātrā, walking, chanting, and depending on the mercy of the Lord.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, bless us so we can make our desire a reality. We must think big for Kṛṣṇa, and with your blessings, everything can happen.

We pray to the dust of your lotus feet. Maybe in that way we can enter eternally into your *saṅkīrtana* family and eternally assist you in spreading the chanting of the holy name to every village and town.

Your servants on Padayātrā North America.  
(written by Avadhūta Śiromaṇi dāsa)

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, the founder and *ācārya* of ISKCON and the well-wisher of all living entities, especially your followers.

It has been a long time since I have written you on your blessed appearance day, and I have no good excuse for that. I have had little to report because of my own lack of determination to make your mission the driving force behind my existence. Now I have been asked to write an offering on behalf of all the devotees in this village. In order to carry out this near-impossible task, I must consider what the members of this community have in common. The way we have handled the differences between us has no doubt tainted our communal offering to you, so the most widely shared thing I can come up with among the devotees in this community is that at one time we all took shelter of your lotus feet. That means we agreed to live by the rules and regulations you gave us for our benefit. This is your mercy. So I ask myself, How do I turn this revelation into a meaningful offering to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda?

Like a child who gives his father a tie for his birthday with money that he got from his father, I must turn to you even for an offering on your appearance day. Here is a quote from your *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (8.1.16):

**The Supreme Personality of Godhead, Kṛṣṇa, works just like an ordinary human being, yet He does not desire to enjoy the fruits of work. He is full in knowledge, free from material desires and diversions, and completely independent. As the supreme teacher of human society, He teaches His own way of activities, and thus He inaugurates the real path of religion. I request everyone to follow Him.**

This is the sum and substance of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. We are simply requesting human society to follow in the footsteps of the teacher of *Bhagavad-gītā*. Follow the instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is,* and your life will be successful. That is the summary of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. The organizer of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is teaching everyone how to follow Lord Rāmacandra, how to follow Lord Kṛṣṇa, and how to follow Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. In this material world, we need a leader for a monarchy or good government. Lord Śrī Rāmacandra, by His practical example, showed how to live for the benefit of all human society. He fought with demons like Rāvaṇa, He carried out the orders of His father, and He remained the faithful husband of mother Sītā. Thus there is no comparison to Lord Rāmacandra’s acting as an ideal king. Indeed, people still hanker for *rāma-rājya,* a government conducted like that of Lord Rāmacandra. Similarly, although Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead, He taught His disciple and devotee Arjuna how to lead a life ending in going back home, back to Godhead (*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna*). All teachings — political, economic, social, religious, cultural and philosophical — are to be found in *Bhagavad-gītā*. One only has to follow them strictly. The Supreme Personality of Godhead also comes as Lord Caitanya just to play the part of a pure devotee. Thus the Lord teaches us in different ways just to make our lives successful, and Svāyambhuva Manu requests us to follow Him.

Svāyambhuva Manu is the leader of mankind, and he has given a book called *Manu-saṁhitā*to guide human society. Herein he directs us to follow the Supreme Personality of Godhead in His different incarnations. These incarnations are described in Vedic literature, and Jayadeva Gosvāmī has described ten important incarnations in summary (*keśava dhṛta-mīna-śarīra jaya jagad-īśa hare, keśava dhṛta-nara-hari-rūpa jaya jagad-īśa hare, keśava dhṛta-buddha-śarīra jaya jagad-īśa hare,* etc.). Svāyambhuva Manu instructs us to follow the instructions of God’s incarnations, especially Kṛṣṇa’s instructions of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*.

Appreciating *bhakti-mārga* as instructed by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya thus depicted the activities of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu:

*vairāgya-vidyā-nija-bhakti-yoga-*  
*śikṣārtham ekaḥ puruṣaḥ purāṇaḥ*  
*śrī-kṛṣṇa-caitanya-śarīra-dhārī*  
*kṛpāmbudhir yas tam ahaṁ prapadye*

**“Let me take shelter of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, who has descended in the form of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu to teach us real knowledge, His devotional service, and detachment from whatever does not foster Kṛṣṇa consciousness. He has descended because He is an ocean of transcendental mercy. Let me surrender unto His lotus feet.” (*****Caitanya-candrodaya-nāṭaka*** **6.74)**

In this age of Kali, people cannot follow the instructions of the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and therefore the Lord Himself takes the part of Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya to teach personally how to become Kṛṣṇa conscious. He asks everyone to follow Him and to become a guru to deliver the fallen souls of Kali-yuga.

*yāre dekha, tāre kaha ‘kṛṣṇa’-upadeśa*  
*āmāra ājñāya guru hañā tāra’ ei deśa*

**“Instruct everyone to follow the orders of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa as they are given in** ***Bhagavad-gītā*** **and** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*****. In this way become a spiritual master and try to liberate everyone in this land.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **7.128)**

The coherent purpose of Lord Rāmacandra, Lord Kṛṣṇa, and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu is to teach human society how to be happy by following the instructions of the Supreme Lord.

Because your purports are divinely inspired, you appreciate them more than we do. Therefore I offer you this purport with the prayer that we who aspire to be your disciples take what Lord Kṛṣṇa has dictated to you here as our life and soul, so that we may one day give you a worthy offering, i.e., the practical application of your teachings. What in this mundane material world could be more satisfying for either the guru or the disciple than the disciple’s sincere endeavor to surrender to the will of his spiritual master? As you say in this purport, “This is the sum and substance of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.” We thank you for your continued blessings, and we look forward to the day when we will be counted as your bona fide representatives.

Your servants in Prabhupāda Village, North Carolina.  
(written by Vatsala dāsa)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet, whose shelter, once achieved, would be given up only by an unfortunate fool.

Out of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s and your own unlimited causeless mercy, Your Divine Grace appeared within this material creation.

You descended and walked among us, within the grasp of our material vision, and then you again walked out of the grasp of our material vision.

Still, for us, the question of being “with you” or “without you” does not arise.

Your Divine Grace has given us and whoever else is willing to accept it your own eternal, sublime, transcendental association. At any moment, to be “with you” or “without you” depends only on our personal choice. If we do not take advantage of this opportunity, we can blame no one but ourselves.

**With or Without Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Without you we’re lost within countless deaths;  
With you we’re alive, always, birth after birth.  
Without you our future is only a lost bet;  
With you it’s our present, beyond birth and death.  
Without you our plans are condemned to fail;  
With you love of Godhead no doubt will prevail.  
Without you we’re glass with no light to reflect;  
With you we shine bright over where you direct.  
Without you our speech is a hedonist’s farce;  
With you every word is sweet, even when harsh.  
Without you our companions are lust, envy, and greed;  
With you your own pleasure is all that we need.  
Without you no hope to go back to our Lords;  
With you “back to Godhead” is not just mere words.  
Without you we suffer — stray dogs on the street;  
With you, in separation, forever we’ll meet.

Most dear Lord Gaurāṅga and Lord Nityānanda, You are causeless mercy personified and the bestowers of all benedictions. Please grant us the boon of always remembering Śrīla Prabhupāda, his sublime teachings and pastimes. Please do not let us forget our extraordinary master, even for the smallest fraction of time.

Please grant us the association of His Divine Grace’s sincere servants, and never let us be “without Śrīla Prabhupāda.”

This is our only real desire.

If any shadow of a different desire arises in our mind, please, out of Your infinite magnanimity, do not take any notice of it and let it dissipate like fog in the sun.

Most dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, Jagat Guru and Patita Pāvana, please keep us always under your protection, in the service of your divine lotus feet, and never allow us to become “stray dogs on the street.”

Your menial servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Terni and Pisa, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Rūpānuga Vedic College

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you so much for this opportunity to actually begin our human existence. We are simply trying to follow your directions to the best of our meager ability. We are daily seeing how, by the careful execution of your instructions, we are able to actually become more and more like civilized persons and not the dogs of our former selves.

We are now trying with our inappreciable efforts to establish a center for the systematic dissemination of your wonderful transcendental teachings. Your call for a Rūpānuga Vidyāpīṭha is our life and soul, and we beg for your mercy to allow us to be the instruments for the manifestation of that vision. We pray to become real Vaiṣṇava *brāhmaṇas* so that we may then be able to recruit and train an entire army of devoted followers of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Your Divine Grace.

Your indebted servants,  
The staff and students of the Rūpānuga Vedic College in Kansas City, Missouri.

## Śaraṇāgati

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It is morning, and outside the window several birds are hopping around in the sunlight on the fallow garden. They are busy throwing aside pieces of straw and pulling worms from the soil. I am busy also, writing a letter of love and appreciation to a personage I never really met and who passed away three decades ago.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

How can you possibly know me? I am nobody from nowhere who has done nothing. We looked at each other for a moment when you entered through the temple doors. You were surrounded by a colorful and frenzied parade of devotees. I inwardly promised then to remain your servant for life. But I was very young and I was caught up in the euphoria of the moment. None of my friends expected me to honor such a flash commitment. Yet curiously, thirty years later, when I set aside the distractions of daily living, it seems that all I really want is to feel the life of that youthful vow, to recall that glimpse of eternity, and to reaffirm my earnest intentions.

For the last ten years or so I have been living in a quiet valley located near a small town in British Columbia. My neighbors are peaceful souls, devotees of Lord Kṛṣṇa, who lead simple, contemplative lives. We raise our children and grandchildren here and grow vegetables in our gardens, and some of us tend a few cows. We get together and talk about life, we chant Hare Kṛṣṇa on beads and with instruments, and we honor *prasādam* together. Over the years we have learned more about one another, and overall we enjoy the intimacy. It is a clean, healthy, and happy life, Śrīla Prabhupāda. In fact, there are moments when it is deeply and profoundly satisfying. Perhaps reminiscent of what Goloka Vṛndāvana might be like.

In any case, this valley, these neighbors, these friendly exchanges, and this dabbling on the doorstep of transcendence would not exist for me if you had not cast your deep glance and I had not made those promises. In one way or another, everything I’ve done from that day until now has been all about you and your teachings. At this point in my life I truly feel that the notion of being without Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the darkest of all thoughts.

And so, yes, we were never formally introduced, and according to normal social conventions I must concede that I don’t know you and that you don’t know me. Still, my very life from morning to night, every day of the year, is some sort of attempt to become pleasing in your eyes. How can that be explained?

The birds I have been watching while I write to you have mostly returned to their nests now; only one or two are still busy. Let me end by saying thank you a thousand times and that I can hardly wait to meet you. Until then, please accept my eternal gratitude.

Your servants in Śaraṇāgati-dhāma, Canada.  
(written by Yogīnātha dāsa)

## Vedic Academy of Spiritual Technology (VAST)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your dear disciple and our late founder and chairman, His Holiness Śrīdhar Swami, while doing his Facilitators Course at the Stephen Covey Leadership Center in the US, prepared the following analysis excerpts, which we wish to share with you on this auspicious day of your divine appearance :

**An Analysis of Śrīla Prabhupāda as a Leader**

One of the most constantly striking themes that emerged for me was Śrīla Prabhupāda’s strong, unwavering private victory. He had spent the time to develop and live by a set of values that in my mind are wholly aligned with principles that came from Kṛṣṇa. For example, he demonstrated humility, faithfulness, devotion to Kṛṣṇa, love, integrity, an impeccable work ethic, sincerity, maturity (the ability to balance courage and consideration), growth, patience, a desire to become and nurture others to become interdependent, proactivity, vision, living by what was important to him, a value of others’ thoughts and capabilities, believing in the potential of people, morality, mutual benefit, and empathy. He committed himself to lead a principle-centered life and encouraged others to do the same. He worked tirelessly eighteen to twenty hours a day to establish and nurture ISKCON and his publishing institution, the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

Probably the most striking principle he lived by was his complete devotion to his mission or vision of creating a Kṛṣṇa conscious society. Śrīla Prabhupāda’s ability to stay focused on his vision of creating Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world was remarkable and unique. Even when he was faced with financial struggles while trying to publish his translations, challenges in getting the finances to go to America, health difficulties during his overseas journey, or troubles when he first arrived in America, he remained true to his goal. Not only did he have a vision, he lived it. He knew what was most important to him, and he spent his time and energy on those things. He put first things first.

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s personal commitment to the ways of achieving Kṛṣṇa consciousness was unwavering. His firm belief in his vision based on *evaṁ paramparā* and his ways of achieving the vision played a superior role in ISKCON’s ability to achieve what it did.

Śrīla Prabhupāda was a father figure in the lives of many of his disciples, encouraging them to make a break from unhealthy habits and practices and commit their lives totally to Kṛṣṇa. He had an inconceivable vision based on principles that he remained true to despite adverse circumstances. He lived out of that vision.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are pleased to inform you that during the last year VAST has conducted stress management workshops titled “The Awakening” for two Mumbai-based companies, namely, Excel Industries Ltd. and Blue Star Ltd. We expect to conduct more such workshops for corporate executives. In Mumbai VAST has also conducted four one-day stress management workshops titled “Joys of Stress” for professionals from various walks of life, who have immensely benefited from these programs. Also, during the Cross Maidan festival held in Mumbai in February 2005, in commemoration of your 108thbirth anniversary, VAST conducted a series of seminars entitled “Kṛṣṇa the CEO,”“Leadership in an Age of Higher Consciousness,”“Karma Chakra (Working Principles),”“Principles for Effective Management” (based on epics), “Stress Management,” and “The Art of Mind Control,” which were well received by the public.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, this innovative method of preaching the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the intelligentsia is very challenging and trying, and without your unalloyed mercy we will never be able to achieve our vision of being recognized as the best provider of spiritual technology in the fields of management and education.

So on this auspicious day of your divine appearance, we fall at your lotus feet and beg you to shower your mercy on us and help us fulfill your cherished desire of making the intelligentsia take up this sublime philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and incorporate its timeless principles into the practical field of their work.

Your servants at the Vedic Academy of Spiritual Technology.

## Vṛndāvana Institute of Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our respectful obeisances. We take shelter at your lotus feet. Let our minds be fixed on fulfilling your desires.

It is said that the recommended path of success is for one to engage constantly in hearing and chanting the glories of the Lord in the company of devotees. How can this practically be done? Many of us wonder and pray to understand. There always seem to be other things to do, and even when we finally sit down to read, our minds wander off.

In the first volume of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* you drill into your reader the importance of hearing. The *Vedānta-sūtra* itself, you point out, ends with the statement *anāvṛttiḥśabdāt:* liberation is obtained by sound. By material sound, a space within which material activities automatically unfold is created, and the soul helplessly struggles with physical existence. But spiritual sound expands a space within which spiritual activities thrive. People who hear about Kṛṣṇa and His devotees, about the spiritual world and the nature of the soul, are drawn to act on the spiritual platform and engage in devotional service. The inner path opens for them. You write that one should hear for a long time, seriously, attentively, and with patience.

Hearing indeed brings about a transformation in consciousness, which actually occurs due to a shift in identification. Then the constant hearing and chanting may take place. You gave us the analogy of the bottle of honey, the sweet contents of which one can taste only after opening the bottle and drinking from it. By lending time to hearing, one can sustain the excitement that initially made us take up Kṛṣṇa consciousness. A routine that lacks the constant freshness of hearing *kṛṣṇa-kathā*is an enemy of the spiritual seeker. Spiritual life is ever new.

It is a fact that the transformation of consciousness you are awarding to us, your hearers, alters our perceptions of the world. *Prasāda* of all kinds feels increasingly pleasing, not just because of the sensual or emotional experience, but because it opens a door onto the spiritual atmosphere surrounding the proximity of the Lord. *Ye tu tvadīya-caraṇāmbuja-kośa-gandhaṁ jighranti karṇa-vivaraiḥśruti-vāta-nītam*. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.9.5) It is expected that one will smell the aroma of the lotus feet of the Lord through the holes of the ears as it is carried by the breeze of the Vedic sounds. Alertness to spiritual presence is your gift. We hanker for a moment of your association. “Thank you” is a small phrase, often overused in today’s world of mistaken values. You said a thing is judged by its result. You made sure we made a distinction between sentimentality and practical engagement in service in Lord Caitanya’s mission, anchored in our realizations. Bestowing upon others what one has received is certainly the engagement of the soul.

This year, besides continuing with courses in Vṛndāvana, the VIHE is seeking to reach out and spread the culture of hearing and chanting through both correspondence and outstation courses, all conducted by VIHE students. Saṅkīrtana dāsa from Switzerland spent this spring teaching the *Bhagavad-gītā* in South India and is now teaching the Third Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in South Africa. He taught the Second Canto there the year before. Mādhavendra Purī dāsa from France taught Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s *Daśa-mūla* in Vṛndāvana to the Bhaktivaibhava students. He has also taught the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in South Africa. He is a seasoned Bhakti-śāstrī as well as Bhakti-vaibhava teacher. Gurusevānanda dāsa, a young devotee from Brazil, is now in South America teaching in Portuguese. This spring he has delivered ten seminars on the ten topics of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and is now teaching *The Nectar of Devotion*. Ādipuruṣa dāsa, our VIHE secretary and seasoned Bhakti-śāstrī teacher from Ukraine, is teaching the *Bhagavad-gītā* in Slovenia. Viśiṣṭa dāsa from Russia is teaching the Bhakti-śāstrī course by correspondence. Two new teachers have joined him in this effort, Bhāgavatī dāsī from Ireland and Siddhi-lālasā dāsī from Mauritius. Madana Gopāla dāsa, from Australia, has been teaching the First Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Avadhūta-priyā dāsī from Poland and Śubhāṅgadā dāsī from Slovenia are lecturing on the Fourth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to congregational members in the UK. Bhakta Damian from Brazil is teaching the *Bhagavad-gītā* in Sanskrit in Benares. Vanamālī dāsa from Śrī Raṅgam is establishing educational courses in Tamil in South India. Sarvajña Kṛṣṇa dāsa, also from South India, has taken up the initiative of teaching Bhakti-śāstrī in the Gulf, while two young devotees from Central India, Gopāṅgaṇā dāsī and Cinmayī dāsī, are heading an educational Girls Youth Forum in Nagpur.

This year however, a complaint was filed — that individuals become proud after studying in India. We would hope that this remark only applies to isolated individuals. Among other measures meant to attempt to tackle this possible tendency, students will be engaged in practical services during their stay, and more attention will be given to their attendance at the morning program.

To provide devotees the opportunity to hear, your VIHE servants in Vṛndāvana-dhāma beg for your mercy to be allowed to continue hearing and offering courses. You wanted ISKCON in Vṛndāvana to provide a battery-charging facility for devotees from around the world. Please bless us with opportunities to serve you. On this day we fervently pray for your continuous guidance and protection, so that we may cut through the knot of material attachment and dedicate our lives to watering the seed of service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Govinda, in search of Their eternal shelter. May you kindly forgive our offenses.

With heartfelt gratitude,  
Your servants at the Vṛndāvana Institute of Higher Education.
